## **SIM-QA-11: Demarchic Sortition Jury DoS / Exhaustion Attack**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-10-28 (Round 2 docket)  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-10-30  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: CANONICAL_MERGED]` (Merged 2026-10-30) — Verdict on prior text **`RED_WINS`**; patch **`INCONCLUSIVE`** (holds in an uncalibrated synthetic model; recalibration required).  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 4.2.1, 4.3.4, 4.3.5 (new)  
* **Reproducible Evidence:** [`simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_11_petition_dos.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_11_petition_dos.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_11_petition_dos_results.md) (`--reps 400 --seed 20261030`; synthetic, uncalibrated)

### **1. Red-Team Findings Against the Current Text**
1. **No petition-intake procedure exists.** Art. 4.3.4 gives delegates direct agenda authority. Jurors must therefore either screen everything (exhaustion) or drop petitions at their own discretion, which invites censorship challenges under Art. 1.4.2.
2. **Flooding by a coordinated faction is not blocked.** The faction ban in Art. 4.1.3 cannot be enforced without surveillance of association, which conflicts with Art. 1.4. The defense has to be procedural (see SIM-QA-15-F).
3. **Provocatio spam in nodes under 50 is not blocked.** The "unconditional" right of appeal has no standing or joinder rule, so the load is exported to other watersheds.
4. **Synthetic model** (300 adults, council of 15, 3 dockets/week):
   * Under the current text, a 5% faction drives the share of good-faith petitions heard within 8 weeks down from 99.7% to 2.0%.
   * Jurors exceed 15 h/week in every week of the year.

### **2. Purple Patch (Merged)**
* **New Art. 4.3.5, Petition Intake and Deliberative Capacity:**
  * publication on the civic register is never filtered;
  * agenda placement needs co-endorsement by a fixed share of constituents;
  * every person holds an equal endorsement allowance, never conditioned on labor, conduct, or opinion;
  * agenda slots per session are finite and filled by a public lottery weighted by distinct support;
  * a 3-citizen Triage Panel drawn by lot checks form and merges duplicates, and never judges merit.
* **Art. 4.2.1:** Provocatio belongs to every *party* to a ruling, and appeals against the same ruling are joined.
* **Operational parameters** go to Organic Law OL-9 (quadratic attention tokens, co-signature thresholds, slot budgets, triage rules).

### **3. Residual Weakness (Honest Limits)**
* With the full defense, burnout disappears in every run, and good-faith petitions heard within 8 weeks reach 72.8–79.9%.
* However, a fully mobilized 20% faction still takes about 50% of agenda slots. The defense converts exhaustion into a capped, roughly proportional tax on agenda time; it does not eliminate it.
* The defense depends on Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood holding at scale, which is unproven.
* **Separate docket recommended:** in 3-person panels, a single hostile panelist can force a plebiscite on every 75% matter. The probability that at least one faction member is seated is 0.14 / 0.27 / 0.49 for factions of 5 / 10 / 20%.

---

## **SIM-QA-18: Refusal of a Sortition Summons — Undefined Procedure**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-12  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-12  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — Verdict on current text **`RED_WINS`** (RED-1, RED-2, small-node fill failure); RED-3 and RED-4 **`BLUE_HOLDS`** under H1/H2. RED-2 survives every hypothesis. Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. **Addendum 2026-11-12 (section 8):** maintainer hypothesis H7 (part-time service) **`BLUE_HOLDS` conditionally** on RED-2 (the first lever found, size assumed); RED-1 only if service also becomes compatible with care; online deliberation, staff dependence and complex matters **`RED_WINS` at Set A, `NARROWED` at Set B**. Set B holds up better than Set A. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-12 (section 9):** H7 **accepted in principle with Set B**; Set A withdrawn; Set C kept as a reserve; 60% and 75% matters deliberated and voted in person at every tier (ratio in Organic Law); civic rooms open for every remote session at the member's choice. Draft OL-9.10 (ceiling on raised hours for a named complex matter) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**. Cross-docket check: three open contradictions (X-4 to X-6) in 9.5, not resolved. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13 (section 10):** X-4 OL-9.3 budget restated as a fraction of each tier's share; X-5 emergency ratification may be voted online; X-10 (from SIM-QA-20) the in-person rule covers decisions whose substance needs 60% or 75%, procedural votes may be online; closed list of remote votes drafted as OL-9.11; X-6 open until the SIM-QA-21 merge decision. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13, second batch (section 11):** deferral window three months; Y-4 raised hours voted online confirmed; OL-9.12 service rules for panels of three, interpreters and the Triage Panel drafted; **SIM-QA-11 re-run: council petition capacity not workable at the modelled rate** under the restated budget (about one good-faith petition in five heard); Y-3 linked regional halls at 1,001 seats and Y-12 60% before a repeated emergency decree drafted, both **`NARROWED`**.  
* **Originating Context:** Maintainer review of Chapter IV  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 4.2.2, 4.3.1, 4.3.2, 4.3.3, 5.2.3; addendum H7: Arts. 4.1.3, 4.2 (service share and drawn mandate length per tier), 4.3.2 (proportional release), 4.3.4  
* **Reproducible Evidence:** [`simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_18_summons_refusal.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_18_summons_refusal.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_18_summons_refusal_results.md) (`--reps 200 --seed 20261112`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**: every share, multiplier and effect size is an assumption); addendum: [`sim_qa_18_h7_part_time.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_18_h7_part_time.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_18_h7_part_time_results.md) (`--reps 200 --seed 20261112`; same caveat)

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 4.3.2 calls civic service by lot an "inviolable duty". Neither the Constitution nor the Organic Law Annex says what happens when a summoned citizen refuses, defers, or drops out, or how the seat is refilled.
* No sanction is available. Tier 1 is unconditional (Art. 5.2.1) and Art. 5.2.3 states that sortition eligibility and political rights are never conditioned (the earlier sanction was withdrawn under SIM-QA-15, `RED_WINS`).
* If refusal is simply free, self-selection returns. Voluntary citizens' assemblies report invitation acceptance rates in the low single digits (about 6% on average across 65 assemblies in the OECD database; 4.2% in the Aargau citizens' panel).
* Small nodes cannot absorb refusals. At 50 adults, a council of 15 with six-month mandates and a twelve-month hiatus (Art. 4.3.3) needs 45 distinct people every 18 months, before counting the Local Mediation Panel and the Triage Panel.
* Long full-time mandates (18 months, two years) will draw far more refusals than short ones, so the bias grows with the tier.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors (to be tested)**
* **RED-1 (silent self-selection):** refusal correlates with age, care load, or distrust. The seated body still passes the age/gender/geography quotas of Art. 4.3.1 while being skewed on everything else.
* **RED-2 (strategic refusal):** a faction declines en masse to starve a body of quorum, or accepts en masse while others decline, and captures it without breaking any rule.
* **RED-3 (excusal gatekeeping):** whoever grants exemptions decides who sits. A panel judging "valid reasons" becomes a selection filter.
* **RED-4 (coerced juror):** a citizen forced to serve complies maliciously or disengages, which degrades deliberation more than an empty seat.

### **3. Hypotheses to Test**
* **H1, duty to attend, right to withdraw:** a summoned citizen must attend a short orientation phase and may then withdraw without penalty.
* **H2, deferral and listed excusals:** one deferral without stated reason; excusal on grounds enumerated in Organic Law (illness, bereavement, non-replaceable care), self-certified, never judged on merit. *(Deferral window set at three months by maintainer decision 2026-11-13; section 11.)*
* **H3, stratum-matched alternates:** the same public draw produces ordered alternates per stratum, so a replacement never shifts the composition or the odd parity.
* **H4, published refusal rates:** refusal and dropout rates are published per stratum. A stratum that refuses well above the others triggers a review of service conditions, never of persons.
* **H5, shorter mandates at higher tiers:** see SIM-QA-19 H4.
* **H7, part-time service (maintainer, registered 2026-11-12):** see section 8.

### **4. Stage 2: Model and Red-Team Findings Against the Current Text** *(synthetic, uncalibrated)*
Model: Python stdlib Monte Carlo, not the Julia SIMONE ABM; HEXACO traits not modelled. Node of 50 / 150 / 500 adults; council of 15 (six-month mandate, twelve-month hiatus) and mediation panel of 3 (three-month mandate, six-month hiatus); quota draw on age, gender and geography; refusal swept 0–0.95, uniform or correlated with age and care load (carers 20% of adults, assumed 2.5× refusal). "B0" reads the current text as free refusal with replacement from the general pool. *(Y-15, 2026-11-13: the mediation-panel rows are evidence on the current text only; under X-1 and X-3 the panel is drawn per dispute from outside and takes no one from the node. The council results stand.)*
* **Small-node fill failure: `RED_WINS`.** At 50 adults the hiatus leaves about 17–20 eligible people for 15 council seats. At 10% refusal 7% of council terms already leave a seat empty; at 30%, about half. No hypothesis rescues this (H1–H3 together: 3% at 10%, 30% at 30%). At 150 adults seats fail only from about 80–90% refusal; at 500, not below 95%. At an invitation-style refusal of about 94%, only the 500-adult node fills its council.
* **RED-1 (silent self-selection): `RED_WINS`.** With correlated refusal from 60% upward, carers fall from 20% of the population to about 1–2% of seated members (−18 to −19 points), while stratum-matched replacement (H3) keeps age, gender and geography on target. The quotas of Art. 4.3.1 are met while the body loses its carers.
* **RED-2 (strategic refusal): `RED_WINS`, analytic.** Under free refusal, a faction of share *f* that always accepts while others refuse at rate *r* holds an expected seat share of *f* / (*f* + (1−*f*)(1−*r*)). A 5% faction holds 12% of seats at *r* = 0.6, 34% at 0.9 and 47% at 0.94; a 10% faction holds 65% at 0.94. No rule breaks. Quorum starvation is the mirror case: at 50 adults a handful of refusers can empty seats.
* **RED-3 (excusal gatekeeping): `NOT_TRIGGERED` under current text** (no excusal procedure exists), **`BLUE_HOLDS` under H2** (self-certified listed grounds, never judged on merit).
* **RED-4 (coerced juror): `NOT_TRIGGERED`.** No sanction exists (Art. 5.2.3), so nobody is coerced. The defect is the reverse: "inviolable duty" is a duty without content.

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team Against the Hypotheses**
* **H1 (attend, then may withdraw): `BLUE_HOLDS`, effect assumed.** The model assumes 30% of would-be refusers stay after orientation; with that assumption the carer gap shrinks to −4 to −9 points and the 50-adult fill threshold moves from 10% to 20%. The size of the effect is the assumption, not a finding. Red: orientation costs time of people who will leave; a faction attends and stays regardless (RED-2 unchanged).
* **H2 (one deferral, listed self-certified excusals): `BLUE_HOLDS`, effect assumed.** If care support is arranged before a deferred carer's next summons, the carer gap shrinks to about −5 to −7 points. Red: a self-certified excusal is free refusal under another name. *Guard:* excusals count in the published rates (H4).
* **H3 (stratum-matched alternates): `BLUE_HOLDS` on the stratified attributes only.** Restores age/gender/geography to the quota-fill floor (TVD ≈ 0.03) at 150 and 500 adults, but does nothing for carers (still −18 to −19 points) and nothing against RED-2, since the faction is spread across strata. At 50 adults cells run empty and fallback undoes it.
* **H1 + H2 + H3: best tested combination.** Carer gap about −2 points and composition at the floor at 150 and 500 adults. Still fails at 50 adults.
* **H4 (published rates): `BLUE_HOLDS` as a diagnostic.** It makes RED-1 visible; it cannot reveal RED-2 without identifying who accepts.
* **H5 (shorter mandates at higher tiers):** not modelled; see SIM-QA-19 H4 (`INCONCLUSIVE`).
* **Additional hypothesis H6 (small-node hiatus)** *(loses its object if SIM-QA-21 is merged: no council sits below 150 adults; kept for the record)*: in the 50-adult node, a council hiatus equal to the mandate (6 months) moves the 5% fill-failure point from 10% to 50% refusal (B0) and to 60% with H1–H3. Red: a shorter hiatus means the same people return every year in small nodes, which is the entrenchment Art. 4.3.3 exists to prevent. `BLUE_HOLDS` with that residual.

### **6. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged; H7 accepted in principle with Set B 2026-11-12 (section 9)*
* **Art. 4.3.2 (replaced; revised for H7, 2026-11-12; in-person and civic-room clauses revised by maintainer decision 2026-11-12; in-person clause narrowed to decisions of substance by maintainer decisions X-5 and X-10, 2026-11-13):** "Civic service by lot is a universal prerogative and a civic duty to answer the summons. A summoned citizen attends an orientation session at a first mandate and may then withdraw without penalty, stigma, or loss of any right (Article 5.2.3). Each citizen may defer one summons without stating a reason; excusal on grounds listed in Organic Law is self-certified and never judged on merit. Service occupies a share of a full working week fixed by Organic Law for each tier; citizens who serve receive release from routine labor in proportion to the hours served, and replacement of domestic and care support for those hours, arranged before service begins. Local bodies meet in person. Higher tiers may deliberate remotely, with sessions in person at the ratio fixed by Organic Law; at every tier, decisions whose substance requires sixty percent (60%) or seventy-five percent (75%) are deliberated and voted only at sessions in person; the procedural votes listed in Organic Law, among them the certification of a matter as complex, and the ratification of an emergency decree under Article 4.5.3 may be voted remotely. For every remote session a civic room is open, which any member may use at their own choice. Final votes cast outside a session in person are cast in a protected civic booth, and a public terminal is provided to any member without connectivity. A body may raise its weekly share for a named complex matter within the limit of Organic Law, with release raised in proportion."
* **Art. 4.3.1 (addition):** "Each draw produces ordered alternates within every stratum; a seat left by withdrawal, deferral or excusal passes to the next alternate of the same stratum. Refusal, withdrawal and excusal rates, including counts by excusal ground, are published per stratum without identifying persons; a rate well above the others triggers review of service conditions, never of persons."
* **Art. 4.3.3 (addition):** "In nodes of fewer than one hundred and fifty (150) adult residents, the hiatus after a Neighborhood Council mandate equals the length of the mandate."
  * *Note (SIM-QA-21, updated 2026-11-12):* this addition **loses its object if SIM-QA-21 is merged**, since nodes under 150 adults then hold no Neighborhood Council. SIM-QA-21 H2 and its threshold are accepted in principle, not merged; the addition is kept, not deleted, until the merge decision.
* **Art. 4.2 (amended mandate clauses, H7 with SIM-QA-19 H6):** each tier's fixed mandate is replaced by a share of a full working week and a drawn mandate range fixed by Organic Law. Art. 4.3.3 unchanged (hiatus twice the mandate served).
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%), H7:** service share and mandate range per tier, **Set B, accepted in principle 2026-11-12**: 15 seats 10%, 3–6 months; 101 seats 20%, 4–8 months; 301 seats 50%, 4–8 months; 1,001 seats 50%, 6–12 months (Set A withdrawn; Set C, 1,001 seats 50%, 9–15 months, kept in reserve if learning at the global tier proves longer than assumed); ceiling for a temporary raise on a complex matter (**drafted as OL-9.10, section 9.2**); ratio of in-person sessions for remote tiers (X-5 resolved 2026-11-13: emergency ratification may be remote); closed list of remote votes (**OL-9.11, section 10.2**); civic booth and public terminal standards. All `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** list of excusal grounds (illness, bereavement, non-replaceable care); length and content of orientation; deferral window: three (3) months before a citizen who defers or withdraws is summoned again to the same tier (**maintainer decision 2026-11-13**, replacing "next term of the same body"; section 11); review trigger (provisional: a stratum refusing at more than 1.5× the median); all `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.

### **7. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)**
* **RED-2 is unsolved.** Any right to withdraw hands seats to whoever always accepts; at high refusal a 5% faction can take about half of a council. The only lever found is lowering refusal itself (service conditions, care support), which this model cannot calibrate. This also weakens SIM-QA-19 (15-seat councils) and SIM-QA-11 (petition flooding).
* **Nodes of 50 adults cannot sustain a 15-seat council** under any tested rule once refusal passes 20% (current hiatus) or 50–60% (hiatus equal to mandate). The 50-adult trigger of Art. 4.2.2 (SIM-QA-01) should be re-examined; a separate docket is recommended rather than reopening SIM-QA-01 here. *(Done: SIM-QA-21. If SIM-QA-21 is merged, this residual and the small-node fill finding of section 4 lose their object for the canon; they stay as the evidence for SIM-QA-21.)*
* All effect sizes for H1 and H2 are assumptions. The model shows *how* these rules act, not *how much*.
* No real refusal data exists for duty-based summons without sanction. The 4–6% acceptance figures come from voluntary invitations, which are a different regime.

**Next action:** calibrate refusal against jury-duty and assembly data; port to the Julia SIMONE ABM with HEXACO traits; open a docket on the 50-adult Council trigger (done: SIM-QA-21).


### **8. Addendum (2026-11-12): Maintainer Hypothesis H7, Part-Time Service** *(synthetic, uncalibrated)*
**H7:** no full-time mandates by default. A share of a full working week is fixed per tier in Organic Law; release from labour is proportional to hours served (Art. 4.3.2 now assumes full release); sessions are short; local bodies meet in person, upper tiers online with periodic in-person sessions; final votes are cast in a protected civic booth; a public terminal is provided for members without connectivity. Members may serve again after the hiatus; orientation is given only at the first mandate. *Rationale:* service conditions are the only lever found against RED-2 (section 7).

**Parameter sets** (share of a 40-hour week, drawn mandate range, SIM-QA-19 H6): **Set A** (maintainer; *withdrawn 2026-11-12*) 15 seats 10%, 3–6 months; 101 seats 20%, 2–4; 301 seats 50%, 4–8; 1,001 seats 100%, 6–12. **Set B** (variant) as A but 101 seats 20%, 4–8 and 1,001 seats 50%, 6–12. **Set C** (proposed here) as B but 1,001 seats 50%, 9–15.

**Measurements** ([results](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_18_h7_part_time_results.md)). Assumed learning time in service hours: 12 / 40 / 80 / 160 h for 15 / 101 / 301 / 1,001 seats; returners need a quarter of it.

| Tier | Current text (full-time) | Set A | Set B | Set C |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 15 seats: hours per mandate; share before up to speed; people per year | 1,040 h; 1%; 30 | 78 h; 15%; 40 | same as A | same as A |
| 101 seats | 2,080 h; 2%; 101 | 104 h; **38%**; 404 | 208 h; 19%; 202 | as B |
| 301 seats | 3,120 h; 3%; 201 | 520 h; 15%; 602 | as A | as A |
| 1,001 seats | 4,160 h; 4%; 500 | 1,560 h; 10%; 1,335 | 780 h; 21%; 1,335 | 1,040 h; 15%; 1,001 |
| 1,001 seats: burden (full-time-equivalent months) | 24 | 9 | 4.5 | 6 |
| 1,001 seats: refusal, assumed curve steep / shallow | 94% / 80% (anchors) | 70% / 52% | **51% / 38%** | 58% / 43% |
| 1,001 seats: seats held by a 5% always-accepting faction | 47% / 21% | 15% / 10% | **10% / 8%** | 11% / 9% |

*The acceptance curve (acceptance = 0.8 × exp(−burden / τ), τ fitted to the two anchors) is an assumption; only the ordering of the sets is meaningful.*

**SIM-QA-18 model re-run (RED-1, RED-2) with refusal lowered under H7, by an assumed factor:**
* **RED-1 (carers): `CONDITIONAL`.** Cutting overall refusal by a quarter barely moves the carer gap at high refusal (−18/−19 pp → −13 to −19 pp). It closes to −2 to −6 pp only if part-time service also lowers carers' extra refusal (multiplier 2.5 → 1.5, assumed). H7 helps RED-1 through compatibility with care, not through lower refusal as such.
* **RED-2 (faction): `BLUE_HOLDS`, size assumed.** With refusal 94% cut to 70% (×0.75), a 5% faction falls from 47% to 15% of seats; to 47% (×0.50), 9%. It is the first rule in this docket that acts on RED-2 at all.

**Which set holds up better: Set B.** Set A leaves the 101 tier with 104 hours per mandate, over a third of it spent learning, and 404 new members a year; it also keeps the 1,001 tier full-time, the tier where self-selection is worst. Set B halves the learning share at 101 and roughly halves refusal at 1,001 against the current text. Set C is held in reserve: it gives up some refusal (58% vs 51% steep) for less learning (15% vs 21%) and less churn (1,001 vs 1,335 people a year), and becomes preferable if learning at the global tier is longer than the assumed 160 hours.

**Red team against H7:**
* **Online participation and deliberation quality: `NARROWED`.** Remote sessions lose attention and the informal exchange that changes minds. *Guard (accepted 2026-11-12):* 60% and 75% matters are deliberated and voted at the in-person sessions; the in-person ratio is fixed in Organic Law. Residual: most deliberation at upper tiers is still remote.
* **No secrecy at home (Art. 4.1.3): `RED_WINS` on deliberation, `BLUE_HOLDS` on final votes.** A member deliberating from home can be watched or instructed by a household or an association, and Art. 4.1.3 voids instruction it cannot see. The civic booth protects the final vote only. *Guard (accepted 2026-11-12):* civic rooms open for every remote session, at the member's choice. Residual: instruction before the vote remains invisible.
* **Digital exclusion: `NARROWED`.** A public terminal solves access, not skill, disability or language. *Guard:* assisted access by rotating staff under Art. 4.3.4, never by a party to the matter. Residual: staff assistance is itself an influence channel.
  * *Cross-reference (2026-11-12, SIM-QA-22):* online upper tiers (301, 1,001) seat members who may share no language, so remote sessions run through interpretation or machine translation. If SIM-QA-22 is adopted, drawn interpreters become a transition measure until most members share the common language, and no remote deliberation or vote may depend solely on machine translation. H7 and Set B are unchanged.
* **Dependence on staff, experts and machine briefs (SIM-QA-20): `RED_WINS` at Set A, `NARROWED` at Set B.** With 4 h a week on a council and 64 hours of useful service per 101-seat mandate under Set A, members will read briefs instead of evidence, which is the framing channel of SIM-QA-20 and the shadow-bureaucracy risk of Art. 4.3.4. Set B gives 168 useful hours at 101. *Guard:* adversarial briefs only (Art. 4.4), length caps, and any member may request the primary sources.
* **Complex matters in too little time: `RED_WINS` at Set A, `NARROWED` with guard.** *Guard:* a body may raise its weekly share for a named complex matter within an Organic Law ceiling, with release raised in proportion; continuous renewal (SIM-QA-19 H6) carries a matter across mandates without a full handover. Residual: the ceiling decides whether this works and is not set.
* **Repeat service: `NARROWED`.** Allowing service again after the hiatus lets the always-accepting citizens of RED-2 return more often than others, toward a semi-permanent class (Art. 4.1.2). The draw itself gives returners no priority, and lower refusal shrinks the effect. Residual: not modelled.

**Residual weaknesses (addendum):**
* The size of the refusal reduction is the whole result and is assumed. Without field data on part-time, duty-based summons, H7 is a plausible lever, not a measured one.
* Part-time service multiplies the number of people serving each year (up to 2.7 times at 1,001 seats) and with it the learning cost spread across the tiers.
* Secrecy holds only at the final vote.
* Set B keeps 50% service at the 1,001 tier; whether 20 hours a week can carry planetary matters is untested.

**Next action (addendum):** look for data on refusal under part-time or paid civic service (jury duty with partial release, long-running citizens' assemblies); run H7 in the Julia ABM with an age-and-care-dependent acceptance curve; ~~draft the Organic Law ceiling for complex-matter raises~~ (done, section 9).

### **9. Revision (2026-11-12): Maintainer Decisions and the Ceiling on Raised Hours** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; arithmetic on Set B; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **9.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-12)**
* **H7 (part-time service) accepted in principle with Set B:** 15 seats 10%, 3–6 months; 101 seats 20%, 4–8 months; 301 seats 50%, 4–8 months; 1,001 seats 50%, 6–12 months.
* **Set A withdrawn. Set C stays recorded as a reserve** (1,001 seats 50%, 9–15 months), to be used if learning at the global tier proves longer than the assumed 160 hours.
* **In person:** 60% and 75% matters are deliberated and voted at sessions in person at every tier; the in-person ratio for remote tiers is fixed in Organic Law.
* **Civic rooms** are open for every remote session, used at the member's choice.
* The Art. 4.3.2 text of Section 6 is revised accordingly. Accepted in principle is not merged; the canon is unchanged.

#### **9.2 Draft Organic Law: OL-9.10, Raised Hours for a Named Complex Matter** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(implements the last sentence of proposed Art. 4.3.2; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **What may be raised.** The weekly service share of the members of one body, for one named matter certified complex under OL-9.7. Nothing else: no raise for a session, a backlog or a theme.
2. **Decision.** Sixty percent (60%) of seated members; the vote may be taken remotely (OL-9.11 (b); consequential change of 2026-11-13, section 10.2, for confirmation). The vote names the matter, the raised share, the start date and the end date.
3. **Ceiling.** The raised share never exceeds twice the tier's share, nor three quarters (75%) of a full working week. Under Set B: 15 seats 10% → at most 20% (8 h); 101 seats 20% → 40% (16 h); 301 seats 50% → 75% (30 h); 1,001 seats 50% → 75% (30 h). No raise makes service full-time.
4. **Duration.** One raise lasts at most [eight weeks] and may be renewed once for the same matter by a new 60% vote. No member serves more than [one third] of their drawn mandate under raised hours, all matters together. A raise never changes the sealed mandate length (SIM-QA-19 H6).
5. **One at a time.** At most [one] raised matter per body at a time. Decisions that serve one project count as one matter (the one-project rule of OL-10 point 4); the Triage Panel (OL-9.5) applies it as a check of form.
6. **Release and care.** Release from routine labor rises in proportion from the start date. Domestic and care replacement for the added hours is arranged before the start, and the start is at least [fourteen (14) days] after the vote.
7. **Members who cannot take the added hours.** A member who cannot, on the excusal grounds of Organic Law (illness, bereavement, non-replaceable care), self-certifies, keeps the seat and the vote, and serves the ordinary share. The added hours are for inquiry and reading. The final deliberation and the vote on the matter are held at a session in person on the ordinary schedule, open to every member.
8. **Known before acceptance.** Every summons states the tier's share and this ceiling, so the worst case is known before a citizen accepts.
9. **Published.** For each body: raises voted, hours, number of self-certifications. Never by person.
10. **Not for emergencies.** Emergency decrees (Art. 4.5.3) do not use this procedure.
11. **Above the ceiling.** A matter that needs more is carried across mandates by continuous renewal (SIM-QA-19 H6) or split into named parts. It is never handed to staff (Art. 4.3.4).

*Worst-case burden per mandate (Set B, mean mandate, one-third cap reached):* 15 seats 78 h → 104 h (+33%); 101 seats 208 h → 277 h (+33%); 301 seats 520 h → 607 h (+17%); 1,001 seats 780 h → 910 h (+17%). Arithmetic on the assumed means, not a measurement.

#### **9.3 Red Team Against OL-9.10**
* **H-1, exhausting a minority.** A majority raises hours so that carers and others with less time drop out of the work on the matter. *`NARROWED`.* A 60% vote, care arranged before the start, the ceiling, and point 7: a self-certified member keeps the vote and the final deliberation is on the ordinary schedule. Residual: members on ordinary hours know the matter less well and may defer to those who did the inquiry.
* **H-2, a permanent raise by chaining matters.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* One renewal, the one-third cap per member, one matter at a time, the one-project rule. Residual: a project renamed as a new one; the Triage Panel can check form, not intent.
* **H-3, a ceiling too low, so staff and briefs fill the gap** (SIM-QA-20; Art. 4.3.4). *`NARROWED`.* At 15 seats the most a member can add is about 26 hours per mandate. Continuous renewal carries a matter across members, and the Evidence Panel brief (SIM-QA-20) is written for lay readers. Residual: whether this is enough for a hard local matter is not measured.
* **H-4, the worst case raises refusal.** The burden stated at summons is up to a third higher at 15 and 101 seats and a sixth higher at 301 and 1,001. *`NARROWED`.* Raises are capped and visible in advance. Residual: H7's whole effect on RED-2 rests on refusal falling with burden (section 8); a higher worst case gives some of that back. Not modelled; the H7 script can be re-run with the worst case.
* **H-5, timing by a faction.** Members learn their exit dates at the notice of H6, so a faction can time a raise while its members stay. *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* A raise adds hours; it changes no vote, and the vote on the matter follows the ordinary rules.
* **H-6, labor balance.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* In a node of 150 adults, a full raise of a council of 15 costs 60 added hours a week for at most eight weeks, about 0.4 h per adult per week, drawn from the rosters. At the upper tiers the release is spread over whole continents and is negligible.
* **H-7, the in-person rule slows raises.** The raise is a 60% vote, so it waits for the next session in person at upper tiers. *`NARROWED`.* Residual: a raise needed between in-person sessions waits; the ratio of Organic Law decides how long (see X-5). *(Closed if the change to point 2 of 2026-11-13 is confirmed: a raise is procedural and may be voted remotely.)*

#### **9.4 Red Team Against the Accepted Decisions**
* **D-1, "in person" at 1,001 seats.** *Open question, `NARROWED`.* One room for 1,001 citizens means intercontinental travel for every 60% and 75% vote, with its energy and time cost. Several rooms linked together bring back remote deliberation between rooms. The decision does not say which. Organic Law must define a session in person before the ratio means anything.
* **D-2, in person and carers.** *`NARROWED`.* Travel to sessions in person adds hours and absence that weigh most on carers (RED-1). Care replacement must cover travel time. Residual: not modelled.
* **D-3, the civic room as a signal.** *`NARROWED`, unchanged.* Choosing the room can be noticed, and a household can pressure a member to stay home. Instruction before the vote stays invisible.

#### **9.5 Cross-Docket Check** *(2026-11-12; contradictions listed, not resolved; see also SIM-QA-19 section 9.5)*
* **Agreements verified.** Set B mandate ranges = SIM-QA-19 H6 ranges. Hiatus twice the mandate served in both. Reading the brief counts as service hours (SIM-QA-20 Section 6 link) and fits proportional release. Raised hours apply to matters certified complex under OL-9.7, the same certification SIM-QA-20 uses for Evidence Panels.
* **X-4, the merged juror-hour budget does not match Set B.** OL-9.3 (merged Annex) fixes agenda slots "within a juror-hour budget of roughly ≤ 15 h per juror per week". Under Set B a council member serves 4 h a week, a bioregional delegate 8 h, and members at 301 and 1,001 seats 20 h. The budget is larger than a council member's whole share and smaller than the upper tiers' share. *Open:* restate OL-9.3 as a fraction of each tier's share? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10):** yes, three eighths of the tier's ordinary share (provisional).
* **X-5, emergency ratification and sessions in person.** Art. 4.5.3 gives an emergency decree a thirty-day sunset and requires ratification at 60%. Under the accepted rule a 60% vote is taken only in person. If the in-person ratio leaves more than thirty days between sessions at an upper tier, every emergency decree there lapses before it can be ratified. *Open:* the Organic Law ratio must guarantee a session in person within thirty days of any emergency decree, or an extraordinary session must be provided; not drafted. **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10):** ratification may be voted online, as an exception to the in-person rule.
* **X-6, Art. 4.3.3 addition against "Art. 4.3.3 unchanged".** This docket proposes a shorter hiatus in nodes under 150 adults; SIM-QA-19 H6 states that Art. 4.3.3 is unchanged. They conflict only if SIM-QA-21 is not merged. *Open until the SIM-QA-21 merge decision.* **Confirmed 2026-11-13: no change.**
* **Also affected:** the notice guard of SIM-QA-19 H6 has no object under Set B (SIM-QA-19 X-2); the small-node figures of SIM-QA-21 were computed on full-time six-month councils (SIM-QA-21 section 8.8).

#### **9.6 Residual Weaknesses (Revision)**
* The refusal reduction that justifies H7 is still assumed, and the raise ceiling gives part of it back.
* "Session in person" is undefined at 1,001 seats.
* ~~OL-9.3 and the in-person ratio must be reconciled with Set B before merge (X-4, X-5).~~ Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10).
* Every number in OL-9.10 is a placeholder.

**Next action (revision):** ~~maintainer decisions on X-4, X-5~~ (2026-11-13, section 10); maintainer decision on D-1; re-run the H7 script with the OL-9.10 worst-case burden; field data on part-time civic service unchanged.

### **10. Revision (2026-11-13): Maintainer Decisions on X-4, X-5, X-10 and X-6** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **10.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **X-4:** the OL-9.3 juror-hour budget is restated as a fraction of each tier's service share instead of a flat 15 hours. Drafted in 10.2.
* **X-5:** ratification of an emergency decree (Art. 4.5.3) may be voted online. It is an exception to the in-person rule.
* **X-10 (SIM-QA-20, applied to this patch because it changes Art. 4.3.2):** the in-person rule covers decisions whose substance needs 60% or 75%. Procedural votes at 60%, including certification of complex status, may be taken online.
* **X-6:** stays open until the SIM-QA-21 merge decision. No change.
* The Art. 4.3.2 text of Section 6 is revised accordingly. Accepted in principle is not merged; the canon and the merged Annex are unchanged.

#### **10.2 Draft Organic Law** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
* **OL-9.3, agenda slots (restated, X-4):** "fixed per session by a 50%+1 procedural motion, within a juror-hour budget for petitions of at most [three eighths (3/8)] of the tier's ordinary weekly service share per member; hours raised under OL-9.10 serve the named matter only and never enlarge this budget." The fraction keeps the merged ratio (15 h of a 40-hour full-time week). Under Set B: 1.5 h a week at 15 seats, 3 h at 101, 7.5 h at 301 and 1,001.
* **OL-9.11, votes that may be taken remotely (new, X-5 and X-10):** a closed list. (a) certification of a matter as complex (OL-9.7); (b) a raise of hours for a named complex matter (OL-9.10); (c) a vote of a council to raise a classification challenge (OL-10 point 8); (d) routine scheduling and procedural motions at 50%+1 (Art. 4.5.3); (e) ratification of an emergency decree (Art. 4.5.3). Every other vote that needs 60% or 75% is a decision of substance and is taken only at a session in person. A vote is on the list only by its kind, never by a body's own description; a disputed case is a check of form for the Triage Panel (OL-9.5) and, failing that, goes in person. Remote final votes are cast in a protected civic booth (Art. 4.3.2).
* **OL-9.10 point 2 (consequential, flagged):** "Sixty percent (60%) of seated members, at a session in person" becomes "Sixty percent (60%) of seated members; the vote may be taken remotely (OL-9.11 (b))". A raise of hours is procedural under X-10. *Applied for consistency with the decision; maintainer confirmation needed, since the decision named complex certification and did not name raises.*

#### **10.3 Red Team Against the Decisions**
* **X-5, an easy ratification. `NARROWED`.** The in-person rule was also a brake: a decree had to survive a meeting in a room. Online ratification removes travel as a cause of lapse (the X-5 defect) and with it that brake. *Guards already in text:* the 60% threshold counted on all seated members (Art. 4.5.4), the civic booth for the final vote, the thirty-day sunset, and Art. 4.6 review. Residual: deliberation on the decree is remote, with the secrecy gap of section 8 (instruction before the vote is invisible).
* **X-5, emergency chaining. `OPEN`, unchanged.** Nothing stops a body from declaring a new emergency on the same matter after the sunset (SIM-QA-21 C-6). Online ratification makes the chain cheaper. The renewal rule is still undrafted.
* **X-10, relabelling substance as procedure. `NARROWED` with OL-9.11.** If "procedural" were open, any 60% decision could be called procedural to move it online. OL-9.11 is closed and goes by kind, and a disputed case goes in person. Residual: the list itself is a 60% Organic Law, and adding an item moves votes online; a change is reviewable under Art. 4.6.
* **X-10, the complex certification as a gate. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Certification only calls an Evidence Panel and allows a raise of hours; the decision on the matter is still taken in person. Taking the certification online removes the wait that X-10 found.
* **X-4, a smaller petition budget at the council tier. `NARROWED`.** Under Set B a council member has 1.5 h a week for petitions, against 15 h assumed by the SIM-QA-11 model that set OL-9. Agenda capacity at 15 seats falls about tenfold; the queue of unscheduled petitions grows and the 13-week expiry (OL-9.4) removes more of them. The fast lane (OL-9.2) keeps widely backed petitions moving. Residual: the SIM-QA-11 calibration no longer holds at 15 seats; the model must be re-run with the restated budget before merge. At 301 and 1,001 seats the budget is 7.5 h, half the merged figure.
* **X-4, raised hours as a petition backdoor. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Raised hours never enlarge the petition budget.

#### **10.4 Verdicts**
* **X-5:** the lapse defect of 9.5 is closed. `BLUE_HOLDS` with the chaining residual.
* **X-10 with OL-9.11:** `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals.
* **X-4:** `BLUE_HOLDS` on coherence; `NARROWED` on capacity at the council tier until SIM-QA-11 is re-run.
* **H-7 of 9.3 (raises wait for an in-person session):** closed if the consequential change to OL-9.10 point 2 is confirmed.

#### **10.5 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 2)**
* "Session in person" is still undefined at 1,001 seats (D-1).
* The emergency renewal rule (SIM-QA-21 C-6) is undrafted, and online ratification makes chaining cheaper.
* Petition capacity of councils under the restated OL-9.3 is not modelled.
* The consequential change to OL-9.10 point 2 needs confirmation.

**Next action (revision 2):** ~~maintainer decisions on D-1 and on the OL-9.10 point 2 change; re-run `sim_qa_11_petition_dos.py` with the restated OL-9.3 budget per tier; draft the emergency renewal rule (with SIM-QA-21 C-6)~~ (2026-11-13, section 11); X-6 at the SIM-QA-21 merge decision.

### **11. Revision (2026-11-13, second batch): Deferral Window, Y-4, Service Rules, Petition Re-Run, and Hypotheses Y-3 and Y-12** *(Stage 2 continued; Y-11 measured, synthetic; the rest reasoning on text)*

#### **11.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **Deferral window (SIM-QA-21 X-8):** a citizen who defers or withdraws is not summoned again to the same tier for **three months**, fixed in Organic Law. Applied to the Organic Law line of Section 6 and noted at H2. Effect on the small-node threshold in 11.2.
* **Y-4 (confirmed):** a vote on raised hours is procedural and may be taken online. The consequential change to OL-9.10 point 2 (section 10.2) stands; H-7 of 9.3 is closed.
* **Y-6 and Y-7 (done without further decision):** panels of three, drawn interpreters and the Triage Panel are civic service; hours actually served are counted and release is proportional. Drafted as OL-9.12 (11.4).
* **Y-11 (done):** SIM-QA-11 re-run with the restated budget (11.5).
* **Y-15 (done):** the mediation-panel rows of section 4 are marked as evidence on the current text only (note added there).
* **Hypotheses to draft and red-team, not decisions:** Y-3 (D-1), a session in person at 1,001 seats is held in regional halls linked together (11.6); Y-12 (SIM-QA-21 C-6), a second emergency decree on the same matter within twelve months needs 60% ratification before it takes effect (11.7).
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon and the merged Annex are unchanged.

#### **11.2 The Deferral Window and the Threshold**
The fill model of SIM-QA-21 section 9.2 found that the length of the window moves the safe threshold more than any other parameter. With a three-month window, both edges of the accepted 150 / 120 band stay fully seated in at least 95% of months up to about 80% refusal, against about 70% with six months; the 150 edge alone holds to about 85%, and neither holds at 90% (SIM-QA-21 section 10.2, synthetic). Against six months, a refuser returns to the draw twice as often, so a small council is easier to fill; the price is that a person who does not want to serve is asked again after three months.

*Red team.* **W-1, repeated summons as pressure: `NARROWED`.** A carer who refuses is summoned again four times a year at most, and each summons is a moment of social pressure in a small node, the coercion H1 avoids. *Guards:* withdrawal stays free (Art. 4.3.2 as revised); excusal on a listed ground is self-certified. *Added in drafting, for maintainer confirmation:* an excusal lasts as long as its self-certified ground, so an excused person is not summoned every three months while the ground holds. Residual: a person who simply does not want to serve is asked again each quarter. **W-2, faction share: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** The window changes how often refusers are asked, not the share of seats an always-accepting faction holds per summons (the RED-2 formula of section 4). **W-3, carers return to the pool sooner: `NARROWED`.** If care support is not arranged by the next summons, a three-month window turns one refusal into four. The H2 guard (care support arranged before a deferred carer's next summons) now has three months, not six.

#### **11.3 Y-15: Evidence of Section 4**
The refusal model of section 4 draws a three-month mediation panel from the node. Under X-1 and X-3 that panel is drawn per dispute from outside and takes no one from the node. The council results stand; the panel rows are evidence on the current text only. Noted in section 4.

#### **11.4 Draft Organic Law: OL-9.12, Service by Lot Outside a Tier Share (Y-6, Y-7)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Who.** Members of panels of three drawn per dispute or request (mediation, classification, certification, evacuation re-authorization), interpreters drawn by lot (Art. 4.4.2), members of the Triage Panel (Art. 4.3.5, OL-9.5), and members of challenge and verification juries (Art. 4.4.6) serve under Art. 4.3.2. Evidence Panel members are already covered (SIM-QA-20 X-11).
2. **Hours counted.** Hours actually served are counted: sittings, travel to a session in person, site checks, and reading of the file. Reading is counted by a fixed allowance per file set in advance for each kind of panel, never by tracking what a member reads.
3. **Release and care.** Release from routine labor and replacement of domestic and care support follow the hours counted, as for every drawn member. They hold no tier share.
4. **Triage Panel.** Its monthly term carries a ceiling of [six (6) hours a week]; petitions above what that allows wait for the next session's lottery, never for a longer Triage term. The hiatus of Art. 4.3.3 applies to the monthly term.
5. **Ledger.** Hours served on a panel called by another node or bioregion are counted in the release ledger of the body that called it (the SIM-QA-20 guard of 10.2).

*Red team.* **S-1, padding hours: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Sittings and travel are on the record; reading is a fixed allowance. **S-2, the reading allowance too small for a hard case: `NARROWED`.** A member who reads more than the allowance is not released for it. Residual: hard cases are served by unpaid extra reading, as today. **S-3, interpreters in rare languages: `NARROWED`.** The same few interpreters are drawn often, and their nodes lose their hours repeatedly; point 5 moves the cost in the ledger, not in physical labor (the SIM-QA-20 residual). **S-4, Triage overload under flooding: `NARROWED`.** The ceiling of point 4 caps Triage hours; under a flood more petitions wait. SIM-QA-11 already measured triage load per member (0.4–0.9 h a week in S3, merged run), well under the ceiling.

#### **11.5 Y-11: SIM-QA-11 Re-Run with the Restated Petition Budget** ([`sim_qa_11_petition_dos.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_11_petition_dos.py) `--budget-h 1.5` → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_11_petition_dos_budget_1p5_results.md); `--reps 400 --seed 20261030`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**)
*Model.* The merged SIM-QA-11 model (council of 15, node of 300 adults, 2 good-faith petitions a week, 4 juror-hours per deliberated docket) with capacity restated from the OL-9.3 budget of 10.2: 1.5 h per member per week, so 0.375 dockets a week (about 19 a year), carried as credit from week to week. Without the flag the script reproduces the merged figures exactly (checked).

| Full defense (S3*), faction share f | 0 | 0.05 | 0.10 | 0.20 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Good-faith petitions heard, merged budget (≈ 12 h) | 79.9% | 79.9% | 79.7% | 75.4% |
| Good-faith petitions heard, restated budget (1.5 h) | **18.4%** | 17.3% | 16.5% | 8.1% |
| Heard within 8 weeks, restated | 16.4% | 15.5% | 14.8% | 3.2% |
| Agenda slots taken by the faction, restated (merged) | 0% (0%) | 6.0% (22.6%) | 10.2% (36.7%) | 55.8% (49.9%) |
| Good-faith petitions expired unheard per year, restated | 47 | 48 | 48 | 54 |

**Is council agenda capacity still workable? No, at the model's petition rate.** With 1.5 h a week a council hears about one good-faith petition in five that reaches the queue; the rest expire after 13 weeks (OL-9.4). The defenses still work as defenses: no burnout week in S3*, and at f ≤ 0.10 the faction takes fewer slots than under the merged budget, because the fast lane and weighted lottery fill most of the few slots with broadly backed petitions. At f = 0.20 the faction takes over half the slots. The limit is the budget, not the intake rule: even the whole Set B share of a council member (4 h a week) gives one docket a week, against about 1.6 qualified good-faith petitions a week in this model.

*What would make it workable (not decided, listed for the maintainer):* a petition rate about a fifth of the model's assumption (which no data support or exclude; a node of 150–300 adults may bring far fewer than two a week); shorter dockets (about 1 hour of each member's time instead of 4); a larger budget fraction or share at 15 seats; or more petitions routed to the node assembly or to the Bioregional Assembly. Every one of these touches an accepted decision (X-4, Set B) and is the maintainer's call.

#### **11.6 Y-3 (D-1): Hypothesis, a Session in Person at 1,001 Seats** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(Organic Law, OL-9.13; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. A session in person of the Global Commons Assembly is held in regional halls linked together, not in one place. Every member sits in a hall; no member takes part from elsewhere.
2. Halls number at most [fifteen (15)] and each seats at least [fifty-one (51)] members; a member sits in the hall nearest their residence that meets this size, assigned by rule, never chosen.
3. Every hall sees and hears every other hall in real time; any member may speak to the whole Assembly from their hall; small-group deliberation is in groups drawn by lot across halls, over the link.
4. Final votes are cast in the hall, in a protected civic booth, and tallied across halls on a public signed count.
5. If a hall loses the link, the vote is postponed for at most [seventy-two (72) hours]; after that the members of the cut-off hall vote in their hall, and their signed tally is counted when it arrives within [seven (7) days]. A vote is never taken without a hall that is reachable.

*Red team.*
* **D-1a, remote deliberation between halls returns. `NARROWED`.** The decision's purpose (attention, informal exchange, no watching household) holds inside each hall; between halls the exchange is mediated, as with remote sessions. Point 3 draws small groups across halls so that no hall deliberates only with itself. Residual: the persuasion that happens in corridors happens within halls, not across them.
* **D-1b, halls become continental caucuses. `NARROWED`.** Members of one hall share a region and see each other daily; a hall can harden into a regional bloc, the macro-region tokenism of SIM-QA-19 RED-4 in a new form. *Guards:* point 2 assigns halls by rule; point 3 mixes deliberation groups. Residual: assignment by residence makes halls regional by design.
* **D-1c, cutting a hall's link to block or to exclude. `NARROWED`.** A hostile actor who cuts one hall's link could delay votes or, if votes went ahead without it, exclude a region. Point 5 postpones for at most 72 hours and then counts the hall's own vote. Residual: a vote taken while one hall cannot hear the others loses that hall's part of the deliberation.
* **D-1d, travel, energy and carers. `BLUE_HOLDS`** against one room: travel is regional, not intercontinental; D-2 (care replacement covers travel) applies unchanged.
* **D-1e, languages. `NARROWED`.** Linked halls depend on interpretation across halls (SIM-QA-22: never solely by machine). Residual: interpretation over a link is slower and loses tone.
* **D-1f, the 301 tier. `OPEN`, flagged.** The decision names 1,001 seats. A Continental Chamber of 301 meets the same problem at smaller scale; whether OL-9.13 also applies there is not decided.

**Verdict:** **`NARROWED`** with residuals. Maintainer decisions needed: the numbers of point 2, the postponement rule of point 5, and D-1f.

#### **11.7 Y-12: Hypothesis, Repeated Emergency Decrees** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`
* **Art. 4.5.3 (addition):** "A second emergency decree on the same matter within twelve (12) months of an earlier one takes effect only after ratification under the sixty percent (60%) qualified threshold."
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** "the same matter" means the same named danger, or the same resources or area, whatever the wording of the decree; the twelve months are counted back from the new decree to the end of any earlier decree on the same matter; a disputed case is a check of form for a panel of three drawn under the rule for outside panels, within [forty-eight (48) hours], and the decree waits meanwhile. `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.

*Red team.*
* **E-1, relabelling the matter. `NARROWED`.** A body renames the danger to escape the rule. The Organic Law test goes by danger, resources or area, not by wording, and a disputed case goes to an outside panel. Residual: a genuinely new danger on the same resources (a flood after a drought) counts as the same matter and needs 60% first.
* **E-2, a real second disaster waits. `NARROWED`.** A second flood in ten months now needs 60% before the decree acts. Ratification may be voted online (X-5), so a body can vote within hours if it can gather 60%. Life-safety acts of the Civic Rescue and Fire Guilds (Art. 8.1.2) need no decree, and the mobilization of Strategic Physical Reserves runs under Art. 3.6.2. Residual: a body that cannot reach 60% quickly cannot act by decree on a repeated disaster; this is the price of closing the chain.
* **E-3, chaining past twelve months. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** A third decree is still within twelve months of the second's end, so the rule applies to each link; a decree every thirteen months is not a chain.
* **E-4, Art. 3.6.2. `OPEN`, flagged.** The 50%+1 mobilization of Strategic Physical Reserves carries its own thirty-day sunset review and can also be repeated. The hypothesis does not reach it; extending it is a separate decision.
* **E-5, the canon's own word. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Art. 4.5.3 already calls the sunset "unextendable"; a chain of decrees extends it in fact. The addition makes the word true.

**Verdict:** **`NARROWED`**. Closes C-6 of SIM-QA-21 for Art. 4.5.3. Maintainer decisions needed: the 48-hour form check, and E-4.

#### **11.8 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 3)**
* Council petition capacity is not workable at the modelled petition rate under the restated budget; the remedy touches X-4 or Set B.
* A refuser is asked again every three months.
* Linked halls bring back mediated deliberation between regions.
* A repeated real disaster waits for 60%.

**Next action (revision 3):** maintainer decision on the petition-capacity options of 11.5; decisions on the Y-3 numbers and D-1f, on the Y-12 form check and E-4, and on the excusal-duration sentence of 11.2; field data on part-time civic service unchanged.
