## **SIM-QA-19: Statistical Representativeness of the 3 / 15 / 101 / 301 / 1,001 Tiers**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-12  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-12  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — Verdict on current text **`RED_WINS`** (RED-1, RED-4); RED-3 **`BLUE_HOLDS`**; RED-2 **`NOT_RUN`** (SIMONE challenge 2). Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. **Revised 2026-11-12 (section 8):** maintainer accepts the Art. 4.1.1 line and the Function of the Tiers paragraph in principle and rejects the referral right; H5 (second reading by a renewed council) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**, H6 (drawn mandate length) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**; RED-2 is made worse by H6 at K = 8, so K is raised to 12 in the proposal. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-12 (section 9):** H5 and H6 **accepted in principle** with K = 12 of 15; H5 not extended to bodies of 101 seats and above; the irreversibility guards, the fixed 30-day request window and the rule-set reading date accepted. Draft OL-11 (closed list of irreversible decisions) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**; the certifier of "routine maintenance" is unnamed (**`RED_WINS`** on that exception). Cross-docket check with SIM-QA-18, 20, 21: three open contradictions listed in 9.5, not resolved. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13 (section 10):** X-1 panels of three drawn per dispute or request, no mandate, no drawn range; X-2 three-month notice where service is at least half of a full working week (301 and 1,001 seats); X-3 one rule for every outside panel; I-3 routine maintenance certified by the same outside panel of three (OL-11 point 5a); patches updated; red-team pass on the decisions in 10.2. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13, second batch (section 11):** Y-1 fill row corrected to the three-month deferral window (smallest fully seated node at r = 0.8: 115 adults under H5 + H6, not 80); Y-5 the outside panel of three certifies routine maintenance at every tier; Y-8 hypothesis (fallback panel for the 24-hour evacuation check) drafted and **`NARROWED`**.  
* **Originating Context:** Maintainer review of Chapter IV  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 4.1.1, 4.2, 4.3.1, 4.3.5, 7.4.4 (revision: Art. 4.2 mandate lengths; Art. 4.3.3 unchanged)  
* **Reproducible Evidence:** [`simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_19_tier_representativeness.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_19_tier_representativeness.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_19_tier_representativeness_results.md) (`--reps 20000 --seed 20261112`; Parts A/B exact, Part C on synthetic strata); revision: [`sim_qa_19_h5_h6_second_reading.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_19_h5_h6_second_reading.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_19_h5_h6_second_reading_results.md) (`--reps 2000 --seed 20261112`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**)

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 4.1.1 grounds all authority in "statistically stratified" lottery. For simple random draws, the smaller tiers cannot carry that claim:

| Seats | 95% margin of error | Population 60% in favour, body lacks a simple majority | Population split 50/50, body still reaches 60% | A 10% minority holds no seat |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 3 | ±57% | 35% | 50% | 73% |
| 15 | ±25% | 21% | 30% | 21% |
| 101 | ±10% | 2% | 2% | ~0 |
| 301 | ±6% | ~0 | ~0 | ~0 |
| 1,001 | ±3% | ~0 | ~0 | ~0 |

*Verified 2026-11-12: the script reproduces every figure in this table exactly (Part A); no correction was needed. The margin-of-error column is the normal approximation 1.96·√(0.25/n); for 3 seats the exact Clopper-Pearson half-width is ±45%.*

* Bodies of 3 and 15 are juries (impartial because drawn), not samples. Yet Art. 4.2.1 vests "sovereign municipal administration" in a panel of 3 for nodes under 50 adults, and a council of 15 governs local commons.
* Art. 4.3.1 stratifies on age, gender, and geography only. Fifteen seats cannot carry more strata. Adding criteria needs larger bodies and more personal attributes, which pulls against the non-identifying registry of Art. 7.4.4.
* At 1,001 seats for the whole planet, one seat stands for roughly eight million people. Small peoples and language groups have an expected seat count below one.
* Sample size is the smaller problem. Non-response (SIM-QA-18) biases a body of any size.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors (to be tested)**
* **RED-1 (unlucky draw):** a 15-seat council enacts a 60% measure that the constituency rejects, with no recall path short of the next rotation.
* **RED-2 (small-body capture):** existing SIMONE falsification challenge 2 (a faction of k ≥ 3 capturing 60% of a 15-member council across three rotations).
* **RED-3 (quota gaming):** with only three strata, a faction concentrated inside one age/gender/geography cell is over-seated by design.
* **RED-4 (global tokenism):** macro-region quotas at 1,001 seats present continental majorities as planetary consent.

### **3. Hypotheses to Test**
* **H1, name the function of each tier:** state that the 3- and 15-seat bodies are juries and that representativeness is claimed from 101 seats upward.
* **H2, whole-node decisions below a size threshold:** under a fixed number of adults (e.g. 150), 60% and 75% matters go to all adult residents, extending the plebiscite rule Art. 4.2.1 already applies to 75% matters in 3-person panels.
* **H3, recall to a larger jury:** a fixed share of constituents can refer a council decision to a larger single-issue jury drawn for that purpose.
* **H4, split functions at upper tiers:** a small standing body for agenda and oversight, and large short-term single-issue juries (one to two weeks) for final votes. Larger samples and lower refusal at the same total service load.

### **4. Stage 2: Red-Team Findings Against the Current Text** *(synthetic where stated)*
* **RED-1 (unlucky draw): `RED_WINS`.** A 15-seat council misreads a 60% constituency (no simple majority) 21% of the time, and adopts a 60% measure on a 50/50 split 30% of the time. Drawing from a finite node barely helps: 17% and 27% at 50 adults, 20% and 29% at 150. No text lets constituents correct an unlucky draw before the next rotation.
* **Stratification does not rescue small bodies.** With a strong assumed age-opinion gradient (45 / 60 / 78%), proportional age strata cut the RED-1 error by 12% (relative) at 15 seats; quota fill on all three marginals gives 12–18% instead of 17–21%. Age × gender × geography has 18 cells, so at 15 seats Art. 4.3.1 can be met only on marginals.
* **RED-2 (small-body capture): `NOT_RUN`.** SIMONE challenge 2 needs the Julia ABM and was not run in this pass.
* **RED-3 (quota gaming): `BLUE_HOLDS` (in this model).** Proportional quotas keep expected seats proportional. A 10% minority concentrated in one zone is slightly better protected by geographic quotas (no seat: 21% → 17% at 15 seats). The risk is variance inside small cells, not systematic over-seating.
* **RED-4 (global tokenism): `RED_WINS`.** At 1,001 seats (one per 8.1 million), a people of one million has 0.12 expected seats and an 88% chance of no seat; five million, 54%. Seats cannot carry small peoples at this tier.
* **Coherence defect found:** Art. 4.1.1 says authority emanates *exclusively* from sortition assemblies, while Art. 4.2.1 already sends unresolved 75% matters to a plebiscite.

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team Against the Hypotheses**
* **H1 (name the function): `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Costless and true. Red: relabeling does not change a single decision, so H1 alone is cosmetic. Kept as the honest basis for H2/H3.
* **H2 (whole-node decisions below 150): `NARROWED`.** *(Loses its object if SIM-QA-21 is merged: below 150 adults the node assembly decides internal matters and there is no council to ratify; kept for the record.)* Red: a plebiscite is itself self-selected by turnout (worse than a lottery under non-response, SIM-QA-18), and Art. 4.5.4 counts thresholds against *all seated members*, which no plebiscite can meet. Sending every 60% matter to the whole node also loads it heavily. Narrowed to: 75% matters always ratified by plebiscite in nodes under 150, with threshold counted on votes cast and a turnout quorum in Organic Law.
* **H3 (recall to a larger jury): `BLUE_HOLDS` with guards.** Red: referral is a relitigation and DoS vector (SIM-QA-11), and a cross-watershed jury deciding local commons breaches subsidiarity. Guards: referral uses the endorsement allowance of Art. 4.3.5; one referral per decision; suspensive only for 60% matters, never for emergency decrees or certified routine maintenance; below 150 adults the referral goes to the whole node, above it to a 101-citizen jury drawn from the same bioregion. *(The below-150 branch loses its object if SIM-QA-21 is merged; the referral itself was withdrawn, see section 6.)*
* **H4 (split functions at upper tiers): `INCONCLUSIVE`, not drafted.** Red: a small standing agenda body is the shadow-bureaucracy risk Art. 4.3.4 forbids, and one-week juries depend more on experts (SIM-QA-20). The claimed lower refusal rests on SIM-QA-18, where it is an assumption. Deferred to roadmap study.

### **6. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged; revised 2026-11-12; H5 and H6 accepted in principle 2026-11-12 (section 9)*
* **Art. 4.1.1 (coherence), accepted in principle:** "… emanates exclusively from assemblies of citizens chosen by stratified random lottery (sortition), and from the node assemblies and plebiscites this Chapter provides." (Wording aligned with SIM-QA-21.) *Revised 2026-11-13 (Y-10, SIM-QA-21 section 10.3):* "… and from the assemblies of all adult residents of Article 4.2.1"; "plebiscites" dropped as having no object after merge.
* **Art. 4.2 (new closing paragraph), Function of the Tiers, accepted in principle:** "Panels of three (3) and councils of fifteen (15) are juries, impartial because drawn, not statistical samples of opinion. Statistical representativeness is claimed only for bodies of one hundred and one (101) or more, and every such claim states its margin of error."
* **Art. 4.2 (closing paragraph, addition), Panels of Three and Outside Panels** *(X-1 and X-3, maintainer decisions 2026-11-13)*: "Panels of three (3) that mediate a dispute, classify a matter or certify a decision are drawn for that dispute or request and dissolved when they rule; they hold no mandate. Where this Constitution or Organic Law requires a panel drawn from outside, it is drawn from nodes that are neither a party to the matter nor in the draw pool of a party. After ruling, each member observes the hiatus of Article 4.3.3, counted on the length of service."
* ~~**Art. 4.2.2 (addition), Small-Node Safeguards** (plebiscite ratification under 150 adults; referral of a 60% decision to a plebiscite or to a jury of 101).~~ **Withdrawn by maintainer decision, 2026-11-12:** a suspensive referral can be used to block decisions. Nodes under 150 adults are covered by SIM-QA-21 (no council there). Replaced by H5 and H6 below. *If SIM-QA-21 is merged, the small-node part of this paragraph also loses its object; kept struck through, not deleted.*
* **Art. 4.3.6 (new), Second Reading by a Renewed Council (H5), accepted in principle 2026-11-12 with K = 12 of 15:** "A sixty percent (60%) decision of a Neighborhood Sortition Council may be re-examined once, on request made within thirty (30) days of its adoption by the endorsement procedure of Article 4.3.5. The re-examination is held by the same Council at its first session after the lot has replaced the number of the members who adopted the decision fixed by Organic Law. The decision stays in force meanwhile; only a decision certified irreversible under a closed list of Organic Law waits, and certification is decided within fourteen (14) days by a panel of three (3) citizens drawn under the rule for outside panels of Article 4.2, failing which the decision takes effect. A decision confirmed on second reading cannot be re-examined again; it remains open to repeal by an ordinary new decision. No new body and no plebiscite is created for this. This Article does not apply to bodies of one hundred and one (101) or more, to emergency decrees, or to certified routine maintenance."
* **Art. 4.2 (amended mandate clauses), Drawn Mandate Length (H6), accepted in principle 2026-11-12 with the ranges of SIM-QA-18 Set B:** in 4.2.2–4.2.5 each fixed mandate becomes "a mandate whose length is drawn within the range fixed by Organic Law for that tier"; in 4.2.1 the Local Mediation Panel "serving three-month mandates" becomes "drawn for each dispute and dissolved when it rules" (X-1, 2026-11-13); and a closing sentence: "The public draw that selects a member also draws the length of the mandate. The length is sealed at the draw by a commitment that anyone can verify once revealed, and is disclosed to the member and to the body at the same time, at a notice fixed by Organic Law (no less than one month, and no less than three months where service is at least half of a full working week)." Art. 4.3.3 is unchanged: the hiatus is twice the mandate served.
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** OL-9.9 renewal count K for second reading (**12 of 15, accepted by maintainer decision 2026-11-12**; 8 of 15 was tested and gives a faction a better chance of confirming its own decision, see section 8); closed list of irreversible decisions (**drafted as OL-11, section 9.2**: demolition, felling of mature trees, extraction, irreversible habitat or aquifer change, disposal of a commons asset, each with a threshold); mandate ranges per tier (SIM-QA-18 H7 **Set B**; Set A withdrawn, Set C in reserve); notice periods (see contradiction X-2, section 9.5). All numbers `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.

### **7. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)**
* Councils of 15 remain juries. The patch adds correction paths; it does not make a 15-seat body representative.
* Plebiscites trade sampling error for turnout bias. Their accuracy depends on the non-response problem of SIM-QA-18.
* RED-4 is unsolved. Seats cannot carry peoples below roughly 10 million at 1,001 seats. **Separate docket recommended:** a non-seat channel for small peoples (guaranteed hearing, consent rights over their own territory under Art. 10.1).
* RED-2 (faction capture over rotations) is still untested.

**Next action:** SIMONE challenge 2 with and without the referral path; external review by a survey statistician.


### **8. Revision (2026-11-12): Maintainer Hypotheses H5 and H6, Tested Together** *(synthetic, uncalibrated)*
**Maintainer decisions:** the Art. 4.1.1 coherence line and the Function of the Tiers paragraph are accepted in principle. The referral right (H3 as drafted) is **not accepted**: it must not be usable to block decisions. It is replaced by:
* **H5, second reading by a renewed council:** a 60% decision may be re-examined once, on endorsement request (Art. 4.3.5), only by the same council after the lot has replaced at least K of its members (maintainer provisional: 8 of 15). The decision stays in force meanwhile; only decisions certified irreversible wait. A decision confirmed on second reading cannot be re-examined again. No new body, no plebiscite.
* **H6, drawn mandate length:** the draw also draws the mandate length within a per-tier range (15 seats: 3–6 months), sealed by commit-reveal and disclosed one to two months before the end. Hiatus stays at twice the mandate served (Art. 4.3.3). Bodies renew continuously.

**Measurements** ([results](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_19_h5_h6_second_reading_results.md); retained members assumed to keep their first vote):

| Measure (15 seats) | Current text (fixed 6 months) | H5 + H6, K = 8 | H5 + H6, K = 12 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 60% decision on a 50/50 constituency that survives (one reading → second reading) | 30% (no second reading) | 16% | 12% |
| Well-supported decision (70%) confirmed | 87% (one reading) | 78% | 77% |
| Wait for second reading: mean / 95th pct (months) | 3.0 / 5.7 (next council, all new) | 2.2 / 3.2 | 3.5 / 4.4 |
| Smallest node with ≥ 95% of months fully seated, r = 0 / 0.6 / 0.8 *(corrected 2026-11-13, Y-1)* | ~~45 / 70 / 120~~ **45 / 85 / 135** | ~~50 / 60 / 80~~ **50 / 75 / 115** | same |
| Members in their first month: average / worst month | 17% / 99% | 22% / 44% | same |
| Faction (f = 5–20%) still holds 9 seats at second reading of its own decision | 0.06–0.48 | 0.27–0.66 | 0.14–0.53 |
| Mean length of a faction majority spell (months) | 6–11 | 1.4–3.1 | same |

*A second reading under fixed mandates is not possible as H5 is written (the whole council changes at once), so the "current text" column for second reading reads as "next council".*

*Correction 2026-11-13 (Y-1, maintainer decision):* the struck fill figures came from a model in which a refuser could be summoned again the next month, which no rule provides. The corrected figures are those of SIM-QA-21 section 9.2 ([results](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill_results.md)): the current-text column is its FIX rule (one summons per person per term); the H5 + H6 column is its SETB rule with the three-month deferral window set by maintainer decision 2026-11-13 (SIM-QA-18 section 11). Both dockets now use the same window. The struck values stay as the record of the one-month case, which SIM-QA-21 reproduces (lock 1: 50 / 60 / 80).

**Red team against H5 + H6:**
* **RED-1 (unlucky draw): `BLUE_HOLDS`, narrowed.** H5 halves the error of a 15-seat council at K = 8 and cuts it by 60% at K = 12, without suspending anything. Cost: about one well-supported decision in ten is lost at second reading, the price of re-drawing. Residual: anchoring is assumed; real retained members may persuade newcomers (worse) or reconsider (better).
* **Irreversibility as a blocking lever: `NARROWED`.** If whoever requests the reading can certify "irreversible", every decision waits and H5 becomes the referral veto the maintainer rejected. *Guard:* a closed list in Organic Law; certification by a Local Mediation Panel drawn from outside the node within 14 days, failing which the decision takes effect; one certification request per decision. Maximum delay of an irreversible decision ≈ the K-renewal wait plus 14 days (≈ 4 months at K = 12). Residual: the list's edges will be litigated.
* **Timing by a faction: `RED_WINS` at K = 8, `NARROWED` at K = 12.** With 7 members retained, a faction that passes a decision while it holds 9 seats confirms it on second reading about half the time, more often than under full renewal; and a faction can itself request the reading to lock the decision against re-examination. Its members also learn their own exit dates 1–2 months ahead, so it can bring a vote when it knows its members will stay. *Guards:* K raised to 12 (wait +1.2 months); the request window is fixed (30 days) and the reading date is set by the rule, not the requester; confirmation bars re-examination only, never an ordinary repeal by a later council. Residual: a faction still gains from retention whenever K < 15.
* **Unequal burden of drawn lengths: `BLUE_HOLDS` if release is proportional, `RED_WINS` under full-time service.** Under 3–6 months one member may serve twice as long as another. With hiatus twice the mandate served and release proportional to hours (SIM-QA-18 H7), the burden per month is equal; under the current full-release, full-time reading it is not. H6 depends on H7.
* **Late disclosure under full-time service: `RED_WINS` at full-time tiers.** One to two months' notice is too short to plan a return to work or care after a full-time mandate. *Guard:* notice of at least three months where service exceeds half a working week (drafted above).
* **H5 at 101 seats and above: `NOT_EXTENDED`.** First-reading error at 101 is already 2.3% (0.1–0.4% after a second reading); the gain is small, upper-tier agendas are heavier and a re-examination right adds petition load (SIM-QA-11). Art. 4.6 constitutional review remains the correction path there.
* **Small nodes (alignment with SIM-QA-21):** H5 and H6 apply to councils, which under SIM-QA-21 exist only from 150 adults. If SIM-QA-21 is merged, the small-node rows of this section (smallest fully seated node) lose their object for the canon and stay as evidence. Nodes below 150 have no council and use the assembly, outside dispute panels and *Provocatio*. The withdrawn Small-Node Safeguards paragraph is not needed.
* **RED-4 (global tokenism): postponed.** A separate docket on a non-seat channel for small peoples remains recommended (section 7).

**Residual weaknesses (revision):**
* Any K below 15 lets retained members carry part of the first decision into the second; K = 12 is a compromise between correction and wait, not a fix.
* The average learning cost rises (22% vs 17% of members in their first month) even though the worst month improves sharply.
* H6 is only fair if release is proportional to hours served (SIM-QA-18 H7); under full-time service drawn lengths are an unequal burden.
* ~~The irreversible list and its edges are not drafted.~~ Drafted as OL-11 (section 9.2); edges in 9.3.

**Next action:** SIMONE challenge 2 (faction capture over rotations) with H5 + H6 and K = 8 / 12; test non-anchored retained members; ~~draft the Organic Law list of irreversible decisions~~ (done, section 9).

### **9. Revision (2026-11-12): Maintainer Decisions and the Closed List of Irreversible Decisions** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **9.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-12)**
* **H5 (second reading by a renewed council) accepted in principle**, with K = 12 of 15 (OL-9.9).
* **H5 is not extended to bodies of 101 seats and above.** Art. 4.6 constitutional review remains the correction path there.
* **H6 (drawn mandate length, sealed at the draw) accepted in principle**, with the ranges of SIM-QA-18 H7 Set B.
* **Guards accepted:** a closed list of irreversible decisions, certified by an outside panel within 14 days, one certification request per decision, the decision taking effect if no ruling comes; a fixed 30-day request window; the reading date set by the rule (first session after the K-th replacement), never by the requester.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

#### **9.2 Draft Organic Law: OL-11, Closed List of Irreversible Decisions** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(implements proposed Art. 4.3.6 and OL-10 point 5 of SIM-QA-21; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Rank and enactment.** OL-11 is a planetary standard, enacted and amended by the Global Commons Assembly at 60%. No Bioregional Assembly, council or node may add or remove an item or change a threshold. A change takes effect twelve months after enactment and never applies to a decision adopted before it.
2. **What the list does, and nothing else.** The list has two procedural effects only:
   * (a) a listed act in a decision of a Neighborhood Sortition Council under a second-reading request does not begin until the second reading (Art. 4.3.6);
   * (b) a listed decision is never an internal matter of a node below the council threshold (OL-10 point 5).
   The list does not define irreversible harm for the Precautionary Invariant (Art. 4.4.4, Art. 4.4.5 in SIM-QA-20) or the reserved grounds of Art. 10.1.4. Those apply in full whether or not a decision is listed.
3. **The list (closed).** A decision is listed only if it falls under one of these items and reaches its threshold:
   * (a) **Demolition:** demolition or permanent removal of a habitable dwelling, or of any building of more than [50 m²] floor area. *Not:* repair or refurbishment that keeps the structure; removal of a structure built as temporary less than [two years] earlier.
   * (b) **Felling of mature trees:** felling of any tree with a trunk diameter of at least [40 cm] at 1.3 m above ground; felling of at least [10] trees of [20 cm] or more; clearing of more than [0.1 ha] of woodland. *Not:* coppicing, pollarding, and woodlot or orchard rotation under a management plan adopted earlier by the competent body (the plan itself is listed when it is adopted, if it reaches a threshold).
   * (c) **Extraction:** opening or extending a quarry, mine or pit; any withdrawal from a non-renewable stock, fossil groundwater included. *Not:* withdrawal from a renewable aquifer within the node's envelope; reuse or recycling of material already extracted.
   * (d) **Irreversible habitat or aquifer change:** draining, filling or cutting off a wetland, pond or spring; permanent damming, diversion or culverting of a watercourse; sealing more than [200 m²] of unsealed soil; injection of any substance into an aquifer; release into the wild of an organism not already established there; destruction of a breeding or resting site listed in the bioregional habitat register.
   * (e) **Disposal of a commons asset:** transfer of a commons asset out of the commons; destruction or dismantling of a commons asset that the node could not rebuild from its own means within [one year]. *Not:* recycling at end of service life under certified routine maintenance; transfer to another node of the commons.
4. **Aggregation.** Decisions that serve one project within twelve months are counted together against the thresholds, whoever proposes them (the one-project rule of OL-10 point 4).
5. **Never delayed by the list.** Certified routine maintenance (point 5a); repair or replacement of critical life support; emergency decrees (Art. 4.5.3). An emergency decree may carry out a listed act only where it names an immediate danger to life that the act removes; the act and the named danger are logged and open to review under Art. 4.6.
5a. **Certification of routine maintenance** *(I-3, maintainer decision 2026-11-13)*. Routine maintenance is certified by the same kind of panel as point 6: three (3) citizens drawn under the rule for outside panels, for one request, dissolved when they rule. The body responsible for an asset files a maintenance plan naming the acts, places and intervals; the panel rules within fourteen (14) days only on whether each act keeps the asset in its existing function and form without reaching an item of point 3, never on the merits. A certified plan holds for [twelve months] and is published; acts within it are "certified routine maintenance" for Art. 4.3.6, for Art. 4.4.5 (SIM-QA-20) and for this Organic Law. An act outside a certified plan is not routine maintenance, whatever its label. Repair of critical life support needs no prior certification; it is logged and open to the same panel afterwards. One certification request per plan; a plan refused may be filed again after correction.
6. **Certification.**
   * *Request:* by anyone entitled to request the second reading, within the same 30-day window; one certification request per decision.
   * *While pending:* the listed act does not begin until the panel rules or the 14 days pass. The other parts of the decision take effect.
   * *Panel:* a panel of three (3) citizens drawn for this request and dissolved when it rules (X-1), from nodes that are neither a party to the matter nor in the draw pool of a party (X-3, maintainer decision 2026-11-13; the same rule as the Classification Panel of OL-10 point 8). For a founding node with no centre, drawn from the nearest other nodes.
   * *Mandate:* the panel rules only on whether the decision falls under an item of point 3 and reaches its threshold, on the records and, where needed, on a site check by citizens of the place drawn by lot (Art. 3.4.5). A ruling on the merits is void and an impeachable offense (Art. 4.3.4), as for the Triage Panel (OL-9.5).
   * *One question, one ruling:* where the same decision is before a Classification Panel under OL-10 and a certification panel under this Organic Law, the first ruling on whether it is listed binds the other.
   * *Register:* rulings are published and bind like matters for twenty-four (24) months, as in OL-10 point 8.
7. **Maximum delay.** The K = 12 renewal wait (mean 3.5 months, 95th percentile 4.4 months; section 8, synthetic) plus at most 14 days.

#### **9.3 Red Team Against OL-11**
* **I-1, the list as a blocking lever** (opponents stretch every item to hold every decision). *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Closed items with countable thresholds; one request per decision; after 14 days without a ruling the act may begin; only the listed act waits. Residual: every decision touching an item can be held for up to 14 days.
* **I-2, salami below the thresholds** (nine trees of 39 cm, five slabs of 190 m²). *`NARROWED`.* Point 4 joins decisions of one project. Red: unrelated projects by different proposers each stay below. A cumulative cap per node was considered and not drafted: like the OL-10 cumulative cap (N-2), it would block ordinary work. Residual accepted.
* **I-3, relabelling** ("routine maintenance", "safety felling", "refurbishment"). *`NARROWED` on safety and refurbishment, `RED_WINS` on routine maintenance.* The panel rules on the item, not on the label, and safety felling must take the emergency route and name the danger. But the canon says "certified routine maintenance" (Art. 4.4.4) without saying who certifies it. Whoever certifies maintenance controls the exception of point 5. Not drafted here; a certifier must be named before merge. *(Certifier named by maintainer decision 2026-11-13: OL-11 point 5a; red team in 10.2.)*
* **I-4, too long or too short** (the list serves two uses). *`NARROWED`, a trade-off.* Each item added lengthens the hold of Art. 4.3.6 and takes a matter away from small nodes (SIM-QA-21 C-7). Each item removed lets one reading of 15 drawn citizens do it. The draft keeps the five items already proposed and adds thresholds rather than items. Considered and not listed: destruction of records and archives (conflicts with mandatory purges, OL-6, and with the right to erasure); burial and remembrance sites (cultural, left to the consent rules of Art. 10.1); release of persistent pollutants (already a reserved ground of Art. 10.1.4, never a local matter).
* **I-5, the ratchet rule has no direction here.** *`RED_WINS` on the Annex rule as written, `NARROWED` with point 2.* Adding an item strengthens precaution and weakens subsidiarity (Art. 10.1.3); removing one does the reverse. Either change can be attacked under Art. 4.6 as weakening a guarantee. Point 2 says the list is procedural and does not implement the Precautionary Invariant, so removing an item lowers no precaution: the invariant still applies to any novel structural intervention. Residual: adding an item still narrows node autonomy, and a review jury decides.
* **I-6, fait accompli during certification.** *`BLUE_HOLDS` with point 6.* As drafted, Art. 4.3.6 makes a decision wait only once certified, so the act could start on day one of the 14. Point 6 holds the listed act while the request is pending. *Reading of Art. 4.3.6 recorded for maintainer confirmation; no change to its text.*
* **I-7, who measures the thresholds.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Trunk diameter, area and registered sites can be checked on site by drawn citizens. Residual: "could not rebuild from its own means within one year" (item e) is an estimate, open to dispute, as in OL-10 point 6.
* **I-8, a needed listed act delayed** (an unsafe building, a failing weir). *`NARROWED`.* An immediate danger to life takes the emergency route; anything else waits at most about four months. Residual: a structure that deteriorates slowly but is not yet an immediate danger waits.
* **I-9, bodies of 101 and above.** *`NOT_APPLICABLE` to effect (a).* With H5 not extended, a quarry approved by a Bioregional Assembly is never held by the list. Precaution there rests on the Precautionary Invariant and Art. 4.6. Recorded; not a defect of OL-11.
* **I-10, two meanings of "irreversible".** *`NARROWED`.* The Precautionary Invariant asks for a pathway to irreversible harm (evidentiary, open); OL-11 is procedural and closed. An unlisted act can still be suspended under the invariant; a listed act may be harmless. Point 2 keeps them apart. Residual: citizens will read "not listed" as "reversible".

#### **9.4 Verdicts**
* **OL-11 draft: `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals.** Holds on I-1, I-6, I-7. Narrows I-2, I-4, I-5, I-8, I-10 and the safety and refurbishment part of I-3. **`RED_WINS` on the routine-maintenance exception** until the canon or Organic Law names who certifies maintenance *(certifier named 2026-11-13; verdict revised in 10.3)*. Every threshold is a placeholder, chosen to catch a felled oak or a new quarry and to leave a garden shed or a pruned hedge alone; none is derived.

#### **9.5 Cross-Docket Check: SIM-QA-18, 19, 20, 21** *(2026-11-12; contradictions listed, not resolved)*
* **Agreements verified.** Councils of 15: drawn 3–6 months (SIM-QA-18 Set B = section 8 here). Hiatus twice the mandate served (SIM-QA-18 Art. 4.2 patch = H6). H5 applies only to councils of 15, which under SIM-QA-21 exist only from 150 adults. 30-day windows (H5 request = OL-10 classification). 14-day panel rulings (certification = classification). The irreversible list has the same five items here and in OL-10 point 5. 60% and 75% thresholds: H5 covers 60% decisions only; SIM-QA-18 puts 60% and 75% votes in person; no conflict.
* **X-1, panels of three have no drawn range.** H6 rewrites the mandates of Arts. 4.2.1–4.2.5, which include the Local Mediation Panel (canon: three months). Set B gives ranges for 15, 101, 301 and 1,001 seats only. SIM-QA-21 draws mediation and classification panels from outside the node, which reads as per dispute. *Open:* panels of three keep a fixed three-month mandate, take a range, or are drawn per dispute? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10):** drawn per dispute, dissolved when they rule, no mandate, no range.
* **X-2, the three-month notice guard has no object under Set B.** H6 sets notice of at least three months "where service exceeds half of a full working week". That guard was written for the full-time 1,001 tier of Set A, now withdrawn. Under Set B no tier exceeds 50%, so every tier, including 20 h a week at 301 and 1,001 seats, gets the one-month minimum. *Open:* delete the clause, or change "exceeds half" to "is at least half" so it reaches 301 and 1,001 seats? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10):** "is at least half"; applied in Section 6.
* **X-3, two outside panels drawn from different pools.** Art. 4.3.6 draws the certification panel "from outside the node". OL-10 point 8 draws the Classification Panel "from neither the node nor the larger node". For a centre-council decision that concerns an attached small node, "outside the node" can draw from that small node, which is a party: the defect SIM-QA-21 found as W-1. *Open:* the alignment proposed in OL-11 point 6. **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 10):** one rule for every outside panel (neither a party to the matter nor in the draw pool of a party); applied in Section 6 and OL-11 point 6; OL-10 point 8 aligned in SIM-QA-21.
* Contradictions on hours and in-person sessions are listed in SIM-QA-18 section 9.5; on the small-node threshold and threshold arithmetic in SIM-QA-21 section 8.8; on complex-matter certification in SIM-QA-20 section 9.

#### **9.6 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 2)**
* ~~The routine-maintenance exception has no named certifier.~~ Named 2026-11-13 (OL-11 point 5a); residuals in 10.4.
* Thresholds are placeholders; no script has replayed real decisions against them.
* Per-project thresholds miss slow loss from unrelated projects.
* The ratchet rule cannot say whether adding an item strengthens or weakens a guarantee; review juries will decide case by case.
* The list delays decisions of councils of 15 only. Upper tiers rely on the Precautionary Invariant and Art. 4.6.

**Next action (revision 2):** ~~maintainer decisions on X-1, X-2, X-3~~ (2026-11-13, section 10); maintainer decision on the reading of I-6; ~~name a certifier of routine maintenance~~ (2026-11-13, OL-11 point 5a); add OL-11 thresholds to the SIM-QA-21 replay script of typical small-node decisions; SIMONE challenge 2 unchanged.

### **10. Revision (2026-11-13): Maintainer Decisions on X-1, X-2, X-3 and I-3** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **10.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **X-1:** panels of three (mediation, classification, certification) are drawn per dispute and dissolved when they rule. They have no mandate and no drawn range. H6 therefore rewrites the mandates of Arts. 4.2.2–4.2.5 only; the three-month mandate of the Local Mediation Panel in Art. 4.2.1 becomes a per-dispute draw. Applied in Section 6 (H6 clause and new closing paragraph of Art. 4.2).
* **X-2:** the three-month notice of the drawn mandate length applies where service is at least half of a full working week, so at 301 and 1,001 seats under Set B. Applied in Section 6.
* **X-3:** one rule for every outside panel: drawn from nodes that are neither a party to the matter nor in the draw pool of a party. Applied in Art. 4.3.6, in the closing paragraph of Art. 4.2 and in OL-11 point 6; OL-10 point 8 aligned in SIM-QA-21.
* **I-3:** certified routine maintenance is certified by the same outside panel of three that certifies irreversibility. Drafted as OL-11 point 5a.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

#### **10.2 Red Team Against the Decisions**
* **X-1, per-dispute panels and urgent matters. `NARROWED`.** The canon gives the Local Mediation Panel "domestic safety" and "housing restitution", and Art. 8.1.2 requires its re-authorization of any evacuation order beyond twenty-four (24) hours. A panel drawn per dispute from outside the node takes time to seat, where a standing panel could sit the same day. Immediate danger to a person falls to the Civic Peace and Rescue Guilds (Art. 8.1.2) and the emergency route, so the delay concerns rulings, not protection; the 24-hour evacuation check is the hard case. *Guard (proposed, Organic Law):* the draw runs the day the request is filed; a maximum time to seat a panel, provisional [twenty-four (24) hours] for evacuation re-authorization and [seventy-two (72) hours] for domestic safety and restitution; remote sitting allowed for these rulings. Residual: a node far from any other node, or cut off, may not seat a panel in 24 hours; the evacuation order then lapses, which is the canon's intent but may be unsafe. Not modelled.
* **X-1, loss of continuity. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** A standing panel learned the node; a per-dispute panel does not. For dispute panels that is the point: no three people become the node's standing judges. Continuity rests on published rulings, as for Evidence Panels (SIM-QA-20 Decision 1).
* **X-1, hiatus with no mandate. `NARROWED`, drafted here.** Art. 4.3.3 counts the hiatus on the mandate. With no mandate, the closing paragraph counts it on the length of service. *Added in drafting, for maintainer confirmation:* this sentence was not part of the decision. Without it, the same three people could be drawn into every dispute from a small pool.
* **X-1, the Triage Panel is not covered. `NOT_APPLICABLE`, recorded.** The Triage Panel of Art. 4.3.5 (OL-9.5, drawn monthly) checks the form of every petition and needs a standing term. It is a panel of three with a mandate and stays outside X-1. Listed in the cross-docket check of 2026-11-13.
* **X-2. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** At 20 hours a week a member needs time to arrange work and care around the end of service; three months covers it. Residual: at 101 seats (8 h a week) one month is the minimum, assumed enough.
* **X-3, the pool shrinks. `NARROWED`.** Excluding every node in a party's draw pool removes the centre and all its attached small nodes from a centre–node dispute. In a sparse region the nearest eligible nodes may be far. *Guard:* the founding-node fallback stays (nearest other nodes). Residual: travel and time for distant panels, not modelled.
* **X-3, "party" undefined. `NARROWED`.** For a dispute between residents, the parties are persons; their node is in their draw pool, so the panel comes from outside the node and outside its centre. For a classification dispute, the node and the centre are parties. For a certification request, the deciding body and the requester are parties. *Guard:* Organic Law names the parties for each kind of panel. Residual: a requester from a third node makes that node a party and removes it from the pool; one request per decision limits this.
* **I-3, maintenance by plan. `NARROWED`.** A panel per act would hold every repair and flood the draw, so OL-11 point 5a certifies a plan for twelve months. Red: a plan written broadly ("all works on the north wing") hides a listed act. *Guard:* the panel rules act by act inside the plan, and an act that reaches an item of OL-11 point 3 is never routine maintenance. Red: whoever writes the plan frames it; the panel sees only what is filed. *Guard:* plans are published and open to challenge; an act outside the plan is not routine maintenance whatever its label.
* **I-3, reach beyond councils. `OPEN`, flagged** *(confirmed 2026-11-13, Y-5: the outside panel of three certifies routine maintenance at every tier; section 11)*. The decision names the panel that certifies irreversibility, which serves councils of 15 only (H5 is not extended to 101 seats and above). "Certified routine maintenance" also appears in proposed Art. 4.4.5 (SIM-QA-20) for every tier. OL-11 point 5a as drafted applies the same panel at every tier. That follows the wording of Art. 4.4.5 but was not stated in the decision; maintainer confirmation needed.
* **I-3, critical life support. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Repair of critical life support needs no prior certification and is reviewed afterwards, so no pump, grid or water repair waits for a panel.

#### **10.3 Verdicts**
* **X-1, X-2, X-3: `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals** (seating time, distant pools, definition of parties).
* **OL-11 routine-maintenance exception: `RED_WINS` → `NARROWED`.** A certifier is named and the plan is checked act by act. The framing power of whoever writes the plan remains.

#### **10.4 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 3)**
* Seating time for per-dispute panels drawn from outside is not measured; the 24-hour evacuation re-authorization of Art. 8.1.2 is the tightest case.
* The hiatus counted on length of service (closing paragraph of Art. 4.2) needs maintainer confirmation; ~~the reach of OL-11 point 5a beyond councils~~ confirmed 2026-11-13 (Y-5).
* The maintenance plan is written by the body that does the work.
* The Triage Panel keeps a standing monthly term outside X-1.

**Next action (revision 3):** maintainer confirmation of the hiatus sentence; ~~and of OL-11 point 5a at every tier~~ (confirmed 2026-11-13, section 11); Organic Law list of parties per kind of panel and seating times; seating time for outside panels in the SIM-QA-21 replay script.

### **11. Revision (2026-11-13, second batch): Y-1, Y-5 and the Hypothesis Y-8** *(Stage 2 continued; Y-1 measured, synthetic; the rest reasoning on text)*

#### **11.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **Y-1:** the fill row of section 8 is corrected so that this docket and SIM-QA-21 use the same three-month deferral window. Applied in section 8 (corrected figures 45 / 85 / 135 for the current text and 50 / 75 / 115 under H5 + H6, at r = 0 / 0.6 / 0.8).
* **Y-5 (confirmed):** the outside panel of three certifies routine maintenance at every tier. OL-11 point 5a stands as drafted; the flag of 10.2 (I-3, reach beyond councils) is closed.
* **Y-8 (hypothesis to draft and red-team, not a decision):** for the 24-hour evacuation check of Art. 8.1.2, the panel may be drawn from the nearest available nodes, including the node itself under the kin rule, when an outside panel cannot be seated in time.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

*What the correction changes.* Section 8 said continuous renewal lowers the smallest fully seated node at 80% refusal from 120 to 80 adults. With the three-month window it lowers it from 135 to 115. The direction stands; the size is about a fifth of what section 8 reported. The small-node rows remain evidence only (SIM-QA-21: no council below 150 adults).

#### **11.2 Y-8: Draft Organic Law, Evacuation Re-Authorization** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(implements Art. 8.1.2 under X-1 and X-3; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Ordinary rule.** The re-authorization of an evacuation order beyond twenty-four (24) hours (Art. 8.1.2) is given by a Local Mediation Panel of three (3) drawn for that order under the rule for outside panels (Art. 4.2), sitting remotely where needed. The draw runs automatically when the order is issued, not when the 24 hours end.
2. **Fallback.** If the published draw log shows that no outside panel can be seated within the 24 hours, the panel is drawn from the nearest nodes that can seat it in time, the node under evacuation included, with no first-degree kin or domestic partners serving together, and with no one who issued, executes or is subject to the order, or who would gain a dwelling or a use right from it.
3. **Confirmation.** A re-authorization given under point 2 holds for at most [seventy-two (72) hours]. Within that time an outside panel under point 1 confirms or ends it. *(Added in drafting; not part of the hypothesis as given.)*
4. **Lapse.** If no panel can be seated under point 1 or point 2, the order lapses at 24 hours, as Art. 8.1.2 intends. Life-safety acts of the Civic Rescue and Fire Guilds that need no order continue.
5. **Record.** Every fallback is logged with the draw log and published; the panel rules only on whether the danger named in the order persists and whether the order is limited to it, never on displacement for any other purpose.

#### **11.3 Red Team Against Y-8**
* **E-1, the node evacuates a neighbour for its own ends** (administrative displacement, which Art. 8.1.2 bars). *`NARROWED`.* The fallback panel may now hold residents of the node, the setting where a majority can single out a household. *Guards:* point 2 excludes anyone who issued, executes or is subject to the order and anyone who would gain from it; point 3 limits the fallback ruling to 72 hours before an outside panel confirms; point 5 limits the ruling to the named danger. Residual: three neighbours not excluded by any rule may still share the majority's interest.
* **E-2, "cannot be seated in time" as a pretext.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* The fallback is triggered by the draw log, not by a claim of the authority that issued the order; the draw starts when the order is issued, so the 24 hours are used for the outside draw first. Residual: a partition or link failure that someone provokes on purpose would trigger the fallback; it still ends at 72 hours.
* **E-3, the fallback becomes the rule in sparse regions.** *`NARROWED`.* Where outside panels routinely fail to seat in 24 hours, most re-authorizations are first given by nearby or local citizens. The 72-hour outside confirmation keeps the outside check, one step later. Residual: the seating time of outside panels is not measured (section 10.4); if it exceeds 72 hours as well, point 3 fails and needs its own fallback. Not drafted.
* **E-4, nobody eligible in a small node.** *`NARROWED`.* In a node of 30, kin, partners, the evacuated and those who gain may leave fewer than three people. The order then lapses (point 4), which is the canon's intent and may be unsafe. Residual accepted; protection of persons rests on the Guilds, not on the order.
* **E-5, coherence with X-1 and X-3.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* The panel is still drawn per order and dissolved when it rules; only the pool changes in the fallback. The founding-node fallback of X-3 (nearest other nodes) is the same idea; Y-8 adds the node itself as the last pool.

**Verdict:** **`NARROWED`**. The hypothesis closes the seating gap of 10.2 (X-1, urgent matters) at the cost of a local panel for up to 72 hours. Maintainer decisions needed: point 3 (72-hour outside confirmation, added in drafting) and point 2 (exclusion of anyone who would gain from the order, added in drafting).

#### **11.4 Red Team Against Y-5**
* **Maintenance plans at the upper tiers. `NARROWED`.** A continental backbone or a bioregional reservoir files a maintenance plan certified by three citizens. The panel rules only on whether each act keeps the asset in its existing function and form without reaching an item of OL-11 point 3; it does not judge the engineering. Red: a large plan has thousands of acts, so three people check a sample, and the body that writes the plan frames it (10.2, I-3). *Guards already drafted:* plans are published and open to challenge; an act outside a certified plan is not routine maintenance. Residual: certification of large plans is thin; a plan may need to be split by asset, which Organic Law can require. Not drafted.

#### **11.5 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 4)**
* The fill figures, corrected or not, are synthetic and uncalibrated.
* The Y-8 fallback puts local citizens on evacuation rulings for up to 72 hours.
* Seating time of outside panels is still not measured.
* Large maintenance plans are certified by small panels.

**Next action (revision 4):** maintainer decisions on Y-8 (points 2 and 3); seating time for outside panels in the SIM-QA-21 replay script; Organic Law list of parties per kind of panel; SIMONE challenge 2 unchanged.

## **SIM-QA-20: Rival Expert Panels — Advocacy Capture and Precautionary Veto**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-12  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-12 (qualitative O-ASIS red-team pass; no simulation)  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — Verdict on current text **`RED_WINS`** on all five vectors; hypotheses H1–H6 hold only with guards, H6 **narrowed**. Addendum (Section 8): H7/H8 and RED-6/RED-7 red-teamed on text, measurement **`NOT_RUN`**. Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-12 (Section 6):** core patch accepted in principle; Decision 1 (per-case Evidence Panels) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residual** (continuity, thin specialties); Decision 2 (open practitioner registers, lay challenge juries) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with guards**, criteria drafting remains a **narrowed** capture surface. **Second revision (Section 6.2):** Decision 3 (machine pre-check, "meets" or "to be verified" only) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with guards**; Decision 4 (one global register, filters at the draw) **`NARROWED`**, with seeding by factions or schools, Art. 7.4.4 privacy, language and rare specialties as residuals; red-team guards pending maintainer decision. **Cross-docket check 2026-11-12 (Section 9):** two open contradictions (X-10, X-11) on complex-matter certification in person and service rules for panel members, not resolved. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13 (Section 10):** all ten red-team guards of Section 6.2 accepted in principle; drawn interpreters a transition measure pending SIM-QA-22; X-10 procedural votes at 60% (complex certification) may be online; X-11 Evidence Panel service is civic service under the revised Art. 4.3.2; privacy review and external critic still required before any merge. **Revision 2026-11-16 (Section 11):** first-pass privacy review (not a specialist's): Art. 4.4.6 as drafted **not compatible with Art. 7.4.4**; maintainer decisions A to J (pseudonym by default, withdrawal, sealed evidence in every field, countable attestations, purpose limit, lighter 4.4.6); Decision I red-teamed: **`RED_WINS`** on longer attestation rings and on injected pre-check filings at the current one-in-twenty re-check, and in part on single false entries and identity-only conflicts; **`NARROWED`** elsewhere; options listed, none chosen; revised 4.4.2 and 4.4.6 drafted (Chapter IV 2,113 of 2,150); specialist privacy review and external critic still required before merge. **Second batch 2026-11-16 (Section 11.8):** cycle exclusion up to four, re-check one in five with public synthetic testing, blind conflict check, sample audit of juries, A and B clauses and the two bounds kept in canon; Decision I now **`NARROWED` on every vector**; Chapter IV 2,136 of 2,150; ceiling decision postponed until Y-12 and Y-3.  
* **Originating Context:** Maintainer review of Chapter IV  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 3.4.4, 3.4.5, 4.3.3, 4.3.5, 4.4.1–4.4.4, 4.6, 7.1.2, 7.2, 7.3, 7.4, 7.4.4, 7.5, 7.6.1, 8.4.3, 10.6.4; Organic Law OL-9.7, OL-1.4
* **Evidence:** reasoning on the text only. No quantitative claim is made; nothing below is empirical.

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 4.1.2 abolishes professional politics, but Art. 4.4.2 stages every complex proposal as a duel between two rival panels. The format rewards advocacy skill. It can bring the professional persuader back in an evidentiary role.
* Internal inconsistency: Art. 8.4.3 abolishes adversarial trials in favour of Fact-Centric Inquiry, while Art. 4.4.2 mandates adversarial cross-examination for policy.
* The text requires panels to be "independent" and free of grants or debt. It does not say who nominates, selects, or seats them. Sortition of practitioners appears only as the deadlock fallback of Art. 4.4.4.
* Two panels imply two sides of equal weight, even where the evidence is lopsided or where there are more than two positions.
* Art. 4.4.4 withholds approval when panels produce confusion. Confusion is therefore a winning strategy for whoever wants to block.
* Experts have no hiatus rule comparable to Art. 4.3.3, so a standing expert class can form.
* Empirical reviews of citizens' assemblies find that expert influence acts through framing, deference to authority, and information asymmetry, and is strongest where time is short and expert selection is opaque.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors (to be tested)**
* **RED-1 (nomination capture):** a guild or faction controls who is eligible for either panel, so both "rivals" share the same premises.
* **RED-2 (manufactured doubt):** one panel deliberately muddies the record to trigger the precautionary suspension of Art. 4.4.4.
* **RED-3 (rhetorical capture):** the more persuasive panel wins regardless of evidence (SIMONE Layer 2 already models rhetorical capture).
* **RED-4 (false balance):** a fringe position gains half the floor time by being assigned a panel.
* **RED-5 (repeat players):** the same experts recur across sessions and tiers and learn to steer jurors.

### **3. Hypotheses to Test**
* **H1, experts by lot:** panels are drawn from open practitioner registers as the default, with the Art. 4.3.3 hiatus applied to experts.
* **H2, separate evidence from advocacy:** a drawn panel writes the factual brief. Proponents and opponents speak afterwards, declared as parties.
* **H3, juror-led inquiry:** jurors conduct the questioning and may call further witnesses, aligning Art. 4.4 with Art. 8.4.3.
* **H4, disagreement map:** experts must file a joint written statement of what they agree on, where they differ, and which observation would change their position.
* **H5, closed deliberation:** final deliberation takes place without experts in the room.
* **H6, symmetric burden:** inaction is presented as an option with its own stated consequences, so that confusion does not default to one side.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team Against the Current Text (Art. 4.4)**
* **RED-1 (nomination capture): `RED_WINS`.** Art. 4.4.2 requires panels to be "independent" but names no selector. Agenda authority rests with delegates (Art. 4.3.4), so either the 15 jurors pick their own witnesses or someone unnamed does. A guild that controls who counts as a practitioner can staff both "rival" panels with people who share its premises. Nothing in Art. 4.1.3 reaches this, because a guild that nominates experts does not nominate *delegates*.
* **RED-2 (manufactured doubt): `RED_WINS`.** Under Art. 4.4.4, confusion produced by a panel is itself the trigger for withholding approval. Any actor who prefers the status quo on a novel structural intervention needs only a panel that muddies the record. The status quo is not always *pro natura*: replacing a failing levee with a new design is a novel structural intervention. The deadlock fallback hands the decision to a panel of practitioners, which contradicts Art. 4.4.1 (experts never vote).
* **RED-3 (rhetorical capture): `RED_WINS` (qualitative).** A duel rewards the better advocate. Art. 4.4.3 puts the burden of intelligibility on experts but gives jurors no inquiry powers, so the side that explains more fluently wins regardless of evidence. Magnitude is unknown; the SIMONE Layer 2 run was not done.
* **RED-4 (false balance): `RED_WINS`.** "Two rival panels" fixes the number of sides at two. A fringe position receives half the floor; a question with three positions is forced into two.
* **RED-5 (repeat players): `RED_WINS`.** Art. 4.3.3 binds jurors, not witnesses. The same experts can appear in every session at every tier and learn how to steer juries: the professional persuader returns in an evidentiary role.
* **Coherence defects:** Art. 4.4.2 mandates adversarial cross-examination while Art. 8.4.3 abolishes adversarial trials for Fact-Centric Inquiry. Art. 4.6.2 makes constitutional review juries deliberate "under Article 4.4", and OL-1.4 runs the biosecurity registry on rival testimony, so every defect above propagates to constitutional review and biosecurity.

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team Against the Hypotheses**
* **H1 (experts by lot, hiatus): `BLUE_HOLDS` with guards.**
  * Red: the gate moves to register admission. If a guild certifies who enters the register, RED-1 returns one step earlier. *Guard:* admission on published criteria of demonstrated work, open to anyone meeting them; no guild, association or body may gatekeep; admissions logged and reviewable under Art. 4.6.
  * Red: a random draw reproduces the field's majority and can bury a correct heterodox minority. *Guard:* any party may add declared witnesses (H2), and jurors may call more (H3).
  * Red: small specialties (four hydrogeologists in a bioregion) cannot carry a hiatus. *Guard:* hiatus per subject matter, with a draw from other bioregions when the local pool is below an Organic Law minimum.
  * Red: a register is a list of named persons with attributes, in tension with the non-identifying registry of Art. 7.4.4. *Guard:* practitioner registers are voluntary and separate from the civic register.
* **H2 (evidence before advocacy): `BLUE_HOLDS` only with H3 and H4.** Red: whoever writes the brief frames the question, and framing is what Art. 4.3.4 forbids staff to do. A drawn brief panel is a single point of framing capture unless dissent is annexed in full (H4) and jurors may reject the brief and inquire further (H3).
* **H3 (juror-led inquiry): `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Aligns Art. 4.4 with Art. 8.4.3. Red: lay questions may be weak, and a facilitator could steer them. Residual: question quality; facilitators remain bound by Art. 4.3.4.
* **H4 (disagreement map): `BLUE_HOLDS` with guard.** Red: experts can collude on a false consensus, or name an unfalsifiable condition for changing their mind. *Guard:* each member signs individually; a position that names no observation able to change it is recorded as such in the brief.
* **H5 (closed deliberation): `BLUE_HOLDS` with guard.** Red: without experts present, jurors misremember technical detail. *Guard:* written questions during deliberation, answered on the public record. Deliberation is closed; evidence and answers stay public.
* **H6 (symmetric burden): `NARROWED`.** Red: a fully symmetric burden repeals the precautionary invariant (*In dubio pro natura et vita*), and a Legacy actor will argue that inaction harms (lost output, lost jobs). Narrowed to: action **and** inaction are both briefed with their consequences, but suspension needs a specific, plausible pathway to irreversible harm that the proposal does not close. Confusion or disagreement alone never suspends. RED-2 must then fabricate a concrete harm pathway, which the evidence panel can check.

### **6. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged; revised 2026-11-12*
**Maintainer decisions (2026-11-12).** Accepted in principle: drawn Evidence Panels in place of the rival-panel duel, the written brief with disagreement map, juror-led inquiry, closed deliberation, and the rule that confusion alone never suspends approval. **Decision 1:** experts are drawn per case, not per mandate; a panel is drawn for one proposal and dissolved when it reports; no standing expert body exists; continuity comes from published briefs; the hiatus per subject matter stays. **Decision 2:** open admission to practitioner registers on written criteria, open challenge, challenges decided by a lay jury on the criteria only, ~~everything logged publicly~~ *(superseded 2026-11-16 by Decision I, Section 11.2: evidence sealed, aggregates only)*. H7 and H8 (Section 8) stay out of this patch until the framing-sensitivity script exists.

**Further maintainer decisions (2026-11-12, second revision).** **Decision 3, machine pre-check of admission:** an open model meeting Art. 7.2 compares the filed evidence with the written, countable criteria and returns only "meets" or "to be verified", never a rejection, citing the document and passage it relied on. "Meets" admits the entry, which stays open to challenge; "to be verified" goes to the drawn citizen jury, which decides. No model decides an exclusion or a challenge (Arts. 7.1.2, 7.5.3). **Decision 4, one global register:** a single Practitioner Register with one set of admission criteria for all; no local or per-bioregion registers. Subject matter, place (fixed local and outside shares), hiatus per subject matter and conflict of interest are filters at the draw, not separate lists. Subject categories can be added by petition. The register is replicated across watersheds under the key dispersion of Art. 7.4. **Rule on evidence (to test):** signed, tamper-evident ~~public~~ records (Arts. 7.3, 7.4) *("public" superseded 2026-11-16 by Decision I, Section 11.2)*, no named ledger technology (SIM-QA-17); evidence published by the practitioner's own choice (Art. 3.4.4); three kinds on the same footing (a verifiable work, an attestation signed by the party that received the work, a diploma recorded by the issuing school); no kind required, none sufficient to exclude the other routes.

*Provenance of clauses in the revised text below.* From the maintainer: everything listed above. From the red-team pass of Section 6.2, **accepted in principle by maintainer decision 2026-11-13** (Section 10): the jurors naming the subject matters; the formal-objection deadline and the signed-contribution rule in the conflict filter; the source-diversity filter; the fixed relaxation order; language as never a filter, with drawn interpreters; the ban on reciprocal attestations counting; one entry per person by Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood (ZK-PoP), with optional pseudonym; plain code for signatures, dates and counts; the fallback when no model meets Art. 7.2; offline draws during a partition. Drawn interpreters are a transition measure pending SIM-QA-22.

Art. 4.4 retitled **"Drawn Expert Evidence and Intelligible Decision-Making"**; 4.4.1 unchanged; 4.4.2–4.4.4 replaced by:
* **4.4.2 Drawn Evidence Panels, per case (revised 2026-11-12):** "On complex proposals, an Evidence Panel is drawn by lot from the Practitioner Register of Article 4.4.6, free from corporate grants or debt. The jurors name the subject matters the proposal involves; any party may ask, before the draw, that one be added. The draw applies only the following filters, and publishes them with its result: subject matter, drawing from the categories named; place, a fixed share of seats from the bioregion concerned and a fixed share from outside it, set by Organic Law; hiatus, excluding anyone in hiatus for that subject matter under Article 4.3.3; conflict of interest, excluding residents of the proposing node and of any node that filed a formal objection before the draw was announced, anyone who signed a contribution to the proposal, and anyone whose admission rests on evidence issued by either side; diversity of source, so that no two members are admitted on evidence from the same school or the same attesting party. Residence is checked by a proof that reveals only that the person does not reside in the excluded nodes. Language is never a filter: the panel works in writing, each member's text in their own language is the authentic version, and translations are made by interpreters drawn by lot from the Register under the same rules and annexed beside the originals. Each panel is drawn for one proposal and dissolved when it reports; no standing expert body exists, and no member of a panel holds any office by reason of having served. Continuity rests on the published briefs of earlier sessions: a panel on a matter already briefed states what it confirms and what it revises in the earlier brief, and why. Service on an Evidence Panel is civic service under Article 4.3.2: members receive release from routine labor in proportion to the hours served, and replacement of domestic and care support for those hours. After reporting, each member observes the hiatus of Article 4.3.3 for that subject matter, counted on the length of the panel's service. Where the filtered pool is below the minimum of Organic Law, filters are relaxed in this order only, each step logged: place shares, then diversity of source, then hiatus. The conflict-of-interest filter is never relaxed."
  * *Notes (2026-11-13):* the service sentence implements X-11. Drawn interpreters are a transition measure pending SIM-QA-22 (Section 10.1); the sentence on them stands either way.
* **4.4.3 Practical Intelligibility and Disagreement Map:** "The Evidence Panel files a plain-language brief stating the consequences, trade-offs and failure scenarios of the proposal and of inaction, what its members agree on, where they differ, and which observation would change each member's position. Each member signs individually; dissent is annexed in full and given the same length and place as the majority text."
* **4.4.4 Juror-Led Inquiry:** "Proponents and opponents then speak as declared parties. Jurors conduct the questioning, may call further witnesses, and deliberate without experts or parties present; written questions during deliberation are answered on the public record. Procedure follows the Fact-Centric Inquiry of Article 8.4.3."
* **4.4.5 Precautionary Invariant:** "Approval of a novel structural intervention is withheld where the record shows a specific, plausible pathway to irreversible harm to life or ecosystems that the proposal does not close (*In dubio pro natura et vita*). Confusion or disagreement alone never suspends approval. Suspension never interrupts certified routine maintenance or critical life support. Persistent disputes of fact pass to a fresh Evidence Panel from non-contiguous watersheds, which reports; the jurors decide."
* **4.4.6 The Practitioner Register (new; revised 2026-11-12):** "A single Practitioner Register serves every assembly, with one set of admission criteria for all applicants in every place; no local, bioregional or parallel register is created. Subject categories are listed in Organic Law, and anyone may propose a new one by petition under Article 4.3.5. Entry is voluntary: an applicant publishes, by their own choice, evidence of their work, and the Register never gathers evidence about a person on its own initiative (Article 3.4.4). Evidence is a signed, tamper-evident public record (Articles 7.3, 7.4) of one of three kinds, all on the same footing: a verifiable work, such as an open design with its history or an installation that exists; an attestation signed by the party that received the work, never by peers; a diploma recorded by the school that issued it. No kind is required; any kind that meets the criteria suffices, and the absence of one kind never excludes an applicant who meets them by another. Criteria are written in advance in Organic Law, countable and documentary, never judgments of quality; the thresholds for the three kinds are enacted together for each category. Attestations exchanged between two applicants in the same category do not count. No title, guild, association or professional body is required for admission or may gatekeep it. Each living person holds at most one entry, proven by Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood (Article 7.4.4); an entry may be held under a pseudonym, and a name is published only by the person's choice. An open model meeting Article 7.2 compares the filed evidence with the written criteria and returns only 'meets' or 'to be verified', citing for each criterion the document and passage it relied on; signatures, dates and counts are checked by ordinary auditable code. 'Meets' admits the entry, which stays open to challenge. 'To be verified' sends the filing to a jury of citizens drawn by lot from outside the field, which decides. No model rejects an entry or decides a challenge (Articles 7.1.2, 7.5.3); where no model meets Article 7.2, every filing goes to the jury. An entry becomes eligible for draws after a waiting period counted from filing, whatever the route, and never for a proposal published before it was filed. Anyone may challenge an entry once on each ground. A challenge is decided by a jury of citizens drawn by lot from outside the field, which rules only on whether the filed evidence is authentic and meets the written criteria, never on the quality of the person or of their opinions; the entry stays eligible while the challenge is pending. ~~Entries, model outputs, challenges and rulings are logged publicly.~~ *(Superseded 2026-11-16 by Decision I, Section 11.2.)* The Register is replicated across watersheds, its signing keys are dispersed under Article 7.4, and any replica cut off from the others can still run a verifiable draw. The Register is separate from the civic register."
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%), OL-9.7 extended:** admission criteria per category, enacted by the Global Commons Assembly (Art. 10.6.1, 10.6.4) on the brief of its own Evidence Panel, dissent annexed (documentary thresholds only; the three kinds of evidence set together; same structure for every category; a change takes effect after twelve months and never removes an entry already admitted); list of subject categories and the petition route for new ones, a new category inheriting the pool of its parent category until it reaches the minimum pool; waiting period before eligibility, counted from filing (provisional: six months); share of "meets" admissions re-checked by a drawn jury (provisional: one in twenty); publication, as aggregates only, of the share of filings sent to verification by kind of evidence, category, bioregion and language, with a disparity threshold that triggers review of the criteria or the model; local and outside shares of a panel (provisional: one third local, rounded up, at least one seat each way); challenge jury size (provisional: fifteen (15)) and the rule that a dismissed challenge bars the same challenger on the same ground; panel size (provisional: an odd number, at least three (3) per discipline involved, at least five (5) on matters under formal objection); minimum filtered pool below which filters are relaxed (provisional: nine (9) practitioners); hiatus per subject matter; reconciliation of replicas after a partition (union of entries, entries admitted during the partition open to challenge). All `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.
* **Consequential (Organic Law, 60%):** OL-1.4 "rival expert testimony (Art. 4.4)" becomes "drawn expert evidence (Art. 4.4)". Art. 4.6.2 needs no change: it already deliberates "under Article 4.4".
* **Link to SIM-QA-18 H7 (part-time service):** part-time jurors will rely mostly on the written brief, so the brief and its annexed dissent carry more weight than under the current text. This is why 4.4.3 gives dissent the same length and place as the majority text, and why reading the brief and annexes counts as service hours under the proportional release of the revised Art. 4.3.2.

#### **6.1 Red Team Against Decisions 1 and 2** *(reasoning on text; nothing measured)*
* **Bulk false or inflated evidence before a known proposal: `RED_WINS` on open admission alone, `NARROWED` with guards.** A faction that knows a proposal is coming can file many thin or inflated entries to raise its share of the draw. *Guards:* an entry is never eligible for a proposal published before it was filed, and only after a waiting period; criteria are documentary, so each filing is a checkable public claim; anyone may challenge; the disagreement map and full dissent limit what one captured seat can do. Residual: a patient faction files early, and an unchallenged weak entry stays in.
* **Challenges used to harass heterodox practitioners: `BLUE_HOLDS` with guards.** The jury rules on the evidence against the criteria, never on opinions; one challenge per ground per challenger; the entry stays eligible while pending; ~~challenges and rulings are public, so a pattern of harassment is visible~~ *(superseded 2026-11-16 by Decision I: the pattern is visible to the holder and the jury only, Section 11.3 I-7)*. Residual: publications reveal opinions, and a lay jury cannot fully un-see them.
* **Lay jury misjudging technical evidence: `BLUE_HOLDS` only if the criteria are countable.** "Ten years of documented practice" or "five completed works with a public record" can be judged by non-experts; "a significant contribution to the field" cannot, and would hand the decision back to peers. Hence the rule in 4.4.6 that criteria are documentary facts, never quality judgments. Authenticity (a forged work record) may need factual witnesses, such as a client or a site record, whom the jury may call; it may not call peers to judge quality. Residual: some fields have no countable proxy that separates competence from mere tenure.
* **Who writes the criteria: `NARROWED`, residual capture surface.** Criteria are enacted by the competent assembly at 60% (Art. 10.6.4; Organic Law may strengthen but never weaken constitutional guarantees). A guild cannot gatekeep admissions any more, but it can lobby for thresholds that only its members meet. *Guards:* the same structure of criteria for every field; any change takes effect after twelve months and never removes an admitted entry; the assembly drafts with its own Evidence Panel brief, dissent annexed; criteria are reviewable under Art. 4.6 as gatekeeping. Residual: the capture surface is now one visible, delayed, reviewable vote instead of many hidden admission decisions. That is smaller, not gone.
* **Loss of continuity on long-running matters: `NARROWED`.** A per-case panel starts cold, and on a matter running for years each panel may contradict the last without knowing why. *Guard:* each panel states what it confirms and what it revises in the earlier brief, and why. Residual: memory moves into documents and into assembly staff, the shadow-bureaucracy risk of Art. 4.3.4.
* **Thin specialties, per-case draw plus hiatus: `BLUE_HOLDS` locally, `RED_WINS` for globally rare fields.** One panel of three plus a hiatus of twice its service needs about nine practitioners per concurrent case; the cross-bioregion draw covers most local shortages. For a field with only a handful of practitioners worldwide the hiatus cannot hold. *Guard:* the hiatus is waived for that case and the waiver logged. Residual: in rare fields the same few people recur, and RED-5 (repeat players) returns there by necessity.

#### **6.2 Red Team Against Decisions 3 and 4 and the Rule on Evidence** *(reasoning on text; nothing measured; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*
* **The machine pre-check itself (Decision 3): `BLUE_HOLDS` with guards.**
  * Coherence. Art. 7.1.2 lists legislative drafting, sentencing, rationing and coercion; register admission is none of these, and the model can only admit, never exclude, so a person's standing is never lowered by a machine. Art. 7.5.3 is met only because the checkable object is the cited passage, as in Section 8.3: anyone can read the passage and see whether it shows the countable fact. Art. 7.3 reproducibility can mean only fixed weight hash, fixed settings, logged inputs, re-run within a tolerance.
  * Red: the applicant writes every document the model reads, so document injection (Section 8.3, RED-2) is easier here than in H7: a filing can carry text written to make the model answer "meets". The failure is a false admission, never a false exclusion. *Guards:* signatures, dates and counts are checked by ordinary code, not by the model; a "meets" whose cited passage does not show the fact is void; a fixed share of "meets" admissions is re-checked by a drawn jury; challenge stays open.
  * Red: few models may meet Art. 7.2 (Section 8.3). *Guard:* the register works without the model; every filing then goes to the jury, at a cost in citizens' time.
  * Residual: injected or captured pre-checks produce false admissions that only audit and challenge catch. Those false admissions feed the seeding vector below.
* **False evidence: `NARROWED`; seeding by a faction or school: `RED_WINS` (residual) on the evidence rule alone, `NARROWED` with the draw filters.**
  * *False statements recorded at the source* (a client attests a work never done, a school records a course never taken): `NARROWED`. The signature proves who said it, not that it is true. Each such record is a public claim by a named or proven signer, open to challenge, and a work that exists can be inspected. With no pay or career at stake, single cases are low priority, as instructed.
  * *Rings attesting each other*: `NARROWED`. Two practitioners in the same category cannot count each other's attestations (the guard is in 4.4.6). Longer rings (A for B, B for C, C for A) and rings across adjacent categories pass. Finding them means analysing the graph of attestations. That is analysis of records the practitioners published themselves, not tracking of persons, but it sits close to the profiling Art. 3.4.4 forbids and is not proposed.
  * *Legacy credentials from before the transition*: `BLUE_HOLDS` with a transition gap. A diploma that no school or successor archive will record is not evidence of the third kind; its holder uses works or attestations, which most working practitioners have. Gap: fields where practice before the transition left mostly paper credentials (medicine, law-adjacent trades) start with a thin pool. This belongs to the transition roadmap, not to canon.
  * *Multiple entries per person*: closed by one entry per living person under ZK-PoP. Without that guard a single person could multiply their draw chance.
  * *Seeding the global pool*: the real vector. A faction or school trains and enrols many adherents whose evidence is genuine. No check on evidence can see this, because nothing is false. Its share of the pool for a subject becomes its share of the draw. Arithmetic, assuming independent draws: a group holding 10%, 20% or 30% of a category's pool wins the majority of a three-seat panel with probability 0.028, 0.104 or 0.216; of a five-seat panel 0.009, 0.058, 0.163; of a seven-seat panel 0.003, 0.033, 0.126. Over many cases in a category, that is the share of briefs it writes. Equal-place dissent (4.4.3) means a captured majority still faces a full counter-text, so a majority is worth less than in the current text.
