The global pool cuts both ways: a group must seed the planet's pool, not a local one, but a seeded category steers briefs everywhere. *Guards:* the source-diversity filter (no two members from the same school or attesting party) caps what one school can seat; five seats or more on contested matters; the outside share dilutes a group concentrated in one bioregion (a group with 50% of the local pool and 10% elsewhere wins the majority of a one-local, two-outside panel with probability 0.10, against 0.50 for an all-local panel). Residual: an ideological movement whose members hold genuine evidence from diverse schools cannot be filtered without judging opinions, which 4.4.6 rightly forbids. The defence then rests on parties' declared witnesses and juror inquiry (4.4.4), not on the register. Category gerrymandering is a second route: a group petitions for a narrow category it dominates. *Guard:* the jurors, not the petitioners, name the subject matters of a proposal, and a new category inherits its parent's pool until it reaches the minimum.
* **Schools as gatekeepers: `BLUE_HOLDS` on admission, `NARROWED` on effective weight.** Schools decide who receives a diploma, so they control the most convenient route, but not the only one. The guild returns only if the other two routes are harder in practice: higher thresholds, or more filings sent to "to be verified". *Guards:* the thresholds for the three kinds are enacted together; the verification rate is published per kind of evidence, and a disparity above the Organic Law threshold triggers review; the source-diversity filter limits any one school to one seat per panel. Residual: informal prestige. Jurors and fellow panel members may defer to diploma holders, and panels drawn from a pool rich in graduates will write in an academic register. That sits outside admission, and the register cannot fix it.
* **Practical trades and thin-paperwork regions sent to "to be verified": `RED_WINS` as a delay filter without guards; `NARROWED` with them.** The pre-check reads documents. A wall, a repaired pump or a handwritten client attestation in a low-resource language gives a model little to cite, and models generally perform worse on such languages and scripts. Trades and poorer regions will therefore be sent to verification far more often than academics with digital records. That is not a rejection, but it costs delay, jury time and a mark of doubt, and that is a hidden filter. *Guards in the text:* the waiting period runs from filing whatever the route, so verification adds no delay as long as the jury rules within it; physical works can be inspected by citizens of the place drawn by lot, the same lay ground-truthing used for sensors in Art. 3.4.5; the verification rate is published by kind, category, bioregion and language, so the filter is visible and triggers review. Residual: a jury backlog in a poor region restores the delay; the guard makes the bias measurable, not absent.
* **One global criteria vote as a larger capture prize: `NARROWED`.** Under Decision 4 the competent assembly for the criteria is the Global Commons Assembly (1,001 citizens, 60%, Art. 10.6.4). One vote now decides admission everywhere, a bigger prize than any local register, and there is no neighbouring register to compare against when it goes wrong. Against that: capturing 60% of 1,001 citizens drawn by lot is far harder than capturing many 15-seat councils; the change is delayed twelve months, never removes admitted entries and is reviewable under Art. 4.6. Residual: a defect is global at once, with no local fallback; and setting thresholds for every category is a heavy load for an assembly that meets rarely (the OL-9.7 caps on complex matters apply). A drafting jury per category may be needed; not proposed until tested.
* **Outside experts without local knowledge; the share: `BLUE_HOLDS` with a provisional parameter.** Local knowledge reaches the decision by three channels: the local seats on the panel, the jurors themselves (the assembly is local), and witnesses called by parties or jurors (4.4.4). The panel's specific job is independent evidence. So the local share should be a minority: provisional one third, rounded up, at least one seat each way (one of three, two of five). A higher local share brings back local interest and amplifies a locally concentrated faction (see seeding). Where no local practitioner exists, the seat passes outside under the relaxation order, and local knowledge comes from witnesses. Residual: the share is a guess, and "the bioregion concerned" is unclear for proposals that cross bioregions.
* **Language and translation: `RED_WINS` on Decision 4 as given, `NARROWED` with guards.** Canon has no language rule. A panel drawn worldwide may share no language. A shared-language filter would quietly turn the global pool into a pool of speakers of a few dominant languages, a hidden filter against most of the world. Machine translation inside a panel puts a model between experts, a framing channel (RED-7). *Guards:* language is never a filter; the panel works in writing; each member's own-language text is authentic; translations are made by interpreters drawn by lot from the register, per case, with a hiatus, and annexed beside the originals. Residual: slower panels, weaker oral exchange, and literature in dominant languages still shapes what members know. The brief reaches jurors in translation, which is one more point where framing can enter.
  * *Cross-reference (2026-11-12, SIM-QA-22):* "Canon has no language rule" is a compression loss from v1.0 Section VII.2, not a decision. If SIM-QA-22 is adopted (one constructed common language taught to all as a second tongue; fluency never a condition of eligibility; no deliberation solely through machine translation), drawn interpreters become a transition measure, needed until most panel members share the common language and kept afterwards for those who do not. This patch is unchanged.
* **Who certifies a conflict of interest, and gaming of the filter: `NARROWED`.** Nobody certifies. The filter is mechanical on signed records at the draw, and residence is checked by a proof that reveals only non-residence in the excluded nodes, which avoids linking the register to the civic register. Gaming routes: a party gets an inconvenient expert's node to object formally so that the expert is excluded; a proponent invites an inconvenient expert to "consult" in order to disqualify them; a contributor hides their part. *Guards:* only a formal objection filed before the draw was announced excludes a node; "worked on the proposal" means a contribution the person signed, and nobody can be added by others; an undeclared contribution is a ground to challenge a drawn member before the panel sits, decided by the lay challenge jury on documents. Residual: a node can still object early, on purpose, to exclude its own residents. That costs the node its residents' presence on the panel, and the cost is public.
* **The register as a single point of failure or attack: `BLUE_HOLDS` on integrity and availability; `RED_WINS` (residual) on logic and on targeting.** Signed records, dispersed keys (Art. 7.4) and replication across watersheds defend against tampering and outage, and replicas that can run verifiable draws when cut off keep assemblies working during a partition. Replication does not defend correctness: one bad criteria change, one bug in the admission code or one injected pre-check model reaches every replica at once. And one global list of the practitioners in contested fields is a target list for a hostile actor (Legacy Engine coercion, threats against people in a disputed field). Pseudonymous entries reduce the second risk; the first has no remedy except delay, review and red-team bounties (Art. 7.6.1).
* **Tension with Art. 7.4.4: `RED_WINS` against a register of named persons; `NARROWED` with pseudonymous entries.** A draw from the register is sortition, and Art. 7.4.4 makes sortition registry access rest on ZK-PoP "without harvesting … personal identity data". A worldwide list of named persons with subject, place and languages conflicts with that. *Guards:* one entry per living person proven by ZK-PoP; an entry may be pseudonymous, and a name is published only by choice; residence is proven, not recorded. Residuals: in a small field, subject, bioregion and published works identify the person whatever the pseudonym; diplomas and many attestations carry real names; and an attestation from the person who received care (medicine, therapy) reveals that they received it. Attestations from such recipients must state only countable facts (dates, kind of work), never content. This needs an Organic Law rule and a privacy review before merge. *(First-pass privacy review recorded 2026-11-16, Section 11.1.)*
* **The rare-specialty failure of Section 6.1: `RED_WINS` (residual), not removed.** The global pool contains the same few practitioners as before; the earlier patch already drew across bioregions when a local pool was short. What the single register removes is the fragmentation caused by different criteria in different registers. The new filters (conflict of interest, place shares, source diversity) shrink the usable pool further. *Guard:* a fixed relaxation order, place shares first, then source diversity, then hiatus, never conflict of interest, each step logged. Residual: in globally rare fields the same few people recur, and petitions for ever narrower categories would create more such fields.

### **7. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)** *(revised 2026-11-12, second revision)*
* ~~Register admission criteria are now the capture surface; unsolved.~~ **Narrowed by Decision 2:** no title, guild or body can gatekeep, and challenges go to lay juries. **Changed by Decision 4:** the criteria are now one global vote of the Global Commons Assembly. It is harder to capture than many local votes, but a defect is global at once and has no local register to compare against.
* **Seeding (new, main residual):** a faction or school that enrols many adherents with genuine evidence gains a proportional share of draws in a category. Source diversity, larger panels on contested matters and the outside share cap it. An ideological movement with genuine evidence from diverse schools cannot be filtered without judging opinions; the defence there is juror inquiry and declared witnesses, not the register.
* A patient faction can file entries well before a proposal; the waiting period stops only last-minute flooding.
* **Machine pre-check (new):** filings are written by the applicant, so injection into the pre-check is easy. It can only cause false admissions, which audit and challenge must catch, and these feed seeding. Few models may meet Art. 7.2; without one, all filings go to juries.
* **Thin paperwork (new):** trades, poorer regions and low-resource languages will be sent to "to be verified" more often. Counting the waiting period from filing, local inspection and published verification rates make this visible and mostly delay-free. They do not make it go away, and a jury backlog brings the delay back.
* Countable criteria measure tenure and output, not competence. Some fields have no good countable proxy.
* Rings of attestation longer than two, or across neighbouring categories, are not caught; catching them would need graph analysis close to the profiling that Art. 3.4.4 forbids.
* **Privacy (new):** a draw is sortition, so Art. 7.4.4 applies. Pseudonymous entries and proven non-residence reduce the conflict. Small fields are identifiable anyway, diplomas carry names, and attestations from care recipients can expose them. This needs a privacy review before merge.
* **Language (new):** written work, own-language originals and drawn interpreters avoid a dominant-language filter, at the cost of speed and of one more framing point between panel and jurors.
* **Single register (new):** replication protects integrity and availability, not correctness; one bad criteria change or admission bug propagates everywhere. One global list of practitioners in contested fields is a target list.
* Per-case panels move continuity into briefs and staff; the Art. 4.3.4 risk grows on long-running matters.
* In globally rare specialties the global pool does not help: the same few people exist, the new filters shrink the pool, and the relaxation order ends with the hiatus waived. Repeat players return. Petitions for narrow categories could multiply such fields.
* The local and outside shares (provisional one third local) are a guess.
* The brief still frames the question. Annexed dissent and juror inquiry reduce, but do not remove, framing power, and part-time service (SIM-QA-18 H7) increases the brief's weight.
* "Specific, plausible pathway to irreversible harm" is a judgment call; a determined actor can still fabricate one, at a higher cost.
* No evidence of magnitude for any vector. The verdicts are reasoning on the text, not measurements. The panel-capture figures in Section 6.2 are binomial arithmetic on assumed pool shares, not observations.

**Next action:** one external critic with field experience of citizens' assemblies reviews Sections 4–6. A SIMONE Layer 2 run of RED-3 (persuasion vs evidence weight) under duel vs juror-led formats, labelled synthetic. Draft example criteria for two categories, one trade and one academic, with all three kinds of evidence, and test whether a lay jury can apply them. A pre-check test on synthetic filings (trade vs academic, two languages and scripts, filings with injected text) measuring the "to be verified" rate and false "meets", labelled synthetic. A privacy review of 4.4.6 against Arts. 3.4.4 and 7.4.4 (required before merge). ~~Maintainer decision on the red-team guards listed under *Provenance of clauses* in Section 6.~~ (Accepted in principle 2026-11-13.)

### **8. Addendum (2026-11-12): Open Models as Evidentiary Aid** *(Stage 2 continued; Sections 4–7 unchanged)*
*Measurement status of every item in this addendum: **`NOT_RUN`**. The verdicts below are a red-team reading of the text, not tests. No quantitative claim is made.*

#### **8.1 New Hypotheses**
* **H7, open models as evidentiary aid.** Open, reproducible models (Arts. 7.3, 7.5) prepare the case for each position in place of, or alongside, the rival panels. Conditions:
  * jurors write the proposal and the positions; each position is worded by its supporters and checked by the other side;
  * one fixed, public prompt template with a single position slot, written before any topic is known, accepting more than two positions;
  * the same attached documents, settings and length limit for every run; any party may add documents;
  * at least two models of different origin, each arguing every position (role swap), with repeated runs and a reworded-proposal run to expose framing sensitivity;
  * every claim cites a passage in the attached documents; uncited claims are discarded for all sides;
  * each output states its assumptions, its weakest point, and the observation that would change the conclusion;
  * human practitioners remain for local and practical knowledge; no model is present in final deliberation.
* **H8, oversight of machine influence.** A control loop with a number, a threshold, a body and a consequence:
  * the civic register publishes prompts, model outputs and votes, and computes per assembly the share of decisions that matched the machine-written brief (concordance index);
  * any citizen can re-run a published prompt and compare the output;
  * Organic Law fixes a concordance threshold (e.g. 90% over one year) that triggers a review;
  * the review is done by a single-purpose panel of 15 citizens drawn from a non-contiguous watershed, dissolved afterwards, which reads the session records and not only the index;
  * the panel may clear the assembly, order a change of model or prompt template, or suspend machine aid in that assembly for a fixed period; it cannot reverse decisions already taken;
  * models and prompt templates used in deliberation fall under the red-team bounties of Art. 7.6.1.
  * *Known limits:* high concordance may only mean the brief was good; an assembly aware of the index can diverge on purpose to lower it.

#### **8.2 New Red Vectors**
* **RED-6 (drift toward the synthesis):** jurors follow the machine-written brief by habit, so the model governs in fact without anyone deciding it should.
* **RED-7 (framing and model capture):** whoever writes the prompt template, selects the documents, or controls the weights steers both sides at once; two "rival" runs of related models share the same blind spots.

#### **8.3 Red Team Against H7** *(measurement `NOT_RUN`)*
* **Coherence with Chapter VII: `RED_WINS` unless the citation rule carries the load.**
  * Art. 7.5.3 voids any governance protocol that resists functional comprehension by ordinary citizens. A neural model does. H7 survives only because the checkable object is the cited passage, not the model: a juror can read the passage and judge whether it supports the claim. Without the citation rule, H7 is void under Art. 7.5.3.
  * Art. 7.1.2 makes delegation of legislative drafting to neural models an absolute nullity. H7 keeps drafting with jurors; RED-6 is the route by which drafting slides to the model anyway (see 8.5).
  * Art. 7.3 demands bit-for-bit reproducibility of *binaries*. Published weights can be hashed and re-run, but training cannot be reproduced by any citizen node, and sampled inference is not deterministic. "Reproducible" in H7 can mean only: fixed weight hash, fixed settings, logged inputs, re-runnable within a tolerance.
  * Art. 7.2 bans proprietary or licence-locked software in public administration. Many "open-weight" models carry use restrictions and undisclosed training data. Eligible models must meet Art. 7.2, which may leave very few.
* **RED-1 (nomination capture) → RED-7: `RED_WINS` (residual).** Capture moves from choosing experts to choosing models, template and documents. "Different origin" is weak protection: few organizations train capable models, and they share much of the same training data, so role-swapped runs on two such models agree on the same blind spots. Role swap exposes *asymmetric* advocacy, not *shared* error. *Guard:* models drawn by lot per case from an open register of models meeting Art. 7.2, with a hiatus per model as for experts. Residual: the register is small.
* **RED-2 (manufactured doubt): `RED_WINS` via documents.** "Any party may add documents" opens two attacks. Flooding: a party adds many documents so the length limit and citation rule favour its material. Injection: a document carries text written to steer the model, and it steers every run on every side at once. Role swap does not reveal injection, since it acts on all positions equally. *Guard:* equal document quota per position; every document shown to jurors as attached; template and documents in scope of the Art. 7.6.1 bounties. Residual: injection detection is an open problem; screening documents would recreate a gatekeeper.
* **RED-3 (rhetorical capture): `BLUE_HOLDS` on symmetry, moves to RED-6.** Every position gets the same fluency, which removes the advocacy-skill gap. The persuasive asymmetry becomes deference to machine text as such.
* **RED-4 (false balance): `PARTIAL`.** More than two positions are allowed (fixes the two-sides defect). But an equal length limit per position gives a fringe position as much text as a well-supported one, which signals equal weight. *Guard:* the human disagreement map (H4) states where practitioners actually stand; each output's "weakest point" is shown first.
* **RED-5 (repeat players): `RED_WINS` (worse than before).** A model is the ultimate repeat player: the same weights in every assembly at every tier. One systematic bias becomes a planet-wide monoculture. *Guard:* the per-case lottery and per-model hiatus above. Residual: model diversity is bounded by how many eligible models exist.
* **Citation rule: `BLUE_HOLDS` with guard.** Red: a model can cite a real passage that does not support the claim. Checking citations by another model is recursion. *Guard:* each side checks the other side's citations by hand; a claim whose cited passage does not support it is discarded like an uncited one.
* **Overall H7: `BLUE_HOLDS_WITH_GUARDS` as an aid alongside drawn human panels; `RED_WINS` "in place of" them.** Replacing human practitioners removes the only check on shared model blind spots (RED-7) and on documents nobody brought.

#### **8.4 Red Team Against H8** *(measurement `NOT_RUN`)*
* **The index is undefined for H7 as written: `RED_WINS` until fixed.** H7 briefs argue every position and make no recommendation, so "matched the machine-written brief" has no referent. *Guard:* each run ends with a sealed line naming the position best supported by its cited passages; the line is withheld from jurors and published only after the vote. Concordance is computed against the sealed lines. The sealed line cannot steer jurors and still measures drift.
* **Small samples: `RED_WINS` at small tiers.** A 15-seat council decides few complex matters a year (OL-9.7 caps them). With 10 decisions, 9 of 10 concordant (90%) arises by chance far too often to be a signal (see SIM-QA-19 for small-sample error). *Guard:* the threshold applies only above a minimum count of decisions, pooled over a longer window if needed.
* **Publishing votes: `RED_WINS` if individual.** Published individual votes let anyone verify how a juror voted, which is what makes instructing or rewarding delegates enforceable and so defeats Art. 4.1.3. *Guard:* publish tallies only.
* **Re-run right: `BLUE_HOLDS` with two guards.** Inference is not exactly deterministic, so comparison needs published settings and a stated tolerance; divergence beyond it is a bounty finding (Art. 7.6.1). Coherence: Art. 5.2.2 can exclude a citizen from advanced computing grids; the re-run right must be exempt, as sortition eligibility is under Art. 5.2.3.
* **Review panel reading session records: `PARTIAL`.** H5 closes final deliberation. The panel can read sealed deliberation records only if they exist. *Guard:* records kept sealed, opened to the review panel alone, never published. Residual: jurors who know they may be read later deliberate differently.
* **Art. 7.6.1 scope: `PARTIAL`.** The bounties cover vulnerabilities, backdoors and centralization vectors. Framing bias in a template is not clearly any of these. *Guard:* Organic Law names framing bias and document injection as eligible findings.
* **RED-7 against H8: `RED_WINS` (residual).** A template that steers all positions equally leaves concordance unchanged. H8 cannot see symmetric capture. *Guard:* the register also publishes sensitivity scores from role swap, repetition and rewording (the script in the next action).
* **Known limits confirmed.** High concordance may only mean good briefs; an assembly aware of the index can game it downward. The panel reads records rather than the number, which is the right answer to both, at the cost of 15 citizens' time per review.

#### **8.5 RED-6 and RED-7 Verdicts**
* **RED-6 (drift toward the synthesis): `RED_WINS` against H7 alone; `PARTIAL` with H8 and the sealed-line guard.** H7 offers no synthesis, but jurors can still adopt whichever case reads best, which is de facto drafting by the model (Art. 7.1.2). H8 with sealed lines measures this; it cannot prevent it. The consequence (suspension of machine aid) is the only brake.
* **RED-7 (framing and model capture): `RED_WINS` (residual after all guards).** Lottery over models, a version-locked template adopted by Organic Law, bounties and sensitivity scores raise the cost. None removes the shared blind spots of models trained on similar data, and none detects a template that steers every side the same way.

#### **8.6 Residual Weaknesses (Addendum)**
* Shared blind spots across models (RED-7) have no detector. Human practitioners are the only check, so H7 must stay "alongside", never "in place of".
* Document injection is unsolved without a gatekeeper.
* The number of models meeting Art. 7.2 may be too small for lottery and hiatus to mean anything.
* Concordance measures drift only after the fact and only above a minimum count of decisions; small councils are effectively unmonitored.
* Every verdict here is reasoning on text. Nothing has been measured.

**Next action (addendum):** a reproducible script that measures, on real case files, how far model output moves under role swap, repetition and rewording (framing-sensitivity scores for H7/H8). H7/H8 are not added to the purple patch in Section 6 until those measurements exist.

### **9. Cross-Docket Check with SIM-QA-18, 19, 21** *(2026-11-12; contradictions listed, not resolved; no change to the patch of Section 6)*
* **Agreements verified.** The Precautionary Invariant of proposed Art. 4.4.5 ("a specific, plausible pathway to irreversible harm") stays separate from the closed list of irreversible decisions (OL-11, SIM-QA-19 section 9.2), whose point 2 says the list does not define irreversible harm for 4.4.5. Reading the brief counts as service hours under the proportional release of SIM-QA-18 H7, now accepted with Set B. Raised hours for a complex matter (OL-9.10, SIM-QA-18 section 9.2) apply to the same OL-9.7 certification that calls an Evidence Panel. Panels work in writing and do not vote, so the in-person rule for 60% and 75% votes does not reach them; final deliberation by jurors stays closed (4.4.4).
* **X-10, certification of complex status and the in-person rule.** OL-9.7 certifies "complex" status by a 60% vote. SIM-QA-18 now requires 60% and 75% matters to be voted in person. Read literally, every complex certification, and so every Evidence Panel draw and every hours raise, waits for the next session in person at the upper tiers. *Open:* does "60% matters" cover procedural certifications at 60%, or only decisions whose substance needs 60%? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (Section 10):** decisions of substance only; complex certification may be voted online (SIM-QA-18 OL-9.11).
* **X-11, service rules for panel members.** Evidence Panel members serve per case, under a hiatus counted on the length of their service, but neither this docket nor SIM-QA-18 gives them a service share, proportional release or care replacement. *Open:* is panel service civic service under the revised Art. 4.3.2? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (Section 10):** yes; applied in Art. 4.4.2.
* **Also affected:** proposed 4.4.5 keeps "suspension never interrupts certified routine maintenance", and no text names who certifies maintenance (SIM-QA-19 section 9.3, I-3). *(Named 2026-11-13: SIM-QA-19 OL-11 point 5a, the outside panel of three, for every tier as drafted; confirmation pending there.)* The OL-9.7 cap on complex proposals per session and the OL-9.10 rule of one raised matter per body count the same matters and must be read together; not a contradiction.

### **10. Revision (2026-11-13): Maintainer Decisions on the Red-Team Guards, X-10 and X-11** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **10.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **All ten red-team guards listed under *Provenance of clauses* (Section 6) are accepted in principle:** the jurors name the subject matters; the formal-objection deadline and the signed-contribution rule in the conflict filter; the source-diversity filter; the fixed relaxation order (place, then source diversity, then hiatus, never conflict of interest); language never a filter, with written work and drawn interpreters; no counting of reciprocal attestations; one entry per person by ZK-PoP with optional pseudonym; plain code for signatures, dates and counts; all filings to the jury when no model meets Art. 7.2; offline draws during a partition.
* **Drawn interpreters are marked as a transition measure pending SIM-QA-22.** If SIM-QA-22 is adopted, they serve until most panel members share the common language and remain afterwards for those who do not; if it is rejected, they are the permanent answer and the residual of Section 7 (one more framing point) stays as it is.
* **Before any merge, two items remain required:** the privacy review of Art. 4.4.6 against Arts. 3.4.4 and 7.4.4, and the review by one external critic with field experience of citizens' assemblies.
* **X-10:** the in-person rule covers decisions whose substance needs 60% or 75%. Procedural votes at 60%, including certification of complex status (OL-9.7), may be taken online. Applied to the Art. 4.3.2 text of SIM-QA-18 and listed in its OL-9.11.
* **X-11:** service on an Evidence Panel is civic service under the revised Art. 4.3.2: proportional release and replacement of care support apply. Applied in Art. 4.4.2.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

#### **10.2 Red Team Against the Decisions**
* **Guards accepted as a set. `NO_NEW_VECTOR`.** Each guard was red-teamed in Section 6.2 and keeps the residual recorded there. Accepting them moves them from proposal to working text; it adds no rule that was not tested.
* **Interpreters as a transition measure. `NARROWED`.** The mark makes the patch depend on another docket that may close either way. *Guard:* the 4.4.2 rule (written work, own-language originals, drawn interpreters) stands on its own and does not wait for SIM-QA-22; only its expected duration depends on it.
* **X-11, release across bioregions. `NARROWED`.** A practitioner drawn from outside the bioregion is released from the rosters of their own node, so outside nodes carry part of the cost of another bioregion's panel. Per case the hours are small; in a rare field the same few people are drawn often (Section 6.1), and their nodes pay repeatedly. *Guard (proposed, Organic Law):* hours released for outside panels are counted in the release ledger of the bioregion that called the panel. Residual: a ledger transfer, not a physical replacement; the node still loses the person's hours on its rosters.
* **X-11, a paid expert class by the back door. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Proportional release is release from routine labor, not payment; no expert gains anything that a juror does not.
* **X-11, other drawn roles not named. `OPEN`, flagged.** The decision names Evidence Panel members. Drawn interpreters (4.4.2) and the challenge and verification juries of 4.4.6 are also drawn by lot. Challenge juries are citizens' juries and fall under Art. 4.3.2 by its own terms; interpreters are drawn from the Register like panel members, and the decision does not say whether they are covered. Not extended here; listed in the cross-docket check of 2026-11-13.
* **X-10. `BLUE_HOLDS`** (see SIM-QA-18 section 10.3: the certification only calls a panel; the decision on the matter stays in person).

#### **10.3 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 3)**
* The residuals of Sections 6.1, 6.2 and 7 stand; nothing in this revision removes one.
* Release for outside practitioners shifts labor between nodes.
* Interpreters' service rules are undecided.
* Privacy review and external critic not yet done; both block merge.

**Next action (revision 3):** privacy review of 4.4.6; external deliberation critic; synthetic pre-check test and framing-sensitivity script unchanged; maintainer decision on whether interpreters' service falls under Art. 4.3.2.

### **11. Revision (2026-11-16): Privacy Review of Art. 4.4.6 and Maintainer Decisions** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

*Scope and limits.* This is a **first-pass review by the drafting team, not a specialist's**. It reads the proposed Art. 4.4.6 (Section 6, and the working text of Part B / Part C in [`chapter_iv_placement_2026-11-13.md`](chapter_iv_placement_2026-11-13.md)) against canon Arts. 3.4.4 and 7.4.4. No data-protection expert has reviewed it; the external review required before merge (Section 10.1) is not replaced by it. Nothing is measured; the figures in 11.3 are arithmetic on assumed shares.

#### **11.1 Privacy Review: Findings**
* **Visible on entry under the proposed text.** Subject category; the filed evidence; filing date; the pre-check output with its cited passages; every challenge and every ruling. The Register is public, replicated and tamper-evident, so none of this can be taken back, and the proposed text has **no exit rule**.
* **Identifiable despite a pseudonym.** A small field plus a bioregion plus published works points to one person. A work "that exists" has an address. Diplomas carry the real name. Attesting parties know the person they attest. A seat drawn in the local share reveals that the member lives in the bioregion. Release from routine labour in the home node during a panel is visible to neighbours. Writing style in signed briefs can be matched across cases.
* **Third parties exposed.** A recipient's attestation says they received the work; in care fields, that they were in care. An installation reveals a home. A client may be called as a factual witness in a public challenge.
* **Target list.** Anyone can enumerate the practitioners of a contested field worldwide. The public draw result shows who is deciding a case while it runs.
* **Art. 3.4.4.** By its wording it covers sensor networks ("Sensor networks are strictly confined …; algorithmic surveillance, biometric tracking, or individual domestic profiling is an absolute constitutional crime"). It does not by itself reach the Register. The Section 6 citation of Art. 3.4.4 for "never gathers evidence on its own initiative" is an analogy, not a rule that applies by its terms.
* **Residuals that no rule removes.** Small fields stay identifiable to people inside them. A global list of practitioners remains a list, even if every entry is a pseudonym.
* **Verdict.** The concern is correct; it does not stop the Register. **Art. 4.4.6 as drafted is not compatible with Art. 7.4.4** (sortition registry access "without harvesting … personal identity data"): a public, permanent record of works, places, diplomas and attestations is personal identity data in all but name.

#### **11.2 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-16)**
* **A, pseudonym by default.** Every entry is held under a pseudonym; a name is published only by the person's choice. Organic Law.
* **B, right to withdraw at any time.** A withdrawn entry enters no further draw; only an unreadable trace stays public; briefs already signed stay public under the pseudonym. Organic Law.
* **C, sealed evidence.** Evidence is seen only by a drawn citizens' jury; the public sees "criteria met"; challenges remain possible before the jury. Principle in the Constitution, operation in Organic Law.
* **D, third-party attestations.** Countable facts only (dates, kind of work); the signer may stay anonymous if proven to be a real person; clients are heard in closed session. One sentence in the Constitution, the rest in Organic Law.
* **E, diplomas.** The school confirms the diploma to the pseudonym without publishing the name. Organic Law.
* **F, place.** Proven, never recorded; the draw result states that the place shares are met, not who is local. Organic Law.
* **G, case pseudonyms.** A different pseudonym per case; release from rosters without a stated reason; the link between cases checkable by a jury. Organic Law.
* **H, purpose limit.** Register data are used only for draws and challenges; no public body may analyse attestation networks. Constitution.
* **I, sealing in every field.** Evidence is always sealed and only aggregates are published; there is no list of sensitive fields. *I overrides C* (sealing is no longer a choice) and changes Decision 2 of this docket (open challenge on public evidence; "everything logged publicly").
* **J, a lighter Art. 4.4.6.** Principles and personal rights stay in the Constitution; mechanics (machine pre-check, waiting periods, replicas) move to Organic Law. No point is dropped, only moved.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

*Superseded earlier text (struck through in place):* Section 6 Decision 2, "everything logged publicly"; Section 6 rule on evidence, "public record"; Section 6 Art. 4.4.6, "Entries, model outputs, challenges and rulings are logged publicly"; Section 6.1 harassment guard "challenges and rulings are public"; Sections 6.2 and 7, the privacy residuals (now 11.1).

#### **11.3 Red Team Against Decision I** *(before drafting; every vector of Sections 6.1 and 6.2 where public scrutiny was a guard)*
*What Decision I removes.* Under Decisions 2 to 4, three checks rested on publicity: anyone could read a filing and challenge it; anyone could read a cited passage and see whether it shows the fact (the ground on which Art. 7.5.3 was met, Section 6.2); anyone could re-run a draw on public records. Under I, only drawn juries see evidence, and the public sees "criteria met" and aggregates. Each vector below is tested on that basis.

* **I-1, false or inflated evidence (6.1 bulk filing; 6.2 false statements at the source). `RED_WINS` against a single well-made false entry at one in twenty; `NARROWED` against bulk filing.** The waiting period and "never for a proposal published before filing" do not depend on publicity and still stop last-minute flooding. A surge of filings in a category shows in published aggregates (filings per category per month). But the main check on a single false claim was the reader who knew it was false. Now the only check is the jury re-check sample. Arithmetic, assuming independent sampling: a false "meets" entry escapes a one-in-twenty re-check with probability 0.95. A faction with ten false entries escapes entirely with probability 0.60, with twenty 0.36, with fifty 0.08. At one in five: 0.80, 0.11, 0.01, below 0.01. One caught entry exposes the rest only if the jury may follow the signers and schools it cites (G allows the link between cases; whether it allows the link between entries sharing a signer is not decided). *Guard that needs no publicity:* the signer of an attestation is notified, under seal, that a filing cites their signature, and may repudiate it. Residual: a real signer who states a false fact is caught only by sampling.
* **I-2, attestation rings. Pairs: `BLUE_HOLDS`; longer rings: `RED_WINS`.** The pair rule (two applicants in the same category) is plain code on signed records and runs on sealed data. Longer rings were already a residual (Section 7). Under public evidence, outside readers could still notice them; under I nobody outside a jury can, and H forbids any public body from analysing attestation networks, which closes the only technical route. **Decision I with H fails on this vector.** *Options for the maintainer (none chosen):* (a) extend the mechanical exclusion at the draw from pairs to closed cycles up to a fixed length, computed by code inside the seal and returning only "these entries are not drawn together" (whether H permits this must be decided: it is analysis of the network, but not by a body and not for any other use); (b) let a drawn jury, not a public body, run a cycle check on one category on a stated ground; (c) accept the residual and rely on source diversity and dissent.
* **I-3, seeding by a faction or school. `NARROWED`, unchanged in mechanics, weaker in visibility.** The guards (source-diversity filter, five seats on contested matters, the outside share) are draw filters and run on sealed data. What I removes is the public's view of a school's or movement's growing share in a category. *Guard:* publish, per category, the share of the pool held by each school or attesting source as an aggregate with a minimum cell size. Residual: as before, a movement with genuine evidence from diverse schools cannot be filtered; under I it is also harder to see.
* **I-4, injected pre-check filings. `RED_WINS` at one in twenty with sealed outputs; `NARROWED` only with a raised re-check share and public testing on synthetic filings.** The guard "a 'meets' whose cited passage does not show the fact is void" worked because anyone could read the passage. Now only juries can, and only on the sample. Independent adversarial auditors (Arts. 7.5.3, 7.6.1) cannot test the model on live filings. *Guards:* the model and its settings stay open (Art. 7.2), and red teams test it on public synthetic filings with injected text; the re-check share of "meets" rises (see I-8). **Decision I fails on this vector unless one of the following is chosen.** *Options:* (a) raise the re-check share (one in five, one in two); (b) end automatic admission: a model "meets" only orders the queue, and every admission is made by a jury; (c) keep one in twenty and accept that most injected admissions pass until challenged.
* **I-5, schools as gatekeepers. `BLUE_HOLDS` on admission; `NARROWED` on weight.** The verification rate per kind of evidence is an aggregate and stays public. New under E: the school learns that a pseudonym holds its diploma, and can delay or refuse confirmation, now invisibly. *Guard:* confirmations requested and refused are counted per school as an aggregate; diplomas remain one route of three. Residual: informal prestige, as before.
* **I-6, thin-paperwork bias. `NARROWED`.** The published rates by kind, category, bioregion and language are aggregates and survive I. Local inspection of a work by citizens drawn in its place reveals the address to them only, under seal. *Residual, new:* a minimum cell size hides the smallest cells, which are the small languages and poor regions where the bias is worst. A disparity there is invisible to the public; only a jury audit of the suppressed cells can see it.
* **I-7, how the public challenges an entry it cannot read. `NARROWED` with the stated-ground re-check; `RED_WINS` on conflicts a party could only spot by identity.** Candidate tested: anyone may ask a drawn jury to re-check an entry on a stated ground (for example "this work does not exist", "this school never taught this course"). It keeps a public trigger and costs one jury sitting per request, under the existing rule of one request per ground per requester. It cannot work for a party who suspects that a drawn member is conflicted: under G the member appears under a case pseudonym, so an undeclared contribution (Section 6.2) cannot be detected from outside. *Guard (option):* a blind conflict check. Each party files the names of persons it believes conflicted; the jury compares them with the sealed identities of the drawn members and answers only "excluded" or "not". Residual: a party must guess names; frivolous lists load juries. Harassment by challenge (Section 6.1): the per-entry count of re-check requests is visible to the holder and to the jury, not to the public; a pattern is seen by the jury only.
* **I-8, the re-check share of "meets". Must rise.** At one in twenty the sample was a supplement to public reading; under I it is the main check (I-1, I-4). Options: one in five (bulk escape at twenty false entries about 1%); one in two; or every admission by a jury (I-4 option b). Load rises in proportion (four times the re-checks at one in five). **Not chosen here.**
* **I-9, filters on sealed data. `NARROWED`.** Source diversity, conflict of interest, hiatus and the pair rule are code on signed records and run inside the seal. What is lost is public re-running of the draw. *Guard:* each sealed entry is published as a commitment that reveals nothing about it; the draw uses the public random beacon over the committed entries; anyone can verify that the drawn pseudonyms come from the committed pool, and a drawn jury verifies on a sample that the filters were applied to the sealed content. Residual: correct filtering is verified by sampling, not by anyone.
* **I-10, load on citizens' juries. `NARROWED`; magnitude `NOT_RUN`.** Under I juries carry verification, the raised re-check sample, public re-check requests, blind conflict checks, case links under G and challenges. Juries are drawn from outside the field and, at full scale, from a very large pool, so the load per citizen can be small. In the early transition, pools are small and the SIM-QA-11 re-run already found council petition capacity not workable at the modelled rate (SIM-QA-18 section 11.5). A load model is required before merge.
* **I-11, who audits the juries. `NARROWED`.** A jury that wrongly admits a faction's entries is no longer visible to the public. *Guards:* juries drawn per case from outside the field; reasons recorded under seal; a second drawn jury audits a sample of rulings; aggregate outcomes per jury published; the Constitutional Review Jury (Art. 4.6) may open sealed files for itself. Residual: the regress ends in sampling, not in publicity; collusion between a jury and a faction is caught only by chance or by a later challenge.
* **I-12, Art. 7.5.3 coherence. `NARROWED`.** Art. 7.5.3 voids a protocol "proven by independent adversarial auditors to resist functional comprehension by ordinary citizens". Sealed outputs are comprehensible to the ordinary citizens on the jury; the code and model stay open for auditors. The earlier reading (Section 6.2: anyone can read the cited passage) no longer holds. Recorded for the cross-docket check.

**Verdict on Decision I.** Privacy: **`BLUE_HOLDS`** against the 11.1 findings, except the two residuals no rule removes. Integrity: **`RED_WINS` on two vectors** (I-2 longer rings, together with H; I-4 injected pre-check at one in twenty) **and on part of a third** (I-7, conflicts detectable only by identity; I-1 single false entries at one in twenty). **`NARROWED`** on the rest. The options listed under I-2, I-4, I-7 and I-8 are for the maintainer; none is applied in 11.4. *(Decided 2026-11-16, Section 11.8: after K-1 to K-4, `NARROWED` on every vector.)*

#### **11.4 Revised Text** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(based on the working Chapter IV text, Part B with Part C; implements A to J only; no red-team option of 11.3 is applied)*
*Reading of A and B under J (for the maintainer to confirm):* J keeps personal rights in the Constitution. "A name is published only by the person's choice" was already in the working text, and the right to withdraw is a personal right. Both therefore keep one clause in the Constitution; the operation (default pseudonym, the unreadable trace, signed briefs) goes to Organic Law as A and B say.

**Art. 4.4.2 (replaces the Part C block):**
> 2. **Drawn Evidence Panels:** On complex proposals, an Evidence Panel is drawn by lot from the Practitioner Register, free from corporate grants or debt.
>    * *Subject matters:* the panel covers the subject matters the jurors name; any party may ask, before the draw, that one be added.
>    * *Filters:* the draw applies only filters of subject matter, place, hiatus, conflict of interest and diversity of source, defined by Organic Law on countable facts, never on opinion; the conflict-of-interest filter is never relaxed. Place is proven, never recorded; the result shows only that the place shares are met.
>    * *Language:* language is never a filter; each member's own text is authentic and drawn interpreters translate it.
>    * *Term:* a panel serves one proposal and is dissolved when it reports; no standing expert body exists and no office follows from service, which is civic service under Article 4.3.2. Members serve under a pseudonym per case; release from rosters states no reason. Members observe the hiatus of Article 4.3.3 for that subject matter.

**Art. 4.4.6 (replaces the Part C block):**
> 6. **The Practitioner Register:** One Practitioner Register serves every assembly; no parallel register exists, and it is separate from the civic register.
>    * *Criteria:* one set of admission criteria applies, written in advance, countable and documentary, never judgments of quality. Categories are listed in Organic Law; anyone may propose one by petition.
>    * *Entry:* entry is voluntary and the Register never gathers evidence about a person on its own initiative (Article 3.4.4). A verifiable work, an attestation by the party that received the work, never by peers, and a diploma recorded by its school stand on the same footing, with thresholds enacted together; none is required and any one suffices. No title, guild or professional body gatekeeps admission.
>    * *Identity:* each person holds at most one entry, proven without revealing identity; a name is published only by the person's choice, and the person may withdraw at any time.
>    * *Sealed evidence:* evidence is seen only by drawn juries; the public sees that the criteria are met, and aggregates. Attestations state only countable facts; a signer proven to be a real person may stay anonymous, and clients are heard in closed session.
>    * *Decisions:* no model rejects an entry or decides a challenge (Articles 7.1.2, 7.5.3); doubtful filings and challenges go to juries drawn from outside the field, which rule only on the evidence and the criteria, never on opinions. Anyone may ask such a jury to re-check an entry on a stated ground; the entry stays eligible meanwhile.
>    * *Use:* Register data serve only draws and challenges; no public body analyses attestation networks.

*Note on "Article 3.4.4".* 11.1 finds that Art. 3.4.4 does not reach the Register by its terms. The citation is kept as the source of the analogy; H now carries the rule on its own.

**Organic Law (proposed, 60%), OL-9.7 extended: Register privacy and mechanics** *(all `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Pseudonym (A).** Every entry is created under a pseudonym generated by the Register. The holder may attach a name at any time and remove it later from future records; no record infers or links a name the holder has not attached.
2. **Withdrawal (B).** Withdrawal takes effect at once: the entry enters no further draw, and its sealed evidence is deleted after [the end of any pending audit or challenge, and at most ninety days]. A trace that reveals nothing about the entry stays public so that past draws can still be verified. Briefs already signed stay public under the case pseudonym.
3. **Sealing (C, I).** All filed evidence, model outputs, challenges and rulings are sealed. Drawn juries see what their task requires, and no more. Published as aggregates, with a minimum cell of [ten (10)]: filings, admissions, verifications, re-checks and challenges per category, kind of evidence, bioregion and language; refusals of confirmation per school; the share of each category's pool by source.
4. **Attestations (D).** An attestation states dates, kind of work and quantities, never content, condition or diagnosis. A signer is proven to be a real person without being named, and is notified under seal when a filing cites their signature. Clients and recipients called as factual witnesses are heard in closed session; their identity is never published.
5. **Diplomas (E).** On request, the issuing school or its successor archive confirms to the Register that the holder of a pseudonym holds a stated diploma, without publishing the name. The school keeps no record of the request beyond the confirmation itself.
6. **Place (F).** Residence is proven at the draw for the bioregion concerned and never stored. The draw result states only that the local and outside shares are met.
7. **Case pseudonyms (G).** A drawn member receives a pseudonym for that case. Release from rosters is recorded as civic service, without stating which body or case. A drawn jury may link a member's cases to check hiatus, repeat service and challenges.
8. **Machine pre-check (moved from Art. 4.4.6, J).** An open model meeting Art. 7.2 compares the filed evidence with the written criteria and returns only "meets" or "to be verified", citing for each criterion the document and passage it relied on. Signatures, dates and counts are checked by ordinary auditable code. A "meets" whose cited passage does not show the fact is void. Where no model meets Art. 7.2, every filing goes to the jury. Share of "meets" admissions re-checked by a drawn jury: [one in twenty] (see 11.3 I-8; the maintainer chooses).
9. **Waiting period (moved from Art. 4.4.6, J).** An entry becomes eligible for draws [six months] after filing, whatever the route, and never for a proposal published before it was filed.
10. **Replicas (moved from Art. 4.4.6, J).** The Register is replicated across watersheds so that no single site or key controls it; signing keys are dispersed under Art. 7.4. A replica cut off from the others can still run a verifiable draw; after a partition, replicas are reconciled by the union of entries, and entries admitted during the partition are open to re-check.
11. **Verifiable draw on sealed entries (11.3 I-9).** Each sealed entry is published as a commitment that reveals nothing about it. The draw uses the public random beacon over the committed pool. A drawn jury verifies, on a sample, that the filters were applied to the sealed content.
12. **Re-check requests (C, 11.3 I-7).** Anyone may ask a drawn jury to re-check an entry on a stated ground; one request per ground per requester; a dismissed request bars the same requester on the same ground.
~~*Not drafted (options under 11.3, for the maintainer):* cycle exclusion for rings (I-2); a raised re-check share or admission only by jury (I-4, I-8); the blind conflict check (I-7); the audit of juries by a second jury (I-11).~~ *(Decided and drafted 2026-11-16, Section 11.8: points 8 changed, 13 to 16 added.)*

#### **11.7 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 4)**
* Small fields stay identifiable to insiders; a global list of pseudonyms remains a list (11.1).
* Under Decision I, longer attestation rings and injected pre-check filings are not caught at the current settings (11.3 I-2, I-4); the options are open.
* A party cannot detect a conflicted member it cannot identify (I-7) unless the blind check is adopted.
* Correct filtering and correct jury rulings are verified by sampling, not by the public (I-9, I-11).
* Jury load is unmeasured (I-10).
* This review is a first pass; a specialist privacy review and the external deliberation critic both remain required before merge.

#### **11.8 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-16, second batch) and Their Effect** *(reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*
*The maintainer's answer numbered the audit of juries as 4; it is recorded here as the decision on I-11, since I-4 and I-8 were answered together.*
* **K-1, longer rings (I-2): option (a).** At the draw, code inside the seal excludes closed cycles of attestation up to four (4) entries and returns only "these entries are not drawn together". *Reading of H:* H bars any body from analysing attestation networks and bars any other use of Register data; a mechanical filter applied at the draw, with no human reader and no output beyond the exclusion, is not such analysis. The filter belongs to diversity of source and is relaxed with it.
* **K-2, pre-check and re-check share (I-4, I-8): option (a).** One in five "meets" admissions is re-checked by a drawn jury, and the model is tested in public on synthetic filings, injected text included, before use and after every change.
* **K-3, blind conflict check (I-7): adopted.** Each party files once the names of persons it believes conflicted; the jury compares them with the sealed identities of the drawn members and answers only "excluded" or "not".
* **K-4, audit of juries (I-11): adopted, small sample.** A second drawn jury re-examines a sample of rulings ([one in twenty], provisional); aggregate outcomes per jury are published.
* **K-5, reading of A and B under J: confirmed.** "A name is published only by the person's choice" and "the person may withdraw at any time" stay in the Constitution; the operation goes to Organic Law.
* **K-6, the two bounds J moved: kept in the Constitution.** "No entry serves a proposal published before it was filed" and "The Register is replicated so that no single site or key controls it" stay in Art. 4.4.6; the detail stays in OL-9.7 extended points 9 and 10.
* **K-7, Chapter IV ceiling (Z-3): option (c).** Decided after Y-12 and Y-3 are decided. Nothing is cut meanwhile.
* **K-8, cross-docket Z-1, Z-2, Z-4: all three resolutions adopted** (cross-docket addendum of 2026-11-16).

*Red team of the decisions (short):*
* **K-1. `NARROWED`.** Rings of five or more pass; a faction that knows the limit builds longer rings, at a growing cost, since each extra member needs genuine work for a genuine recipient. In globally rare fields the filter is relaxed with diversity of source, so rings return where the pool is thinnest. The H reading is a line the maintainer has drawn; a later majority could stretch "mechanical filter" into profiling, so the bound "no output beyond the exclusion" is written into OL point 13.
* **K-2. `NARROWED`.** Arithmetic as in 11.3: a single well-made false entry still escapes four times in five; twenty escape together about once in a hundred. Public synthetic testing catches injection patterns, not one clever filing. Re-checks rise fourfold against one in twenty; load `NOT_RUN`. *Residual stated plainly: single false entries are caught mainly by later re-check requests and challenges, not by the sample.*
* **K-3. `NARROWED`.** A party must guess names; a long list can be used to fish for identities ("not" for every name narrows who the member is). *Guard in OL point 14:* one list per party per case, of limited length, and the answer is given once for the whole list ("one or more excluded" or "none"), not name by name. Residual: a party with a short, well-aimed list still learns something.
* **K-4. `NARROWED`.** The regress ends in a sample; collusion between a jury and a faction is caught with the probability of the sample.
* **K-5, K-6. `BLUE_HOLDS`** under the placement test; cost 23 words.
* **K-7. `NO_NEW_VECTOR`.** A postponement; the conflict stays open and listed.

*Verdict on Decision I after K-1 to K-4:* **`NARROWED` on every vector**, with the residuals above. No vector of 11.3 is `RED_WINS` any longer; single well-made false entries remain the largest residual.

*Revised Art. 4.4.6, the two bullets that change (K-6; the other bullets as in 11.4):*
> * *Decisions:* no model rejects an entry or decides a challenge (Articles 7.1.2, 7.5.3); doubtful filings and challenges go to juries drawn from outside the field, which rule only on the evidence and the criteria, never on opinions. Anyone may ask such a jury to re-check an entry on a stated ground; the entry stays eligible meanwhile. No entry serves a proposal published before it was filed.
> * *Use:* Register data serve only draws and challenges; no public body analyses attestation networks. The Register is replicated so that no single site or key controls it.

*OL-9.7 extended, changed and added points* `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`:
* **Point 8 (changed, K-2):** share of "meets" admissions re-checked by a drawn jury: [one in five]. The model and its settings are tested in public on synthetic filings, injected text included, before first use and after every change; results are published.
* **13. Cycle exclusion (K-1).** At each draw, code inside the seal excludes from being drawn together any entries joined by a closed cycle of attestations of up to four (4) entries. The code returns no output except the exclusion, keeps no record of the cycles, and its result is used for nothing else. The exclusion is part of the diversity-of-source filter and is relaxed with it.
* **14. Blind conflict check (K-3).** Before a panel sits, each party may file once a list of at most [twenty (20)] names. The jury compares the list with the sealed identities of the drawn members, replaces any member named, and answers only "one or more excluded" or "none".
* **15. Audit of juries (K-4).** A second drawn jury re-examines [one in twenty] rulings on admission, re-check and challenge, under seal. Outcomes per jury and per category are published as aggregates.
* **16. Public record and the Register (Z-4).** An answer given on the public record under Art. 4.4.4 refers to Register evidence only as "criteria met" or "not met".
