## **SIM-QA-21: Governance of Nodes Below the Council Threshold**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-12  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-12  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — Verdict on current text **`RED_WINS`** (fill failure at 50 adults, labour drain, three-person sovereignty). **H1 `RED_WINS`** (RED-1, RED-2, RED-3). **H2 `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals** (RED-4 and RED-5 narrowed, not solved). Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. **Revised 2026-11-12 (section 8):** maintainer accepts H2 and the 150-adult threshold with 150 / 120 hysteresis in principle and withdraws H1; draft Organic Law definition of an internal matter (OL-10) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**; the Section 6 wording alone **`RED_WINS`** on three vectors (centre general rules, admission through "reception of newcomers", classification panel drawn from the centre); the three changes that close them are applied to the Section 6 text by maintainer decision. **Cross-docket check 2026-11-12 (section 8.8):** the irreversible list is now drafted as OL-11 (SIM-QA-19); three open contradictions (X-7 to X-9) on hours, threshold evidence and threshold arithmetic, not resolved. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13 (section 9):** X-7 RED-6 restated (labor drain holds for the current text only); X-9 votes-cast carve-out drafted in Art. 4.2.1 with the consequential change to Art. 4.5.4; X-3 and X-1 applied to the node's panels. **X-8 re-run (measured, synthetic):** under Set B a council of 15 holds from about 100 adults only if refusal stays at or below about 60%; the 150 / 120 band itself holds only to about 70%; no lowering proposed; the 150 decision stands. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13, second batch (section 10):** 150 / 120 stands; deferral window set at three months; **with it the band holds to about 80% refusal** (150 edge to about 85%), against about 70% with six months; consequential wording for Arts. 2.6.1, 2.6.2, 3.6.1 and 4.1.1 drafted (Y-9, Y-10; "plebiscites" dropped), red-teamed; X-6 still open.  
* **Originating Context:** Residual weaknesses of SIM-QA-18 (nodes of 50 adults cannot sustain a 15-seat council) and SIM-QA-19 (bodies of 3 are juries, not samples). SIM-QA-01 is not reopened: its finding (a 15-seat council cannot be staffed from 28 people without a labour deficit) stands; this docket asks what should govern instead.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 4.2.1, 4.2.2, 4.3.3, 10.1.3; revision: Arts. 4.5.3, 10.1.4, 10.2.2, 10.4.2  
* **Reproducible Evidence:** [`simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_21_small_node.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_21_small_node.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_21_small_node_results.md) (`--reps 2000 --seed 20261112`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**: hour budgets, turnout, quorum, household sizes, centre pool and refusal rates are assumptions)

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 4.2.1 vests "sovereign municipal administration" in a drawn panel of 3 for nodes under 50 adults. Three people, often close to each other, hold full local power. The kin rule bars first-degree kin and partners from sitting together; it does not bar friends, allies or a dominant extended family.
* Art. 4.2.2 triggers a 15-seat council at 50 adults. SIM-QA-18 shows its seats cannot be filled once refusal passes 10 to 20%.
* Small settlements are many places but few people. India, 2011 census: 82,151 of 597,608 inhabited villages (13.7%) have fewer than 200 inhabitants and hold under 1% of the rural population. France, 2015: 910 communes under 50 inhabitants (31,357 people in all) and 2,567 between 50 and 99. *No global count of settlements under 150 adults was found.* These figures are recorded as supplied for this docket and must be checked against the primary census tables before any canon use.
* What such a node decides is narrow and practical: shift rosters, water, food growing, the local microgrid, allocation of free dwellings, disputes between neighbours, reception of newcomers. Health care, higher education, industry and general rules already come from a larger centre.

### **2. Hypotheses Tested Against Each Other**
* **H1, attachment** *(withdrawn by maintainer decision, 2026-11-12; kept as the tested alternative)*: a node below the threshold has no governing body of its own. It is governed by the council of the nearest larger centre, and its adults enter that centre's draw.
* **H2, two levels:** no drawn body below the threshold. (a) Matters internal to the node are decided by the assembly of all adult residents. (b) Everything else goes to the council of the nearest larger centre, whose draw includes the node's adults. (c) Disputes are heard by a panel of 3 drawn from outside the node. (d) The node holds a right of appeal (*Provocatio*) against a centre decision that concerns only the node. (e) An isolated or founding node with no larger centre nearby runs on (a) alone until one exists.
* **For both:** threshold at 50, 100 or 150 adults.

### **3. Model Findings** *(synthetic, uncalibrated)*
Python stdlib Monte Carlo (quarterly, 10 years, 2,000 reps) for seat filling; analytic hour budgets, exact hypergeometric seat shares and binomial quorum. Seat filling has no strata, so failure starts slightly later than in SIM-QA-18.

| Node (adults) | Council of 15: terms with an empty seat at r = 0.1 / 0.3 / 0.7 / 0.8 | Governance h/week per adult: B0 / H1 / H2 (below T) | Adults on full release under B0 | P(no seat in a centre council of 15, pool 2,000) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 30 | 85% / 93% / 100% / 100% (30% at r = 0) | 2.0 / 0.4 / 1.3 | 0% (3 half-time) | 80% |
| 50 | 1% / 36% / 93% / 99% | 12.2 / 0.4 / 1.3 | 30% | 68% |
| 100 | 0% / 0% / 4% / 47% | 6.1 / 0.4 / 1.3 | 15% | 46% |
| 150 | 0% / 0% / 0% / 1% | 4.1 / 0.4 / 1.3 | 10% | 31% |

* **Threshold:** at T = 50 the council fails from about 10–20% refusal; at T = 100 from about 70%; at T = 150 from about 85–90%. 150 is the only tested value where the council holds across the refusal range SIM-QA-18 considers plausible for a duty-based summons, short of invitation-style refusal (~94%), where no node below 500 holds.
* **Assembly (H2a):** with a quorum of one third of adults, quorum fails in 59–79% of sessions at 30% turnout, 4–18% at 40%, and ≤ 2% from 50%.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team**
* **Current text: `RED_WINS`.** At 50 adults the council fails on fill (SIM-QA-18) and takes 30% of adults off the roster (12.2 h per adult per week). Below 50, three drawn people hold all local power.
* **RED-1, voiceless periphery.** *H1: `RED_WINS`.* A node of 50 in a pool of 2,000 holds no council seat 68% of the time, and under H1 that council decides everything, including the node's water, rosters and dwellings. *H2: `NARROWED`.* Internal matters stay with residents; for other matters the node is as voiceless as any 50 people in a large centre, which is the ordinary condition of sortition, not a defect specific to small nodes. (d) adds an appeal where a centre decision targets the node alone. Residual: matters that concern the node among others (regional water allocation) still carry a 68% chance of no local voice. *Guard:* the centre council hears the node assembly before deciding a matter that concerns the node specifically.
* **RED-2, founding node.** *H1: `RED_WINS`.* At the start of the transition no larger O.N.E. centre exists, so H1 leaves the first nodes with no government at all. *H2: `BLUE_HOLDS`* via (e). Residual: (c) has no outside pool for a founding node. *Guard:* draw the dispute panel from the nearest other O.N.E. nodes; if none exists, from the node itself under the current kin rule, until one does.
* **RED-3, subsidiarity (Art. 10.1.3).** *H1: `RED_WINS`.* Rosters, a shared greenhouse or a free dwelling are resolvable inside the node; sending them to a centre council breaches "the lowest ecological scale possible". *H2: `BLUE_HOLDS`.* (a) keeps them local and (b) sends only what is not.
* **RED-4, assembly capture.** *H2: `RED_WINS` on open voting, `NARROWED` with guards.* A bloc that always attends gains weight when others stay home: a 20% bloc holds 29% of votes cast at 60% turnout, enough to block every 75% matter; a 30% bloc holds 42–52%. For comparison, under B0 the same 30% bloc holds 2 of 3 panel seats 22% of the time, and then holds everything. Hand-raising in front of 30 neighbours adds social pressure no number captures. *Guards:* secret ballot on any vote at the request of one adult, and always for dwellings, newcomers and disputes; a chair drawn by lot for each session; thresholds counted on votes cast with the quorum of Organic Law; the existing *Provocatio* for every party to a decision on dwellings or admission. Residual: a secret ballot among 30 people leaks, and a charismatic speaker still frames the debate.
* **RED-5, boundary gaming.** *Both: `NARROWED`.* A node of 152 that splits on paper into two of 76 escapes the council; a dispute over what is "internal" is a jurisdiction grab in either direction. *Guards:* node boundaries follow the watershed register (Art. 10.1) and change only by decision of the Bioregional Assembly; the threshold has hysteresis (council from 150 adults sustained for twelve months; back to the assembly only below 120 sustained for twelve months); "internal" means a matter whose resources and effects stay inside the node's own balance without changing its net flows with the centre beyond limits fixed by Organic Law; a dispute over classification goes to the outside panel of (c), whose ruling is final for that matter. *(Superseded 2026-11-12 by OL-10 point 8, section 8: panel drawn from neither the node nor the centre, one Provocatio to 15.)*
* **RED-6, labour drain.** *H2: `BLUE_HOLDS`, effect assumed.* No one is on full release inside the node; at an assumed 1.5 h per attending adult per week the assembly costs about a tenth of the current council at 50 adults (1.3 vs 12.2 h per adult). The SIM-QA-01 problem (too many people in meetings, too few on shifts) does not return. Red: if every matter goes to the floor, meetings grow without limit. *Guard:* a fixed number of ordinary sessions per month in Organic Law; shift rosters are set by the assembly and run by rotation, not debated each week.
  * *Restated 2026-11-13 (X-7, maintainer decision):* this finding holds for the current text only, which puts councils on full release. Under the accepted Set B (councils at 10% of a working week), a council of 15 in a node of 50 costs about 1.2 h per adult per week, about the same as the assembly (1.3 h), and less from 100 adults. Labor no longer separates H2 from a council. Under Set B the two-level model rests on seat filling (section 9.2), subsidiarity (RED-3) and the end of three-person sovereignty.
* **Coherence:** an all-adult assembly is not a sortition body, so Art. 4.1.1 ("exclusively from … sortition") must name it, as SIM-QA-19 already proposes for node plebiscites.

### **5. Comparison: H1 vs H2**
H1 is cheap in hours (0.4 h per adult per week) and removes every small-body risk, but loses three vectors outright: it hands a place's daily life to a council it usually has no seat on, it fails at founding when no centre exists, and it breaches Art. 10.1.3. H2 costs about 1 h more per adult per week (under the current text; under Set B the hour difference with a council disappears, RED-6 restated), keeps the practical matters where they are known, and survives founding; it trades the risk of three drawn people for the risk of a dominant bloc in an open room, which secret ballot and outside dispute panels narrow but do not remove. **H2 held up better.** Threshold: **150**.

### **6. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged; Arts. 4.2.1 and 4.2.2 accepted in principle 2026-11-12; the three changes to 4.2.1 of section 8.6 applied by maintainer decision 2026-11-12*
* **Art. 4.2.1, sub-paragraph (replaced), Nodes Below the Council Threshold:** "A node of fewer than one hundred and fifty (150) adult residents holds no drawn governing body. Matters internal to the node (work rosters, water, food growing, the local energy grid, allocation of free dwellings, settling of newcomers admitted under Article 10.4), within the limits fixed by Organic Law, are decided by the assembly of all adult residents, voting by secret ballot at the request of any one of them and always on dwellings, newcomers and disputes, under a chair drawn by lot for each session; as an exception to Article 4.5.4, thresholds in this assembly are counted on votes cast, with the quorum fixed by Organic Law. A rule of the larger node on these matters does not bind the node. All other matters belong to the Neighborhood Sortition Council of the nearest larger node of the same bioregion, whose draw includes the node's adult residents and which hears the node's assembly before deciding a matter that concerns the node specifically. Disputes between residents are heard by a Local Mediation Panel of three (3) citizens drawn for each dispute under the rule for outside panels of Article 4.2. A dispute over whether a matter is internal is heard by a panel of three (3) citizens drawn under the same rule, so from neither the node nor the larger node nor any node in its draw pool, with one appeal (*Provocatio*) to a cross-watershed blind panel of fifteen (15). The node holds a right of appeal (*Provocatio*) to a cross-watershed blind panel of fifteen (15) citizens against any decision of that Council that concerns the node alone. Until a larger node exists, the assembly alone governs, and the mediation panel is drawn from the nearest other nodes or, if none exists, from the node itself with no first-degree kin or domestic partners serving together."
* **Art. 4.2.2 (amended trigger):** "… (constituted upon a node sustaining one hundred and fifty (150) adult residents for twelve months, and dissolved into the assembly of Article 4.2.1 only upon falling below one hundred and twenty (120) for twelve months)."
* **Art. 4.1.1 (coherence):** "… and from the node assemblies and plebiscites this Chapter provides." *(Revised 2026-11-13, Y-10: section 10.3.)*
* **Art. 4.5.4 (consequential, X-9, 2026-11-13):** "… of all seated members, not merely of those present (e.g. 9 and 12 of 15; 61 and 76 of 101). In the assemblies of all adult residents of Article 4.2.1, thresholds are counted on votes cast, with the quorum fixed by Organic Law."
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** definition of an internal matter and the net-flow limits that bound it; assembly quorum (provisional: one third of adult residents for 50%+1 and 60% matters, one half for 75% matters, section 9.3) with thresholds counted on votes cast under the Art. 4.2.1 carve-out; maximum ordinary sessions per month; node boundaries fixed by the watershed register and changed only by the Bioregional Assembly. All `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.
* **Interaction with other dockets:** if adopted, the SIM-QA-18 addition to Art. 4.3.3 (shorter hiatus under 150 adults) and the council clauses of the SIM-QA-19 Small-Node Safeguards for nodes under 150 lose their object, since no council sits there. The SIM-QA-11 finding on a single hostile panelist forcing plebiscites in 3-person panels no longer applies to node administration.

### **7. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)**
* **Assembly capture is narrowed, not solved.** A disciplined bloc of 20–30% that always attends carries or blocks matters in a small room; secret ballot among neighbours leaks.
* **Nodes of 100–149 adults could staff a council** up to about 70% refusal, and lose sortition for internal matters under this patch. The 150 choice favours robustness at high refusal over sortition coverage in that band.
* **"Internal" will be litigated.** The definition carries the whole of H2(a); it sits in Organic Law. *Drafted and red-teamed in section 8 (OL-10); residuals in 8.7.*
* **Peripheral voice on shared matters is the normal sortition deficit,** made worse by distance: travel to the centre council is assumed at +25% time and not otherwise modelled.
* Every hour budget, turnout and dispute rate is an assumption; the census figures are as supplied, not re-verified.

**Next action:** ~~draft the Organic Law definition of an internal matter and red-team it~~ (done, section 8); calibrate assembly turnout and duration against small-commune and village-assembly data; check the census figures against primary tables.

### **8. Revision (2026-11-12): Maintainer Decisions and the Definition of an Internal Matter** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **8.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-12)**
* **H2 (two levels) accepted in principle.** The Art. 4.2.1 sub-paragraph of Section 6 is the working text.
* **Threshold of 150 adults with hysteresis 150 / 120 accepted in principle.** Council from 150 adults sustained for twelve months; back to the assembly only below 120 sustained for twelve months (Art. 4.2.2 of Section 6).
* **H1 (attachment) withdrawn.** Kept in Sections 2–5 as the tested alternative; not proposed.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged and the docket stays at Stage 2 until the definition below and the open items of Section 7 are settled.

#### **8.2 Draft Organic Law: OL-10, Internal Matters of Nodes Below the Council Threshold** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(implements proposed Art. 4.2.1; all numbers `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Rank and enactment.** OL-10 is a planetary standard, enacted and amended by the Global Commons Assembly under the Annex rules (60%, ratchet rule). A Bioregional Assembly cannot narrow it; it sets only the envelopes of point 3.
2. **The list (closed).** A matter is internal only if it falls under one of:
   * (a) **work rosters** for tasks performed inside the node by its residents;
   * (b) **water** drawn, stored, treated and distributed inside the node, within the node's envelope;
   * (c) **food growing** on land held in usufruct by the node or its residents;
   * (d) the **local energy grid**: generation, storage and distribution on the node's side of its connection point;
   * (e) **allocation of free dwellings** in the node's housing stock, under the usufruct rules of Chapter II and the Civic Housing Pool;
   * (f) **reception of newcomers**: the order and manner of settling people the basin's carrying-capacity status already admits (Art. 10.4.2): which dwelling, which rosters, which introductions. Never whether a person may reside;
   * (g) use and upkeep of facilities that serve only the node (greenhouse, tool library, workshop, meeting room). *(Added in drafting; not in the Section 6 list.)*
3. **Envelopes.** For each of the four conserved flows (energy, water, food, compute) the Bioregional Assembly fixes each node's envelope: the net import from, and net export to, the rest of the bioregion over a rolling twelve months. Envelopes follow one published formula for every node of the bioregion; a change that applies to one node only is a decision concerning that node alone and open to *Provocatio*. An envelope never falls below what the Tier 1 provision and the 2,200 kcal/day floor require for the node's residents.
4. **The flow test.** A listed matter stays internal only if the decision:
   * (a) is met from the node's own stocks and its envelope;
   * (b) changes the node's projected twelve-month net import or export of any conserved flow by no more than **5%** of the node's consumption of that flow (provisional);
   * (c) together with every other internal decision of the previous twelve months, changes it by no more than **10%** (provisional), and never past the envelope.
   Decisions that serve one project are counted as one decision, whoever proposes them.
5. **Exclusions (never internal, whatever the list says).**
   * the three reserved grounds of Art. 10.1.4: pollution leaving the node, abstraction from or discharge into a shared aquifer or watercourse beyond the envelope, invariant rights;
   * any decision on the closed list of irreversible decisions (OL-11, drafted in SIM-QA-19 section 9.2: demolition, felling of mature trees, extraction, irreversible habitat or aquifer change, disposal of a commons asset, each with a threshold);
   * refusal, delay or conditioning of residence, and any rule binding people who do not reside in the node;
   * node boundaries, which follow the watershed register and change only by the Bioregional Assembly.
6. **Presumption.** A listed matter is presumed internal. Whoever claims it is shared bears the burden, on the node's open flow ledger or, where the node has no meters, on estimates made by a published method. A matter not on the list is presumed shared.
7. **General rules of the centre.** A rule of the centre council on a listed matter does not bind the node. Only the Constitution, Organic Law and the envelopes of point 3 bind it on listed matters.
8. **Classification dispute.**
   * *Who may raise it:* any adult resident of the node; the centre council by a 60% vote; a Guardian of the Biosphere (Art. 10.2.2). One challenge per party per matter, counted against the endorsement allowance of OL-9.
   * *Window:* from the proposal until thirty (30) days after the decision.
   * *Who rules:* a **Classification Panel** of three (3) citizens drawn for this dispute and dissolved when it rules, from nodes that are neither the node nor the centre, and not from the centre's own draw pool: the one rule for outside panels (neither a party nor in the draw pool of a party; SIM-QA-19 X-1 and X-3, 2026-11-13). For a founding node with no centre, drawn from the nearest other nodes.
   * *Mandate:* the panel rules only on whether the matter is internal under points 2–5, on the ledger, within fourteen (14) days. A ruling on the merits is void and an impeachable offense (Art. 4.3.4), as for the Triage Panel (OL-9.5).
   * *Appeal:* one *Provocatio* per party to the cross-watershed blind panel of fifteen (15); appeals on the same ruling are joined; that ruling is final for the matter.
   * *Meanwhile:* the body favoured by the presumption may carry out reversible measures; anything that cannot be undone waits. A measure later ruled outside its competence is undone at the cost of the node or the centre that took it, counted against its envelope.
   * *Register:* rulings are published. A later panel follows an earlier ruling on the same kind of matter unless the measured flows differ. A ruling stops binding after twenty-four (24) months unless a new challenge confirms it.

#### **8.3 Red Team: the Node Calls a Shared Matter Internal**
* **N-1, list stretch (a large generator, a bottling line or a server room sold as "the local grid", "water" or "upkeep").** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Point 4 caps the change in net export at 5% / 10%; above it the matter is shared whatever its label. Red: a node can export just under the limit every year. That is within the envelope by definition, so it is not a grab.
* **N-2, salami (ten decisions of 4% each).** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* The cumulative 10% cap in 4(c) and the one-project rule close it. Red: unrelated decisions by different proposers still add up, and the node reaches the cap with harmless ones, then has no room for a needed one. *Residual:* the cumulative cap can block good decisions; the remedy is to ask the Bioregional Assembly for a larger envelope, which is slow.
* **N-3, exclusion through "reception of newcomers" and "free dwellings".** *Section 6 wording: `RED_WINS`.* "Reception of newcomers" decided by an assembly of residents, on a secret ballot, reads as a vote on who may come; a 30% bloc that always attends (RED-4) could keep dwellings empty or hand them to friends. *Draft: `NARROWED`.* Point 2(f) limits reception to the manner of settling people the basin already admits under Art. 10.4.2, and point 5 makes refusal or delay of residence never internal. Red: delay by slow procedure is hard to prove (dwelling "under repair" for a year). *Guard:* the Section 6 *Provocatio* for any party to a decision on dwellings or admission, plus a proposed allocation deadline: a free dwelling not allocated within a period fixed by Organic Law (provisional: ninety (90) days, by analogy with the usufruct rule on abandoned dwellings) passes to the Civic Housing Pool of the bioregion. *Change to Section 6 (applied):* "reception of newcomers" becomes "settling of newcomers admitted under Article 10.4".
* **N-4, shared aquifer and runoff ("we only water our gardens", "we only spray our own fields").** *`BLUE_HOLDS` on text, `NARROWED` in practice.* Point 5 keeps the Art. 10.1.4 grounds out of the list. Red: diffuse pollution and slow drawdown are known only after the fact, and a node of 80 people often has no meter on its wells. *Residual:* the flow test is only as good as the ledger. Where nothing is metered, the estimates of point 6 decide, and the method is open to dispute.
* **N-5, fait accompli (decide, act, wait out the challenge).** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Irreversible decisions are excluded from the list (point 5), and a reversible one ruled shared is undone at the node's cost (point 8). Red: "reversible" is itself contested (a cleared field regrows in ten years). *Guard:* the panel rules on reversibility first; in doubt, the measure waits.
* **N-6, boundary split (152 adults filed as two nodes of 76).** Covered by the RED-5 guard of Section 4 (watershed register, Bioregional Assembly only, hysteresis). Not reopened.
* **N-7, rosters as punishment (dissenters put on the worst shifts).** *`NARROWED`.* Invariant rights are excluded (point 5) and every party holds *Provocatio*. Red: a pattern of bad shifts is not one decision, and no ruling covers a pattern. *Residual:* a published roster rotation rule is needed (Section 4, RED-6 guard); this draft does not supply it.

#### **8.4 Red Team: the Centre Calls an Internal Matter Shared**
* **C-1, the spillover claim ("your greenhouse uses our pipe").** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Presumption in favour of the node (point 6); the centre must show a flow change on the ledger above the limits of point 4.
* **C-2, regulation by general rule (a centre "dwelling policy for the whole area").** *Section 6 wording: `RED_WINS`.* The patch sends "all other matters" to the centre council and is silent on whether a centre rule reaches listed matters; a general rule would empty the list without one classification dispute. *Draft: `BLUE_HOLDS`* through point 7.
* **C-3, shrinking the envelope.** *`NARROWED`.* The centre council does not set envelopes; the Bioregional Assembly does, under one formula for all nodes, with *Provocatio* against a node-specific change (point 3). Red: the large centres of a bioregion hold most of its 101 seats and can vote a formula that hurts every small node at once. That is not node-specific, so no *Provocatio* applies. *Residual:* the floor of point 3 protects survival, not autonomy. A further guard (envelope per adult never below the bioregion's average per adult) was considered and not drafted: it would forbid real differences between a valley node and an urban one.
* **C-4, narrowing the definition itself.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* OL-10 is a planetary standard (point 1); a bioregion dominated by its centres cannot amend it, and the ratchet rule forbids an Organic Law from weakening the autonomy of Art. 10.1.3. Red: the Global Commons Assembly is remote, slow and has few seats from small nodes (SIM-QA-19 RED-4). Correction runs through Art. 4.6 review, which is slow too.
* **C-5, challenge by attrition.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* One challenge per party per matter, counted against OL-9, a 60% centre vote to raise one, and the precedent register. Red: the centre can still challenge every new kind of matter once. That is the cost of a first ruling, paid once per kind.
* **C-6, emergency.** *`NARROWED`.* An emergency decree by simple majority (Art. 4.5.3) could take over a listed matter. Its thirty-day sunset limits it, but nothing stops a centre from declaring a new emergency on the same matter. *Residual:* a rule that a renewed emergency on the same matter needs the 60% ratification of Art. 4.5.3 belongs in emergency Organic Law, not here. Not drafted.
* **C-7, the "irreversible" list as a lever.** *`NARROWED`.* Every felling of a mature tree or every disposal of a commons asset leaves the list, even in a node of 30. Red: whoever writes the irreversible list can widen it until ordinary upkeep becomes shared. *Guard:* the list is closed and planetary, like OL-10. *Residual:* the list is not drafted (SIM-QA-19); its length decides how much H2(a) is worth. *(Update 2026-11-12: drafted as OL-11 with thresholds, so a single felled young tree or a dismantled shed stays internal; see SIM-QA-19 9.3, I-4.)*

#### **8.5 Red Team: Who Rules on Classification**
* **W-1, the Section 6 / RED-5 guard: panel drawn "from outside the node".** *`RED_WINS`.* The nearest outside pool is usually the centre, which is a party to the dispute. Three centre residents ruling on whether their own council may decide a matter is a jury drawn from one side. *Draft: `BLUE_HOLDS`* by drawing from neither the node nor the centre (point 8).
* **W-2, three people with the final word on jurisdiction.** *`NARROWED`.* A panel of 3 is a jury, not a sample (SIM-QA-19), and errs. The draft adds one *Provocatio* to 15 (the 3 → 15 path the canon already uses) and limits the panel to the test of points 2–5 on the ledger. Red: the test leaves room for judgment ("serves only the node", "one project"), so the panel still decides more than facts. Accepted residual.
* **W-3, the panel deciding the merits.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* Point 8 copies the Triage Panel rule: merit rulings void and impeachable. Red: classification and merits overlap when the question is whether a flow is "needed". The test asks only about the size of the flow, not whether it is needed.
* **W-4, precedent that hardens one bad ruling.** *`NARROWED`.* Rulings bind like matters, but only while flows are unchanged and for 24 months. Red: in 24 months a bad ruling shapes dozens of decisions. Without a register, every matter is fought again (SIM-QA-11 relitigation). The draft chooses stability with an end date.
* **W-5, founding node.** *`BLUE_HOLDS`.* With no centre, there is no node-versus-centre dispute; disputes with the bioregion or a Guardian draw from the nearest other nodes. *Residual:* a lone founding node challenged by a Guardian has no outside pool. One option is to draw from the Bioregional Assembly where one exists; where none exists, the dispute has no forum. Not drafted.
* **W-6, the meter-keeper.** *`NARROWED`.* Every flow test depends on the ledger. If the centre keeps the meters, the centre writes the evidence; if the node does, the node does. *Guard:* the ledger is open, readable by both and by the panel, without identifying persons (Art. 7.4.4), and meters at the connection point are read by both sides. *Residual:* small nodes without meters fall back to estimates, and the method of estimate becomes the field of dispute.

#### **8.6 Verdicts**
* **Section 6 wording on its own: `RED_WINS`** on three vectors: C-2 (centre general rules empty the list), N-3 (reception of newcomers reads as admission), W-1 (classification panel drawn from the centre).
* **OL-10 draft: `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals.** It holds on N-1, N-2, N-5, C-1, C-2, C-4, C-5, W-1, W-3. It narrows N-3, N-4, N-7, C-3, C-6, C-7, W-2, W-4, W-6. Nothing in this pass is measured; the 5% / 10% limits are placeholders chosen to be large enough for a greenhouse and small enough to stop a factory, not derived.
* **Changes to the Section 6 patch** *(applied there by maintainer decision, 2026-11-12):*
  * "reception of newcomers" → "settling of newcomers admitted under Article 10.4";
  * after the list: "within the limits fixed by Organic Law; a rule of the larger node on these matters does not bind the node";
  * replace "Disputes between residents are heard by a Local Mediation Panel of three (3) citizens drawn from outside the node" with the same panel, plus: "A dispute over whether a matter is internal is heard by a panel of three (3) citizens drawn from neither the node nor the larger node, with one appeal (*Provocatio*) to a cross-watershed blind panel of fifteen (15)."
  * The RED-5 guard of Section 4 ("goes to the outside panel of (c), whose ruling is final") is superseded by point 8 of OL-10.

#### **8.7 Residual Weaknesses (Revision)**
* The flow test needs a ledger. Most small settlements today meter little or nothing. Until they do, "internal" is decided on estimates.
* A bioregion's large centres can vote envelopes that squeeze every small node at once (C-3). The survival floor stops starvation, not loss of autonomy.
* ~~The irreversible list (SIM-QA-19)~~ (drafted as OL-11, 2026-11-12) and an emergency renewal rule (C-6) are not drafted, and both shape how much the list is worth. The emergency rule is still missing.
* The cumulative cap can block a needed decision as well as a grab (N-2).
* Patterns of abuse (rosters, slow dwelling allocation) escape a test built on single decisions.
* The 5% / 10% limits, the 14-day ruling time, the 30-day window and the 24-month precedent term are `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.

**Next action (revision):** ~~maintainer decision on the three changes of 8.6~~ (applied 2026-11-12); a synthetic script that replays a year of typical small-node decisions (greenhouse, well, dwelling allocation, microgrid upgrade) against the 5% / 10% limits to see how many fall out of the list; ~~draft the irreversible list jointly with SIM-QA-19~~ (done, OL-11; add its thresholds to the replay script); ~~maintainer decisions on X-7, X-8, X-9~~ (2026-11-13, section 9); ~~re-run the fill model under Set B and H6~~ (done, section 9.2); turnout calibration and census check (Section 7) unchanged.

#### **8.8 Cross-Docket Check with SIM-QA-18, 19, 20** *(2026-11-12; contradictions listed, not resolved)*
* **Agreements verified.** The irreversible list of point 5 is now OL-11 (SIM-QA-19 section 9.2), with the same five items and thresholds added; OL-11 point 6 makes a classification ruling and a certification ruling on the same decision bind each other. 30-day windows and 14-day rulings match SIM-QA-19. Node assemblies meet in person, so the accepted in-person rule of SIM-QA-18 adds nothing here. H5 second reading applies only to councils, which exist from 150 adults, so no node assembly decision is re-read.
* **X-7, the labor-drain finding was computed on full-time councils.** The hour budgets of Section 3 and RED-6 read the current text: six-month mandates with full release, 12.2 h per adult per week at 50 adults and 30% of adults on full release. Under the accepted SIM-QA-18 Set B (councils at 10% of a working week, 4 h), a council of 15 in a node of 50 costs about 15 × 4 / 50 = 1.2 h per adult per week, about the same as the assembly (1.3 h), and less from 100 adults. Under the accepted rules H2 no longer rests on labor; it rests on seat filling, subsidiarity and the end of three-person sovereignty. *Open:* re-state RED-6 under Set B, or keep it as a finding on the current text only? **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 9):** restated; holds for the current text only.
* **X-8, the 150 threshold was computed on fixed mandates.** The fill model of Section 3 used fixed six-month mandates and a twelve-month hiatus. Under Set B with H6 (3–6 months drawn, hiatus twice the mandate served) a council needs more distinct people a year (40 against 30, SIM-QA-18 section 8), which pushes fill failure up; SIM-QA-19 section 8 found that continuous renewal lowers the smallest fully seated node at 80% refusal from 120 to 80 adults (a different measure), which pushes it down. The threshold has not been re-run under the accepted rules. *Open:* re-run the fill model with Set B and H6 before the merge decision; if councils then hold from about 80–100 adults, the residual of Section 7 (nodes of 100–149 lose sortition) weighs more. **Re-run 2026-11-13 (section 9.2):** they do only at refusal ≤ about 60%; the 150 / 120 band itself holds only to about 70%; no lowering proposed. **With the three-month deferral window decided 2026-11-13 (section 10.2):** the band holds to about 80%.
* **X-9, votes cast against all seated members.** The Organic Law line of Section 6 counts assembly thresholds on votes cast, with a quorum. Art. 4.5.4 (merged) counts every threshold on "all seated members, not merely of those present". An Organic Law cannot override the Constitution, and an assembly of all adults has no seated members in the sense of 4.5.4. *Open:* add a carve-out to the Art. 4.2.1 patch or to Art. 4.5.4 itself; not drafted. **Resolved 2026-11-13 (section 9):** carve-out in Art. 4.2.1, consequential sentence in Art. 4.5.4.
* **Also affected:** the certification panel of SIM-QA-19 Art. 4.3.6 draws "from outside the node", the Classification Panel of point 8 "from neither the node nor the larger node" (SIM-QA-19 X-3); the mandate of a Local Mediation Panel drawn per dispute is undefined under the drawn ranges of SIM-QA-19 H6 (SIM-QA-19 X-1). *(Both resolved 2026-11-13 in SIM-QA-19 section 10.)*

### **9. Revision (2026-11-13): Maintainer Decisions on X-7, X-8, X-9 and the Fill Re-Run** *(Stage 2 continued; X-8 measured, synthetic and uncalibrated; the rest reasoning on text)*

#### **9.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **X-7:** RED-6 is restated. The labor-drain argument holds for the current text only. Under Set B the two-level model rests on seat filling, subsidiarity and the end of three-person sovereignty. Applied in Section 4 (RED-6) and Section 5.
* **X-8:** re-run the fill model under the accepted rules at 50, 80, 100, 120 and 150 adults. If a 15-seat council holds from about 100 adults, propose lowering the threshold and the hysteresis band. The 150 decision stands until the maintainer sees the result.
* **X-9:** an explicit carve-out: in an assembly of all adult residents, thresholds are counted on votes cast with a quorum, as an exception to Art. 4.5.4. Drafted in the Art. 4.2.1 patch, with the consequential change to Art. 4.5.4 (Section 6).
* **X-3 and X-1 (SIM-QA-19), applied here:** the mediation and Classification Panels are drawn per dispute under the one rule for outside panels; Section 6 and OL-10 point 8 aligned.

#### **9.2 X-8: Fill Model Re-Run** ([`sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill_results.md); `--reps 1000 --seed 20261113`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**)
*Model.* Council of 15, monthly steps, ten years after a two-year warm-up. **FIX** copies the original model of Section 3 (whole council drawn every six months, twelve-month hiatus, one summons per person per term, no refill during a term); it reproduces the Section 3 figures within sampling error (e.g. 50 adults at r = 0.3: 39% of terms with an empty seat, against 36%; 100 adults at r = 0.8: 50% against 47%). **SETB** applies the accepted rules: each seat draws its own mandate of 3–6 months (H6, Set B), the member then enters a hiatus of twice the mandate served, and the seat is refilled the month it opens. A summoned adult who refuses is not summoned again for six months (the deferral window of SIM-QA-18 H2); locks of three months and one month are sensitivity cases. Panels of three are drawn from outside the node (X-1, X-3) and take no one from it. Part-time service changes hours, not head count, so it enters only through the refusal rate r, which is swept because its level under part-time service is unknown.

| Adults | FIX: months fully seated at r = 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.7 / 0.8 / 0.85 | SETB: same |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 50 | 27% / 13% / 4% / 0% / 0% | 18% / 7% / 2% / 0% / 0% |
| 80 | 100% / 95% / 62% / 17% / 4% | 97% / 78% / 37% / 7% / 1% |
| 100 | 100% / 100% / 96% / 50% / 18% | 100% / 99% / 79% / 24% / 6% |
| 120 | 100% / 100% / 100% / 83% / 44% | 100% / 100% / 97% / 54% / 19% |
| 150 | 100% / 100% / 100% / 99% / 82% | 100% / 100% / 100% / 89% / 51% |

| Smallest node with ≥ 95% of months fully seated | r = 0.5 | 0.6 | 0.7 | 0.8 | 0.85 | 0.9 |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| FIX (original rules) | 70 | 85 | 100 | 135 | 175 | > 200 |
| SETB, refusal lock 6 months | 80 | 95 | 115 | 165 | > 200 | > 200 |
| SETB, lock 3 months | 65 | 75 | 90 | 115 | 145 | 200 |
| SETB, lock 1 month | 60 | 60 | 70 | 80 | 95 | 120 |

**What it shows.**
* **The accepted rules do not lower the threshold; with a six-month deferral they raise it slightly.** Shorter drawn mandates call more people a year (about 40 against 30), and each refusal then keeps a person out of the draw for longer relative to the mandate. A council needs about 10 to 30 more adults than under the original rules for the same reliability.
* **A 15-seat council holds from about 100 adults only if refusal stays at or below about 60%** (SETB, lock 6: 95 adults at r = 0.6; 115 at 0.7; 165 at 0.8). At the 80–90% range on which the 150 decision was built (Section 3), it does not hold at 100.
* **The 150 / 120 band is less robust under Set B than Section 3 said.** Both edges reach 95% of months fully seated up to about r = 0.7 (120 adults: 97%); at r = 0.8, 150 adults are fully seated 89% of months and 120 adults 54%; at 0.85, 51% and 19%. Section 3 stated that 150 holds to about 85–90%; under the accepted rules that is no longer true.
* **The result depends on how long a refusal lasts.** If a refuser can be summoned again the next month (lock 1), a council holds from 80 adults at r = 0.8 and from 95 at 0.85. The deferral window of Organic Law is therefore a threshold parameter, not a detail.

**Proposal: none.** The maintainer's condition (a council holding from about 100 adults) is met only for refusal at or below about 60%, which no data support or exclude. Lowering is not proposed. *Reserve, recorded only:* if field data on part-time civic service show refusal at or below 60%, a band of the same width as the accepted one (30 adults) would be 125 / 95 with both edges at ≥ 95%; with a three-month deferral window, about 105 / 75. **The 150 decision stands.** Two consequences are stated plainly: the 150 / 120 band itself fails above about 70% refusal under Set B, and the length of the deferral window moves the safe threshold by more than any other parameter tested.

#### **9.3 Red Team Against the X-9 Carve-Out**
* **A 75% matter carried by a quarter of adults. `NARROWED`.** With a quorum of one third and thresholds on votes cast, 75% of a bare quorum is 25% of adults, the always-attending bloc of RED-4. *Guard (proposed, Organic Law):* the quorum may differ by threshold, provisional one third for 50%+1 and 60%, one half for 75%. Residual: a bloc of a little over a third still carries 75% at a bare one-half quorum (75% × 50% = 37.5% of adults). Not modelled; turnout calibration (Section 7) applies.
* **Strategic abstention to sink quorum. `NARROWED`.** A minority stays away so the session lacks quorum. Under 4.5.4 counting on all members, absence already counted as no; under votes cast, absence counts as nothing unless quorum fails. *Guard:* a matter that fails quorum twice goes to the next session with the quorum of the lowest threshold. *Added in drafting, for maintainer confirmation.*
* **Scope creep of the exception. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** The carve-out names assemblies of all adult residents only. Every drawn body keeps Art. 4.5.4.
* **Coherence with Art. 2.6.2. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Revocation of usufruct needs 75% "of the seated assembly"; a node below 150 has no seated assembly, and revocation for public infrastructure is not on the OL-10 list, so it goes to the centre council and keeps 4.5.4.

#### **9.4 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 2)**
* The safe threshold depends on refusal under part-time service and on the deferral window, neither measured.
* Under Set B the accepted 150 / 120 band holds only to about 70% refusal *(six-month window; about 80% with the three-month window decided 2026-11-13, section 10.2)*.
* The X-9 carve-out makes small-assembly decisions depend on turnout; quorum levels are placeholders.

**Next action (revision 2):** ~~maintainer review of the X-8 result; Organic Law decision on the deferral window with its effect on the threshold stated~~ (2026-11-13, section 10); turnout calibration and census check unchanged; replay script of small-node decisions unchanged.

### **10. Revision (2026-11-13, second batch): Deferral Window, Threshold Robustness, Y-9 and Y-10** *(Stage 2 continued; X-8 measured, synthetic and uncalibrated; Y-9 and Y-10 reasoning on text)*

#### **10.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **X-8:** the 150 / 120 threshold stands. The deferral window after a refusal is set at **three months** instead of six, in Organic Law (SIM-QA-18 section 11). The resulting robustness is stated in 10.2.
* **Y-1:** the SIM-QA-19 fill row is corrected to the same three-month window (SIM-QA-19 section 11).
* **Y-2 (X-6):** stays open until the merge decision of this docket. No change.
* **Y-9 and Y-10 (done without further decision):** consequential wording for Arts. 2.6.1, 3.6.1 and 4.1.1; "plebiscites" dropped if it has no object after merge. Drafted in 10.3.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

#### **10.2 X-8: Robustness of the 150 / 120 Band with a Three-Month Window** ([`sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_21_x8_set_b_fill_results.md); `--reps 1000 --seed 20261113`; the months table now also reports SETB with a 3-month lock; earlier rows unchanged; **synthetic, uncalibrated**)

| Adults | SETB, lock 3: months fully seated at r = 0.6 / 0.7 / 0.8 / 0.85 / 0.9 | SETB, lock 6 (section 9.2), same |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 120 | 100% / 100% / 97% / 81% / 35% | 100% / 97% / 54% / 19% / 2% |
| 150 | 100% / 100% / 100% / 97% / 69% | 100% / 100% / 89% / 51% / 9% |

Smallest node with ≥ 95% of months fully seated, lock 3: 75 adults at r = 0.6, 90 at 0.7, 115 at 0.8, 145 at 0.85, 200 at 0.9 (section 9.2, unchanged).

**Stated plainly.** With a three-month window, both edges of the 150 / 120 band are fully seated in at least 95% of months up to 80% refusal (150 adults: 100%; 120 adults: 97%). At 85% refusal the upper edge still holds (150: 97%) but the lower edge does not (120: 81%), so a node that shrinks toward 120 adults runs short of members before it dissolves into the assembly. At 90% neither edge holds (150: 69%; 120: 35%). Against the six-month window of section 9.2 the band holds about ten more points of refusal (to about 80% instead of about 70%), but it still falls short of what Section 3 claimed for the original rules (150 holding to about 85–90%): under the accepted rules the 150 edge holds to about 85%, the 120 edge to about 80%. Every figure is synthetic; refusal under part-time service is not measured.

*Section 9.2 superseded in part:* its sentence "the 150 / 120 band itself holds only to about 70%" is true for a six-month window; with the decided three-month window, read the paragraph above. The reserve of section 9.2 (a lower band if refusal proves ≤ 60%) is unchanged: about 105 / 75 with this window.

#### **10.3 Y-9 and Y-10: Consequential Wording** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`
* **Art. 4.1.1 (coherence, revised; Y-10):** "… emanates exclusively from assemblies of citizens chosen by stratified random lottery (sortition), and from the assemblies of all adult residents of Article 4.2.1." *"Plebiscites" is dropped:* the only plebiscite in canon is the 4.2.1 escalation of three-person panels (one occurrence in the whole text), which the Section 6 sub-paragraph replaces, and the SIM-QA-19 plebiscite safeguards are withdrawn. "Node assemblies" is replaced by the defined term, so the line names one thing. The same wording replaces the SIM-QA-19 line (its section 6).
* **Art. 2.6.1, first item (Y-9):** "Neighborhood land-use adjustments: adjudicated by Neighborhood Sortition Councils comprising fifteen (15) citizens by a sixty percent (60%) qualified majority; for a node without a Council (Article 4.2.1), by the Council of the nearest larger node, after hearing the node's assembly." Land use is not on the closed list of internal matters (OL-10 point 2), so it belongs to the centre council.
* **Art. 3.6.1, first item (Y-9):** "Neighborhood utility monitoring: adjudicated by Neighborhood Sortition Councils comprising fifteen (15) citizens; in a node without a Council, its own water and local grid are monitored by its assembly of all adult residents (Article 4.2.1), and its connection to the larger node by the Council of that node." Water and the local grid are internal matters (OL-10 point 2 (b), (d)); the connection point is shared.
* **Art. 2.6.2 (Y-9):** "… certified unanimously by the Local Mediation Panel" becomes "… certified unanimously by a Local Mediation Panel drawn for that decree under the rule for outside panels of Article 4.2". *Drafting choice, for maintainer confirmation:* the docket did not say whether this panel is drawn from outside. Under X-3 the assembly that revokes and the usufructuary are parties, so a panel from the node would sit on its own assembly's decree; the outside rule follows from the one rule for outside panels. The "seated assembly" of 2.6.2 is unchanged: a node below 150 has none, and revocation for public infrastructure goes to the centre council (section 9.3).

#### **10.4 Red Team Against the Consequential Wording**
* **2.6.1, the centre decides a small node's land use. `BLUE_HOLDS`**, same finding as RED-1 under H2: land use reaches beyond the node; the hearing duty and the *Provocatio* against a decision concerning the node alone apply. Residual: a centre rezoning many small nodes at once is not "the node alone".
* **3.6.1, a node monitors its own utilities. `NARROWED`.** Self-monitoring is a conflict of interest, as W-6 found. *Guards already drafted:* the open ledger, meters at the connection point read by both sides, and the Classification Panel on the flow test. Residual: unmetered nodes monitor themselves on estimates.
* **2.6.2, an outside panel must agree unanimously. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Revocation for infrastructure is never urgent in the sense of Art. 8.1.2, so seating time is not critical. Red: unanimity of three outsiders is a strong brake; that is the canon's intent for a 75% decree, and a refusal is already appealable to fifteen.
* **4.1.1 without plebiscites. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** If a later docket brings back a plebiscite, 4.1.1 must name it again; the line is a closed list by design.

#### **10.5 Residual Weaknesses (Revision 3)**
* Robustness depends on refusal under part-time service, which is not measured; the window only moves the curve.
* The 2.6.2 outside-panel choice needs confirmation.
* The residuals of section 9.4 on turnout and quorum stand.

**Next action (revision 3):** maintainer confirmation of the 2.6.2 drafting choice; X-6 at the merge decision; turnout calibration, census check and the replay script of small-node decisions unchanged.

## **SIM-QA-22: Lost Provision — Planetary Auxiliary Language**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-12  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-12 (qualitative O-ASIS red-team pass on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — Provenance: the provision was **lost in compression, not withdrawn by a recorded decision** (section 1). Verdict on current text (silence) **`RED_WINS`** on four of seven vectors. H1 (technology-neutral principle in Art. 5.3) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**, on three conditions: a constructed language, never an existing national one; an explicit clause that fluency is never a condition of eligibility; and a relative, not absolute, rule on machine translation. Those three conditions are the drafting; without any one of them H1 **`RED_WINS`**. Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. **Maintainer decision 2026-11-12 (section 5.1):** H2 accepted in principle (own-language right also in Art. 1.4.1); H1 alone withdrawn; the Art. 1.4.1 phrase reads as a liberty of use, not a claim on services, **`BLUE_HOLDS`**. Chapter V word ceiling waived for this paragraph by maintainer decision (section 5.1). **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-16 (section 7.1):** every remaining side-audit item decided: seven restored in principle (D-1 to D-7, drafting pending), six moved (D-8 to D-13), four dropped with reason (D-14 to D-17); gene therapy and contraception opened as SIM-QA-27 and SIM-QA-28. **Drafting 2026-11-16 (section 8):** D-1 to D-7 drafted and red-teamed (`BLUE_HOLDS` or `NARROWED`; D-7 `RED_WINS` on the liar's-dividend vector, residual); Organic Law OL-13 to OL-21 and the D-8 roadmap entry drafted, not entered; D-15 options listed; Chapter I at 1,181 of 1,200 and a possible Chapter IV overrun of 9 words reported.  
* **Originating Context:** Maintainer review: the three-layer linguistic model of the v1.0 constitution is absent from the current canon.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.4.1, 4.3.1, 4.4 (SIM-QA-20 Art. 4.4.6), 5.3, 6.5, 7.1.2, 10.4, 10.6; Organic Law (SIM-QA-17 separation)  
* **Evidence:** reasoning on the text only. No quantitative claim below is measured; the one external figure is recorded as supplied and must be checked against its primary source before any canon use.

### **1. Provenance: When and Why It Was Dropped** *(forensic; reported before drafting)*
* **What v1.0 held.** `bible/ONE_NETWORKED_EARTH_v1.0_canonical.bak.md` carries the model in four places: the opening overview ("a balanced linguistic model", line 19); **Section VII.2, The Tri-Level Linguistic Architecture** (lines 407–419: Layer 1 regional or ancestral language chosen by communities, with archives of every language; Layer 2 ambient machine translation; Layer 3 a constructed Universal Auxiliary Language taught worldwide as a second tongue, so that deliberation can happen without machine mediation); **Section XIV.3, Human Linguistic Continuity** (lines 899–901: when electronics fail, a Civic Linguistic Guild of human mediators fluent in the auxiliary language staffs public squares and triage stations); and the glossary entry *Universal Auxiliary Language* (line 1178).
* **What the current canon holds.** Nothing. The only hits for "language" in the 67 articles are plain-language duties (Arts. 3.6.1, 4.4.3, 7.5.2). Art. 1.4.1 protects "cultural expression", not schooling in one's own language. Art. 5.3 is silent.
* **When.** The intermediate backup `bible/ONE_NETWORKED_EARTH_pre_v2_batch.bak.md` already has no language provision. The loss therefore happened in the v1.0 → v2 rewrite into ten chapters, before the pre-v2 batch. This repository cannot date it more precisely: here `bible/` is git-ignored and has never been tracked, and the modification times of both backups are copy dates, not authorship dates. The date comes from the dated finding below.
* **Dated finding (recorded 2026-11-13, from the private parent repository, which tracked `bible/` in git):** the v1.0 text (30,102 words, with the auxiliary language) was committed on **2026-09-09**. On **2026-09-11** the commit "ratify 10-chapter architecture" replaced it with a 7,396-word text that no longer contains the language provision. The loss is therefore dated to that one commit, two days after v1.0 entered version control, and is a roughly fourfold cut in a single step. The v1.0 backup used for this docket and its side audit is identical to the parent repository's copy and differs from the original v1.0 only by the diagrams added later. *Word counts and dates as supplied by the maintainer from that history; not re-measured here (a plain word count of the backup, diagrams included, gives a different figure because tools count differently).*
* **Why: no recorded decision.**
  * `bible/methodology.md` Section II.1 ("Sci-Fi Purge") lists four removals: an omniscient network, molecular fabrication, asteroid mining and planetary defense fleets, instant fiat dissolution. Language is not among them. The Layer 2 devices (bone-conduction transceivers, optical displays) would fail its realism filter, but Layers 1 and 3 are not technology.
  * `bible/redline.md` maps legacy sections into the ten chapters ("Consolidates Legacy Sections"). No chapter consolidates Section VII by topic (neither religion nor language). Chapter V takes education, Chapter VI takes black-sky resilience; neither carries the language clauses. The redline's legacy section numbers do not match the v1.0 file's numbering, so the map was written against an earlier outline, which is consistent with a section falling through.
  * `bible/ledger.md`, `bible/revision_roadmap.md`, the SIM-QA board and resolution log contain no language decision.
  * The concision filter (600–900 words per chapter) is the probable mechanism.
* **Verdict: loss during compression.** High confidence that no decision is recorded in the files present. The date is fixed by the parent repository's history: the 2026-09-11 commit that ratified the ten-chapter architecture. Its message, as supplied, names the restructuring, not the removal of any provision.
* **Public inconsistency (side finding, not changed here).** The public portal dataset `webapp/data/synoptic.json` (principle 2.7, "Communication: Local Languages + One Universal Global Language") still promises every child their mother tongue plus one shared global auxiliary language. The public summary states a principle the canon does not contain. If this docket is rejected, the synoptic must be corrected; if adopted, the two agree again.

### **2. The Friction**
* **Upper tiers have no common tongue.** Art. 4.3.1 stratifies by geography, so Continental (301) and Global (1,001) bodies and the globally drawn Evidence Panels of SIM-QA-20 will seat people who share no language. SIM-QA-20 Section 6.2 already found language `RED_WINS` on its global register and fell back on drawn interpreters.
* **Machine mediation of deliberation.** Without a common language, deliberation across bioregions runs through translation. A model between citizens is a framing channel (SIM-QA-20 RED-7) and sits uneasily with Art. 7.1.2, which voids the delegation of legislative drafting to models: a text that most of its drafters can read only through a model comes close to that delegation.
* **Black-sky.** Art. 6.5 keeps water, power and medical stores operable without electronics. It keeps nothing for communication between people who share no language once translation fails. v1.0 Section XIV.3 did.
* **Movement.** Art. 10.4 guarantees freedom of movement; a newcomer who shares no language with the receiving node is admitted but not able to take part.
* **Schooling in one's own language.** Without Layer 1, nothing in canon prevents a node from schooling every child only in the dominant language of the area. *UNESCO's Global Education Monitoring Report (policy paper, 2016) estimates that about 40% of the world's population lacks education in a language they speak or understand.* Recorded as supplied; check against the primary source before canon use.

### **3. Hypotheses to Test**
* **H0, silence:** keep canon silent; handle language case by case (drawn interpreters per docket).
* **H1, technology-neutral principle in Art. 5.3 (maintainer):** (a) every child's right to their own language; (b) one neutral common language taught to all as a second tongue; (c) deliberation never dependent on machine translation; all devices and methods in Organic Law (SIM-QA-17).
* **H2, split placement:** as H1, but (a) goes into Chapter I as a phrase in Art. 1.4.1 (invariant), and (b)–(c) into Art. 5.3.
* **H3, voluntary common language:** the common language is offered in every school, never required.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team**
* **Current text (H0): `RED_WINS`** on RED-4 (by default, per docket, language becomes a filter, as SIM-QA-20 nearly did), RED-6 (deliberation across tiers runs through models), black-sky communication, and schooling in one's own language. H0 is not neutral: silence leaves the dominant languages to decide.
* **RED-1, which language and who chooses.**
  * *An existing national language: `RED_WINS`.* Its native speakers would deliberate in their mother tongue in every upper-tier body, everyone else in a second language, for ever. That turns linguistic inheritance into a permanent deliberative advantage, the kind of unearned power Art. 4.1 abolishes, and it rewards the languages spread by past empire.
  * *A constructed language: `NARROWED`.* No one is a native speaker in the first generation, so no group starts ahead. It is not neutral: a vocabulary "synthesized from major language families" (v1.0) favours speakers of those families. The best-known existing constructed language draws most of its roots from Romance and Germanic languages and is easier for Europeans. Less unequal, not equal.
  * *Who chooses: `NARROWED` with guard.* The choice is close to irreversible (switching after a generation of schooling is ruinous) and global, so it is a large capture prize. *Guard:* canon fixes the criteria (no people's national language; owned by no one; regular grammar and spelling; grammar and vocabulary held as an open commons) and requires 75% of the Global Commons Assembly to choose or change it. It is a candidate for the closed list of irreversible decisions (SIM-QA-19 OL-11, proposed; not changed here).
  * *Who maintains it: `NARROWED`.* A standards body that admits new words becomes an academy that can gatekeep. *Guard:* the vocabulary is an open commons; additions are open to anyone; changes to grammar go to a drawn body with a slow ratchet fixed by Organic Law. Residual: informal prestige of early fluent speakers.
* **RED-2, a generation-long transition.** Children who start after adoption reach working fluency years later; most adults never will. For decades assemblies will hold fluent young members and non-fluent older ones.
  * *Absolute wording ("deliberation never depends on machine translation"): `RED_WINS`.* It is false on the day of adoption and for decades after.
  * *Relative wording ("never depends solely on machine translation; a human route always exists"): `NARROWED`.* During the transition the human route is the drawn interpreter of SIM-QA-20; later it is the common language, with interpreters kept for those who do not speak it. Red: fluent young members and interpreters become a bridge class, an age gradient of influence in upper tiers and a small new priesthood (the Art. 7.5 concern, applied to language). *Guards:* every member speaks their own language in every body; interpreters are drawn by lot with a hiatus, never chosen by a party; original statements are authentic, translations annexed. Residual: oral exchange is slower and thinner until most members share the common language.
* **RED-3, pressure on small languages despite Layer 1: `NARROWED`, residual large.** A child of a small language community may face three languages: the home language, the dominant language of the area, and the common language. Families choose what seems useful, and language shift is driven by use, not by law. Red: a mandatory common language adds a third load and may hasten the loss of the first. Blue: a common language removes the need to learn a dominant national language for wider contact, so the pressure on small languages comes mainly from the regional dominant language, not from the auxiliary; whether this holds is unproven. *Guards:* Layer 1 is a right to be schooled in the language, not tolerance of it; communities choose the languages of their schools; the common language is a second tongue, never the medium of early schooling; archives of every language preserve, but do not replace, living use. Residual: no law stops a language that families stop speaking.
* **RED-4, a hidden eligibility filter.** *Without an explicit clause: `RED_WINS`.* Once a common language exists, every upper-tier body and global panel has a reason to prefer people who speak it. During the transition that means the young and the schooled; the global pool quietly becomes a pool of speakers, against Art. 4.3.1 stratification and against the SIM-QA-20 guard that "language is never a filter". *With the clause* (fluency is never a condition for sortition, for any register, or for any office; every member speaks their own language): **`BLUE_HOLDS`** on formal filters. Informal dominance of fluent members in mixed bodies remains, as the academic register does in SIM-QA-20: `NARROWED`. *Candidate guard, not proposed until tested:* language community as a stratification variable for upper tiers in Organic Law.
* **RED-5, school hours against the Art. 5.3 triad: `NARROWED`.** Ages 0 to 7 are reserved for tactile nature, soil, geometry, acoustic music and unmeasured play; formal language lessons would breach that. Young children acquire languages by exposure, so the common language fits 0–7 only as oral use, songs and play, never as a subject. From 7 to 13 it competes with de-biasing, statistics, systems and dialogue. Assumed cost: two to three hours a week (`PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`; no curriculum measured). *Guard:* hours, age of introduction and methods in Organic Law; Art. 5.3.1 already requires replicated evidence, and the research literature on mother-tongue-based multilingual schooling must be cited from primary sources before merge. Residual: for children with a home language different from the regional one, a third language is a real load; no figure.
* **RED-6, machine translation and technology neutrality: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** The clause names no device. It sets a functional requirement (a human route exists) and does not ban translation as an aid. It also closes the framing channel of SIM-QA-20 RED-7 for deliberation, not for daily life.
* **RED-7, cultural monoculture by the back door: `NARROWED`.** A common language with no native speakers carries less culture than a national one, but a shared second tongue becomes the language of global media and of the archive, and prestige follows. *Guard:* the common language is a bridge, never the language of a node's own institutions unless its residents choose it. Residual: prestige cannot be legislated.
* **H3, voluntary: fails the friction.** An optional language offered beside two others is learned by few; it gives no common route for deliberation and no black-sky fallback. It also concentrates the language in those already advantaged, which RED-4 then turns into influence.
* **H2 against H1.** The right to one's own language is a right of the person, not only an education rule; placing a phrase in Art. 1.4.1 gives it Chapter I standing and the primacy of Art. 1.7. The rest is education and belongs in 5.3. H2 costs a few words in Chapter I; H1 alone leaves the right amendable as an ordinary education clause. **H2 holds up slightly better.** *(Maintainer decision 2026-11-12: H2, section 5.1.)*

### **5. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged; H2 accepted in principle 2026-11-12 (section 5.1)*
* **Art. 5.3, new paragraph 3, Languages:** "Every child has the right to be schooled in the language of their family or community; local communities choose the languages of their schools, and the commons keeps open archives of every human language. Every child also learns, as a second tongue, one common language, the same everywhere, that is no people's national language and is owned by no one. It is chosen or changed only by seventy-five percent (75%) of the Global Commons Assembly. Fluency in it is never a condition for sortition, for any register, or for any office, and every citizen may speak and write in their own language in every assembly and panel. No deliberation or vote depends solely on machine translation; a human route always exists. Methods, hours and the transition are fixed by Organic Law."
* **Art. 1.4.1 (H2, accepted in principle 2026-11-12):** "… voluntary association, and cultural expression, including the use of one's own language."
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** criteria for the common language (regular grammar and phonetic spelling; open commons licence; no people's national language); oral introduction at ages 0–7 through song and play, formal study from 7, provisional two to three hours a week; maintenance of the vocabulary as an open commons; the register of drawn interpreters and its hiatus; the transition measure (until most members of a tier speak the common language, drawn interpreters for every member who asks). All `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.
* **Editor:** Chapter V is 885 words; the new paragraph adds about 120, to roughly 1,000, above the 600–900 ceiling. *(Maintainer decision 2026-11-12: the ceiling is waived for Chapter V for this paragraph; Arts. 5.3–5.4 are not tightened. See section 5.1.)*
* **Interaction with other dockets:** SIM-QA-20 (language of global Evidence Panels; drawn interpreters) and SIM-QA-18 H7 (online upper tiers): if SIM-QA-22 is adopted, drawn interpreters become a transition measure, not the permanent answer; cross-references added there, patches unchanged. SIM-QA-19 OL-11: the choice of the common language is a candidate irreversible decision; not added. Art. 6.5: the v1.0 Civic Linguistic Guild (human mediators at public squares during black-sky) is not restored; a later docket may decide whether the common language enters the Black-Sky Protocol.

#### **5.1 Maintainer Decision (2026-11-12) and Red Team Against It** *(reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*
* **Decision:** H2. The right to one's own language goes into Art. 1.4.1 with Chapter I standing; the schooling right, the common language, the non-filter clause and the rule on machine translation stay in Art. 5.3. H1 alone is withdrawn. Canon is unchanged until the merge decision.
* **Procedure: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Art. 4.5.1 allows expansions of Invariant Rights by 75% and Art. 1.7 forbids their diminution; adding the phrase is an expansion. Once merged, it cannot be narrowed later, which is the purpose.
* **Over-reading as a claim on services: `RED_WINS` on a broad wording, `BLUE_HOLDS` on the drafted one.** A Chapter I right "to one's own language" could be read as a claim to every public service, document and session in any language, a cost no node can meet for hundreds of languages. The drafted phrase extends a liberty ("cultural expression, including the use of one's own language"): no one may be barred or penalised for using their language. The positive claims are bounded and sit in 5.3 (schooling chosen by the community; one's own language in every assembly and panel, served by drawn interpreters under Organic Law). *Guard:* keep the 1.4.1 phrase as a liberty of use; do not widen it at merge.
* **Conflict with the common language: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** A Chapter I right to one's language could be cited against the compulsory second tongue of 5.3. The common language is learned in addition to, never instead of, one's own; learning a second tongue bars no use of the first. Residual: a community that refuses the common language for its children has a Chapter I argument to raise before the Art. 4.6 review; that case is not decided here.
* **Primacy over the Organic Law: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** School-hour rules or interpreter rules that in practice suppress a language (no hours left for it, no interpreter offered) are void under Art. 1.7.2. This gives Layer 1 teeth against RED-3, within the limit that law cannot stop families from abandoning a language.
* **Chapter V word ceiling (maintainer decision 2026-11-12): waived** *(retired 2026-11-13 by maintainer decision, Y-16: replaced by board instruction 10a; Chapter V with this paragraph counts 986 words, under the general ceiling of 1,000)* for the Languages paragraph; Chapter V rises from 885 to about 1,000 words. Red: a waiver is a precedent, and every later docket can claim one, so the 600–900 rule of `bible/methodology.md` erodes chapter by chapter. *Guard:* the waiver covers this paragraph only, is recorded here by name, and gives no general allowance; any further excess in Chapter V needs its own decision. Verdict: **`NARROWED`**. Residual: the methodology rule is now a default with recorded exceptions, not a hard limit. *Update 2026-11-13: the maintainer raised the chapter ceiling from 900 to 1,000 words for every chapter. Chapter V at about 1,000 words sits at the new ceiling; the waiver may no longer be needed once the paragraph is counted exactly.* *Confirmed 2026-11-12: the waiver applies to this chapter only. The maintainer also adopts a Compression Disclosure Rule (`bible/methodology.md` III.2b; board instruction 8): any shortening that would remove or weaken a point is listed and decided point by point, never done silently. The 21 losses of section 7 are the failure this rule prevents.*

### **6. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)**
* No constructed language is neutral in vocabulary; the patch makes it less unequal, not equal.
* The transition lasts a generation or more. During it, the patch changes little in practice: interpreters carry the load, as in SIM-QA-20.
* Language shift cannot be legislated away. Layer 1 protects schooling, not the choice of families to stop speaking a language.
* Informal dominance of fluent speakers in mixed bodies survives the formal non-filter clause.
* School hours are assumed, not measured; the three-language load on minority children has no figure.
* The choice itself is a one-shot global vote with no local fallback, the same structural risk SIM-QA-20 found in one global register.
* Real-time interpretation of every member's own language at 1,001 seats is a large standing cost; its size is not estimated.

**Next action:** ~~maintainer decision on H1 or H2~~ (H2, 2026-11-12); ~~maintainer decision on the Chapter V word ceiling~~ (waived for this paragraph, 2026-11-12); maintainer decision on canon diff; primary-source check of the UNESCO figure and of the evidence on mother-tongue-based multilingual schooling; a criteria test of existing constructed languages against the Organic Law criteria (outside canon, no language named in canon); a decision on whether the common language enters Art. 6.5; correction or confirmation of `webapp/data/synoptic.json` principle 2.7 once the docket closes.
