#### **8.1 D-1, Anti-Haste Amendment (Art. 4.5, new point 5; Chapter IV)**
*Draft:*
> 5. **Amendment:** A change to this Constitution passes two votes of the Global Commons Assembly, each by the 75% Supermajority. The second, held by newly drawn members who did not sit for the first, follows a cooling period of one to three years set by Organic Law.

*Red team:*
* **A-1, urgent repair.** A defect in the text is being exploited and the fix waits at least a year. *Guard:* Art. 4.6 review voids an application that breaches the Constitution, and Art. 4.5.3 emergency decrees cover administration for thirty days. Neither can change the text. **`NARROWED`**; residual: a genuine textual flaw stays for a year. Accepted as the price of the rule; an urgent-amendment exception is the hole v1.0 closed.
* **A-2, freezing by Organic Law.** A 60% majority sets the pause so long that the Constitution never changes. *Guard:* the canon bound of one to three years (ceiling and floor). **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.
* **A-3, the same faction twice.** The second vote is held by the same seated members, so the pause only delays. *Guard:* newly drawn members who did not sit for the first vote. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**; residual: seating a second body of 1,001 adds a session at the tier where in-person attendance is already the hardest (SIM-QA-18 Y-3).
* **A-4, which assembly.** Art. 4.5.1 names no body; a bioregion could claim to amend for itself. *Guard:* the draft names the Global Commons Assembly. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**. Open: whether expansions of rights (Art. 4.5.1) count as a "change to this Constitution". By the wording they do; the maintainer confirms.
* **A-5, text altered between votes.** A changed text passes the second vote without its own pause. *Guard in OL-13 point 3.* **`BLUE_HOLDS`** with the Organic Law.

*Count:* +47 words. Chapter IV working text 2,064 → **2,111 of 2,150 (39 left)**. **Conflict reported (instruction 8, rule 10a(5)):** with Y-12 and the Y-3 bound (2,112 without D-1) the chapter would reach about **2,159, 9 over**. Not cut. The approved plain-language Part D saves 18 words on Part B; if the maintainer adopts Part D with the layout, the chapter fits. Otherwise the maintainer decides between Y-12, the Y-3 bound and D-1.
*Not carried in the canon sentence:* the plain-language check and red-team testimony before the first vote (to OL-13 point 2); pilots during the cooling period (to OL-13 point 4); the digital-twin simulation and the "compiler kernels" signing (device, methodology purge (a)); the 500-member size and the 0% overlap figure (replaced by the 1,001 tier and "did not sit for the first").

#### **8.2 D-2, Inter-Basin Deadlock Forum (Art. 10.3.3; Chapter X)**
*Draft (replaces Art. 10.3.3):*
> 3. A dispute between basins over shared flows goes first to a forum drawn by lot from basins with no stake in it, which rules within a deadline set by Organic Law, never longer than thirty (30) days. Until it rules, no basin cuts any flow below the survival baseline of Article 1.3. Hoarding life-support resources, forming cartels, or cutting off downstream water in defiance of its ruling triggers automatic Systemic Decoupling (*Disiunctio Reticularis*): immediate suspension of inter-basin grid interties, transit corridors, and advanced medical buffers until reciprocity is restored.

*Red team:*
* **B-1, delay as a weapon.** The upstream basin cuts water and lets the forum run. *Guard:* the survival floor applies at once, before any ruling. **`NARROWED`**; residual: flows above the survival floor (irrigation, industry) are lost for up to thirty days.
* **B-2, forum stacking.** "No stake" is read loosely. *Guard:* drawn by lot; OL-14 point 1 defines "no stake" on countable facts (not adjacent, not sharing the catchment, not party to a compact with either side), the same form as the SIM-QA-20 filters. **`BLUE_HOLDS`** with the Organic Law.
* **B-3, hoarding without a dispute.** Cartels and hoarding are not "disputes between basins"; the draft makes decoupling wait for a forum that no one convenes. *Guard:* OL-14 point 2 lets any affected basin or a Guardian of the Biosphere (Art. 10.2.2) file. **`NARROWED`**; residual: decoupling is no longer automatic on the first act, by design of D-2.
* **B-4, ruling ignored in part.** A basin complies in name and sends a trickle. *Guard:* the ruling states flows in measured quantities; telemetry under Art. 3.4 decides compliance. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.

*Count:* +58 words. Chapter X 870 → **928 of 1,000**. Total effect noted in 8.8.
*Not carried in the canon sentence:* the twenty-twenty-ten composition (replaced by a forum drawn from outside, the single rule for outside panels of SIM-QA-19 X-3; its size goes to OL-14); each side argues the other's case first (to OL-14 point 4); **compensation in physical flows or infrastructure instead of cutting flows** (to OL-14 point 5); telemetry grounding (already Art. 3.4); hardware lockouts on valves and the reserve-dispatch device (device, not carried).

#### **8.3 D-3, Safety Interventions (Art. 8.5, new point 4; Chapter VIII)**
*Draft:*
> 4. Every safety intervention is entered in a public log that names no restrained person. A panel of citizens drawn by lot reviews each physical restraint. Guilds may separate a person from immediate danger but hold no power to detain.

*Red team:*
* **C-1, identification by context.** In a node of fifty, a log entry with date and place names the person without a name. *Guard:* none in the sentence; OL detail would aggregate small nodes. **`NARROWED`**; residual recorded for an Organic Law entry (not among the items the maintainer listed; proposed as OL-6 extension, not drafted).
* **C-2, who restrains.** Art. 8.5.3 assumes an emergency detention exists; if guilds cannot detain, no one can. *Guard:* separation from immediate danger stays with guilds; any hold beyond it falls under Art. 8.5.3 (validation within 72 hours). **`NARROWED`**; residual: the line between "separating" and "detaining" is not timed. Candidate bound: separation ends within hours unless Art. 8.5.3 validation is sought. Not drafted; for the maintainer.
* **C-3, review backlog.** Every restraint reviewed overloads juries. *Guard:* the panel of three drawn per case (SIM-QA-19 X-1). **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.

*Count:* +40 words. Chapter VIII 862 → **902 of 1,000**.
*Not carried in the canon sentence:* independent ethicists on the review panel (not carried; the maintainer decides whether OL adds them); the cryptographic form of the log (Arts. 7.3 and 7.4 apply by their own terms).

#### **8.4 D-4, Right to Remain Unaugmented (Art. 1.2, new point 4; Chapter I)**
*Draft:*
> 4. No right or baseline service depends on accepting a technology on or in one's body; every person may remain unaugmented.

*Red team:*
* **E-1, vaccination in an epidemic.** A vaccine is a technology in the body; the clause bars making care or travel conditional on it. Art. 1.2.3 already bars a mandate on the body. Quarantine and distance measures remain available. **`BLUE_HOLDS`** as consistent with Art. 1.2.3; residual: the epidemic case is decided by the existing text, not by this clause.
* **E-2, soft exclusion.** No right is denied, but meetings and work are run through devices worn on the body. *Guard:* "baseline service" covers access to civic service and the commons. **`NARROWED`**; residual: informal pressure is not reached.
* **E-3, authentication.** Consistent with Art. 7.4 (non-biometric). **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.

*Count:* +21 words.
*Not carried:* the v1.0 list (civic participation, sortition eligibility, education, culture) is covered by "no right"; "without subtle marginalization" is weakened to E-2's residual. Listed for decision.

#### **8.5 D-5, Care (Art. 1.3.2 list and new Art. 1.3.4; Chapter I)**
*Draft:*
> (in the Art. 1.3.2 list) * Comprehensive primary, preventative, emergency, palliative, rehabilitative, and geriatric healthcare, with care and support for disability;
> 4. Care of the ill, the aged, and the disabled is never cut to raise output elsewhere.

*Red team:*
* **F-1, true scarcity.** In a famine or a grid failure every allocation is cut. *Guard:* the clause bars cutting care *to raise output elsewhere*, not sharing a shortfall. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**; residual: proportional cuts in a crisis are allowed, which matches the v1.0 wording ("to prioritize aggregate output").
* **F-2, Leontief floor.** A guarantee of rehabilitative and geriatric care is a claim on labour and compute. It sits in the baseline that Art. 3.3 already ranks first. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**; quantity is a SIMONE question (SIM-QA-16).

*Count:* +9 and +17 = +26 words.
*Not carried:* "high-standard" (no measure; recorded as vague, kept out); the robotic care device (not carried).

**Chapter I after D-4 and D-5:** 1,134 → **1,181 of 1,200 (19 left)**. Under rule 10a(5) a chapter within fifty words of its ceiling sends new detail to Organic Law by default. D-4 and D-5 are rights, so the placement test keeps them. **Conflict reported (instruction 8):** SIM-QA-24, 25, 27 and 28 all propose Chapter I text; nineteen words cannot hold them. Not resolved here; the maintainer decides when those dockets open.

#### **8.6 D-6, Vulnerability Disclosure (Art. 7.6.1, added sentence; Chapter VII)**
*Draft (appended to Art. 7.6.1):*
> Anyone who finds a security flaw in common infrastructure reports it, in the time and manner set by Organic Law, so that it is fixed and then published. No person, guild, or assembly may keep an unreported flaw.

*Red team:*
* **G-1, defence holds exploits.** The transitional defence of Art. 8.1.4 (standoff disablement of uncrewed control infrastructure) may want flaws in hostile systems. The sentence covers common infrastructure only, so it does not bar this. **`NARROWED`**; residual: a flaw in an open standard used by both sides is both; OL-15 point 4 resolves it in favour of reporting.
* **G-2, publication before the fix.** Immediate publication arms attackers. *Guard:* "fixed and then published"; OL-15 keeps the v1.0 24-hour commitment as a sealed record. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.
* **G-3, finder punished.** *Guard:* OL-15 point 5. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.

*Count:* +38 words.
*Not carried in the canon sentence:* the 24-hour broadcast (to OL-15 as a sealed commitment, with publication after the fix: **weakened** from v1.0, which made the hash public at once; listed for decision); the registry device (Arts. 7.3, 7.4).

#### **8.7 D-7, Synthetic Media (Art. 7.5, new point 4; Chapter VII)**
*Draft:*
> 4. Synthetic Media: Images, sound, or video generated or altered by machine are disclosed as such whenever presented as a record of real persons or events. Media offered as evidence to any assembly or jury carries a verifiable record of its origin, which need not name its author. The means are set by Organic Law.

*Red team:*
* **H-1, art and satire.** A rule on all synthetic output burdens expression (Art. 1.4.1). *Guard:* only media presented as a record of real persons or events. **`BLUE_HOLDS`**.
* **H-2, provenance as tracking.** A record of origin can expose a whistleblower. *Guard:* it need not name its author. **`NARROWED`**; residual: device metadata can identify; OL-16 point 3.
* **H-3, unprovable claims.** Real footage without a record is dismissed as fake (the liar's dividend). *Guard:* none in the sentence; lay ground-truthing of Art. 3.4.5 applies to sensors only. **`RED_WINS` on this vector**, residual recorded.

*Count:* +55 words. Chapter VII 712 + 38 (D-6) + 55 → **805 of 1,000**.
*Not carried:* the v1.0 duty to watermark all generative output (**weakened** to media presented as a record of real events; listed for decision, because a general watermark rule fails H-1); crowd attestation of events (device, not carried).

#### **8.9 Organic Law Drafts** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(proposed, 60%; not entered in the Annex)*
**OL-13, Amendment Procedure** *(implements Art. 4.5.5, D-1)*
1. The cooling period is [eighteen months] from the first vote.
2. Before the first vote the text is published with a plain-language summary (Art. 7.5) and heard with rival testimony (Art. 4.4).
3. The second vote is on the same text. Any change starts the procedure again.
4. During the cooling period, a part of the amendment that can be tested without breaching Chapter I may run as a pilot under OL-20 point 3; its results go to the second assembly.
5. If the second vote fails, the same text returns no sooner than [two years].

**OL-14, Inter-Basin Forum** *(implements Art. 10.3.3, D-2)*
1. The forum holds fifteen (15) members drawn from basins that are not adjacent to either party, do not share its catchment and hold no compact with either party.
2. Either party, any affected basin, or a Guardian of the Biosphere may file. The forum is seated within [seven days] and rules within [twenty-one days].
3. The ruling states flows in measured quantities and is checked against the telemetry of Art. 3.4.
4. Each party first presents the other party's case; the forum disregards a presentation it finds unfaithful.
5. The forum may order compensation in physical flows, energy or infrastructure from either party or from third basins that consent, instead of a cut in flows.
6. Rulings are public and reviewable under Art. 4.6.

**OL-15, Vulnerability Disclosure** *(implements Art. 7.6.1, D-6)*
1. A finder records a sealed, time-stamped commitment to the flaw within [24 hours] in the public red-team registry.
2. The maintainers of the affected system fix it within [ninety days]; the flaw is then published in full.
3. A flaw under active exploitation is published with mitigations at once.
4. A flaw in an open standard used both by the commons and by hostile systems is reported as a flaw of the commons.
5. No one is penalised for finding or reporting a flaw in good faith; the finder is credited if they wish.

**OL-16, Synthetic Media** *(implements Art. 7.5.4, D-7)*
1. Systems of the commons that generate or alter media mark their output as synthetic in an open, technology-neutral format.
2. A record of origin states the capturing or generating system, time and any later alteration, signed under Art. 7.4.
3. No record of origin requires the identity of a person, and device identifiers are removed on request of the author except for evidence before a jury, where the jury may see them in closed session.

**OL-17, Assisted Dying Safeguards** *(implements Art. 1.2.3, D-9)*
1. A request is followed by a reflection period of [ninety days], shortened for an untreatable condition near death.
2. A panel of three drawn from outside the node checks that every part of the baseline of Art. 1.3, palliative care included, is provided to the person in full, and that no economic or care pressure is present.
3. The person may cancel, pause or extend at any time.
4. *Bound (ratchet rule):* these safeguards secure that the exit is uncoerced; they never refuse a settled choice. A panel finding of pressure suspends the request only until the pressure is removed.

**OL-18, Anti-Ostracism** *(implements Arts. 1.4, 4.3.4; D-10)*
1. A person who is shut out of rosters, project collectives or allocations by an informal group may complain to a panel of three drawn from outside (SIM-QA-19 X-3); no new body is created.
2. The panel inquires into facts under Art. 8.4 and orders only restorative measures.
3. Where an allocation is made on merit, the assessors see proposals without names when the panel so orders.
4. *Bound:* no one is compelled to associate privately with anyone (Art. 1.4.1); the panel reaches allocations, rosters and public bodies only.

**OL-19, Extraction Governance** *(implements Arts. 1.6.2, 3.1.4; D-11)*
1. Recovery from waste, decommissioned infrastructure and landfill comes before virgin extraction.
2. Virgin extraction is approved only if recovery and substitution cannot meet a need of the baseline, as found by a panel with rival expert testimony (Art. 4.4), half drawn from the host bioregion and half from outside.
3. Methods listed by Organic Law as destroying land or water beyond recovery (for example open evaporation ponds and wet tailings dams) are barred.
4. No person works underground in deep extraction or in toxic leaching. Where no machine can do the work, the need is met by recovery or substitution, or waits.
5. The host bioregion is compensated in infrastructure, never in money.

**OL-20, Rulings and Pilots** *(implements Arts. 4.6, 8.4; D-12)*
1. A ruling binds the parties only. Later juries may cite it as reasoning; it never binds them.
2. Rulings are published with their reasons.
3. A pilot runs in one node or sector, with the consent of its assembly, for at most [twelve months], with its success measure fixed in advance; it ends by itself unless adopted at the ordinary threshold.
4. *Bound:* no pilot suspends a right of Chapter I.

**OL-21, Knowledge Never Made Public** *(implements Arts. 1.1.1, 1.5; D-14)*
1. Knowledge that its holders have never made public is not recorded, published or used to train a model without the consent of the persons who hold it.
2. Once made public by its holders, knowledge is a commons under Art. 1.5.
3. *Bound:* consent is given by the persons who hold the knowledge, never by a body speaking for a group; no one may withdraw knowledge another person has made public.

*Red team on the drafts (one line each):* OL-13 point 5 can be read as a bar on related texts (**`NARROWED`**: "same text" only); OL-14 seating in seven days is unshown in sparse regions (same residual as SIM-QA-19 Y-8); OL-15 ninety days is long for systems of the survival baseline (open); OL-17 point 2 is a gate a hostile panel can use (**`NARROWED`** by point 4); OL-18 is the closest of all to policing social life (**`NARROWED`** by point 4, residual: complaints as harassment); OL-19 point 4 can halt supply of a metal before machines exist (**`RED_WINS` on transition timing**: the roadmap must stage it); OL-20 point 3 consent of a node lets a majority impose a pilot on its minority (residual); OL-21 point 3 closes the group-veto route that D-14 dropped (**`BLUE_HOLDS`**).

#### **8.10 Roadmap Draft for D-8, Legacy Arsenals** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(not entered in the roadmap)*
*Destination:* [`roadmap/roadmap.md`](../roadmap/roadmap.md) covers seed nodes (Phases 0 to 3) and has no phase for the accession of states; this entry needs a new section after Phase 3. Placement is for the maintainer.
> **Legacy arsenals at accession.** A state or region that accedes hands its nuclear, chemical and biological arsenals to joint custody teams of its own former technical staff and members drawn by lot from other regions; no single person or authority keeps launch capability. Warheads are separated from delivery systems first, under open monitoring. Fissile material is diluted into civil fuel or immobilised; chemical and biological agents are destroyed under verification. Infrastructure is delivered in step with verified transfers. Arts. 8.1.3 and 8.1.4 bound every step.

*Not carried:* the warhead count, the reactor-fuel figures and the orbital jamming of holdouts (device and transition detail; the last also collides with the non-somatic limits of Art. 8.1.4).

#### **8.11 D-15 Open Question: Who Stops a Dangerous Practitioner** *(options only; nothing decided)*
D-15 dropped peer certification and revocation. Without them, harm by an unsafe surgeon, engineer or pilot has no stated stop. Options, each consistent with the no-gatekeeping rule of SIM-QA-20:
* **O-1, interim stop on facts.** On a complaint backed by evidence of harm, a panel of three drawn from outside may suspend the person's use of commons facilities for that practice pending inquiry under Art. 8.4, for a time set by Organic Law. The stop rests on facts of harm, never on missing credentials.
* **O-2, public record in the Register.** A lay challenge jury of SIM-QA-20 may attach a finding of harm to the person's Register entry; patients and users decide with it.
* **O-3, facility safety rules.** The usufruct of a hospital, fab or vehicle pool carries safety rules for its dangerous equipment, applied to everyone, set by the facility's council and reviewable under Art. 4.6.
*Red team (short):* O-1 is revocation by another name if prolonged (needs a ceiling); O-2 protects informed users only, not third parties (bridges, patients in emergencies); O-3 lets a facility council exclude rivals (review is the only guard). A combination of O-1 with a short ceiling and O-3 is the narrowest stop. For the maintainer.

**Status after section 8:** D-1 to D-7 drafted and red-teamed (all `BLUE_HOLDS` or `NARROWED` except H-3, `RED_WINS` on one vector, residual recorded); OL-13 to OL-21 drafted; roadmap entry drafted; D-15 options listed. Two ceiling conflicts and five weakened or uncarried v1.0 points await the maintainer (list on the board).

## **SIM-QA-23: Lost Provision — Equal Protection**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-13  
* **Stress-Tested:** 2026-11-13 (qualitative O-ASIS red-team pass on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — opened at Stage 1 from the SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7) by maintainer decision 2026-11-13; full pass run the same day by maintainer instruction. Current text (silence) **`RED_WINS`**. H1 (restore the v1.0 invariant word for word) **`RED_WINS`** on three vectors. H3 (open list of grounds) **`NARROWED`**, rejected on review load. H2 (closed grounds, a justification test, indirect effect, no sensitive registers) **`BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals**. Purple patch drafted below as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged. Compression disclosure in section 6: two v1.0 points weakened and two not carried, listed for a point-by-point decision. Chapter I word ceiling conflict reported, not resolved. **Maintainer decisions 2026-11-13 (section 8):** Art. 1.8 accepted in principle; every line of the compression disclosure decided (two points kept as drafted, two moved to Organic Law, five additions accepted, none dropped); ceiling waived for Chapter I for Art. 1.8 only; restorative measures allowed only under a new Art. 1.8.6 at 75% with a sunset, **`NARROWED`** (two open questions: which body adopts, and a constitutional ceiling on duration); order of application with SIM-QA-22 stated (Y-14).  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit: lost in compression at the 2026-09-11 ratification of the ten-chapter architecture; no recorded decision.  
* **v1.0 location:** Section VI.1, Class 0 Invariant 1, *The Rawlsian Veil of Ignorance Symmetry Invariant* (line 312: "No rule may introduce demographic, ethnic, or regional legal asymmetries"); Section II.2.C (line 93: rules must be ones a rational actor would endorse without knowing their personal identity, biological health status, cultural heritage or geographic location; lines 91–92: blind symmetry testing of arbiters by permuting demographic labels).  
* **Closest current canon:** none. Partial: the Preamble ("a perpetual covenant among equals"); Art. 1.3.1 (survival never conditioned on ideological compliance); Art. 1.4 (liberties); Art. 5.2.3 (eligibility never conditioned). Art. 4.3.1 itself draws on age, gender and geography for stratification.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I (new Art. 1.8), Arts. 1.7, 4.3.1, 4.6, 7.4.4, 10.1, 10.4; cross-references SIM-QA-19 (RED-4), SIM-QA-21 (node assemblies), SIM-QA-22 (language), SIM-QA-24 (religion and exit).  
* **Evidence:** reasoning on the text only. No quantitative claim is made.

### **1. The Friction**
* The canon has no equality or non-discrimination clause. Chapter I protects listed liberties and the survival baseline, and Art. 1.7 protects those. A rule that treats people differently by sex, descent, religion or origin, without touching a listed liberty or the baseline, has no constitutional bar.
* The occasions are ordinary: allocation of free dwellings, rosters, admission to registers, settling of newcomers (SIM-QA-21), Tier 3 surplus, access to tools and labs, triage in a crisis (SIM-QA-16).
* Small bodies decide many of these: an assembly of all adults in a node below 150 (SIM-QA-21 H2) is the setting where a majority can single out a minority by name.
* Automated allocation can discriminate through a proxy (place of birth, language of filing, household type) without naming any group.
* Art. 4.6 review resolves doubts "under Chapter I in favor of the person", but without an equality clause there is nothing for the jury to apply.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors**
* **RED-1 (direct targeting):** a node assembly or council adopts a rule that names or plainly describes a group.
* **RED-2 (proxy):** a neutral criterion chosen because it falls on one group (years of residence for dwellings, fluency for registers).
* **RED-3 (literal reading against the architecture):** "no regional asymmetry" read against bioregional envelopes, subsidiarity and carrying-capacity admission.
* **RED-4 (literal reading against protections):** "no demographic asymmetry" read against care, education, ergonomic multipliers, stratification and age thresholds.
* **RED-5 (review flooding):** every rule draws on some attribute; an open clause lets every rule be challenged (SIM-QA-11).
* **RED-6 (proof by surveillance):** proving unequal effect needs data on ethnicity or religion, against the non-identifying registry of Art. 7.4.4.
* **RED-7 (associations):** does the clause bind voluntary fellowships in their membership (SIM-QA-24)?

### **3. Hypotheses to Test**
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, restore v1.0 word for word** as a Chapter I invariant: "No rule may introduce demographic, ethnic, or regional legal asymmetries."
* **H2, equal protection with closed grounds:** equality before the law; a closed list of grounds; a difference of treatment lawful only where required by a protection, service or condition this Constitution itself sets, or by the physical condition of a place, and no wider; neutral rules with markedly unequal effect reviewed on the same test; no register of sensitive attributes for the purpose.
* **H3, open list:** as H2, but "on any ground" in place of the list.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team**
* **H0: `RED_WINS`** on RED-1 and RED-2. Nothing in canon bars either. Art. 4.1.3 bars instruction of delegates, not the content of their decisions.
* **H1: `RED_WINS`** on RED-3, RED-4 and RED-5.
  * *RED-3:* bioregions hold different envelopes, statutes and admission status by design (Arts. 3.4, 10.1, 10.4; OL-10 point 3). Read literally, "no regional legal asymmetry" voids every rule that applies in one watershed and not another, which is the whole of subsidiarity. v1.0 meant persons, not places, but the text does not say so.
  * *RED-4:* care leave for new parents (Art. 5.4.3), the education triad by age (Art. 5.3.2), ergonomic multipliers (Art. 5.1), adulthood for civic service and stratification by age and gender (Art. 4.3.1) are all demographic distinctions. Read literally, H1 voids them. A clause that voids the protections it exists to serve will be read down by review juries case by case, which leaves its meaning to them.
  * *RED-5:* "asymmetry" has no test, so every rule touching an attribute is open to review.
  * H1 holds on RED-1 and RED-6 (it creates no register).
* **H3: `NARROWED`, rejected.** "Any ground" catches what a list misses (a group not foreseen). Red: residence, usufruct status, adulthood and skill are grounds too, and the architecture rests on them; every allocation becomes reviewable and Art. 4.6 juries of 101 are drawn for each (RED-5, SIM-QA-11). The justification test of H2 would then carry the whole load for every rule in the commons.
* **H2: `BLUE_HOLDS` with residuals.** Vector by vector:
  * *RED-1: `BLUE_HOLDS`.* Direct targeting on a listed ground is void unless the test is met, and the test cannot be met by preference.
  * *RED-2: `NARROWED`.* "Through a criterion chosen to have that effect" reaches intended proxies; the indirect-effect paragraph reaches unintended ones with markedly unequal weight. Red: "markedly" is a judgement; unequal effects below it pass. Residual accepted, since a lower bar re-opens RED-5.
  * *RED-3: `BLUE_HOLDS`.* The draft says that differences between places that follow their physical condition and carrying capacity are not differences between persons, and protects origin and prior residence as personal grounds. A node may not favour people born there; a basin may close admission under Art. 10.4 for everyone alike.
  * *RED-4: `BLUE_HOLDS` on text, `NARROWED` in practice.* Differences required by a protection, service or condition this Constitution itself sets are lawful (care, education by age, health, ergonomic adjustment, adulthood, stratification of the lot to mirror the population). Red: only the Constitution's own protections qualify, so a measure enacted in Organic Law to restore participation of an excluded group (the small-peoples channel recommended by SIM-QA-19 RED-4; language-community stratification considered in SIM-QA-22) qualifies only if it is stratification or is placed in canon. *Recorded, not resolved:* whether Organic Law may enact time-limited restorative measures is a maintainer decision; allowing it opens a lobbying route for group privileges, forbidding it closes one tool for small peoples.
  * *Conflicts between grounds (sex and gender in single-sex shelters; religion against sexual orientation in a fellowship's use of a common hall): `NARROWED`.* The test decides each case: a shelter for survivors of violence is a protection the Constitution sets (domestic safety, Art. 4.2.1), and its rules must be no wider than that requires. Residual: review juries will decide contested cases, and their decisions will not satisfy every side.
  * *RED-5: `NARROWED`.* A closed list, the justification test and the existing endorsement allowance for petitions (OL-9) limit volume. Red: a disciplined group can still bring one review per rule. Residual: review load is not modelled.
  * *RED-6: `BLUE_HOLDS`.* No register may record ethnicity, descent, religion or belief, sexual orientation or opinion for this Article. Direct discrimination is proven on the act and the complaint of the person concerned; indirect effect on aggregate evidence that identifies no one. Red: without such data, indirect discrimination against a group no one counts is hard to prove. Accepted: the alternative is a register the canon forbids.
  * *RED-7: `BLUE_HOLDS`.* The clause binds rules, decisions and allocations of the commons and of public bodies, by any method. A voluntary fellowship remains free in its membership (Art. 1.4.1); it never controls access to dwellings, food, tools or common halls on a listed ground. The detailed line between fellowship and commons belongs to SIM-QA-24.
  * *Automated allocation (cyberneticist): `BLUE_HOLDS`.* "By any method, human or automated" with the proxy and effect paragraphs covers allocation models; Art. 7.5 already requires them to be comprehensible, so a proxy can be found by reading the method.
  * *Thermodynamic architect: no effect on flows.* Equal treatment allocates the same flows differently; it creates no matter or energy. The 2,200 kcal floor and Tier 1 already apply to all.
  * *Ratchet (jurist): `NARROWED`.* Once merged in Chapter I, the list can be extended by 75% (Art. 4.5.1) and never shortened (Art. 1.7). A ground added in error cannot be removed. *Guard:* the list is short and limited to grounds the v1.0 text and the canon already name or imply.
  * *Foundation law (jurist): `BLUE_HOLDS`.* Swiss foundation law (ZGB Art. 80 ff.) and the federal constitution's own equality article sit easily with an equality clause in the statutes' purpose; no conflict found on the text. *Not verified by counsel.*

### **5. Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` — *not merged; canon unchanged*
* **Art. 1.8 (new), Equal Protection** *(appended after Art. 1.7, so nothing is renumbered; it falls under the primacy of Art. 1.7 as part of this Chapter)*:
  1. "Every person is equal before this Constitution and before every law, decision and allocation made under it.
  2. No rule, decision or allocation of the commons, made by an assembly, a panel, an office or any automated method, may treat persons differently, directly or through a criterion chosen to have that effect, on grounds of sex, gender, age, ethnicity or descent, language, religion or belief, origin or prior residence, disability or state of health, sexual orientation, or opinion.
  3. A difference of treatment is lawful only where a protection, service or condition set by this Constitution requires it, such as care, education by age, health, ergonomic adjustment, adulthood for civic service, or stratification of the lot to mirror the population, and only as far as it requires. Differences between places that follow their physical condition and carrying capacity are not differences between persons.
  4. A rule neutral on its face that falls with markedly unequal weight on persons who share one of these grounds is reviewed under Article 4.6 by the same test.
  5. No register may record a person's ethnicity, descent, religion or belief, sexual orientation or opinion for the purpose of this Article. Proof rests on the act, on the complaint of the person concerned, and on aggregate evidence that identifies no one."
* **Art. 4.6.2 (consequential, none):** review already resolves doubts under Chapter I in favour of the person; Art. 1.8 is applied through it.
* **Organic Law (proposed, 60%):** standing and form of an equal-protection complaint (any person concerned, or any citizen for a rule that names a group); method for aggregate evidence of unequal effect without identifying persons; publication of review rulings under Art. 1.8 by ground and tier. All `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`.
* **Editor:** about 210 words. Chapter I is 840 words now (headings included), so it would rise to about 1,050, above the 600–900 ceiling. *Update 2026-11-13: the ceiling is now 1,000 words (maintainer decision); Chapter I would still exceed it by about 50 words.* **Conflict reported, not resolved:** no point is cut to meet the ceiling (compression disclosure rule). The maintainer decides whether the ceiling is waived for Chapter I, as it was for Chapter V (SIM-QA-22), or which text moves.

### **6. Compression Disclosure** *(binding rule, `bible/methodology.md` III.2b; maintainer decision needed on each line)*
| v1.0 point (location) | In the patch | Change | Proposed destination |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| No demographic legal asymmetry (VI.1, line 312) | Art. 1.8.2 and 1.8.3 | **Weakened:** a difference required by a protection, service or condition the Constitution sets is lawful; v1.0 had no exception. Reason: RED-4. | Keep as drafted, or restore without exception |
| No ethnic legal asymmetry (VI.1, line 312) | Art. 1.8.2 ("ethnicity or descent") | Kept | — |
| No regional legal asymmetry (VI.1, line 312) | Art. 1.8.2 ("origin or prior residence") and 1.8.3 last sentence | **Weakened:** regional differences between places are allowed when they follow physical condition and carrying capacity; only persons are protected. Reason: RED-3. | Keep as drafted, or restore as written |
| Non-amendable Class 0 status (VI.1, line 311) | Chapter I under Art. 1.7 | Kept in the canon's form: amendable only to expand (Art. 4.5.1), never diminished (Art. 1.7) | — |
| Veil-of-ignorance test for every rule: endorsable without knowing one's identity, health, heritage or location (II.2.C, line 93) | Not carried as a binding test | **Not carried:** a general test without content gives review juries no criterion (RED-5). Its four attributes are carried as grounds (identity → sex, gender, age; health → disability or state of health; heritage → ethnicity, descent, language, religion; location → origin or prior residence). | Interpretive rule for Art. 4.6 juries in Organic Law, or drop; maintainer decides |
| Blind symmetry testing of arbiters by permuted demographic labels (II.2.C, lines 91–92) | Not carried | **Not carried:** it qualified arbiters of a Guardian Assembly that the canon does not have; a device, not a right. A functional equivalent exists: review rulings could be audited by swapping the ground in otherwise identical cases. | Organic Law audit of Art. 1.8 rulings, or drop; maintainer decides |
| *New, not in v1.0:* sexual orientation, opinion, language, indirect effect, the register ban | Art. 1.8.2, 1.8.4, 1.8.5 | **Added.** Opinion and language follow Arts. 1.4.1 and SIM-QA-22; the register ban follows Art. 7.4.4. | Maintainer decides each addition |

### **7. Residual Weaknesses (Honest Limits)**
* "Required by" and "markedly unequal" are judgements; review juries will set their meaning case by case.
* Indirect discrimination against a group no one counts is hard to prove without the data the patch forbids.
* ~~Restorative measures in Organic Law for excluded groups are barred unless they are stratification or are placed in canon; the maintainer has not decided this.~~ Decided 2026-11-13: allowed only under Art. 1.8.6 (section 8).
* Conflicts between grounds are left to review juries.
* Review load under Art. 4.6 is not modelled.
* A ground added in error cannot be removed later (Art. 1.7).
* ~~Chapter I ceiling conflict open.~~ Waived for Art. 1.8 only (2026-11-13).

**Next action:** ~~maintainer decisions on the six lines of section 6, on restorative measures in Organic Law, and on the Chapter I word ceiling~~ (2026-11-13, section 8); a synthetic review-load estimate using the SIM-QA-11 petition model; one external reader with equality-law experience reviews section 5; alignment with SIM-QA-24 on fellowships and commons halls.

### **8. Revision (2026-11-13): Maintainer Decisions, Restorative Measures and the Order of Application (Y-14)** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **8.1 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **Art. 1.8 accepted in principle as drafted** (section 5), with paragraph 6 added below.
* **Compression disclosure, line by line (section 6):**

| v1.0 point | Decision |
| :--- | :--- |
| No demographic legal asymmetry | **Kept as drafted:** the exception for differences the Constitution itself requires stays (Art. 1.8.3). |
| No ethnic legal asymmetry | Kept (no decision needed). |
| No regional legal asymmetry | **Kept as drafted:** the place-versus-person distinction stays (Art. 1.8.3, last sentence). |
| Non-amendable Class 0 status | Kept in the canon's form (no decision needed). |
| Veil-of-ignorance test | **Moved to Organic Law** as an interpretive rule for Art. 4.6 juries (8.3). |
| Blind symmetry testing of arbiters | **Moved to Organic Law** as a swapped-ground audit of Art. 1.8 rulings (8.3). |
| New: sexual orientation, opinion, language, indirect effect, the register ban | **All five accepted.** |

  No point is dropped; nothing is recorded under the drop rule of board instruction 8.
* **Chapter I word ceiling:** *(waiver retired 2026-11-13 by maintainer decision, Y-16: Chapter I now has its own ceiling of 1,200 under board instruction 10a; with Art. 1.8 it counts 1,124, with the Art. 1.4.1 phrase 1,134)* waived for Chapter I for Art. 1.8 only. The waiver gives no general allowance; any other excess in Chapter I needs its own decision. Count in 8.5.
* **Restorative measures:** allowed only as a time-limited exception written into Art. 1.8 itself, never created by Organic Law alone. Conditions in the Constitution: adopted at 75%, with a sunset, founded on published aggregate evidence that identifies no one, never reducing anyone's Tier 1 provision or political rights, renewable only by a new 75% vote, open to review under Art. 4.6. Organic Law sets only the maximum length of the sunset and the form of the evidence. Drafted as Art. 1.8.6.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon is unchanged.

#### **8.2 Art. 1.8.6 (new), Restorative Measures** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`
"6. A measure that restores the participation of persons excluded on a ground of this Article is lawful only if it is adopted by seventy-five percent (75%), ends on a date it states, and rests on published aggregate evidence of that exclusion that identifies no one. It never reduces any person's Tier 1 Vital Biological Baseline or political rights. It is renewed only by a new vote at seventy-five percent (75%) on renewed evidence, and is open to review under Article 4.6. Organic Law fixes only the longest permitted duration and the form of the evidence."

*Drafting notes, for maintainer confirmation.* (a) "Tier 1 provision" is written as "Tier 1 Vital Biological Baseline", the canon's name for it (Art. 3.3); the canon also uses "Tier 1" for personal craft gear (Art. 2.2), so the short form is ambiguous. (b) "on renewed evidence" is added in drafting: without it a renewal could rest on the evidence of the first vote. (c) The decision does not name the body that adopts a measure; see R-3.

*Consequential (Art. 4.5.1):* the 75% list of Art. 4.5.1 is closed ("required exclusively for…"); it gains "restorative measures under Article 1.8.6". Counted in the Chapter IV total of the cross-docket section (Y-13).

**Organic Law (proposed, 60%), OL-12, Equal Protection** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*:
1. *Standing and form of a complaint* (section 5, unchanged).
2. *Longest duration of a restorative measure:* [five (5) years]. *Form of the evidence:* counts or shares by ground, by tier and by place, from sources that record no person's ground (Art. 1.8.5), with the method published and open to challenge.
3. *Interpretive rule for Art. 4.6 juries (veil of ignorance):* in applying Art. 1.8.3 and 1.8.4, the jury asks whether the rule could be endorsed by someone who did not know their own sex, gender, age, descent, language, religion or belief, origin, state of health, sexual orientation or opinion. The question guides the test of Art. 1.8.3; it is not a separate ground of nullity.
4. *Swapped-ground audit:* each year a drawn panel of [fifteen (15)] takes a sample of [twenty (20)] Art. 1.8 rulings, rewrites each case with the ground swapped (a woman for a man, one religion for another) and all else equal, and publishes whether the reasoning of the ruling would still hold. The audit annuls nothing; it is published with the rulings register.

#### **8.3 Red Team Against the Decisions** *(one paragraph on restorative measures, as instructed; then the two Organic Law transfers)*
* **Restorative measures: `NARROWED`.** The lobbying route the 2026-11-13 decision was warned about (R-1, a group privilege dressed as restoration) is closed for a simple or qualified majority: 75%, a stated end date, aggregate evidence of exclusion and Art. 4.6 review are all in the Constitution, so a 60% Organic Law can neither create a measure nor relax its conditions. Four residuals remain. *R-2, renewal as permanence:* each renewal needs a fresh 75% vote on renewed evidence, so a measure can last as long as the exclusion lasts, which is the intent, but a measure whose beneficiaries hold 25% of a body can block its own expiry only by blocking a vote, never by renewing it; a measure lapses by default. *R-3, which body:* "adopted by seventy-five percent" without a body means a council of 15 (12 votes) could adopt one for its node, where SIM-QA-19 shows small bodies misread their constituency; *open, maintainer decision:* name a minimum tier (for example the Bioregional Assembly) or leave it to the body competent for the matter. *R-4, the duration bound:* the Organic Law sets the longest duration, and a 60% majority could set it at fifty years; under the placement test (board instruction 10) the Constitution should keep a ceiling the Organic Law cannot cross; *open, maintainer decision:* a constitutional ceiling (for example ten years) or none. *R-5, evidence without registers:* aggregate evidence must come from sources that record no one's ground, which makes exclusion of an uncounted group hard to show (the residual of RED-6 in section 4); a measure for such a group cannot be adopted. *Coherence:* a restorative measure is a difference of treatment, so it also meets Art. 1.8.3 only through paragraph 6; no measure can rest on Organic Law and Art. 1.8.3 alone.
* **Veil-of-ignorance rule in Organic Law: `BLUE_HOLDS`.** It guides the existing test and voids nothing by itself, so it adds no review load (RED-5). Red: a 60% majority can repeal it; the test of Art. 1.8.3 still applies without it.
* **Swapped-ground audit: `NARROWED`.** A rewritten case is a judgement of the audit panel, not a fact, and a sample of twenty is small. It costs fifteen citizens a few days a year and annuls nothing. Residual: an audit that keeps finding asymmetry has no consequence except publication; the remedy is a new review petition.

#### **8.4 Y-14: Order of Application with SIM-QA-22 (Languages)** *(cross-docket check 2026-11-13)*
A node that schools only in its majority language could be challenged under Art. 5.3.3 (SIM-QA-22: every child's right to schooling in the language of the family or community) and under Art. 1.8.4 (a neutral rule falling with markedly unequal weight on a language group). The order of application, stated for the merge:
1. **The specific right first.** The child's schooling right in Art. 5.3.3 is applied first; if it is breached, the rule is void on that ground and Art. 1.8 need not be reached.
2. **Then the exception of Art. 1.8.3.** The community's choice of school languages in Art. 5.3.3 is a condition this Constitution sets; a difference that choice requires (one school teaches in language A, not B) is lawful under Art. 1.8.3, as far as it requires and no further.
3. **Then Art. 1.8.4** for effects that go beyond what the choice requires (for example, admission to a register, a roster or a dwelling made to depend on the school language).
4. **Chapter I prevails in conflict** (Art. 1.7): if the reading of Art. 5.3.3 and Art. 1.8 cannot be reconciled, Art. 1.8 decides.

*Red team:* `BLUE_HOLDS`. The two texts point the same way; the order only prevents a double review of one rule. Residual: a review jury may still find that a community's choice of languages is itself a criterion chosen to exclude (Art. 1.8.2); step 2 then fails, which is intended.

#### **8.5 Word Count** *(tokens containing a letter or digit, headings included; same method as the Chapter IV count of the 2026-11-13 cross-docket section)*
Chapter I today: 808. Art. 1.8 as drafted in section 5 with its heading: 220. Paragraph 6: 96. Chapter I with Art. 1.8: **1,124**, 124 above the 1,000-word ceiling, within the waiver for Art. 1.8. The Art. 1.4.1 phrase of SIM-QA-22 ("and cultural expression, including the use of one's own language") adds up to 10 more (about 1,134); that phrase is not covered by this waiver and is reported for decision. *(Corrected 2026-11-13: the first figure, 830, counted every whitespace token, not the stated method; 808 is the stated method, the same as the Chapter IV counts.)*

#### **8.6 Residual Weaknesses (Revision)**
* R-3 (which body adopts a restorative measure) and R-4 (a constitutional ceiling on duration) are open.
* Exclusion of uncounted groups is hard to prove, so restorative measures may not reach them.
* The swapped-ground audit has no consequence except publication.
* The residuals of section 7 stand; the ceiling conflict is closed for Art. 1.8 by waiver.

**Next action (revision):** maintainer decisions on R-3, R-4 and the Art. 1.4.1 phrase under the Chapter I ceiling; review-load estimate with the SIM-QA-11 model; external equality-law reader; alignment with SIM-QA-24.

## **SIM-QA-24: Lost Provision — Religion and Exit from Collectives**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-13  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` (Stage 1: HYPOTHESIS) — opened from the SIM-QA-22 side audit by maintainer decision 2026-11-13; held at Stage 1 until the maintainer says otherwise. Nothing below is red-teamed; vectors and hypotheses are preliminary.  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7): lost at the 2026-09-11 ratification of the ten-chapter architecture; v1.0 Section VII is mapped by no redline consolidation line ("unmapped"); no recorded decision.  
* **v1.0 location:** Section VII.1 (lines 397–400): *Rule of Universal Supersedence* (no text, tradition, priesthood or revelation holds judicial standing); *No Territorial or Financial Enclaves* (no religious ownership of territory, capital, civil-law tithes or autonomous civic jurisdictions; worship in common halls under ordinary usufruct reservations); *Inviolable Right of Exit* (no penalty, harassment or blacklisting for leaving a fellowship); *Protection of Youth* (no non-therapeutic physical alteration of children, genital cutting included). Also the overview (line 36) and the comparison table (line 1102).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.4.1 (freedom of thought and conscience), 1.4.2 (no mandated orthodox belief), 1.2.2 (no physical subjugation), 1.7.2, 2.1 (no absentee title for anyone), 8.6.3 (exit from a bioregion only). None names religious standing, enclaves, exit from a fellowship or alteration of children.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I, Arts. 1.2, 1.4, 2.1, 2.2, 8.4, 8.6.3; cross-references SIM-QA-23 (religion as a protected ground; fellowships and commons halls), SIM-QA-25 (children).
* **Not opened with this docket (same v1.0 section):** *Autonomous Voluntary Cultural Covenants* and their Golden Triad (subsidised exit from 14, no bodily battery, ecological limits) and the protection against forced technologisation. Listed here so that no point is dropped silently; they stay in the SIM-QA-22 side audit until the maintainer opens them.

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 2.1 bars absentee title for everyone, which covers religious land ownership indirectly; nothing covers a fellowship that pools many usufructs, levies labour hours on members, or runs its own dispute forum for family matters.
* Art. 1.4.1 protects belief and association but not the person who leaves: shunning, loss of housing in a fellowship's block, loss of access to shared tools or rosters.
* Nothing bars non-therapeutic alteration of a child that a family's tradition requires; Art. 1.2.2 bars "coercive genetic alteration" and "physical subjugation", which a review jury may or may not read to cover it.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, parallel jurisdiction:** a fellowship's own forum rules on marriage, custody or inheritance of usufruct and members comply under social pressure → canon silent; Art. 8.4 Fact-Centric Inquiry is available but not exclusive.
* **RED-2, enclave by pooling:** members allocate their dwellings, plots and roster hours to a fellowship's plan; a node becomes a religious enclave without any transfer of title → Art. 2.1 is not triggered.
* **RED-3, retaliation for exit:** a leaver loses housing, work slots and family contact inside the node → no clause names it; Art. 1.3 protects survival, not standing in the community.
* **RED-4, alteration of children:** a tradition requires a non-therapeutic procedure on infants → unclear under Art. 1.2.2.
* **RED-5, over-reach:** a rule written against religion only breaches Art. 1.4.1 and the equal-protection ground of SIM-QA-23; a rule that polices shunning in private life reaches into association and intimacy (Art. 1.2.3).
* **RED-6, mass practice:** a ban on non-therapeutic alteration of children reaches practices held by very large communities (male circumcision); enforcement without surveillance of homes is unclear.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, restore v1.0 for religion:** the four points as religious rules.
* **H2, general rule for every collective, religious or not (technology-neutral):** (a) no text, tradition or association holds standing in public adjudication; (b) no association holds territory, levies on members under public rule, or runs a public jurisdiction; worship and meeting use common halls under ordinary usufruct; (c) leaving any association is free, and no one may lose dwelling, food, tools, rosters or civic standing for leaving; (d) the child's bodily integrity as in SIM-QA-25.
* **H3, exit guarantee only:** only (c), leaving (a), (b), (d) to existing articles and review.

**Note 2026-11-20:** point (d) of this docket (non-therapeutic alteration of children) was decided in SIM-QA-25 (D-25.4, option a) and is no longer open here. This docket stays held at Stage 1 and has not been red-teamed; when it is, RED-6 (mass practice; enforcement without surveillance of homes) is to be tested against that decision.

**Next action:** held at Stage 1 by maintainer decision. When opened: red team H1–H3 against RED-1 to RED-6; align (d) with SIM-QA-25 so that one article carries children's bodily integrity; compression disclosure against the four v1.0 points.

## **SIM-QA-25: Lost Provision — Children**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-13  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST) — opened from the SIM-QA-22 side audit by maintainer decision 2026-11-13; held at Stage 1 until 2026-11-20, when the maintainer opened it (Chapter I batch, item 13). Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`** with eight points; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H2, and under current text on the age of consent, the age of responsibility and a parent's sanction falling on the child; bodily integrity left open. Reasoning on text; first pass; no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7): v1.0 Section XI is unmapped in the redline; lost at the 2026-09-11 ratification; no recorded decision.  
* **v1.0 location:** Section XI.3 (line 643): children and adolescents "are not the property of their parents" and hold progressive autonomy ("Class 2 Personhood with progressive somatic autonomy"), with self-governed youth spaces. **Corporal punishment:** v1.0 bars it in two narrower places only: the founding-node *Compact 5, Sanctuary of the Children* (Section XVI, line 1051: physical punishment, public shaming, ridicule and emotional withdrawal barred for pioneers) and the Golden Triad of cultural covenants (Section VII.1, line 403: no covenant may practise corporal violence). *A general ban in every home would be wider than v1.0; recorded so the scope is decided, not assumed.*  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.2 (bodily sovereignty, general; no child-specific text), Art. 5.3 (education by age), Art. 5.4 (parenting education, alloparenting), Art. 1.3 (baseline for every inhabitant).  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I, Arts. 1.2, 1.3, 1.4, 5.3, 5.4, 4.2.1 (domestic safety), 8.1.2 (Civic Peace Guilds); cross-references SIM-QA-22 (schooling language), SIM-QA-23 (age as a ground), SIM-QA-24 (alteration of children; exit from covenants at the age of discernment).
* **Added 2026-11-16 (maintainer decision, SIM-QA-22 section 7.1, D-13):** v1.0 III.2 (lines 126–127) *free choice of teacher* moves into this docket; to be red-teamed with H1–H3 when the docket opens. The peer-certification half of III.2 is dropped (D-15).
* **Not carried by this docket (noted 2026-11-16):** v1.0 XI.3 and XVI also hold *access to contraception* and *no coercive population control*; the board line of this docket did not carry them. They are opened separately as SIM-QA-28.

### **1. The Friction**
* Chapter I rights are written for "every human being" and "every person", so children hold them in principle, but nothing says how they are exercised while a child cannot exercise them alone, or by whom.
* No text says a child's own view counts in decisions about them, or that its weight grows with age.
* Nothing bars corporal punishment; Art. 1.2.2 bars "physical subjugation", which a review jury may or may not read to include it.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, parental dominion:** a parent decides schooling, medical care and residence against an adolescent's clear will → no clause gives the adolescent a voice.
* **RED-2, collective dominion:** a node, covenant or alloparenting hub claims authority over children in place of parents → Art. 5.4 creates shared care but no limit on it.
* **RED-3, violence at home:** corporal punishment is ordinary practice in a family → no clause names it; intervention depends on the domestic-safety jurisdiction of Art. 4.2.1.
* **RED-4, intervention as surveillance:** a ban enforced by inspection of homes breaches Art. 1.2.3 (private intimacy) and the non-surveillance invariant.
* **RED-5, the "not property" phrase:** declaratory; with no inheritance and no title in the commons, it changes nothing unless it carries consequences (custody, voice, exit).
* **RED-6, age thresholds:** any fixed age (14 for covenant exit in v1.0) is arbitrary; no threshold leaves the question to case-by-case judgement.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, Chapter I paragraph:** no person owns a child; a child holds the rights of this Chapter, exercised with guidance whose weight decreases as capacity grows; the child is heard in every decision about them, with weight growing with age; no corporal punishment or degrading treatment of a child by anyone; protective measures are non-punitive, least intrusive, and decided on complaint, never by inspection.
* **H2, as H1 without the corporal-punishment clause** (left to Art. 1.2 and review).
* **H3, Chapter V placement** (with Arts. 5.3–5.4) instead of Chapter I.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, item 13):** this docket is **opened for the red team now** (13a B), ahead of SIM-QA-24, 27 and 28, because SIM-QA-36, 37 and 39 wait on it (a child in danger tonight; the age of consent; who decides for a child). At most one short sentence of right per docket enters Chapter I; the rest goes to Organic Law (13b B).

**Next action (as recorded at opening; superseded by the line at the end of section 4):** red team (done, section 4). Then: decide the scope of the corporal-punishment ban (v1.0 founding-node and covenant scope, or every home); red team H1–H3; take over children's bodily integrity from SIM-QA-24 (d); compression disclosure against the v1.0 points.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, opened by maintainer decision of the same day, Chapter I batch item 13; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass; **no child-protection practitioner, paediatrician, teacher or family lawyer has read it**; no article text drafted)*

*Scope taken in this pass:* H1 to H3 and RED-1 to RED-6; free choice of teacher (SIM-QA-22 D-13); the three matters passed from SIM-QA-36 (a child in danger tonight; the age of consent; who decides for a child); children's bodily integrity, taken over from SIM-QA-24 (d). *Constraint already decided:* at most one short sentence of right enters Chapter I (item 13b); Chapter I is closed after the lock (D-35.2).

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 1.2.1 gives "Every human being" "absolute, unconditional sovereignty over their biological, physical, and cognitive substrate".** A child of four holds it on the words. Nothing says who exercises it for the child. A review jury could void a parent's decision, or uphold a small child's refusal of care, with equal support in the text.
* **Striking a child is already inside the canon.** It is "physical assault" under Art. 8.4.4 and falls under Art. 1.2.2. What the canon lacks is a fitting answer: Art. 8.3.2 says that in physical violence "the transgressor is relocated outside the victim's transit radius". Applied as written to a parent who slaps a child, it separates the family at the first act.
* **Art. 5.3 prescribes the content of education by age** and bars "ideological indoctrination". It does not say whether education is compulsory, or who chooses for a child. A node could call a family's religious teaching indoctrination (RED-2).
* **Art. 5.2.2 lets a panel reassign "dwelling floor area" of an adult who refuses shifts.** The children of that adult live in the dwelling.
* **Art. 5.3.2 places adolescents from thirteen in work collectives "alongside adult mentors",** with no limit of hours or hazard.
* **The canon has no age for anything:** not for consent to a sexual act, not for answering before a tribunal (Art. 8.4), not for custody (Art. 8.5), not for deciding one's own care.
* **Two decisions taken today need care in drafting because of children.** (a) Batch item 2: Chapter I will name the cases in which "a person may be held" and prohibit holding outside them. A parent who carries a child or holds one back from a road holds a person. (b) Batch item 5: every item of Art. 1.2.2 will depend on "the absence of the person's consent". A small child cannot consent, so on those words a child could be given no medicine. Both sentences must provide for those who decide for a child.
* **SIM-QA-29 already gives persons who cannot be drawn "Advocates drawn by lot"** with voice and standing (draft Art. 7.4.5). Children are among them.

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Recorded Vectors**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1, RED-2 and RED-3 succeed, and section 4.1 adds that the same text supports opposite rulings.
* **H2 (as H1 without the clause on corporal punishment): `RED_WINS`.** Leaving it to Art. 1.2 and to juries gives either nothing or the automatic relocation of Art. 8.3.2.
* **H3 (Chapter V in place of Chapter I): partly forced by decision.** One sentence goes to Chapter I (item 13b); the rest must live elsewhere. Chapter V is within fifty words of its ceiling (section 4.6).
* **RED-1, parental dominion. H1: `NARROWED`.** "Heard, with weight growing with age" changes nothing if the parents do the weighing. *Guards:* for a closed list of major matters (where the child lives, schooling, serious medical decisions, leaving a fellowship), a child may take a disagreement to the outside panel of SIM-QA-36 point 5, which hears the child; a young person able to understand a decision about their own body makes it themselves. *Residual:* panels inside family life. The list must stay short or every quarrel becomes a case.
* **RED-2, collective dominion. `NARROWED`.** The sentence "no person owns a child" must say "no person and no body". The upbringing of a child belongs first to those recorded as carers (SIM-QA-36 point 5); the commons offers education and shared care and takes a child only by the protective measure of section 4.3. A family's beliefs, way of life or choice among lawful forms of education are never a ground.
* **RED-3 and RED-4, violence at home and enforcement. H1: `NARROWED`.** *Scope:* v1.0 barred physical punishment only in covenants and in the founding compact. Since striking a child is already an assault (section 4.1), a rule for every home adds no new prohibition; it adds a fitting answer. *Answer by steps:* support and the parenting formation of Art. 5.4.1 first; Art. 8.3.2 does not apply to a single act without injury; injury or repetition opens the protective measure. *Without inspection:* a complaint by the child or by any person; the Advocate of SIM-QA-29; and the fact that under Art. 5.4.3 several adults share a child's daily life. *Not carried as a prohibition:* v1.0 also barred "public shaming, sarcastic ridicule, and emotional withdrawal". No definition of these by conduct would survive the test applied in SIM-QA-33 (section 4.3 there); they fit the parenting formation of Art. 5.4, as guidance (compression disclosure, line 4).
* **RED-5, "not property" as a phrase. `NARROWED`.** It carries consequences only through the rest: a voice with a forum, a way to complain, access to education that nobody can block, and a child's own Tier 1.
* **RED-6, ages. `NARROWED`.** Chapter I states the principle (rights exercised more and more by the child as capacity grows) and no number. Two kinds of age then differ. *Ages of autonomy* (deciding one's own care, choosing a teacher, leaving a fellowship) can be presumptions in Organic Law. *Ages of protection* (consent to a sexual act; answering before a tribunal) are bounds: a 60% majority that moved them could remove a protection, so the placement test keeps them in the Constitution, outside Chapter I. The ratchet rule does not help with ages, since lowering one strengthens autonomy and weakens protection at once. No number is proposed here; each needs a specialist.

#### **4.3 The Matters Passed From Other Dockets**
* **A child in danger tonight (SIM-QA-36 RED-12). `NARROWED`, with a residual.** *Who acts at once:* anyone, when violence against the child is in progress or about to begin; this is already among the cases accepted for Chapter I today (batch item 2, cases i and ii). *Then:* the child stays that night with another recorded carer, kin, or a household of the shared care of Art. 5.4; a panel drawn from outside the node validates within 72 hours (the model of Art. 8.5.3), hears the child, and hears the carers; an Advocate stands for the child; review at short intervals; return is the aim; the measure is never a punishment. *Grounds, closed:* harm to the child's body, or grave neglect of the child's Tier 1 needs. *Residual:* where carers refuse and there is no violence and no danger to life, no force is available (the Chapter I list of item 2), and the panel's decision rests on persuasion and support. *RED-7 (new), removal as a weapon:* a node against parents who dissent; one parent against the other in a separation (SIM-QA-33 RED-9). The closed grounds and the outside panel are the guards.
* **The age of consent (SIM-QA-36 RED-13). `RED_WINS` under current text.** Nothing bars an adult's sexual act with a child. *To write:* an age of protection in the Constitution, with an exception for young people close in age, and the act added to the closed list of SIM-QA-33. The number is for a specialist and the maintainer.
* **Who decides for a child. `NARROWED`.** The recorded carers together, with the child's voice; a disagreement between them goes to the panel. *Health:* the record is under the carers' key until an age of autonomy and then under the young person's; a young person may seek care in confidence (SIM-QA-28; the "young person's secret" of OL-22). *RED-8 (new), treatment refused for a child:* carers refuse a treatment needed to save the life of a child too young to decide. SIM-QA-39 point 3 says no treatment is ever imposed; that rule protects a person who can decide. *Proposed:* the panel may authorise a treatment needed to save the child's life or prevent grave irreversible harm. Vaccination is not such a case: the carers decide, and later the young person.
* **Children's bodily integrity (from SIM-QA-24 d). Open; the most contested point of this docket.** Art. 1.2.1 read plainly reserves an irreversible, non-therapeutic change to a body to the person whose body it is. That reaches the circumcision of boys, practised by very large communities, the surgery done on intersex infants, and less grave things. Art. 1.8.4 (SIM-QA-23) reviews a neutral rule that falls with unequal weight on a religion. Two provisions of Chapter I would then pull against each other, and juries would decide either way (SIM-QA-35 RED-9). It has to be settled in words before the lock. *Options:* **(a)** state that an irreversible, non-therapeutic change to a child's body waits until the person can decide, applied through the commons' facilities and practitioners, which do not perform it; **(b)** silence. The red team reads Art. 1.2 as leading to (a) and records its cost: communities for whom the practice is a duty will not join, or will have it done outside the commons' facilities.
* **Free choice of teacher (SIM-QA-22 D-13). `NARROWED`.** v1.0 abolished compulsory assignment and expected that "An ineffective, abusive, or disengaged instructor naturally experiences zero enrollment". *Attacks:* (a) choice follows liking, and a demanding teacher can lose to an indulgent one; (b) families choose teachers of their own persuasion and children are sorted by it, which Art. 5.3.1 opposes and Art. 1.4 protects; (c) a small node has one teacher; (d) an abusive teacher must be stopped at once, not by falling enrolment (the interim stop of SIM-QA-32 point 7); (e) with no compulsory enrolment a child can be kept from all education. *Revised:* carers choose among teachers and forms with the child, and the young person chooses from an age of autonomy; nobody is assigned; and a child's own access to the commons' education can be blocked by no one, so a child who wants to go, goes. No enrolment is compelled.

#### **4.4 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-7, removal as a weapon.** Under 4.3.
* **RED-8, life-saving treatment refused for a child.** Under 4.3.
* **RED-9, the parent's sanction falls on the child. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Section 4.1 (Art. 5.2.2). *Guard:* no reciprocity measure lowers the conditions of a child; a child's Tier 1 is the child's own.
* **RED-10, no age of responsibility. `RED_WINS` under current text.** A child of ten who hurts another could stand before a tribunal of fifteen or be placed in custody. An age of protection is needed below which only care and mediation apply.
* **RED-11, work from thirteen. `NARROWED`.** Art. 5.3.2 needs limits of hours and hazard in Organic Law; an apprenticeship is never counted as a shift obligation (Art. 5.1.1 speaks of adults). During the transition state law governs (SIM-QA-40 RED-15).
* **RED-12, the youth spaces of v1.0. Open.** v1.0 gave young people "self-governed maker-spaces", "transit mobility" and "safe autonomous dormitories". None is in the canon. Autonomous dormitories meet the age of consent and RED-10. For the maintainer (D-25.9).
* **RED-13, children who arrive alone. Cross-docket.** An unaccompanied child at a node (SIM-QA-30) has no recorded carer; the node cares, a panel names carers, and the register entry is made by a drawn jury (SIM-QA-29, version B).

#### **4.5 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the eight points below; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H2, and under current text on the age of consent, the age of responsibility and the parent's sanction falling on the child; bodily integrity left open as the contested point.
1. **The sentence for Chapter I.** A child holds every right of the Chapter; those who care for the child exercise them for the child's benefit and with the child's voice, and the child exercises them more and more as capacity grows; no person and no body owns a child.
2. **A voice with a forum.** For a short closed list of major matters, the child may go to an outside panel that hears them. A young person able to understand a decision about their own body makes it.
3. **No violence against a child, in any home.** The answer goes by steps: support first; the protective measure on injury or repetition.
4. **The protective measure.** Anyone acts against violence in progress. The child stays with another carer that night; an outside panel within 72 hours, with an Advocate for the child; closed grounds (harm to the body, grave neglect of Tier 1); never beliefs or way of life; return is the aim.
5. **Deciding for a child.** The recorded carers together. A panel may authorise a treatment needed to save the child's life. Records under the carers' key, then the young person's; care in confidence for a young person.
6. **Ages.** Ages of autonomy as presumptions in Organic Law. Ages of protection (consent, responsibility) as bounds in the Constitution.
7. **Education.** No one is assigned to a teacher and no enrolment is compelled; a child's own access is blocked by no one.
8. **A child's own Tier 1.** No measure against a carer lowers a child's conditions. Work in apprenticeship has limits and is never a shift.

#### **4.6 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
Point 1 is the one sentence for Chapter I (about forty words as sketched; Chapter I stands at 1,181 of 1,200 before the changes decided today). Points 2 to 5, 7 and 8 concern rights and belong in Chapter V with Arts. 5.3 and 5.4 (855 words today, about 956 to 986 with accepted dockets: no room). The ages of protection and the act go to Chapter VIII (98 left, already claimed by SIM-QA-33 and SIM-QA-37). Nothing is drafted. The conflict goes to the maintainer with the others; one decision on ceilings is still owed (consolidation, section 5).

#### **4.7 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8; against v1.0 Sections III.2, VII.1, XI.3 and XVI Compact 5, and against the canon)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | children "are not the property of their parents" | v1.0 XI.3 | carried, widened to "no person and no body" | **carried** |
| 2 | "Class 2 Personhood with progressive somatic autonomy" | v1.0 XI.3 | carried as rights exercised with growing capacity; the term "Class 2 Personhood" is not carried | **carried**, term dropped |
| 3 | self-governed youth spaces, transit mobility, autonomous dormitories | v1.0 XI.3 | not in the surviving points; proposed for Organic Law or to drop (D-25.9) | **uncarried**, for decision |
| 4 | "Physical punishment, public shaming, sarcastic ridicule, and emotional withdrawal are fundamentally barred" (for pioneers) | v1.0 XVI Compact 5 | physical punishment carried and widened to every home; the other three become guidance in parenting formation | **weakened** for three of four |
| 5 | no heated debate in courtyards, dining rooms and workshops | v1.0 XVI Compact 5 | not carried; a house rule | **uncarried**, for decision |
| 6 | no "physical corporal violence, non-therapeutic genital alteration, forced marriage, honor retribution, or somatic confinement" in a covenant | v1.0 VII.1 | corporal violence and confinement carried by points 3 and 4 and by the Chapter I list of cases; genital alteration is D-25.4; forced marriage has no civil effect under SIM-QA-36 point 1 and needs an act in SIM-QA-33 | **carried in part** |
| 7 | exit from a covenant at fourteen, with a transition package | v1.0 VII.1 | stays with SIM-QA-24 (held); an age of autonomy under point 6 | **not decided here** |
| 8 | free choice of teacher | v1.0 III.2 | carried, with the rule that a child's access is blocked by no one | **carried** |
| 9 | Art. 8.3.2, relocation of the transgressor "in physical violence" | canon | not applied to a single act without injury against a child | **qualified** |
| 10 | Art. 5.2.2, reassignment of floor area | canon | never where it lowers a child's conditions | **qualified** |

*Seen and not opened:* v1.0 XI.3 also gave caregivers "premium discretionary allocations" and funded "Vitality Sabbaticals"; neither is in the canon (Art. 5.6.1 keeps the exemption from shifts only).

#### **4.8 Residual Weaknesses**
* Where carers refuse and there is neither violence nor danger to life, the commons has persuasion and support only.
* A complaint needs a child who can make one or an adult who sees; a closed household is not reached.
* No age is proposed. Every number in this docket is missing and needs a specialist.
* Bodily integrity is open, and either answer excludes someone.
* Three points lean on dockets not merged: SIM-QA-29 (Advocates), SIM-QA-33 (the list of acts), SIM-QA-36 (recorded carers and the outside panel).
* During the transition the law of the state governs children in every respect (SIM-QA-40); a node is one voice among those the law recognises.
* No one who works with children has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-25.1 to D-25.11):**
* **D-25.1:** accept the eight points of section 4.5 as the direction, or change them.
* **D-25.2:** the content of the one sentence for Chapter I (point 1); and that the two Chapter I sentences decided today (batch items 2 and 5) provide for those who decide for a child.
* **D-25.3:** no violence against a child in every home (wider than v1.0), answered by steps; shaming, ridicule and emotional withdrawal as guidance, not prohibition.
* **D-25.4:** irreversible, non-therapeutic change to a child's body: (a) it waits until the person can decide, applied through the commons' facilities and practitioners; (b) silence. To be decided with SIM-QA-24.
* **D-25.5:** ages of autonomy in Organic Law; ages of protection (consent; responsibility) in the Constitution; the numbers after a specialist.
* **D-25.6:** the protective measure of point 4, with its closed grounds.
* **D-25.7:** a panel may authorise a treatment needed to save a child's life when carers refuse.
* **D-25.8:** education: no assignment, no compelled enrolment, and a child's own access blocked by no one.
* **D-25.9:** the youth spaces of v1.0 (RED-12) and the house rule of Compact 5: Organic Law, or dropped.
* **D-25.10:** a child's own Tier 1 (RED-9) and limits on apprenticeship (RED-11).
* **D-25.11:** placement and ceilings (Chapters I, V, VIII).

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch 2, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md); answered item by item; directions only):** **item 4a** (D-25.1): the eight points of section 4.5 are the direction. **Item 4b** (D-25.2): the four short sentences of Draft F all go in Chapter I, as an exception to the one-sentence rule of batch 1 item 13b, because Drafts B and C need two of them. **Item 4c** (D-25.4): option (a). The carers' consent never covers an irreversible change to a child's body that no medical need requires; it waits until the person can decide; applied through the commons' facilities and practitioners, which do not perform it. The text is to say that Art. 1.8 does not alter this. *Recorded with the decision:* it was taken before SIM-QA-24 (religion) was red-teamed; communities for whom the circumcision of boys is a duty will not join, or will have it done outside the commons' facilities. **Still open:** D-25.3, D-25.5 to D-25.11.

**Next action (after the red team):** maintainer decisions D-25.1 to D-25.11; a specialist for every age and for the protective measure; then text as hypothesis, shown beside the current text, with a second red-team pass.
