## **SIM-QA-26: Lost Provision — Sentient Animals**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-13  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` (Stage 1: HYPOTHESIS) — opened from the SIM-QA-22 side audit by maintainer decision 2026-11-13; held at Stage 1 until the maintainer says otherwise. Nothing below is red-teamed.  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7): v1.0 Section XII was mapped but the principle did not survive ("compression"); lost at the 2026-09-11 ratification; no recorded decision. The canon never uses the words "animal" or "sentient".  
* **v1.0 location:** Section XII.3, *Non-Human Sentience, Animal Ethics & The Abolition of Exploitation* (lines 715–737: ban on concentrated feeding operations, industrial slaughter and commercial live transport; sentient animals recognised as persons with bodily integrity and freedom from commodification; total ban on invasive experiments on live animals); Section XII.4 (lines 754–757: companion animals "never chattel property"; end of commercial breeding mills and of breeding for painful deformities).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.6.3 (non-human living systems and biomes hold standing, defended by drawn guardians), Art. 1.6.2 (destruction of habitats a crime), Art. 1.1.1 (the biosphere belongs to all living beings). Systems and biomes are protected; individual sentient animals are not.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I (Art. 1.6), Arts. 1.3.2 (nutrition), 1.4.3 (research commons), 3.3 (needs tiers), 10.2.2 (Guardians of the Biosphere); Organic Law OL-1.4 (biosecurity registry).
* **Not carried, device or sci-fi (methodology purge):** the v1.0 food-system devices (gas fermentation, cultivated meat, vertical aeroponics) and the land-dividend figure; listed so the omission is visible. The principle, not the device, is what this docket opens.

### **1. The Friction**
* Nothing in canon bars confinement farming, experiments on live animals, or the sale of animals as goods; Art. 1.6.3 protects ecosystems, not individuals.
* The calorie-sufficient nutrition of Art. 1.3.2 (the 2,200 kcal per day floor of the Tier 1 baseline) depends today on a food system with large animal components; any ban interacts with the survival baseline during transition.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, industrial confinement returns** as an efficient route to the calorie floor → nothing bars it.
* **RED-2, a ban against the floor:** an immediate ban on animal farming breaks nutrition in regions without alternatives (Art. 1.3.2; Leontief balance of Chapter III) → famine risk if the transition is not staged in the roadmap.
* **RED-3, pastoral and subsistence ways of life:** herding, hunting and fishing communities are hit by rules written for industrial systems (interaction with SIM-QA-23 descent and SIM-QA-24 covenants).
* **RED-4, research and biosecurity:** a total ban on experiments with live animals collides with vaccine and pathogen work that OL-1.4 and the biosecurity boundary of Art. 1.4.3 rely on, where non-animal methods are not yet adequate; an exception reopens the ban.
* **RED-5, which animals:** "sentient" needs an evidentiary line (which taxa), which belongs in Organic Law under the replicated-evidence rule; a closed list ages badly, an open test is litigated.
* **RED-6, companion animals "not property":** in a commons without absentee property, the change has content only as duties (care, no sale for profit, guardianship transfer); otherwise declaratory.
* **RED-7, culling and restoration:** rewilding and disease control (Art. 1.6, 10.5) sometimes require killing animals; a personhood framing forbids it.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, restore v1.0 principles in Art. 1.6:** sentient animals hold standing as individuals; no confinement system that prevents their natural behaviour; no experiment on a live sentient animal; companion animals held under guardianship, never as goods. Any exception (research where no alternative exists; staged transition for food; culling for ecosystem health) would weaken a v1.0 point and must go through the compression disclosure.
* **H2, principle in canon, stages in the roadmap:** the prohibitions as H1, with the transition (food, research) fixed in the roadmap and the evidentiary line of sentience in Organic Law.
* **H3, welfare floor only:** a duty to avoid unnecessary suffering, without prohibitions.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 7, items 7a and 7b, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); from SIM-QA-36 D-36.2):** *The boundary:* this docket holds the rights of the child and every protective measure; SIM-QA-36 holds residence, household and the record of care. *A child in danger tonight (SIM-QA-36 RED-12), assigned here:* anyone may take a child to safety for the moment; within 72 hours a panel of three drawn from outside the node hears the child and the carers, decides whether the child returns, and reviews later. The red team's pick, adopted. *Cost accepted:* a child can be taken wrongly, and the carers are without the child for up to three days before anyone checks. *To check at drafting:* whether removal from the carers needs a sentence in Chapter I, since Draft F gives the carers the exercise of the child's rights; who acts is the on-call figure of SIM-QA-37 point 1. No one who works with children has read this. Direction only; no wording.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 7, item 7c; from SIM-QA-36 D-36.2):** *the age of consent (SIM-QA-36 RED-13), assigned here as the rule and to SIM-QA-33 as a listed act:* the canon states that a sexual act of an adult with a child is violence under Art. 8.4.4, whatever the child said; the age is a number in Organic Law; the Constitution states the bound that number must keep (instruction 10). The red team's pick, adopted. *Open for drafting:* the bound itself (not proposed; no figure is written without a source); relations between young persons close in age; in which chapter the sentence sits. The first rule of the canon that turns on a person's age. No one who works with children has read this. Direction only; no wording.

**Next action:** held at Stage 1 by maintainer decision. When opened: thermodynamic check of the calorie floor under H1/H2 (SIMONE, synthetic); red team RED-1 to RED-7; compression disclosure against the v1.0 points of XII.3 and XII.4.

## **SIM-QA-27: Lost Provision — Gene Therapy**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-16  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` (Stage 1: HYPOTHESIS) — opened from the SIM-QA-22 side audit by maintainer decision 2026-11-16 (SIM-QA-22 section 7.1); held at Stage 1 until the maintainer says otherwise. Nothing below is red-teamed; vectors and hypotheses are preliminary.  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7): v1.0 Section XI is unmapped in the redline; lost at the 2026-09-11 ratification; no recorded decision.  
* **v1.0 location:** Section XI.1, *CRISPR, Germline Editing and the Genetic Commons* (lines 585–587): *Universal Therapeutic Gene Correction* (elimination of high-penetrance monogenic and severe polygenic disease guaranteed as a birthright); *Prohibition on Designer Vanity Monocultures* (no edits for aesthetic traits, narrow intelligence or athletic optimisation, or behavioural dispositions); *Preservation of Heterozygosity and Neuro-Diverse Alleles* (genetic and neurological variation protected as an asset, not a defect). The genetic-commons point of the same section (no patents on sequences or editing tools) survives in Art. 1.5.2.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.2.1 (sovereignty over one's own biological substrate), Art. 1.2.2 (coercive genetic alteration prohibited), Art. 1.3.2 (healthcare in the baseline), Art. 1.5.2 (genetics as a public good). None names therapeutic correction, enhancement or protection of variation.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I, Arts. 1.2, 1.3.2, 1.4.3 (research commons, biosecurity), 1.5.2, 5.5 (neuro-medicine); Organic Law OL-1.4 (biosecurity registry); cross-references SIM-QA-23 (disability and descent as grounds), SIM-QA-24 (non-therapeutic alteration of children), SIM-QA-25 (children).  
* **Not carried, device (methodology purge (a)):** v1.0 enforced the enhancement ban through the compiler ("locked out by the compiler") and promised machine-verified germline repair. The compiler was purged by recorded decision; the principle, not the device, is what this docket opens.  
* **Tension recorded at opening (maintainer note 2026-11-16):** Art. 1.2.1 gives every person sovereignty over their own biological substrate. A ban on enhancement edits limits that sovereignty for adults acting on themselves; for embryo and heritable edits, the person affected is not the person deciding. Any hypothesis must say which case it covers.

### **1. The Friction**
* Nothing in canon guarantees access to gene therapy; Art. 1.3.2 lists "comprehensive primary, preventative, and emergency healthcare", which a review jury may or may not read to include it.
* Nothing bars enhancement edits; Art. 1.2.2 bars only *coercive* alteration, so a parent's voluntary choice for an embryo is not covered.
* Nothing protects genetic or neurological variation; a therapy programme could define autism-spectrum traits or other variation as disease to be corrected.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, enhanced class:** parents with access edit embryos for advantage; over generations a biological upper class forms → canon silent.
* **RED-2, therapy against variation:** "correction" is extended to deafness, autism-spectrum traits or other variation that many carriers do not consider disease → no protection; interaction with the SIM-QA-23 disability ground.
* **RED-3, ban against self-sovereignty:** a ban on enhancement for adults acting on their own bodies breaches Art. 1.2.1 → the hypothesis fails review unless scoped.
* **RED-4, the therapy / enhancement line:** the line is contested (resistance to infection, slower ageing); a fixed list ages badly, an open test is litigated → the line belongs in Organic Law under the replicated-evidence rule, with the bound in canon.
* **RED-5, scarce therapy:** a guaranteed right to costly therapy collides with Leontief limits and the needs tiers of Art. 3.3 → priority rules needed.
* **RED-6, germline risk:** heritable edits affect people not yet born and cannot be undone → candidate for the closed list of irreversible decisions (SIM-QA-19 OL-11).

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, restore v1.0 points in Arts. 1.2 and 1.3.2:** right to therapeutic correction of serious disease; no enhancement edits; genetic and neurological variation protected and never treated as disease by itself.
* **H2, scoped by who decides:** adults decide on their own somatic edits under Art. 1.2.1; edits on embryos and heritable edits are limited to therapy of serious disease, with the evidentiary line in Organic Law; variation protected as in H1.
* **H3, variation and non-coercion only:** protection of variation and a ban on coerced or conditioned therapy, without a right to therapy or an enhancement ban.

**Next action:** held at Stage 1 by maintainer decision. When opened: resolve the Art. 1.2.1 tension (which cases a ban covers); red team H1–H3 against RED-1 to RED-6; Chapter I ceiling check (instruction 10a); compression disclosure against the three v1.0 points.

## **SIM-QA-28: Lost Provision — Contraception and Coercive Population Control**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-16  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` (Stage 1: HYPOTHESIS) — opened from the SIM-QA-22 side audit by maintainer decision 2026-11-16 (SIM-QA-22 section 7.1); held at Stage 1 until the maintainer says otherwise. Nothing below is red-teamed; vectors and hypotheses are preliminary.  
* **Originating Context:** SIM-QA-22 side audit (section 7), row XI.3 / XVI, which lists these two points together with children's autonomy. **The SIM-QA-25 board line did not carry them** (it names only "not their parents' property, progressive autonomy, no corporal punishment"); this docket carries them so that no point is dropped silently.  
* **v1.0 location:** Section XI.3, *Dynamic Demographic Equilibrium* (line 632: coercive population control, forced sterilisation, mandatory birth quotas and reproductive licensing barred under bodily autonomy; line 635: reversible, non-invasive, long-acting fertility control available to all sexes and genders, free of patriarchal or religious mandates). *Source note:* the side-audit row also cites Section XVI (line 1051); that line is Compact 5 (children) and holds neither point. Recorded so that the provenance is exact.  
* **Closest current canon:** Arts. 1.2.1 and 1.2.3 (bodily sovereignty and autonomy, general), Art. 1.2.2 (no pharmacological manipulation, no physical subjugation), Art. 1.3.2 (healthcare in the baseline). None names contraception, sterilisation, birth quotas or reproductive licensing.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Chapter I, Arts. 1.2, 1.3.2, 1.3.3, 1.7; Art. 3.4 (carrying capacity); cross-references SIM-QA-16 (demographic inversion), SIM-QA-23 (sex and gender as grounds), SIM-QA-24 (religious mandates), SIM-QA-25 (adolescents' access).  
* **Not carried (same v1.0 passage):** carrying-capacity signalling to inform reproductive choices, the *Vitality Deficit Signal* and pro-natal allocations (lines 633–643); policy devices, not rights. Listed so that the omission is visible.

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 1.2 protects bodily autonomy in general words; a carrying-capacity crisis (Art. 3.4) gives an assembly a reason to cap births, and nothing names the cap as forbidden.
* Contraception is not named in the baseline of Art. 1.3.2; access depends on how "comprehensive primary, preventative" care is read.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, quota by ecology:** a bioregion over its carrying capacity sets birth quotas or licences "temporarily" → only the general words of Art. 1.2 stand against it; Art. 1.7 protects rights, but this right is not named.
* **RED-2, coercion by allocation:** no quota, but housing, rosters or discretionary allocations are tied to family size → Art. 1.3.3 covers survival only, not discretionary tiers.
* **RED-3, pro-natal coercion:** the reverse case under demographic inversion (SIM-QA-16): pressure or conditions to bear children, restricted contraception → nothing names it.
* **RED-4, access gate:** contraception available in principle but gated by a local guild, a fellowship or parental consent for adolescents → interaction with SIM-QA-24 and SIM-QA-25.
* **RED-5, scope creep:** a named right to contraception read as a claim on any device in any quantity → bound by the Leontief limits of Chapter III.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence** (rely on Art. 1.2).
* **H1, restore v1.0 points in Arts. 1.2 and 1.3.2:** no body may limit, license or require the bearing of children, or sterilise anyone without consent; contraception is part of the healthcare baseline for every person.
* **H2, prohibition only:** the anti-coercion clause of H1 in Art. 1.2, with access left to Art. 1.3.2 as it stands.
* **H3, prohibition both ways:** H1 plus an explicit bar on allocations or penalties tied to the number of children, in either direction.

**Next action:** held at Stage 1 by maintainer decision. When opened: red team H1–H3 against RED-1 to RED-5; align adolescent access with SIM-QA-25; Chapter I ceiling check (instruction 10a); compression disclosure against the two v1.0 points.

## **SIM-QA-29: Civic Register — Entry, Exit and Who Cannot Serve**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-16  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), red team run 2026-11-16 on maintainer instruction after the decisions of section 4. Verdict `NARROWED` overall, with `RED_WINS` residuals listed in section 5. Purple patch drafted as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged; canon unchanged.  
* **Originating Context:** working session of 2026-11-16, first pass, not reviewed by a specialist. Maintainer decisions of 2026-11-16 in section 4.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 4.3.1 (jurors drawn "from universal civic registers", stratified by age, gender and geography); Art. 7.4.4 (personhood proven by Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood, "without harvesting biometric traits, facial imagery, or personal identity data"); Art. 7.4.2 (keys dispersed by threshold computation); Art. 10.4.2 (Green Status grants "immediate residential usufruct and civic assembly enrollment"); Art. 1.3.1 (baseline held by "every inhabitant"). None says who is in the register, who enrols a person, or how birth, death, a lost credential, children and incapacity are handled.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.2, 1.3.1, 3.4.4, 4.3.1, 4.3.2, 4.6.1, 5.1.3, 5.2, 6.1.2, 7.4.2, 7.4.4, 10.2.2; Organic Law OL-2 (anti-brokerage of scarce slots), OL-9 (petition intake); cross-references SIM-QA-18 (summons refusal), SIM-QA-20 (sealed Register, closed-session juries), SIM-QA-25 (children).  
* **Chapter IV gap:** civic register referenced in Art. 4.3.1, never defined; who is in it (age, length of residence) is not stated: gap G-1 ([`oasis/chapter_iv_gaps_2026-11-13.md`](chapter_iv_gaps_2026-11-13.md)); same gap as R-IV-13 and term T-12 of the plain-language pass. *Source note:* the gap list of the 2026-11-13 chat trial was added to the repository on 2026-11-16, and the reference is restored next to the plain wording.  

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 7.4.4 says how personhood is proven, but no article says who is in the civic register, who enrols a person, or what happens at birth, at death, when a credential is lost, for children, or for persons who cannot follow a deliberation.
* Art. 4.3.1 stratifies every draw by age, gender and geography, so each row must carry those three values and cannot be empty. A register that holds nothing readable cannot be stratified.
* Art. 7.4.4 bars harvesting "personal identity data". A register that must re-link a person after a lost credential or close a row at death has to keep something linkable somewhere. Where it is kept, and who may open it, is not written.
* *Noted at opening:* Art. 1.3.1 and Art. 5.2.1 make the baseline independent of any status, so the register must never gate water, food, shelter or care. Art. 5.2.3 keeps sortition eligibility unconditioned on labour. Both bound every hypothesis below.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, double enrolment:** one person enrols in two distant nodes and holds two draw chances and two petition allowances → nothing in canon checks uniqueness across nodes.
* **RED-2, invented persons:** a colluding node registers people who do not exist, gaining draw weight, petition tokens (Art. 4.3.5, OL-9) and headcount for allocations → no audit of rows is written.
* **RED-3, the dead keep voting:** an heir keeps using a dead person's credential → no rule closes a row at death.
* **RED-4, second voice by loss:** a person reports a lost credential and is issued a new one while keeping the old → no rule binds the new key to the same row.
* **RED-5, lending:** a scarce place (a seat, a fabrication slot, a booking) or a credential itself is lent or traded → OL-2 covers fabrication slots only.
* **RED-6, carer harvesting:** a carer gathers the credentials of frail persons and uses them for remote petition endorsements → nothing separates assistance from use.
* **RED-7, incapacity as silencing:** a declaration of incapacity is used to remove an inconvenient person from the draw → no procedure, no standing to contest.
* **RED-8, the steered member:** a seated member who cannot follow the deliberation is steered by a companion; in a panel of three that is one vote of three → no rule on companions in deliberation.
* **RED-9, custodians collude:** the holders of a neighbourhood's sealed records open them together → threshold custody (Art. 7.4.2) exists for network keys, not for records.
* **RED-10, re-identification in small nodes:** in a node of a few dozen people, year of birth, gender and node identify the person → the index row is a name in all but form.
* **RED-11, time balances:** extra hours are hoarded into a store of claims, or whoever certifies hours inflates them → reintroduces accumulation against Arts. 3.2 and 5.1.2.
* **RED-12, captured guardians:** the pool of guardians for those who cannot be drawn is filled by carers with their own interests → the represented person's voice becomes the carer's.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H1, three layers.** (a) A global index replicated per bioregion: one row per person with a uniqueness fingerprint, year of birth, gender, node, status and public key. (b) A sealed record held in the neighbourhood: the name as known locally, date and place of birth, the row reference, guardians, and a reserve copy used to re-link accounts. (c) A personal secret held by the person.
* **H2, birth as the door.** The fingerprint is computed from birth data with a dispersed key (Art. 7.4.2). In steady state, birth is the only way in. Birth data is a sealed anchor for uniqueness and is never shown as an identifier.
* **H3, sealed opening.** The sealed record opens only before a jury drawn by lot, on a closed list of grounds: death, lost secret, challenge.
* **H4, pseudonyms per use.** A different pseudonym for each use: petitions, rosters, bookings, the Practitioner Register (SIM-QA-20).
* **H5, extra hours stay local.** Hours above the roster go to a local account. They cannot be transferred and can be spent only against the person's own future shifts (Art. 5.1.3).
* **H6, travel.** Each node debits the absence of its own member. Scarce places cannot be transferred and return to the lot when unused.
* **H7, newborns.** A row with no key is created at birth. The secret is created at a fixed age and confirmed by those who raised the child. No marking of the body.
* **H8, death.** A doctor and a witness certify the death; a jury closes the row. Fallback: the row lapses after a fixed period without a sign of life given in person.
* **H9, lost or stolen secret.** A jury revokes the old key and binds a new one to the same row.
* **H10, exemption from the draw.** Asked for by the person, or by the guardian with a medical opinion, never by an authority. Decided by a panel from another node. Lifted on simple request. It removes only the draw, nothing else. Safeguards: orientation offered first as a safety net; an alternate from the same stratum takes the seat; no companion in deliberation or in the voting booth; exemption rates published per stratum.
* **H11, guardians for those who cannot be drawn** *(maintainer proposal)*. Exempted persons and children are represented by guardians drawn by lot on the model of Art. 10.2.2: voice, access to the agenda, and standing under Art. 4.6.1. Mixed pool, exclusion for conflict of interest, rotation. Guardians receive counts above a minimum, in broad bands, never names. *Whether guardians hold voice without vote is not yet confirmed.*
* **H12, official contact point.** A contact point bound to the row for summonses.

**Rejected at opening, with reason:**
* Pure anonymity with no link: fails on a lost secret and on death at scale.
* A global register of readable personal data: a single point of capture, a complete list of persons with their location, and unusable when a node is islanded (Art. 6.1.2).
* Photographs or face recognition: barred by Art. 7.4.4.
* Marking of the body: barred by Art. 1.2.

**Open points:**
* The sealed record of H1(b) touches Art. 7.4.4 ("personal identity data"); a change there needs 75%.
* Adults entering during the transition have no witnessed birth; H2 gives them no door.
* Guardianship of orphans and of incapable adults has not been checked against canon (Arts. 5.4, 5.6).
* Custody of the sealed records: who holds them, how many custodians, what threshold.
* Art. 4.3.2 calls service "a universal prerogative"; whether an exemption under H10 is compatible.
* Remote endorsements by frail persons (RED-6).
* Acts done with a stolen secret before revocation: void, valid, or reviewed.
* Ceiling on time balances and who certifies hours (RED-11).
* Physical delivery of summonses and credentials (H12).
* Small nodes (RED-10).
* Parameters: age at which the secret is created, sign-of-life period, minimum count for guardians.
* *Noted at opening:* Art. 10.4.2 grants civic enrolment on arrival. Under H2 an arrival from another basin is a change of node on an existing row. An arrival from outside the commons falls under the transition point above.

**Honest residual:** no scheme without biometrics excludes double enrolment worldwide with certainty.

### **4. Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-16)**
* **D-29.2, direction:** H1 to H3 are the direction, with one exception to H2: during the transition, a drawn jury may admit newcomers who have no witnessed birth. In steady state, birth stays the only door.
* **D-29.3, no photographs:** confirmed; no photograph or face image in any layer.
* **D-29.1, H11:** the Advocates of persons who cannot be drawn hold **voice without vote**: they speak, place items on the agenda and hold standing under Art. 4.6.1; they do not vote. Same model as the Guardians of Art. 10.2.2.
* **D-29.5, jury door (decided after the red team of section 5): version B.** The jury door stays open after the transition for persons whose birth no register of the commons holds (Art. 10.4.1). The maintainer's concern behind the transition limit (legacy arrivals passing from node to node without contributing) is met outside the door: the door gives voice, not food or shelter, which Arts. 1.3, 5.2.1 and 10.4.3 give to everyone; see OL-22 point 18 and SIM-QA-30 H3.
* **D-29.1b, name of the H11 role: Advocate** (in full, *Advocate for the Undrawn*). "Guardian" (Italian *tutore*) is rejected for this role because it names the legal representative of an individual person (a minor or a person under incapacity), which this role is not: it speaks for a group, never knows names, and holds no power over anyone. The Guardians of Art. 10.2.2 keep their name; the two roles differ and are named apart (cross-reference: open term T-21 of the plain-language pass).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-16, on maintainer instruction; RED-10 measured, synthetic; the rest reasoning on text; first pass, no specialist review)*

*Scope.* H1 to H12 with the decisions of section 4, against RED-1 to RED-12, the transition jury door of D-29.2, and six vectors found while testing (RED-13 to RED-18). Guards named "point n" refer to the Organic Law draft OL-22 in 5.3. Verdicts: `BLUE_HOLDS`, `NARROWED` (holds with a residual), `RED_WINS`.

#### **5.1 Verdicts**
* **RED-1, double enrolment in two distant nodes. `NARROWED`.** In steady state a second row needs a second birth, so the attack becomes RED-2 (an invented birth). Through the jury door (RED-13) a person with two legacy identity documents can enrol twice under two sets of birth data, and no fingerprint collides. *Guard:* the sign of life in person (point 9) and the sample audit (point 10) make a double life cost two regular appearances in two distant places. *Residual:* the one already declared; no non-biometric scheme excludes it.
* **RED-2, a colluding node invents persons. `NARROWED`.** An invented person needs a false birth certificate (birth attendant and witness, point 3) or a captured jury door, and then someone to hold its secret. It gains draw weight, an endorsement allowance and headcount. *Guards:* a drawn invented person never takes the seat (the alternate sits, and a node with many no-shows stands out); sign of life in person (point 9); a yearly sample audit in person by an outside panel (point 10), which needs **"sample audit" added to the closed grounds of H3** (point 6). *Residual:* collusion between a node and the outside panel drawn to audit it, with the probability of the sample.
* **RED-3, an heir keeps using a dead person's credential. `NARROWED`.** H8 closes the row only when the death is reported. An unreported death keeps the credential alive until the sign-of-life period ends (point 9). *Residual:* the length of that period, a parameter (D-29.4).
* **RED-4, a false loss report for a second voice. `BLUE_HOLDS` on the second voice; `NARROWED` on the window.** H9 binds the new key to the same row and revokes the old one, so one row never holds two keys. The window between a sale of the old key and its revocation remains. *Guard (point 11):* the report suspends the old key at once; acts signed after it are void; acts in a short period before it are reviewable on challenge.
* **RED-5, lending a scarce place or a credential. Places: `NARROWED`. Credentials: `RED_WINS` for remote acts and for seats in large bodies.** H6 makes places non-transferable and returns them to the lot. A lent secret cannot be told apart from its holder in a remote act; lending gains nothing beyond the lender's own allowance (no amplification), so it is vote buying, already void under Art. 4.1.3. In bodies of 101 or more, members do not know each other, and **without photographs (D-29.3) nothing checks that the person seated is the person drawn**. *Option O-1 (not drafted, for the maintainer):* at the first session, two residents of the member's node drawn by lot confirm in closed session that the person seated is the person they know. Cost: travel and a small loss of privacy toward two neighbours.
* **RED-6, a carer collects the credentials of frail persons. `RED_WINS` on H1 to H12** (no hypothesis covers it). *Guard drafted (point 13):* a person who needs help with an endorsement or a vote is helped by a helper drawn by lot from outside their household and care, never by the carer; counts of assisted acts per petition are published. With it: `NARROWED`; residual: a carer who keeps the secret and acts unassisted is not seen.
* **RED-7, a declaration of incapacity used to silence someone. `BLUE_HOLDS`** on what the exemption removes: only the draw, lifted on the person's request, never asked for by an authority, decided by a panel from another node (H10). *`NARROWED`* for a person whom the carer isolates so that the request to lift is never made. *Guard (point 14):* an exempted person is offered a yearly visit by a drawn outside visitor who asks only whether they want the exemption lifted. *Note:* H10's "guardian" is a legal representative of one person; the canon has no rule on legal representation of adults (open point), and the H11 role is now named Advocate (D-29.1b) so the two are not confused.
* **RED-8, a seated member who cannot follow is steered. `NARROWED`.** No companion in deliberation or the booth (H10) holds in session. Coaching outside sessions is the same exposure as any member's household (Art. 4.1.3). A challenge to a member's capacity by another party would reopen RED-7, so none is drafted. *Residual accepted:* one vote of three in a panel can be steered from outside the room.
* **RED-9, custodians collude to open a neighbourhood's sealed records. `NARROWED`.** *Guard (point 5):* each record is encrypted with shares held by custodians from different nodes and a share held by the deciding jury; every opening is logged in public with its date and ground, never its content. *Residual:* neighbours already know names; the seal protects mainly the link between a name, a birth and a row.
* **RED-10, re-identification in small nodes. `RED_WINS` on H1 as written (measured); `NARROWED` with point 1.** [`sim_qa_29_reidentification.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_29_reidentification.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_29_reidentification_results.md) (`--reps 400 --seed 20261116`; **synthetic, uncalibrated**; two genders at 50/50, so a lower bound):

  | Adults in node | Alone in cell, year of birth + gender | Alone, 10-year band | In a cell under 5, three age bands |
  | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | 15 | 89.8% | 34.5% | 75.2% |
  | 50 | 69.3% | 4.8% | 5.8% |
  | 150 | 34.7% | 0.4% | 0.0% |
  | 1,000 | 1.0% | 0.0% | 0.0% |

  A readable index with node, gender and year of birth names most people in a node of fifty. Coarser bands help only above about a hundred adults. *Guard (point 1):* the index holds an age band, never the year (the year stays in the sealed record); it is readable only by the draw code running under seal; only draw results and aggregates with a minimum cell are published, and a node whose cells would fall below the minimum is reported only inside its bioregion's total. *Residual:* in a node of fifteen, even the published draw result (who sits) tells neighbours a great deal. **Cross-docket finding (not resolved):** the per-stratum publication of refusal, withdrawal and excusal rates in Part B Art. 4.3.1 (SIM-QA-18 H4) has the same flaw in small nodes; point 1's minimum cell should govern it too.
* **RED-11, time balances hoarded or hours inflated. `NARROWED`.** H5 makes balances local, non-transferable and usable only against one's own future shifts: no exchange, no interest, no speculation (Art. 3.2). Hoarding remains possible without a ceiling, and inflation by whoever certifies hours. *Undecided:* the ceiling and the certifier (D-29.4). *Placement:* H5 and H6 belong to the roster rules of Chapter V and its Organic Law, not to the register; not drafted in OL-22.
* **RED-12, the Advocate pool captured by carers' interests. `BLUE_HOLDS`** on decisions: under D-29.1 Advocates do not vote. **`NARROWED`** on the agenda: a captured pool could fill agenda slots. *Guards (point 15):* exclusion of anyone who cares for or lives with a person of the represented group, checked blind as in OL-9.7 extended point 14; rotation; Advocates' agenda items enter the same slot lottery as petitions. *Open:* whether their items need co-endorsement like any petition (they speak for persons who cannot endorse).
* **RED-13, the jury door (new, from D-29.2). Invention: `NARROWED`. "During the transition" only: `RED_WINS` against Art. 10.4.1.** *Guards (point 4):* a panel drawn from outside the node, the person present in person, documents seen in closed session and not kept, admissions counted per node and covered by the sample audit. *Finding:* the door is needed after the transition too. Art. 10.4.1 gives every human being the right to seek refuge; a person born outside the commons, or whose birth no register holds, has no witnessed birth. If the door closes with the transition, such a person can live in the commons but never be drawn, never petition and never be counted. **Decision needed (D-29.5):** keep the door limited to the transition (as decided), or keep it permanent for persons whose birth no register of the commons holds. Both are drafted in 5.2.
* **RED-14, the fingerprint as a tracking tool (new). `NARROWED`.** Birth data have little entropy (a date and a place). Whoever can compute fingerprints can test whether a known person is in the register and in which node. *Guards (point 2):* the fingerprint key is dispersed under Art. 7.4.2; each computation is logged with its ground; the index is readable only under seal (point 1). *Residual:* a collusion that reaches the key threshold.
* **RED-15, an islanded node (new). `NARROWED`.** A node cut off under Art. 6.1.2 cannot check uniqueness worldwide. *Guard (point 17):* it registers births locally; replicas are joined by union on reconnection, and colliding fingerprints go to a jury. *Residual:* a double enrolment made during a long partition is found only at reconnection.
* **RED-16, the register as a gate to survival (new). `BLUE_HOLDS` with one canon sentence.** A register invites refusing food, shelter or care to a person without a row, a lapsed row or an unknown status, which Arts. 1.3.1, 5.2.1 and 10.4.3 forbid but do not name for this case. *Guard:* the 7.4.5 sentence "Lacking or losing a row never affects Articles 1.3 or 10.4.3"; a lapsed row suspends only draws and petitions (point 9).
* **RED-17, twins and collisions (new). `NARROWED`.** Two persons born the same day in the same place get the same fingerprint from date and place alone. *Guard (point 2):* a birth sequence number assigned at certification enters the fingerprint; a collision goes to a jury.
* **RED-18, a parent keeps the young person's secret (new, H7). `NARROWED`.** If the people who raised the child create the secret, they hold the child's voice. *Guard (point 7):* the young person creates the secret alone in a civic booth; those who raised the child only confirm identity and never see the secret; the young person may re-key at any time.

**Verdict:** `NARROWED` overall. `RED_WINS` remains on RED-5 (credentials lent for remote acts; persons substituted in large bodies, the cost of D-29.3), on RED-6 without point 13, on RED-10 without point 1, and on the "transition only" limit of the jury door (RED-13). Honest residuals: worldwide double enrolment; lent credentials; in-person substitution in large bodies.

#### **5.2 Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(not merged; canon unchanged)*
*Placement (instruction 10).* Kept in the Constitution: the register and its one-row rule, the bar on row-by-row reading, the sealed record and who may open it, the bar on body marking, the survival guarantee, the bounds of exemption, and the Advocates with their powers. Moved to Organic Law: grounds, procedures, numbers and tools (OL-22). Placed in Chapter VII, next to Art. 7.4.4, because Chapter IV is within fifty words of its ceiling (rule 10a(5)) and the register is already governed there; Chapter IV is not changed.

* **Art. 7.4.4 (amended):** "4. Civic participation, petitioning, and sortition registry access rely on Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood (ZK-PoP), verifying living human individuality without harvesting biometric traits, facial imagery, or personal identity data, save the sealed records of point 5."
* **Art. 7.4.5 (new), version A, as decided (D-29.2):**
> 5. Civic Register: The civic register of Article 4.3.1 holds one row per person, entered at birth or, during the transition (Chapter IX), by a jury drawn by lot, never by an authority. It is never read row by row; draws run under seal and only aggregates are published. Data that keep one row per person, close it at death or restore a lost key stay in a sealed local record, opened only by a drawn jury on grounds listed in Organic Law. No mark is placed on the body. Lacking or losing a row never affects Articles 1.3 or 10.4.3. Exemption from the draw is granted only on request of the person, or of their legal representative with a medical opinion, by an outside panel; it removes only the draw and ends on the person's request. Persons who cannot be drawn are heard through Advocates drawn by lot, who speak, place agenda items and hold standing under Article 4.6.1, without a vote.
* **Version B (RED-13; adopted by D-29.5, 2026-11-16, replaces version A):** "entered at birth or, during the transition (Chapter IX), by a jury drawn by lot" becomes "entered at birth or by a jury drawn by lot for a person whose birth no register holds".
* *Term note:* "The civic register of Article 4.3.1" avoids deciding T-12 (one register or two; R-IV-13) here.

**Organic Law (proposed, 60%), OL-22, Civic Register** *(implements Arts. 4.3.1, 7.4.4, 7.4.5; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Index.** One row per person: fingerprint, age band of [five years], gender, node, status (active, suspended, closed), public key. Replicated per bioregion. Readable only by the draw code running under seal. Published: draw results, and aggregates with a minimum cell of [ten (10)]; a node whose cells would fall below it is reported only inside its bioregion's total. The same minimum governs the per-stratum rates of Art. 4.3.1.
2. **Fingerprint.** Computed from the date and place of birth and a birth sequence number assigned at certification, with a key dispersed under Art. 7.4.2. Each computation is logged with its ground. Collisions go to a jury.
3. **Entry at birth.** Certified by the person who attended the birth and one witness from outside the household. The row is created without a key.
4. **Entry by jury.** A panel of [three (3)] drawn from outside the node; the person is present in person; documents are seen in closed session and not kept; only the decision and the fingerprint input are recorded. Admissions are counted per node and published under point 1. Open to everyone in version B; limited to the transition in version A.
5. **Sealed record.** Holds the name as known locally, the date and place of birth, the row, any legal representative, and the reserve copy used to re-link accounts. Encrypted; shares held by [three (3)] custodians drawn from [three (3)] different nodes, threshold [two (2)], plus a share held by the deciding jury. Every opening is logged in public with its date and ground, never its content.
6. **Grounds to open (closed list).** Death; lost or stolen secret; a challenge of a row (duplicate or invented person); the sample audit of point 10. Never for policing, allocation or research.
7. **The young person's secret.** Created at age [fourteen to sixteen] by the young person alone in a civic booth. Those who raised the child confirm identity before a drawn verifier and never see or hold the secret. The person may re-key at any time on their own request.
8. **Death.** Certified by a doctor and a witness; a panel of three drawn by lot closes the row.
9. **Sign of life.** Any civic act in person counts (a vote in a booth, a session, an orientation, a check at a civic point). After [five (5) years] without one, the row is suspended for draws and petitions only, never for anything under Arts. 1.3 or 10.4.3, and restored at the next appearance. A person who cannot travel asks for a home visit by a drawn verifier. A suspended row is closed only under point 8.
10. **Sample audit.** Each year a panel drawn from outside the bioregion checks in person a random sample of [one percent (1%)] of each node's rows; results are published as counts.
11. **Lost or stolen secret.** A report suspends the old key at once; acts signed after the report are void; acts in the [thirty (30) days] before it are reviewable on challenge. A jury binds a new key to the same row within [seven (7) days].
12. **Pseudonyms.** A different pseudonym for each use (petitions, rosters, bookings, the Practitioner Register).
13. **Assisted acts.** A person who needs help to endorse or vote is helped by a helper drawn by lot from outside their household and care, never by their carer. Counts of assisted acts per petition are published under point 1.
14. **Exemption.** Decided by a panel of three drawn from another node; rates published per stratum under point 1. An exempted person is offered, every [year], a visit by a drawn outside visitor who asks only whether they want the exemption lifted.
15. **Advocates.** A mixed pool drawn by lot; anyone who cares for or lives with a person of the represented group is excluded, checked blind as in OL-9.7 extended point 14; rotation under Art. 4.3.3. Advocates receive counts in bands, never names, with a minimum of [ten (10)]. Their agenda items enter the slot lottery of Art. 4.3.5.
16. **Contact point.** Each row has one official contact point for summonses, held in the sealed record and used only to deliver them.
17. **Partition.** A cut-off replica registers births locally; on reconnection replicas are joined by union and colliding fingerprints go to a jury.
18. **Reciprocity status follows the row** *(added 2026-11-16 with D-29.5)*. The only Art. 5.2.2 status a row carries is "Tier 3 access suspended for refused shifts". It follows the person to any node, so a change of node does not reset it, and it ends *ipso jure* at the first reciprocity shift or agreed equivalent contribution in any node (Art. 5.2.3). No history, reason or count of refusals is carried; only the current status, readable by the roster of the node where the person lives. It never touches Tier 1, shelter, sanctuary or political rights.
    * *Red team.* **F-1, node hopping. `BLUE_HOLDS`:** a move no longer restores Tier 3; a guest without a row starts every node at hospitality (SIM-QA-30 H3), so hopping gains nothing over staying. **F-2, perpetual stigma (Art. 5.2.3). `BLUE_HOLDS`:** one shift anywhere ends the status, and no history is kept. **F-3, a false status set by a hostile node. `NARROWED`:** the status is set only after the Local Mediation Panel of Art. 5.2.2, and the person may ask a panel from another node to lift it. **F-4, the cost that remains. Not a vector:** a free rider keeps Tier 1 everywhere by design (Art. 1.3); the bearable share is SIMONE challenge 1, not measured, and for seed nodes it is SIM-QA-30.

#### **5.3 Word Count** *(tokens containing a letter or a digit, headings included; the script reproduces the recorded canon counts: Chapter I 808, Chapter IV 926, Chapter VII 712)*
| Text | Words | Against the ceiling |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Art. 7.4.4 amendment | +7 | — |
| Art. 7.4.5, version A | +163 | — |
| Art. 7.4.5, version B | +166 | — |
| Chapter VII, canon with version A (B) | 882 (885) | 1,000: 118 (115) left |
| Chapter VII with SIM-QA-22 D-6 and D-7 and version A (B) | 975 (978) | 25 (22) left: **within fifty words** |
| Chapter IV | unchanged | — |

Under rule 10a(5), Chapter VII would then receive further detail in Organic Law by default. The total ceiling (11,500) needs the full recount already pending under Z-3; this patch adds 170 to 173 words to it.

#### **5.4 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Registry access without harvesting personal identity data | Art. 7.4.4 | an exception for the sealed records of 7.4.5 | **weakened**; for decision |
| 2 | H2: birth is the only door in steady state | H2 | version B opens a permanent jury door for persons whose birth no register holds | **changed** if B is chosen (D-29.5) |
| 3 | H5, H6 (time balances, travel) | H5, H6 | not drafted here; placed with the roster rules of Chapter V | **moved**, not dropped |

#### **5.5 Residual Weaknesses**
* Worldwide double enrolment is not excluded.
* A lent credential cannot be told apart from its holder in remote acts.
* Without photographs, a person can be substituted at a seat in a large body unless option O-1 is adopted.
* A carer who holds a frail person's secret and acts without asking for help is not seen.
* In very small nodes, even the published draw result says much about who serves.
* Every number in OL-22 is unverified.

**Next action (revision 1):** maintainer decisions D-29.4 to D-29.9 (board); after them, the purple patch is revised and the docket moves to Stage 3.

## **SIM-QA-30: Admission to Seed Nodes During the Transition**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-16  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened and red-teamed 2026-11-16 on maintainer instruction (section 4). Verdict `NARROWED` with all four strategies together; any one alone fails (measured, synthetic). Purple patch drafted as `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]`, not merged; canon unchanged.  
* **Originating Context:** working session of 2026-11-16, first pass, not reviewed by a specialist.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 10.4.2 (Green, Amber and Red Status on Ecological Carrying Capacity telemetry; Red pauses permanent tenure), Art. 10.4.3 (Inviolable Sanctuary Guarantee: hospitality, water, food, emergency care and safe transit for every traveller, even under Red), Art. 10.4.4 (Federal Rebalancing and Transit Bypass to contiguous Green and Amber basins), Art. 3.1.6 (Tier 1 Autarkic Closure audit), Art. 5.2 (reciprocity; Tier 1 shelter never conditioned). Chapter IX (Arts. 9.1 to 9.6) gives no admission rule for a node in transition.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.3.1, 3.1.6, 5.2, 9.1 to 9.6, 10.4.2 to 10.4.4; roadmap (Stage 1 seed node, 180-day caloric cache, `roadmap/roadmap.md`); SIMONE challenge 1 (free-rider threshold that collapses Tier 1, `simone/simone-specs.md`); cross-reference SIM-QA-29 (enrolment).  
* **Source note:** the 180-day reserve is the roadmap's Stage 1 cache. Art. 3.5.2 asks for 12 to 24 months of Tier 1 output. The seed node is therefore below the canon reserve by design during the transition. Recorded, not resolved.

### **1. The Friction**
* Chapter IX has no admission rule for a node in transition.
* Art. 10.4 is written for whole basins. Its transit bypass (Art. 10.4.4) assumes neighbouring Green and Amber basins that do not exist yet during the transition.
* A seed node with a 180-day reserve inside the legacy economy can attract more people than it can feed. Art. 10.4.3 obliges it to feed every traveller "under no circumstances" less.
* *Noted at opening:* Art. 5.2.2 says Tier 1 shelter "is never conditioned". H3 (shifts before stable usufruct) has to be read against it.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, overrun:** open arrivals exceed capacity and Tier 1 fails for everyone → Art. 10.4.3 binds without a neighbour to share the load.
* **RED-2, club:** members choose who enters and the node becomes a club → nothing bars selection by affinity.
* **RED-3, ceiling as back door:** a hospitality ceiling is used to deny Art. 10.4.3 → the ceiling becomes a border by another name.
* **RED-4, unknown carrying share:** the share of non-contributors a node can carry is unknown (SIMONE challenge 1) → any ceiling is set without evidence.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H1, declared capacity.** Capacity is declared from the node's physical accounts (Arts. 3.1.6, 3.4).
* **H2, lot.** When places are fewer than requests, admission is by lot among applicants.
* **H3, entry period.** An entry period in hospitality, with shifts, before dwelling usufruct becomes stable.
* **H4, declared ceiling and referral.** Hospitality up to a declared ceiling; beyond it, referral to the services of the existing state. Stated openly as a time-limited derogation from Art. 10.4.3, never hidden.

### **4. Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-16)**
* **D-30.1:** opened now, after SIM-QA-29.
* **D-30.2, direction:** strategies 1, 2 and 3 together, with 4 as the last resort, written openly:
  1. cap residence, not hospitality: capacity declared from the node's physical accounts; residence by lot when applicants exceed it (H1, H2), after an entry period with shifts (H3);
  2. a network of seed nodes that transfer guests and resources to each other (a transitional form of Art. 10.4.4);
  3. core hospitality for everyone, without exception: emergency care, water, food and shelter for a fixed number of nights;
  4. beyond the ceiling, accompanied referral to the services of the existing state (H4), as an explicit, time-limited derogation from Art. 10.4.3.
  
  Safeguard against a club for every strategy: the ceiling comes from physical accounts and is checked by a panel drawn from outside; no vote is ever taken on persons.
* **Reasoning recorded (2026-11-16 session):** a node cannot turn anyone away under Arts. 1.3, 2.3, 5.2.1 and 10.4.3, and Art. 4.5.1 allows no diminution of fundamental rights even at 75% (Art. 1.7). The canon reconciles "no one is turned away" with the physical ceiling of Art. 3.1.4 only through neighbouring basins (Art. 10.4.4), which do not exist for a seed node.

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-16, on maintainer instruction; capacity measured, synthetic; the rest reasoning on text; first pass, no specialist review)*

#### **5.1 Model** ([`sim_qa_30_seed_node_capacity.py`](../simone/benchmarks/sim_qa_30_seed_node_capacity.py) → [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_30_seed_node_capacity_results.md); deterministic; **synthetic, uncalibrated**; not SIMONE challenge 1, which remains `NOT_RUN`)
A seed node of 60 persons, 80% contributing; installed capacity (land, water, energy) of 72 rations a day; a reserve of 180 days; failure when the reserve falls below 30 days of the current population (Art. 6.1.2). Arrivals eat from day one and contribute from week five with probability G.

| Arrivals a week | Open (P0) | Residence cap only (strategy 1 alone) | Network, half accept transfer (2) | Core hospitality 7 nights, then referral (3 + 4) | 2, then 3 + 4 |
| ---: | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | fails day 408 | fails day 408 | fails day 556 | holds 2 years (92 referred) | holds (46 referred, 46 transferred) |
| 3 | fails day 198 | fails day 198 | fails day 281 | holds (300 referred) | holds (150 referred, 152 transferred) |
| 10 | fails day 89 | fails day 89 | fails day 134 | holds (1,030 referred) | holds (515 referred, 520 transferred) |

*Findings.* (a) **A cap on residence alone changes nothing**: guests eat as residents do. (b) The network delays failure by about 35% to 50% at 50% acceptance; it does not prevent it. (c) Only a limit on how long arrivals above capacity stay keeps Tier 1 for everyone, and its price is the number of people referred. (d) **Contribution does not matter at capacity:** with installed capacity binding, the share of arrivals who work (G = 20% to 80%) leaves every result unchanged. Where land is plentiful and labour binds (section 4 of the results), G matters: at three arrivals a week, failure on day 189 at 20%, day 349 at 80%. For a seed node the threat is numbers above capacity, not free riders.

#### **5.2 Verdicts**
* **RED-1, open arrivals exceed capacity. Strategy 1 alone: `RED_WINS` (measured). Strategies 1 + 2: `RED_WINS`, delayed. Strategies 1 + 2 + 3 + 4: `BLUE_HOLDS` on Tier 1 (measured),** at the price of referrals.
* **RED-2, members choose who enters. `NARROWED`.** Residence by lot among applicants; capacity from physical accounts; an outside panel checks it; no vote on persons. *Residual:* members can discourage applicants informally; the lot covers residence, not the welcome.
* **RED-3, the ceiling as a back door. `NARROWED`.** A node can understate capacity to refer more people. *Guards (OL-23):* capacity by published formula from the Art. 3.1.6 accounts, checked by an outside panel; referral counts published; spare capacity visible; strategy 3 never suspended. *Residual:* the formula itself; a node that under-builds (does not expand capacity) is not caught by a formula.
* **RED-4, the bearable share of non-contributors is unknown. `NARROWED`, reframed.** At a seed node capacity binds before labour (finding d). The ceiling must come from installed capacity and the reserve floor, not from a share of free riders. The free-rider share matters where labour binds; there it is SIMONE challenge 1, still `NOT_RUN`.
* **RED-5, referral as abandonment (new). `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` where no legacy service exists.** If the legacy service refuses or does not exist (war, a failed state), referral is abandonment. *Guard:* referral only to a named public service that has confirmed it can receive the person; accompanied; otherwise the person stays under strategy 3. *Residual:* where no service exists and no seed node has room, the node faces the overrun with no rule that keeps both Tier 1 and Art. 10.4.3. No text removes this; it is a physical limit.
* **RED-6, free riding inside the network (new). `NARROWED`.** One node always sends and never receives. *Guard (OL-23):* a ledger of transfers in physical units (persons and rations), settled by the reciprocity of Art. 10.3; transfers follow declared capacity.
* **RED-7, transfer as forced displacement (new). `NARROWED`.** A transfer is offered, never imposed (Art. 10.4.3 bars involuntary deportation). A person who refuses stays under strategy 3, then strategy 4 applies, so refusal has a consequence. *Residual accepted:* the choice is between two places with room, or referral.
* **RED-8, the entry period against Art. 5.2.2 (new). `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Shifts condition stable residence (usufruct of a dwelling), never Tier 1 shelter, which the hospitality of strategy 3 and the sentence "shelter at Tier 1 is never conditioned" keep.
* **RED-9, gaming the lot (new). `NARROWED`.** Applicants enter several nodes' lots at once, or a household enters each member separately. *Guards (OL-23):* the lot is per person; one application at a time per person, checked through the SIM-QA-29 register where a row exists; a household that wants to stay together applies together and is drawn as one.
* **RED-10, the derogation outlives the transition (new). `NARROWED`.** *Guard:* it lapses for each node as soon as Art. 10.4.4 can operate for it (a federation with neighbours that can receive), and for all at the end of the transition (phase gate, Art. 9.6.2, 75%). *Residual:* a federation that delays the end of the transition keeps the derogation alive.
* **RED-11, visibility attracts arrivals (new). Not a text vector.** A seed node known to feed everyone draws more arrivals. Publicity is an outreach matter, outside this repository and outside this docket (D-30.6).

**Verdict:** `NARROWED` with strategies 1 + 2 + 3 + 4 together. Any one strategy alone fails. `RED_WINS` remains where no legacy service exists and no seed node has room (RED-5): there the canon's two promises, Tier 1 for all and no one turned away, cannot both be kept.

#### **5.3 Purple Patch (Proposed)** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(not merged; canon unchanged)*
*Placement (instruction 10).* Kept in the Constitution, in Chapter IX because it is transitional: capacity from physical accounts, no vote on persons, residence by lot, unconditioned shelter, the core hospitality floor, the order transfer then referral, and the derogation with its end. The derogation is also named in Art. 10.4.3, so that it is never hidden. Moved to Organic Law (OL-23): the formula, the entry period, the ledger, the procedures.

* **Art. 9.1.4 (new):**
> 4. Admission to Transition Nodes: A transition node declares its capacity from its physical accounts (Article 3.1.6), checked by a panel drawn from outside it; members never decide on persons. Where applicants for residence exceed capacity, residence is assigned by lot, after an entry period of hospitality with shifts set by Organic Law; shelter at Tier 1 is never conditioned. Every arrival receives water, food, emergency care and shelter for no fewer than seven (7) nights. Beyond declared capacity the node first offers transfer to another transition node with room; only where none exists may it refer an arrival, accompanied, to a public service of the legacy jurisdiction that has confirmed it can receive them. This derogation from Article 10.4.3 lapses for each node when Article 10.4.4 can operate for it, and for all at the end of the transition.
* **Art. 10.4.3 (amended):** "Inviolable Sanctuary Guarantee: Under no circumstances, save the transitional rule of Article 9.1.4, shall any human being face …" (rest unchanged).

**Organic Law (proposed, 60%), OL-23, Admission to Transition Nodes** *(implements Arts. 9.1.4, 10.4.3; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
1. **Capacity.** The number of persons whose Tier 1 the node can supply from installed capacity while its reserve stays above [thirty (30)] days of the current population (Art. 6.1.2) and above the roadmap cache. Computed by a published formula from the Art. 3.1.6 accounts, reviewed every [three months], after each harvest and after any change of installed capacity; checked by a panel of three drawn from outside the node.
2. **Entry period.** [Eight (8)] weeks of hospitality with shifts on the node's ordinary rosters before a dwelling is allocated in usufruct; shifts are counted as for any member (Art. 5.1).
3. **Lot.** Per person; a household may apply together and is drawn as one. One application at a time per person, checked through the civic register where a row exists (SIM-QA-29).
4. **Core hospitality.** Water, food, emergency care and shelter for [seven (7)] nights, the canon floor (changed from fourteen by D-30.4: at ten arrivals a week fourteen nights exhausts a full node by day 461 in the model, results section 5); a person in medical need stays until fit to travel, and a person awaiting a confirmed transfer stays until departure.
5. **Transfer.** Seed nodes and allied communities keep a common list of declared capacity. An *allied community* (Art. 9.1.4) is a community outside the commons that has signed the seed-node compact and keeps the ledger of point 6; it may keep its own admission rules. A transfer is offered with transport and the receiving community's confirmation; it is never imposed.
6. **Network ledger.** Transfers are recorded in persons and rations; a node that has received transfers takes them in turn when it has room; settlement follows the reciprocity of Art. 10.3.
7. **Referral.** Only to a named public service of the legacy jurisdiction that has confirmed in writing it can receive the person; the person is accompanied and given the information in their language; a person who returns is received again under point 4.
8. **Publication.** Capacity, occupancy, transfers and referrals are published monthly as counts, never names.
9. **End of the derogation.** For a node, when a neighbour basin can receive under Art. 10.4.4, as certified by a panel of three drawn from outside the node (added with D-30.5); for all, at the phase gate that ends the transition (Art. 9.6.2).

#### **5.6 Residual Weaknesses**
* Where no legacy service exists and no seed node has room, the canon's two promises cannot both be kept.
* Every strategy alone fails; the rule works only as a whole.
* The capacity formula is only as honest as the accounts behind it.
* The numbers of the model are synthetic; SIMONE challenge 1 remains `NOT_RUN`.

**Next action (revision 1):** maintainer decisions D-30.4 to D-30.7 (board).

### **6. Revision (2026-11-16): D-30.7, Shared Shortage** *(Stage 2 continued; reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`)*

#### **6.1 Maintainer Decision**
**D-30.7: option B.** When the node is full, the network has no room, no legacy service can receive arrivals and the External Trade Interface (Art. 9.3) cannot buy enough, no one is turned away. The shortage is shared among all present, members and arrivals alike, after an emergency ladder. Rejected: A (accept the residual with no rule: in the worst moment the strongest decide, usually those already inside); C (priority to those already present: a turning-away under another name, against Art. 10.4.3 and the equal-protection line of SIM-QA-23, and the club the maintainer wanted to avoid).

*Note recorded with the decision:* the dead end is rarer than RED-5 suggests. In the transition a seed node can buy food through the External Trade Interface; the physical limit is local, not global. The dead end needs, at once, no room in the network, no legacy service and no market.

#### **6.2 Draft**
*Placement.* The rule is not transitional: a basin in Red Status whose neighbours cannot help (Art. 10.4.4) meets the same dead end. It goes in Art. 10.4.3, next to the promise it qualifies, not in Art. 9.1.4.
* **Art. 10.4.3 (addition, at the end):** "Where physical limits prevent full Tier 1 for all present, the shortage is shared among all present in proportion to need, whatever their origin; a panel drawn from outside the node certifies the shortage, which is reviewed every thirty (30) days."
* *Wording changed in drafting:* "equally" became "in proportion to need" (F-3 below). A flat equal share starves children, the ill and the pregnant first.

**OL-23 (additions), Shared Shortage** *(applies to every node under Art. 10.4.3, not only transition nodes; every number in brackets is `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`)*
10. **Emergency ladder, before any shortage is shared.** In order: (a) Tier 3 output, energy and labour turned to Tier 1; (b) purchases through the External Trade Interface (Art. 9.3) in the transition, or mutual aid under Art. 10.4.4 after it; (c) transfer offers through the network (point 5); (d) referral under Art. 9.1.4 while it applies. A shortage may be certified only when the record shows each step taken or impossible.
11. **Certification.** A panel of three drawn from outside the node, under the rule for outside panels, certifies within [seventy-two (72) hours] (changed from forty-eight by D-30.4, after Y-8: seating an outside panel fast is not shown possible in sparse regions; the ladder of point 10 does not wait for it), on the published accounts and a site check, that the shortage is real and the ladder exhausted. Review every thirty days by a fresh panel; the shortage ends when supply returns or the panel does not confirm it.
12. **Sharing.** Rations follow need: by age, pregnancy, illness and labour load, on one published table that applies to every person present. Common reserves, kitchens and stores are pooled; no one's ration depends on membership, origin, register status or past contribution. Personal possessions (Art. 2.2) are untouched; food stores beyond [two weeks] of a household's need are common reserves for the duration.
13. **Survival trigger.** If the shared ration falls below [the survival ration of the published table: figure to be set on specialist review in nutrition and humanitarian practice; not set by this docket], the node declares a general alarm: transport is offered to anyone who wishes to leave for a place with food, and appeals go to every node of the network, every legacy public service and every humanitarian body that can reach it. No one is turned away, and no one is fed less than another in proportion to need.
14. **Publication.** The certificate, the ladder record, the ration table and daily totals are public, as counts.

#### **6.3 Red Team**
* **F-1, deliberate depletion (maintainer's question). `NARROWED`.** A hostile actor, or a faction inside, sends or brings people in order to force a shortage and empty a node's reserves; or insiders waste stocks to trigger rationing. *Guards:* the ladder comes first (point 10), and in the transition referral (Art. 9.1.4) and transfer still apply before any sharing; a shortage exists only when an outside panel certifies it on the accounts and a site check (point 11); every step is public (point 14). *Residual:* a large, organised flow in a war, with no services and no market, exhausts any node; the rule decides how the loss is shared, not whether it happens.
* **F-2, hidden stocks. `NARROWED`.** Households keep private food stores out of the pool. *Guard (point 12):* stores beyond two weeks of a household's need count as common for the duration; personal possessions are untouched. *Residual:* hidden stores are found only by visible consumption; no search of homes is drafted, and none should be (Arts. 1.2, 3.4.4).
* **F-3, flat equality starves the vulnerable. `RED_WINS` on "equally"; `BLUE_HOLDS` on "in proportion to need"** with one table for everyone.
* **F-4, the emergency as a pretext. `NARROWED`.** A shortage could be used to suspend other rights. *Guards:* the certificate covers rations only; Art. 1.7 and the review every thirty days; a repeated certificate within twelve months is subject to the same scrutiny as Y-12 (SIM-QA-18 11.7). *Residual:* a long genuine shortage.
* **F-5, attraction. Not a text vector.** A node known to share attracts more arrivals; the same as RED-11, an outreach matter outside this repository (D-30.6).
* **F-6, everyone below survival. `RED_WINS`, physical.** If arrivals exceed every capacity for long, a shared ration sinks below survival for all and the node fails. Point 13 opens every exit; it creates no food. Written as a residual, as decided.
* **F-7, the calorie floor. Disclosed, not a vector.** The public description of the project names a 2,200 kcal daily floor (the canon says "calorically sufficient", Art. 1.3.2). Under a certified shortage the shared ration may fall below it. The rule does not lower the floor; it states who bears a shortage that physics imposes. Listed under instruction 8 below.
* **F-8, who declares. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** The node does not certify its own shortage; an outside panel does (point 11), so a node cannot invent one to stop admissions.

**Verdict on D-30.7:** `NARROWED`, with one physical `RED_WINS` (F-6) recorded as residual.

For decision with D-30.8 |

#### **6.6 Maintainer Decision D-30.8 (2026-11-16)**
**Option 1 accepted.** The Tier 1 floor stays the rule. A certified shortage is the only exception: reached only after the emergency ladder, certified by an outside panel, reviewed every thirty days, and borne by all present in proportion to need. Disclosure item 3 of 6.5 is accepted as **qualified**, and residual F-6 is accepted as a physical limit that no text removes. Rejected: a second, lower floor in the Constitution (when food falls short of it too, it forces a choice of who eats, which is option C again), and a ban on any ration below the floor (impossible to keep in a real shortage; same result).
*Consequence at merge:* public descriptions that state the 2,200 kcal floor (among them `PLAY.md`, the game docs, `habitat/MHU_MODULAR_HABITAT_UNIT.md` and the roadmap Stage 1 blueprints) are to read "2,200 kcal a day, except in a certified shortage shared by all", so that the qualification is never hidden. Not changed now: the patch is not merged.

#### **6.7 Maintainer Decision D-30.5 (2026-11-16): Art. 9.1.4 Prevails in Transition Nodes**
**Option B.** The entry period is transitional. Art. 10.4.2 ("immediate residential usufruct" in Green Status) is not amended; Art. 9.1.4 prevails over it in transition nodes and lapses with the derogation. Rejected: A (amend Art. 10.4.2 for every basin: weakens a strong welcome promise where Art. 5.2 and OL-22 point 18 already deal with free riders, and Chapter X is near its ceiling); C (no text: leaves two readings). Civic enrolment is not in conflict: under SIM-QA-29 D-29.5 a person can be entered at once.

*Also fixed in the same revision:* the section 5.3 draft could be read as applying the entry period only when residence is drawn by lot. The revised text applies it to every new resident of a transition node, and the lot only when applicants exceed capacity (OL-23 point 2 already read so).

**Art. 9.1.4 (revised, replaces the draft of section 5.3):**
> 4. Admission to Transition Nodes: A transition node declares its capacity from its physical accounts (Article 3.1.6), checked by a panel drawn from outside it; members never decide on persons. Every new resident first spends an entry period of hospitality with shifts set by Organic Law; where applicants exceed capacity, residence is assigned by lot. Shelter at Tier 1 is never conditioned. Every arrival receives water, food, emergency care and shelter for no fewer than seven (7) nights. Beyond declared capacity the node first offers transfer to another transition node or allied community with room; only where none exists may it refer an arrival, accompanied, to a public service of the legacy jurisdiction that has confirmed it can receive them. In transition nodes this Article prevails over Articles 10.4.2 and 10.4.3; it lapses for each node when Article 10.4.4 can operate for it, and for all at the end of the transition.

*Red team.*
* **H-1, the whole article lapses together. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** After the lapse, a node is governed by Art. 10.4 as written (statuses on carrying-capacity telemetry, neighbours under 10.4.4, and the shared-shortage addition to 10.4.3, which does not lapse).
* **H-2, the entry period reaches members of other nodes. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** A person moving between transition nodes also passes it; the rule is the same for every origin (SIM-QA-23), and a member who contributes loses nothing but the wait.
* **H-3, keeping the derogation alive by denying that neighbours can receive. `NARROWED`.** The lapse condition of OL-23 point 9 is certified by a panel of three drawn from outside the node, not by the node (added to point 9). *Residual:* as RED-10 of section 5.2.

*Count:* Art. 9.1.4 140 → 149 (+9). Chapter IX 817 → **966 of 1,000** (34 left, within fifty words). Chapter X unchanged by this decision. Compression disclosure item 2 of 5.5 now reads: Art. 10.4.2 yields to Art. 9.1.4 in transition nodes only, until the lapse; **weakened** for transition nodes, decided.

#### **6.8 Maintainer Decision D-30.6 (2026-11-16): Seed-Node Network in the Roadmap**
**The network goes into the roadmap; publicity does not belong here.** Rules on publicity are outreach, which is kept outside this repository; RED-11 and F-5 are recorded as outside the scope of this docket.

*Finding behind the decision:* the roadmap places the inter-node mesh in Phase 3 (Years 7–15). Strategy 2 of section 4 has no network to use before then, and the model of 5.1 shows referrals doubling without it.

**Roadmap entry (draft; revised the same day by maintainer correction: the network exists before the seed nodes; not entered in `roadmap/roadmap.md` until the maintainer says so, together with SIM-QA-22 D-8):**
> **Before Phase 0: the Seed-Node Mutual Aid and Transfer Compact.** The compact is written and signed before any seed node acquires land, by the seed teams preparing nodes and by allied communities willing to take transfers under the same rules (community land trusts, cooperatives, intentional communities). Every seed node is founded inside the compact. The compact holds a common list of declared capacity, offers transport and a place to arrivals at a full node, and keeps a ledger of transfers in persons and rations so that no member only sends (OL-23 points 5 and 6). It is rehearsed before the first node opens: a dry run of a transfer between two members, alongside step 7 of the seed teams' next steps. Core hospitality (Art. 9.1.4) is owed to every arrival from the first day. The Phase 3 inter-node mesh (trade within a watershed) is unchanged.
* *Placement in the roadmap:* a step before Phase 0 and a new item in section 6 ("Immediate Next Steps for Seed Teams"), ahead of land acquisition.
* *Maintainer correction (2026-11-16):* the first draft made a node join the compact before taking residents beyond its founders, with at least two other nodes. Nothing requires waiting for nodes to exist; the compact comes first.

*Red team (one line each).* **N-1, a compact whose members are all full. `NARROWED`:** transfers go only to declared room; when all are full, referral and then the shared shortage apply. **N-2, the first node has no other node. `NARROWED`:** allied communities fill the list from the start; residual: a first node with no allies has no transfer route until a second member exists. **N-3, the compact as a cartel that turns people away together. `BLUE_HOLDS`:** capacity is certified per node by an outside panel (OL-23 point 1); the compact cannot lower it. **N-4, allies outside the commons apply their own admission rules. `NARROWED`:** a transfer is only offered and the person chooses; an ally that selects whom it takes still lowers the load, but the ledger counts only persons actually received. *Open (not drafted, for the maintainer):* Art. 9.1.4 offers transfer "to another transition node"; allied communities are not transition nodes. Either the article reads "another transition node or a community of the compact" (+6 words), or transfers to allies stay a roadmap practice outside the article.

#### **6.9 Maintainer Decision D-30.10 (2026-11-16)**
**Allied communities named in Art. 9.1.4.** In the revised text of 6.7, "transfer to another transition node with room" reads "transfer to another transition node or allied community with room" (+3 words; Art. 9.1.4 149 → 152; Chapter IX **969 of 1,000**). OL-23 point 5 defines an allied community: a community outside the commons that has signed the compact and keeps the ledger; it may keep its own admission rules (N-4).
*Red team.* **A-1, a "community" set up only to receive referrals cheaply. `NARROWED`:** an ally must have signed the compact and keep the ledger, and the person chooses; residual: the receiving side's conditions are not checked by an outside panel, unlike a node's capacity. **A-2, transfer to allies replaces building capacity. `NARROWED`:** the compact's ledger shows a node that only sends; settlement under point 6 applies to allies too.

#### **6.10 Maintainer Decision D-30.4 (2026-11-16)**
**OL-23 values accepted as provisional**, all `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED` until SIMONE challenge 1 (D-30.3) and field data from the first nodes: core hospitality seven nights, with stays for medical need and for a confirmed transfer (point 4); entry period eight weeks (point 2); capacity review every three months and after each harvest (point 1); certification within seventy-two hours (point 11); household stores beyond two weeks pooled in a shortage (point 12); the survival ration left to specialist review (point 13). Evidence for point 4: [`results`](../simone/benchmarks/results/sim_qa_30_seed_node_capacity_results.md) section 5 (synthetic): seven nights holds two years at three and ten arrivals a week; fourteen fails on day 461 at ten; thirty fails on day 686 at three and day 196 at ten. The entry period does not change the model results, because capacity binds before labour; its length is a human choice, not a physical one.

#### **6.11 Maintainer Decision D-30.3 (2026-11-16)**
**Option C.** SIMONE challenge 1 is run first internally, extending the existing agent model (Agents.jl, HEXACO-H, fatigue) with a sweep of the non-contributor share, the Art. 5.2.2 ladder and arrivals under draft Art. 9.1.4 / OL-23; results labelled synthetic and not externally reviewed; then queued for external review (SIMONE Task 8.1). Done in a separate work session, not in this docket. Recorded as Task 8.0 in [`simone/TODO.md`](../simone/TODO.md). It does not block the decisions of this docket; it may revise the OL-23 values and the residuals.

**Next action (revision 6):** none pending from the maintainer on this docket; SIMONE Task 8.0; roadmap entry of D-30.6 when the maintainer says so; Stage 3 (purple patch consolidated) after Task 8.0 or on maintainer instruction; entry of the D-30.6 draft in the roadmap when the maintainer decides.
