## **SIM-QA-31: AI and Robots as the Labour Engine**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 1 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`** with seven points together; **`RED_WINS`** on every hypothesis placed in Organic Law alone, on H2 and H5 as worded, and on two limits no text removes. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20: the founding question assumes machines do the work, and the canon has no chapter on them.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 5.1.4 (total automation of arduous, hazardous, repetitive and routine labour as the strategic goal, "through open robotics and automated machinery"; shifts "contract toward zero"), Art. 5.1.3 (multipliers "prioritizing robotic automation"), Art. 6.2.4 (machine-learning workloads as interruptible loads), Art. 7.1 (code is non-sovereign; 7.1.2 voids delegation of "biophysical resource rationing" and of coercive force to "autonomous algorithms, neural models, or artificial agents"; 7.1.4 restricts public automation to "verifiable oracle architectures devoid of subjective agency, legal personhood, or sovereign standing"), Art. 7.3.1 (every binary "running on public life-support infrastructure" reproducible bit for bit "from verified, human-readable source code"), Art. 7.4.2 (keys to hyperscale compute dispersed), Art. 7.5.3 (allocation routines shown "to resist functional comprehension by ordinary citizens" are void), Art. 8.1.3 (no robotics intended to injure), Art. 8.2.1 ("Automated machinery contains optical interlocks triggering kinetic shutdowns upon human approach"), Art. 9.3.1 (the External Trade Interface procures "specialized microelectronics").  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 2.2.3, 3.1.5, 3.3.4, 5.1.3, 5.1.4, 6.2.4, 7.1.2, 7.1.4, 7.3.1, 7.4.2, 7.5.3, 8.1.3, 8.2.1, 9.3.  
* **Kept apart from:** SIM-QA-20 section 8 (H7/H8, models as evidentiary aid in deliberation; this docket takes machines that work, not machines that brief a jury), SIM-QA-16 (how fast automation arrives against an ageing node), SIM-QA-09 (hostile machines), SIM-QA-22 D-17 (personhood of synthetic sentience, dropped 2026-11-16; not reopened here).

### **1. The Friction**
* Art. 5.1.4 promises that machines take over the work; no article says which machines may do it, who holds them, or under what limits.
* Read strictly, Arts. 7.1.4, 7.3.1 and 7.5.3 exclude trained models from public infrastructure: their weights are not human-readable source, are not reproduced bit for bit, and resist comprehension.
* Art. 8.2.1 stops every automated machine when a person approaches. A care robot, a surgical robot or a machine working beside a person cannot exist under it.
* Art. 7.1.2 voids the delegation of resource rationing to algorithms; Art. 3.1.5 allocates by input-output matrices. The line between computing an allocation and deciding it is not drawn.
* During the transition, robots and chips come from outside (Art. 9.3); the most capable models may stay with outside powers.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, strict reading:** a review jury (Art. 4.6) voids every learned model on public infrastructure under Arts. 7.1.4, 7.3.1 and 7.5.3 → the trajectory of Art. 5.1.4 cannot be met and the shifts of Art. 5.1.1 never contract.
* **RED-2, loose reading:** "oracle" is stretched to any model that "only recommends"; the people who sign follow it every time → deciding moves to the machine against Art. 7.1.2, with a human signature on top.
* **RED-3, the matrix as rationing:** the output of the solver of Art. 3.1.5 is applied as the allocation; a challenge under Art. 7.1.2 either stops planning or is ignored.
* **RED-4, the interlock:** Art. 8.2.1 is disabled case by case for care, surgery and shared work, with no rule on who may do it → the safety rule erodes by exception.
* **RED-5, capture by the holder:** whoever trains and hosts the models (a programming guild, a node with a fab or a data centre) holds the labour engine; Art. 7.4.2 disperses keys, not training or data.
* **RED-6, outside dependence:** capable models and chips stay with outside powers; the commons imports closed systems through the External Trade Interface against Art. 7.2, or goes without and stays on human shifts; an embargo (SIM-QA-10) stops the supply.
* **RED-7, published is not reproducible:** weights are published in full and still cannot be rebuilt bit for bit from source → Art. 7.3.3 de-authorises them.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, classes by function:** a scale from fixed-program machines to learned models, stating what each class may do; the control loop of life support stays deterministic and reproducible (Art. 7.3.1); learned models work outside it.
* **H2, the line between computing and deciding:** a machine computes options and their physical consequences; an allocation takes effect only under a rule or decision adopted by a drawn body, which a person can trace and reverse.
* **H3, models as commons:** training data, code, weights and the training record are public; training compute is held like Tier 2-A capital under dispersed keys; for weights, bit-for-bit reproduction is replaced by a published training record and independent re-evaluation (to be tested against Art. 7.3.1).
* **H4, certification near people:** Art. 8.2.1 stays the default; a class of machine may work near people only after certification by a panel drawn from outside, under the evidence rules of SIM-QA-20, with limits of force and speed fixed in Organic Law.
* **H5, transition supply:** a stated rule on what may be imported through the External Trade Interface (open designs; no closed software on public infrastructure), and a stated fallback when it is not available: the shifts of Art. 5.1 remain.

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team H1 to H5 against RED-1 to RED-7~~ (done, section 4); decide the chapter that would carry any text under instructions 10 and 10a (options in section 4.5); no quantitative claim without a script (instruction 7).

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist; corrects two lines of section 1)*
* **Art. 7.5.3 does not reach working machines.** By its own words it voids an "algorithmic distribution formula, allocation routine, or governance protocol". A vision model that guides a harvester is none of these. What it does bar is a learned model used as the allocation routine itself. Section 1 read it wider than the text.
* **Art. 7.3.1 reaches "public life-support infrastructure", a term the canon never defines.** Food and water are Tier 1 (Art. 3.3.1), so a strict jury counts a harvester or a dosing pump. The breadth of the bar depends on one undefined term.
* **Art. 7.1.4 uses "oracle" and "verifiable" without defining either.** A robot that plans its own motions inside an assigned task has operational autonomy; whether that is "subjective agency" is open. A learned model is not verifiable in the sense a compiled program is.
* **Art. 7.2.1 lists its domains** (public administration, infrastructure telemetry, manufacturing commons, microgrids, communication backbones). Field machines and care machines are not on the list. The canon is strict in Art. 7.3.1 and has a gap in Art. 7.2.1 for the same machine (RED-14).
* **Organic Law cannot settle any of this alone.** Under the ratchet rule (instruction 10) Organic Law may strengthen a guarantee and never weaken it. A definition that lets learned models onto life-support infrastructure, or lets a machine keep moving near a person, narrows Arts. 7.3.1, 7.1.4 and 8.2.1. Each needs canon text and a line in the compression disclosure (section 4.6).

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Recorded Vectors**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1 and RED-2 are both open at once. The same model is void before one jury and unlimited before another, and Art. 4.6.3 lets a jury only void, never draw the line.
* **RED-1, strict reading. H1 in Organic Law only: `RED_WINS`** (section 4.1, ratchet). **H1 as canon text: `NARROWED`.** H1 says learned models work "outside" the control loop, and a model that steers an arm or sets a dose is inside it. The form that holds: a learned component may command a machine only through a fixed, reproducible supervisor that bounds what it can do (force, speed, range of settings) and that meets Art. 7.3.1 in full; and every life-support function keeps running with the learned component switched off, which Art. 6.5.3 already requires for electronics in general. *Residuals:* the supervisor bounds physical harm, not the quality of the work; a model can do poor work inside its bounds for a long time. Outputs that are not physical (schedules, plans, forecasts) have no such bound and fall under RED-2.
* **RED-7, published is not reproducible. H3 alone: `RED_WINS`. H1 + H3: `NARROWED`.** Rebuilding large weights bit for bit across independent toolchains and processors (Art. 7.3.2) is not practical today, and "by any citizen node" (Art. 7.3.3) would mean repeating the whole training cost. More important, reproducibility would not deliver the purpose of Art. 7.3.1: a model trained reproducibly on poisoned data still carries the backdoor. Published research describes backdoors in learned models that testing from outside cannot find (`PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`, to check against primary sources). So the "independent re-evaluation" of H3 is weaker than what it replaces. The defence has to be the supervisor of H1, not the evaluation. *Open option, not tested:* a class of small models that two independent nodes do retrain bit for bit, allowed closer to life support; no size is proposed without a script.
* **RED-2, loose reading. H2 as worded: `RED_WINS`. Revised: `NARROWED`.** H2 lets an allocation take effect "under a rule or decision adopted by a drawn body". A body that adopts the rule "apply the solver's output" satisfies it, and the delegation is complete. Revised form: the drawn body adopts the objective, the priority order and the tie-break; the allocation is then computed by an open, deterministic routine that anyone can re-run from published inputs and get the same result; a learned model is never the allocation routine (Art. 7.5.3 as read in 4.1). *Residual, not removed by any text:* people who sign what the machine proposes every time. Guards reduce it (sampled independent re-computation, in the spirit of Art. 3.4.5); none ends it.
* **RED-3, the matrix as rationing. `NARROWED`.** The Leontief solver is deterministic and open, so it can meet Arts. 7.3.1 and 7.5. Where a constraint binds, the objective decides who receives less, and that is rationing. The tier order is in Art. 3.3; inside a tier the rule in a shortage must be adopted in advance (SIM-QA-30 D-30.7 has one: in proportion to need). A solver also picks arbitrarily among equal solutions, so the tie-break must be published. One case no hypothesis covers: a microgrid sheds load in milliseconds, which is rationing no person can decide in time. Art. 7.1.2 has a sentence for pre-authorised interception under Art. 8.1.4 and none for a pre-adopted dispatch rule. *Needs canon text* (one sentence in Art. 7.1.2).
* **RED-4, the interlock. H4 in Organic Law only: `RED_WINS`** (it would weaken the categorical sentence of Art. 8.2.1). **H4 as canon text: `NARROWED`.** *Guards:* certification per class of machine and per setting, so a class certified for care is not moved to a workshop; rival panels under SIM-QA-20; limits of force and speed in Organic Law; the stop of Art. 8.2.1 remains the rule for every machine not certified. *Residuals:* the limits need a specialist (industrial practice has contact limits for machines that share a workspace with people; not checked here); a lay panel certifying a class depends on the evidence rules of SIM-QA-20, which are not merged.
* **RED-5, capture by the holder. `NARROWED`.** Art. 7.4.2 already disperses administrative access to hyperscale compute. Not covered: who chooses training data and objectives, and who pushes an update to every machine at once. *Guards:* a model release is a software release under Art. 7.6.3 (60% at the bioregion); every node may keep running the previous version; weights are public, so any node with compute can run them, and only training is concentrated. *Residual:* few people can train or audit a model. That is the "software priesthood" of Art. 7.5.1 and it goes to SIM-QA-32 (guilds), not solved here.
* **RED-6, outside dependence. H5: `NARROWED` on the rule; `RED_WINS` on what no rule reaches.** (a) Art. 7.2.2 requires firmware in every microprocessor to be free of proprietary locks. Almost no chip that can be bought today meets it, with or without AI. This is an existing gap in the canon, wider than this docket; recorded for SIM-QA-40 (which articles are in force in each phase). (b) Models published from outside with weights and no training record fail H3. They can be admitted only behind the supervisor of H1, never in a role without one. (c) If the most capable systems stay closed and outside, the commons stays on human shifts while others automate. Art. 5.1.4 is a goal, not a guarantee, and no text changes that.

#### **4.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-8, the hollow fallback (Thermodynamic architect). `RED_WINS` under H5 as worded.** H5 says that without machines "the shifts of Art. 5.1 remain". After years of contraction the rosters are empty and the skills are gone; under an embargo (SIM-QA-10) or an ageing node (SIM-QA-16) the fallback may not be staffable. *Guard to test:* shifts contract toward zero (Art. 5.1.4) but never below a practice floor that keeps each Tier 1 task doable by hand, sized in Organic Law. Size `NOT_RUN`.
* **RED-9, machines against the autarky audit (Thermodynamic architect). `RED_WINS` under current text.** Art. 3.1.6 requires every sector supplying Tier 1 to meet demand "from local resources and installed capacity alone, with external inputs at zero". Robots and chips come through the External Trade Interface (Art. 9.3.1) and wear out. A node whose harvest depends on imported machines fails the audit, or the audit quietly ignores the dependence. *Guard to test:* the audit counts Tier 1 as met by machines only as far as the node can maintain them from local resources and a stated stock of spares, and otherwise by rosters that are actually staffed. This ties the contraction of shifts to local repairability (Art. 6.3). It also shows a conflict inside Art. 6.2.4: machine-learning workloads are interruptible buffers, but inference for a machine doing Tier 1 work cannot be interrupted in a deficit; that load has to be counted as Tier 1 or Tier 2. Labour hours saved against exergy and parts consumed: `NOT_RUN`, a SIMONE question.
* **RED-10, machine-only care. `RED_WINS` under H4 as worded.** A node saves care hours by leaving people who cannot refuse (the very old, people with dementia, small children) to machines. H4 certifies the machine and says nothing about the person. *Guard to test:* a machine touches or attends a person only with that person's revocable consent, and no one is left to machine care alone. Art. 5.1.4 already names caregiving as what people are freed for. Close to SIM-QA-22 D-4 (right to remain unaugmented) and D-5 (care).
* **RED-11, the robot as a sensor (Cyberneticist). `NARROWED`, and a conflict between H3 and SIM-QA-41.** A care or household machine sees and hears people at home, against Arts. 3.4.4, 8.2.2 and 1.2.2 if anything is kept or sent. *Guards:* processing on the machine; nothing leaves it; nothing is kept beyond the task (the purpose rule of SIM-QA-41). *Conflict:* H3 makes training data public; data from dwellings and bodies cannot be. H3 has to exclude personal data from training unless the person consents, and published data that was once personal cannot be recalled.
* **RED-12, publication arms the adversary. Accepted residual, no verdict.** Public weights for manipulation and navigation are also available to outside powers and to hostile machines (SIM-QA-09). Arts. 1.5 and 7.2 chose openness. Recorded so the choice is visible.
* **RED-13, no one answers for a machine. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Arts. 8.3 and 8.4 deal with acts of persons. When a machine injures someone, no article names who owes reparation. *Guard to test:* every working machine has a named stewarding body, answerable under the reparative rule of Art. 8.3. Depends on SIM-QA-32 (what a guild is) and SIM-QA-33 (forums).
* **RED-14, the gap in Art. 7.2.1. `RED_WINS` under current text.** A closed imported machine in a field or a care home is outside the listed domains (section 4.1). H5 closes it only if its rule covers every machine in common use, not only "public infrastructure".
* **RED-15, private machines as capital. Open, cross-docket.** Art. 2.2.1 makes "private tools of craft" and "personal computational interfaces" inviolable. A person with several robots that produce for others holds private means of production; Art. 5.1.2 de-commodifies human effort and does not mention machine output. The redundancy clause of Art. 2.2.1 helps in part. Goes with SIM-QA-34 point 3 (gifts, swaps, barter).
* **RED-16, personal agents in civic procedures. Open, cross-docket.** Art. 7.1.4 restricts public automation only. A person's own model can draft and file petitions at volume (SIM-QA-11) or advise a juror in private (SIM-QA-20 H7/H8). Proof of personhood (Art. 7.4.4) shows a person exists, not who wrote the filing. Not treated here.
* **RED-17, who declares a task automated. `NARROWED`.** A guild can over-claim (shifts cut while the machine does not deliver) or under-claim (multipliers of Art. 5.1.3 kept). *Guard:* roster hours follow measured machine output in the public ledgers (Art. 3.6.1). Unequal automation between basins goes to SIM-QA-38.

#### **4.4 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the seven points below taken together; **`RED_WINS`** on every hypothesis placed in Organic Law alone, on H2 and H5 as worded, and on the two limits no text removes (capable systems that stay outside; people who sign whatever the machine proposes).
1. A learned component acts on the physical world only through a fixed, reproducible supervisor that bounds it; life support runs without it (H1 revised).
2. A drawn body adopts objectives, priorities and tie-breaks; allocations are computed by an open, deterministic, re-runnable routine; a learned model is never the allocation routine; a pre-adopted dispatch rule is not delegation (H2 revised).
3. Models used in common are commons: code, weights and training record public; personal data never in training without consent; releases by the vote of Art. 7.6.3; each node may hold a version back (H3 revised).
4. The stop on human approach stays the rule; a class of machine may work near people only after outside certification for a stated setting; a machine attends a person only with consent, and never as the only care (H4 revised).
5. Every machine in common use falls under the open-software rule, not only the domains listed in Art. 7.2.1; imported models without a training record work only behind the supervisor (H5 revised).
6. The Tier 1 audit counts machines only as far as they are locally maintainable; shifts keep a practice floor (new, RED-8, RED-9).
7. Every working machine has a named steward that answers for harm (new, RED-13).

#### **4.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a; count: tokens containing a letter or a digit, headings included; canon as of 2026-11-20)*
Points 1 to 5 and 7 change a prohibition, a right or who answers, so the placement test keeps their core in the Constitution; numbers, classes, limits and procedures go to Organic Law. No text is drafted, so no word count of the patch exists yet. Room by chapter:

| Chapter | Canon today | With pending dockets | Left of 1,000 |

| V | 855 | about 956 to 986 (SIM-QA-22 language paragraph; that docket counted 885 before it, recount needed) | 14 to 44, **within fifty** |
| VI | 765 | 765 (no pending text found) | 235 |
| VII | 712 | 975 (SIM-QA-22 D-6, D-7; SIM-QA-29 Art. 7.4.5) | 25, **within fifty** |
| VIII | 862 | 902 (SIM-QA-22 D-3) | 98 |

* **O-1:** a new article in Chapter VI (machines as infrastructure, beside Art. 6.3), with short amendments in Arts. 7.1.2, 7.1.4, 7.3.1 and 8.2.1 that point to it. Fits the present ceilings if the amendments in Chapter VII stay under 25 words.
* **O-2:** the text in Chapter VII, where the bars are. Needs a ceiling decision first (instruction 10a point 4).
* **O-3:** a twelfth chapter. The canon holds 9,264 words today with its title block (**corrected 2026-11-20**; first stated as 9,219) against a total ceiling of 11,500, which the text accepted in principle already passes ([`word_recount_2026-11-20.md`](word_recount_2026-11-20.md)); the pending total has not been recounted.

#### **4.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8; points the surviving rule would weaken; each needs a maintainer decision before any draft)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "every executable binary running on public life-support infrastructure" is reproducible bit for bit from human-readable source | Art. 7.3.1 | learned components excepted when bounded by a supervisor that does meet the rule | **weakened** |
| 2 | binaries that cannot be reproduced "by any citizen node" are de-authorised | Art. 7.3.3 | same exception | **weakened** |
| 3 | public automation "restricted to verifiable oracle architectures" | Art. 7.1.4 | bounded task autonomy allowed; no agency in law, no personhood, no standing kept | **weakened or clarified**, depending on the reading of "oracle" |
| 4 | automated machinery stops "upon human approach" | Art. 8.2.1 | certified classes excepted in stated settings | **weakened** |
| 5 | shifts "contract toward zero" | Art. 5.1.4 | a practice floor remains | **qualified** ("toward" is kept) |

#### **4.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* Nothing here is measured. The practice floor, the stock of spares, the exergy cost of training and inference and the hours machines save are all `NOT_RUN`.
* The claim about backdoors that testing cannot find is from memory of published work and is unverified.
* The supervisor bounds harm to bodies and equipment. It does not bound bad plans, bad forecasts or slow sabotage.
* Human sign-off stays thin wherever the machine is usually right.
* Three guards rest on dockets not yet red-teamed or merged: SIM-QA-32 (guilds), SIM-QA-33 (forums), SIM-QA-41 (personal data); one on SIM-QA-20 (evidence rules).
* No robotics, machine-safety or machine-learning specialist has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-31.1 to D-31.6):**
* **D-31.1:** accept the seven points of section 4.4 as the direction, or change them.
* **D-31.2:** the five lines of section 4.6, each: accept the weakening, or keep the article as it stands (then learned models stay off life support, or machines stay away from people).
* **D-31.3:** placement: O-1, O-2 or O-3.
* **D-31.4:** machine-only care (RED-10): a rule in this docket, or left to the care text of SIM-QA-22 D-5.
* **D-31.5:** private machines (RED-15) and personal agents (RED-16): open a docket, or attach them to SIM-QA-34 and SIM-QA-20.
* **D-31.6:** the chip firmware gap of Art. 7.2.2 (RED-6 a): pass to SIM-QA-40.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 3):** no machine of restraint (SIM-QA-37 D-37.5). Point 4 of section 4.4 (a machine attends a person only with consent) needs no exception. This does not decide D-31.1.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 6):** point 3 of section 4.4 stays as worded on consent ("personal data never in training without consent") and gains the conditions recorded in SIM-QA-41: anonymised first, a minimum cell, rare cases pooled across the federation or used with a second consent, consent never a condition of care. The training record is public in its anonymised form. This does not decide D-31.1.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 11; SIM-QA-38 D-38.6):** imports of machines while a node is being built are a certified exception with a plan to end them, not a failed audit; a machine counts in the audit only as far as it is locally maintainable (point 6 of section 4.4, confirmed on this point). This does not decide D-31.1.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 12, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** who answers for harm, in three layers. *Reparation:* the commons repairs at once, whoever is at fault (Art. 8.3.3). *The steward:* every working machine and every mandate has a named steward, which is a body or a guild, never one person; it answers by review of the facts and by loss of the mandate or of the machine's certification. *The person:* answers only for their own act on the closed list of SIM-QA-33; never for a vote, never for a machine's fault while following the adopted procedure. The red team's pick, adopted. Point 7 of section 4.4 is settled in this form. *Residual accepted:* a diffuse fault (a bad adopted rule, followed correctly) leaves the harm repaired and nobody answering. This does not decide the other points of D-31.1. Direction only; no wording.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-31.1 to D-31.6; then article text and Organic Law draft as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass on the wording; a SIMONE task for RED-8 and RED-9 before any number; primary-source check on model backdoors.

## **SIM-QA-32: Guilds — Who Executes Day to Day**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 2 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`** with eight points together; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H1, H3 and H5 as worded, on O-1 as worded, and on three residuals no text removes. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20. Citation check: guilds are also named in Arts. 5.3.2 and 7.4.2, added to the list given at opening.  
* **Closest current canon:** guilds are named and never defined: Art. 2.2.1 ("the local tool guild"), Art. 2.2.3 (commons "administered by municipal sortition councils and dedicated operational guilds"; Tier 2-A facilities "entrusted to specialized worker guilds under sortition assembly oversight"), Art. 5.3.2 ("Guild Apprenticeship & Mastery"), Art. 5.5.4 ("No medical guild or assembly may establish a dogmatic monopoly over clinical science"), Art. 7.4.1 (no "programming guild" holds root keys), Art. 7.4.2 ("guild administrative capture"), Art. 7.5.2 (duties of "Technical guilds"), Art. 8.1.2 (Civic Peace Guilds; Civic Rescue & Fire Guilds). Art. 4.3.4 limits assembly staff only. Art. 5.1 distributes rotational chores, Art. 5.6 exempts carers; neither covers long skilled work.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 2.2.1, 2.2.3, 4.1.1, 4.3.4, 5.1, 5.3.2, 5.5.4, 5.6, 7.4.1, 7.4.2, 7.5.2, 8.1.2.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-05 (rescue guilds, incident command), SIM-QA-06 (open question 2, certification in Tier 2-A facilities), SIM-QA-20 (open practitioner registers; no gatekeeping), SIM-QA-22 section 8.11 (D-15 open question, who stops a dangerous practitioner, options O-1 to O-3: taken over here), [`restore_and_elaborate.md`](restore_and_elaborate.md) R-IV-28 (does "sovereign" in Art. 4.1.1 leave administrative authority to guilds).

### **1. The Friction**
* Drawn bodies decide and rotate; somebody runs the hospital, the grid and the fab every day. The canon calls them guilds and says nothing on entry, coordination, removal or oversight.
* Art. 4.3.4 guards against a shadow bureaucracy of assembly staff. Nothing equivalent guards against permanent specialists facing a council that changes every six months (Art. 4.2.2).
* Long skilled work (surgeon, engineer) is bound by nothing before automation: Art. 5.1 covers chores, Art. 5.1.2 bars contracts for labour, Art. 5.6 covers carers.
* A plan that runs twenty years outlasts every mandate; nobody is named to keep it.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, specialists against rotating overseers:** the guild holds the knowledge and the continuity; each new council depends on it → the shadow government that Art. 4.3.4 forbids for staff, by another route.
* **RED-2, closed entry:** a guild chooses its own members and passes places to kin → the hereditary caste Art. 5.1.1 sets out to end.
* **RED-3, no removal:** an incompetent or abusive coordinator stays; no body is named to remove.
* **RED-4, the gap between sessions:** daily decisions with public effect are taken while no drawn body sits.
* **RED-5, skilled work unbound:** the only surgeon leaves or withholds work → no instrument secures continuity; the opposite failure is a binding that becomes compelled labour against Arts. 1.3.3 and 5.2.1.
* **RED-6, the dangerous practitioner:** harm by an unsafe surgeon, engineer or pilot has no stated stop (SIM-QA-22 D-15).
* **RED-7, the keeper of the plan:** the guild that keeps a long plan decides what the plan means → custody becomes ownership.
* **RED-8, over-correction:** rotating every post destroys skill; licensing entry contradicts the no-gatekeeping rule of SIM-QA-20.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, definition:** a guild is an open working association that holds a commons facility in usufruct under a written mandate from the competent drawn body; entry is open through apprenticeship (Art. 5.3.2); nobody is excluded except on facts of harm.
* **H2, coordination:** coordinators are drawn or rotated among qualified members for a limited term with a hiatus, on the model of Art. 4.3.3.
* **H3, standing oversight:** a small drawn panel sits with each guild between sessions, sees the public work log, and may suspend a decision until the council meets.
* **H4, removal and stop:** removal by the drawn body, on facts; for the dangerous practitioner, options O-1 and O-3 of SIM-QA-22 section 8.11.
* **H5, skilled service:** voluntary, time-limited service commitments counted as reciprocity like care (Art. 5.6.1), never enforced through Tier 1.
* **H6, custody of plans:** a plan adopted at 60% (Art. 4.5.2) is kept in the public register; the guild executes it; each new assembly confirms or amends it; no guild veto.

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team H1 to H6 against RED-1 to RED-8~~ (done, section 4); align with the registers of SIM-QA-20; decide whether one definition serves every guild or the public-safety guilds of Art. 8.1.2 need their own (section 4.3, RED-13).

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 2.2.3 gives guilds a title and no terms.** Commons are "administered by municipal sortition councils and dedicated operational guilds"; Tier 2-A facilities are "entrusted to specialized worker guilds under sortition assembly oversight". A guild can read this as a constitutional entrustment of its own, which no council may take back.
* **Art. 4.1.1 claims "sovereign legislative, deliberative, and constitutional authority" for drawn bodies.** Administrative authority is not on the list (R-IV-28). Read with Art. 2.2.3, the text leaves room for a guild to hold administrative authority in its own right.
* **Art. 2.2.1 already has a guild that judges.** "Negligent damage is restored through guided maintenance chore hours under the local tool guild": the guild finds the negligence and sets the hours, outside Chapter VIII.
* **Oversight is named four times and never described** (Arts. 2.2.3, 3.6.1, 4.2.2, 6.6.1). No article says what an overseeing body may ask for, stop or undo.
* **Terms.** The canon uses "guild", "worker guild", "operational guild", "technical guild", "medical guild" and, in Art. 5.3.2, "collectives" for what seems one thing. Art. 4.3.4 puts staff on "contracts", which Art. 5.1.2 bars for labour. Both go to the plain-language pass.

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Recorded Vectors**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1, RED-2 and RED-3 are all open, and Art. 2.2.3 supports the guild's side of each (section 4.1).
* **RED-2, closed entry. H1 as worded: `RED_WINS` on training places; revised: `NARROWED`.** H1 opens entry "through apprenticeship", and the mentors decide whom they teach and when a contribution counts (Art. 5.3.2, "peer-reviewed"). The gate moves to the apprenticeship. Training places are also finite: a surgical team can take few learners, and kin get them. *Revised:* membership is open; where applicants exceed training places, places go by lot among those who meet published prerequisites; a dangerous task (not membership) requires a practical demonstration under the facility's safety rules, which apply to everyone (option O-3 of SIM-QA-22 section 8.11). *Residuals:* (a) who writes the prerequisites and the demonstration. If the guild writes them, the gate is back; if the council does, lay people write surgical criteria and depend on the rival panels of SIM-QA-20, not merged. (b) A competence test per dangerous task brings back part of what the maintainer dropped on 2026-11-16 (D-15, peer certification in high-consequence fields). Section 8.11 listed O-3 as consistent with that drop; the overlap is real and is for the maintainer (D-32.3).
* **RED-1, specialists against rotating overseers. H3 as worded: `RED_WINS`; revised: `NARROWED`.** Three attacks on the standing panel. (a) Three lay people sitting permanently with hospital staff learn from the staff and end by speaking for them, the same drift Art. 4.3.4 fears. (b) "May suspend a decision" lets a lay panel stop a clinical or grid operation in progress. (c) One panel of three per guild, all year, multiplies drawn seats; SIM-QA-18 already shows small nodes cannot fill the seats they have. Load `NOT_RUN`. *Revised:* oversight by record and by outside peers. The guild keeps a public work log against the measures written in its mandate (Art. 3.6.1 already makes ledgers public); at intervals, practitioners of the same trade drawn by lot from other watersheds audit it and report to the drawn body in plain language (the device of Art. 3.4.5, applied to work instead of sensors); a suspension reaches only acts outside the mandate, never an operation in progress. *Residual, not removed:* a council that changes every six months (Art. 4.2.2) faces people who stay for decades. Memory sits with the guild. Outside peers reduce the gap in knowledge; nothing closes the gap in continuity.
* **RED-4, the gap between sessions. `NARROWED`.** The canon has a model in Art. 8.1.2: incident command acts at once, "strictly bounded to physical life-safety", and an evacuation beyond twenty-four hours needs re-authorisation. Generalised: inside its mandate a guild acts without asking; outside it, only for immediate life-safety, logged, for a short fixed time, then ratified or ended by the drawn body. *Residual:* the line between inside and outside the mandate needs a definition of the kind drafted for small nodes (SIM-QA-21, OL-10, "internal matter").
* **RED-3, no removal. H2 + H4: `NARROWED`.** *On H2:* "qualified members" needs someone to say who is qualified (same gate as RED-2); the hiatus of Art. 4.3.3 (twice the term) cannot be filled in a guild of four, so it must scale with size; and rotation ends formal entrenchment only, since the senior surgeon still leads whoever holds the post. *On H4:* a council can remove a coordinator for a correct and unpopular decision (a refused unsafe request, a triage order). *Guards:* removal only on stated facts (harm, breach of the mandate, a falsified log), after a hearing, with appeal to a cross-watershed panel of fifteen as in Art. 2.6.2; removal from coordination is not exclusion from the practice.
* **RED-6, the dangerous practitioner. O-1 + O-3 as worded: `RED_WINS` on one point; revised: `NARROWED`.** O-1 acts "on a complaint backed by evidence of harm". That waits for the first victim; near misses and a failed demonstration trigger nothing. The inquiry it points to (Art. 8.4) is limited by Art. 8.4.4 to assault, somatic violation, enclosure and poisoning, and negligent harm is not clearly inside (SIM-QA-33). *Revised:* the interim stop rests on facts of harm or of demonstrated danger (including a failed demonstration under the facility rules), is ordered by a panel of three drawn from outside, has a short ceiling, and is followed by an inquiry in a forum that SIM-QA-33 must name. *Residuals:* complaints used against a rival (a form check and the outside panel reduce this); practice outside commons facilities is not reached.
* **RED-5, skilled work unbound. H5: `RED_WINS` as worded on two points; `NARROWED` on continuity; one physical residual.** (a) H5 does not secure continuity. A voluntary commitment that is never enforced leaves the only surgeon free to go, and Art. 8.6.3 guarantees the exit. No text can do more without compelled labour. The remedy is redundancy, not binding: the mandate requires the guild to train successors and to leave no task with a single holder; nodes cover each other under Art. 10.3; the Tier 1 audit of Art. 3.1.6 ("including shelter and healthcare") counts trained people, not only equipment. *Physical residual:* a node of sixty cannot hold two surgeons; it depends on the network. (b) "Counted as reciprocity like care" gives guild members the full exemption of Art. 5.6.1. The pleasant, skilled work is then exempt and the sanitation shifts fall on everyone else: the caste Art. 5.1.1 sets out to end, by another door. Options for the maintainer in D-32.4. (c) *New:* a guild that withholds work together gains leverage over the council that oversees it. Open entry and the power to give the mandate to another group reduce this where skill is common; where skill is scarce they do not.
* **RED-7, the keeper of the plan. H6: `NARROWED`.** "Each new assembly confirms or amends" reopens a twenty-year plan every six months at the council tier. *Revised:* a plan adopted at 60% stands until amended at the same threshold; no confirmation is needed. The guild executes and logs every deviation; a dispute over what the plan means goes to the drawn body. Two further attacks: the guild that drafts the plan sets the agenda, which Art. 4.3.4 forbids to staff (guard: a plan is a complex proposal and goes through rival panels, Art. 4.4.2); and knowledge that is not in the register stays with the builders (guard: the plain-language duty of Art. 7.5.2, which today covers algorithms only, extended to plans). *Residual:* tacit knowledge.
* **RED-8, over-correction. `NARROWED`.** Coordination rotates, practice does not. Membership needs no licence; a dangerous task needs a demonstration. The tension with D-15 is the one stated under RED-2.

#### **4.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-9, members overseeing their own guild. `NARROWED`.** A council of fifteen drawn in a node will seat members of the guild it oversees; in a small node most adults belong to some guild. A guild is also an association under Art. 4.1.3 and may not instruct them, which no one can observe. *Guard:* a drawn member takes no part in a decision on the mandate of their own guild; where too few remain, a panel of three drawn from outside decides (the device of SIM-QA-19 and SIM-QA-21).
* **RED-10, the guild that allocates and judges. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Whoever runs a facility controls access to it: fab-lab hours, the order of patients, the tool library. Art. 2.2.1 lets the tool guild find negligence and impose hours (section 4.1). *Guard to test:* a guild applies access rules adopted by the drawn body and never its own (the rules themselves belong to SIM-QA-34); a disputed finding against a person goes to the Local Mediation Panel.
* **RED-11, federations of guilds. `NARROWED`.** Guilds of one trade will link across nodes. With the knowledge on their side, their common standards become rules that no drawn body adopted. *Guard:* a standard binds only when adopted by the competent assembly (Art. 7.6.2 already says so for firmware); guild networks advise. The same networks supply the outside peers of RED-1, so they cannot be barred.
* **RED-12, the guild of a unique facility. `RED_WINS`, residual.** A lithography fab or a chemical complex (Tier 2-A, Art. 2.2.3) may be the only one of its kind in a continent. There are no outside peers to audit it and no second group to take the mandate. *Partial guards:* dispersed keys (Art. 7.4.2), public records, apprentices drawn from several basins. Same concentration as SIM-QA-31 RED-5.
* **RED-13, one definition or two. Answer to the opening question.** One general definition can serve every guild. The Peace and the Rescue & Fire Guilds (Art. 8.1.2) keep the extra bounds they already have, and what they may do with force is SIM-QA-37 (D-37.1 point 1). Nothing in the general rule should be read as widening Art. 8.1.2.
* **RED-14, who answers for the guild's harm. Open, cross-docket.** SIM-QA-31 point 7 makes a "named steward" answer for a machine. If the steward is a guild, the reparative rule of Art. 8.3 meets a collective. Whether the guild, its coordinator or the member answers goes to SIM-QA-33.

#### **4.4 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the eight points below taken together; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H1, H3 and H5 as worded, on O-1 as worded, and on three residuals no text removes (the continuity gap between a rotating council and permanent specialists; the scarce specialist in a small node; the guild of a unique facility).
1. A guild is an open working association that carries out a written, public mandate of the competent drawn body for a commons facility or service. It holds no authority of its own (this settles R-IV-28). The mandate has a term and can be given to another group on stated grounds.
2. Membership is open. Training places go by lot when applicants exceed them. A dangerous task requires a demonstration under safety rules that apply to everyone.
3. Coordinators serve a limited term with a hiatus scaled to the guild's size. The drawn body removes them on stated facts, after a hearing, with an outside appeal.
4. Oversight works by a public work log and by periodic audit from outside peers drawn by lot. Drawn members step aside on their own guild.
5. A guild acts freely inside its mandate; outside it, only for immediate life-safety, logged and time-limited.
6. A guild never allocates by its own rules and never judges a person.
7. An interim stop on a practitioner is ordered by a panel of three drawn from outside, on facts of harm or demonstrated danger, with a short ceiling.
8. Continuity comes from redundancy: successors trained, no task with a single holder, trained people counted in the Tier 1 audit. Service is never bound. Plans stand until amended at the threshold that adopted them.

#### **4.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a; same counting method as SIM-QA-31 section 4.5)*
Points 1, 3, 6 and 7 and the open entry of point 2 concern the powers of bodies or a person's rights, so the placement test keeps their core in the Constitution. Terms, intervals, the lot, the log and the audit procedure go to Organic Law. No text is drafted. Room by chapter:

| Chapter | Canon today | Pending | Left |
| :--- | ---: | :--- | ---: |
| II | 869 | SIM-QA-21 Y-9/Y-10 wording for Arts. 2.6.1 and 2.6.2, not recounted here | about 130 of 1,000 |
| IV | 926 | 2,136 of 2,150 with the accepted dockets (SIM-QA-20, section 11.8) | 14, **within fifty** |
| V | 855 | about 956 to 986 | 14 to 44, **within fifty** |

* **O-1:** a new article in Chapter II, after Art. 2.2.3 where the entrustment is made. Room exists if the text stays near one hundred words.
* **O-2:** Chapter IV, beside Art. 4.3.4. Needs a ceiling decision first.
* **O-3:** Chapter V, with labour. Same.

#### **4.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | Tier 2-A stewardship "entrusted to specialized worker guilds under sortition assembly oversight" | Art. 2.2.3 | the entrustment becomes a mandate with a term, which the drawn body may give to another group | **qualified** (the guild's position is weaker; the commons' is stronger) |
| 2 | negligent damage restored "under the local tool guild" | Art. 2.2.1 | a disputed finding goes to the Local Mediation Panel | **qualified** |
| 3 | recorded drop D-15 (no peer certification or revocation) | board, Recorded Drops, 2026-11-16 | a demonstration per dangerous task and an interim stop return part of it in another form | **not a canon point; touches a recorded maintainer decision** |

#### **4.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* Nothing is measured: the seat load of oversight, the number of outside peers a trade can supply, the redundancy a small node can hold.
* Four guards rest on other dockets: SIM-QA-20 (rival panels, not merged), SIM-QA-33 (forum for negligent harm), SIM-QA-34 (access rules), SIM-QA-37 (force).
* Outside peers of one trade share its habits; an audit by peers can miss what the whole trade gets wrong.
* Informal leadership is untouched by any rule on terms.
* No one with experience of running a hospital, a utility or a cooperative has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-32.1 to D-32.6):**
* **D-32.1:** accept the eight points of section 4.4 as the direction, or change them.
* **D-32.2:** R-IV-28: confirm that guilds hold no authority of their own (point 1).
* **D-32.3:** a demonstration per dangerous task and the interim stop, against the D-15 drop: accept as different in kind, or reopen D-15.
* **D-32.4:** how guild work counts against rotational shifts: (a) full exemption as for carers; (b) hour for hour, with a minimum of rotational shifts kept for every able adult; (c) no credit. The red team supports (b).
* **D-32.5:** oversight by record and outside peers in place of the standing panel of H3, or both.
* **D-32.6:** placement: O-1, O-2 or O-3.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-32.3; conflicts batch, conflict 4, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** **different in kind; the recorded drop D-15 stands** (no peer certification or revocation). A dangerous task requires a practical demonstration, the same for everyone, tied to the task and not to a title; the criteria are adopted by the drawn body on rival evidence (SIM-QA-20), never written by the guild alone; an interim stop is ordered by a panel of three drawn from outside, on facts, with a short ceiling. The red team's pick, adopted. *Residual accepted:* lay bodies adopt the criteria and depend on the rival panels of SIM-QA-20, not merged. This settles points 2 and 7 of section 4.4 on this conflict only; it does not decide D-32.1. Direction only; no wording.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 12):** a guild is the named steward of its mandate and of the machines it works; it answers as a group, by review of the facts and by loss of the mandate (point 1 of section 4.4); no single member answers for the guild. Recorded in full in SIM-QA-31. This does not decide the other points of D-32.1.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-32.1 to D-32.6; then article text and Organic Law draft as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass on the wording; a load estimate for oversight seats and outside peers before any number.

## **SIM-QA-33: Harms That Are Not Physical**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 3 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`** for acts that leave a record, with six points; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H4, on H2 as worded, on proof, on coercive control as an offence, and at both ends of the scale of consequences. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no legal review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20. Citation check: the canon does not call the falsifying of ledgers or sensor data a crime (Art. 3.4.5 answers it by audit); it is listed below among the acts with no name and no forum. Two further acts the canon names without a forum were found and added (Arts. 2.3.1, 4.3.4).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 8.4.4 ("Tribunals possess jurisdiction exclusively over physical assault, somatic violation, enclosure, or ecological poisoning, barring ideological prosecution"); Art. 8.5.2 (custody for "predatory violence" only); Art. 8.3 (victims' charter, written for physical violence).  
* **Acts the canon names, with no forum and no consequence:** an association that finances, nominates, instructs or rewards a drawn member (Art. 4.1.3: "void", "a structural assault on the civic commons"); surveillance, biometric tracking and domestic profiling (Art. 3.4.4: "an absolute constitutional crime"); disabling biosecurity interlocks (Art. 1.4.3: "a structural crime against biospheric continuity"); eviction into homelessness (Art. 2.3.1: "a structural crime"); staff covertly influencing jurors (Art. 4.3.4: "an impeachable offense").  
* **Acts with no name and no forum:** falsifying ledgers or sensor data; threats; stalking; coercive control; theft of personal possessions (inviolable under Art. 2.2.1, no remedy stated); sabotage of code.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.2.2, 1.4.1, 1.4.2, 1.4.3, 2.2.1, 2.3.1, 3.4.4, 3.4.5, 4.1.3, 4.3.4, 8.3, 8.4, 8.5; cross-references SIM-QA-20 (false Register entries), SIM-QA-29 (RED-5, lent credentials).

### **1. The Friction**
* The canon limits tribunals to four physical matters so that opinion can never be prosecuted. The same limit leaves acts it calls crimes without any body to establish them.
* With prison abolished (Art. 8.5.1) and Tier 1 untouchable (Art. 1.3.3), the canon states no consequence for a proven non-violent act.
* Harm between persons that leaves no physical trace has only the first two stages of Art. 8.4, which depend on both sides taking part.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, a crime without a court:** the rewarding of a drawn member is proven; the act is void and nothing else follows.
* **RED-2, ledger fraud:** a node falsifies its accounts to draw a larger allocation; the audit of Art. 3.4.5 finds it; the figures are corrected and nobody answers.
* **RED-3, stalking and coercive control:** persistent, never physical; Art. 8.4.4 excludes it; the person targeted has only dialogue with the author.
* **RED-4, theft:** personal possessions are taken; no forum.
* **RED-5, stretched words:** tribunals stretch "somatic violation" or "enclosure" to reach these acts → uneven and unpredictable rulings.
* **RED-6, opinion through the new door:** "threat", "sabotage" or "instructing a member" is used against speech and advocacy, which Arts. 1.4 and 4.1.3 protect.
* **RED-7, empty consequence:** a finding with no consequence deters nobody; a consequence that touches survival or liberty breaches Arts. 1.3.3 and 8.5.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, a closed list:** the jurisdiction of Art. 8.4.4 gains a closed list of acts, each defined by conduct and proof, never by the content of an opinion.
* **H2, two forums:** acts against the integrity of the commons (reward of drawn members, falsified ledgers or sensor data, interlock bypass, surveillance, code sabotage) go to a tribunal drawn from another watershed; acts between persons (threats, stalking, coercive control, theft) go through the ladder of Art. 8.4 with the tribunal as its third stage.
* **H3, consequences:** restitution; nullity of the act; removal from the function or facility that was abused; distance measures on the model of Art. 8.3.2; publication of the finding. Never Tier 1, never custody without violence.
* **H4, existing means only:** review under Art. 4.6 for void acts, mediation for the rest; nothing added.

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team H1 to H4 against RED-1 to RED-7~~ (done, section 4); for each listed act decide forum, body and consequence (table in section 4.3, for the maintainer); ~~test every definition against RED-6~~ (done, section 4.3).

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no legal review; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 4.6 does not reach private acts.** It reviews an "enactment, decree, algorithm, or Organic Law" and declares "conformity or nullity only". A reward handed to a drawn member by an association is none of these.
* **Art. 8.4.3 admits "strictly verifiable physical evidence and open logs".** Whether the word of a witness counts is not stated. Read strictly, it does not. Arts. 3.4.4 and 8.2.2 forbid the recording of persons. Together these make most acts without a physical trace impossible to prove, whatever the list of acts says (RED-8).
* **The canon has no accuser and no investigator.** "Adversarial courtroom trials are abolished" (Art. 8.4.3); Peace Guilds mediate (Art. 8.1.2). Nobody is named to bring a case for the commons or to gather the facts (RED-10).
* **The canon has no notion of attempt.** Jurisdiction over "ecological poisoning" (Art. 8.4.4) reads as requiring the poisoning (RED-11).
* **"Enclosure" and "somatic violation" are not defined in Chapter VIII.** Enclosure in Art. 1.5 concerns the commons, so the theft of a personal possession is not enclosure.
* **Art. 2.3.3 already sends "irreconcilable interpersonal domestic crises" to a panel that may order a relocation, and Art. 8.3.2 puts the burden of moving on the transgressor only "in physical violence".** Under coercive control without blows, the person who moves may be the one targeted.
* **Art. 8.3.1 lets a victim decline dialogue "without penalty", while Art. 8.4 resolves harms "sequentially".** Whether declining stages 1 and 2 opens stage 3 is not stated.
* **Art. 5.2.2 is a precedent for consequences without custody:** exclusion from Tier 3 fabrication, advanced computing and inter-basin transit, restored at once when the conduct ends (Art. 5.2.3).
* **More acts named without a forum, found in this pass:** mercenary syndicates ("prohibited as structural crimes", Art. 8.1.2), the design or manufacture of weapons ("categorically prohibited", Art. 8.1.3), the use of emergency equipment for coercion ("unconstitutional", Art. 8.1.2). The list in the header is still not complete; a full sweep of the canon for "crime", "prohibited", "unconstitutional" and "void" is to do.

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Hypotheses**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1 to RED-4 succeed as written. RED-5 follows: tribunals will stretch the four words they have.
* **H4, existing means only: `RED_WINS`.** Review under Art. 4.6 does not reach private acts (section 4.1), and mediation needs the author to take part.
* **H1, a closed list: `NARROWED` for seven acts, `RED_WINS` for two** (table in section 4.3). A list by conduct works where the conduct leaves a record. It fails for coercive control, where no definition by conduct survives RED-6 and RED-9, and for threats made by word of mouth, which cannot be proven under Art. 8.4.3.
* **H2, two forums: `RED_WINS` as worded; revised: `NARROWED`.** As worded it names the court and not who brings the case or who gathers the facts (RED-10). *Revised:* any person may file; a panel of three drawn from outside checks form and a minimum of evidence (the model of the Triage Panel, Art. 4.3.5) and gathers the records, with power to obtain the logs of bodies and guilds, never the contents of a person's own devices (Arts. 1.2.3, 2.2.1); a person who declines stages 1 and 2 goes straight to stage 3. *Residuals:* evidence held on personal devices is out of reach by design; tribunals of fifteen for every theft add seats that SIM-QA-18 shows are hard to fill (`NOT_RUN`).
* **H3, consequences: `NARROWED` for a first act and for the abuse of a function; `RED_WINS` at both ends of the scale.** *Low end, the persistent author:* a person under a distance measure for stalking keeps coming back. The breach is not violent, so custody is excluded (Art. 8.5.2) and so is force under the direction of SIM-QA-37 (D-37.1 point 4: force only for violence in progress or a ruling "that concerns violence"). The measure is words. *High end, preparation of mass harm:* a person who bypasses a biosecurity interlock or builds a weapon has committed the gravest acts the canon names, without violence so far. "Never custody without violence" leaves the commons waiting for the release. Options for both in D-33.3 and D-33.4. *On the single measures:* publication of a finding is a permanent mark in a small node, against the spirit of Art. 5.2.3 ("zero perpetual civic stigma") and the erasure rule of SIM-QA-41, so it is limited to acts done in a public function and for a fixed time; removal from a function is sound; exclusion from future draws touches a political right (Art. 4.3.2) and belongs with SIM-QA-29 (who cannot serve); restitution in hours has a precedent in Art. 2.2.1 and is pressed only through Tier 3 access, as in Art. 5.2.2.

#### **4.3 Each Act Against RED-6** *(definition by conduct; proof; verdict)*
| Act | Conduct that could define it | Proof available | Verdict |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Rewarding or instructing a drawn member (Art. 4.1.3) | a material advantage given or promised to the member or their household in connection with the mandate; or an instruction backed by a sanction (for example expulsion). Public speech addressed to members is never the act | ledgers, bookings, the association's own records | `NARROWED`. *Residual:* without money, rewards are favours and leave no trace |
| Surveillance and profiling (Art. 3.4.4) | operating a device or routine that records identifiable persons, or combining records to profile a person (SIM-QA-41) | the device, the code, the logs | `NARROWED`. Observation of the public acts of bodies must stay outside the definition |
| Interlock bypass (Art. 1.4.3) | tampering with the interlock | tamper evidence, by design | `BLUE_HOLDS` on definition; consequence under H3 fails (section 4.2) |
| Eviction into homelessness (Art. 2.3.1) | usually a decree | the decree | covered by Art. 4.6 when a body acts; physical ejection by persons is already inside Art. 8.4.4 |
| Covert influence on jurors by staff (Art. 4.3.4) | the covert channel, not the opinion | records of the assembly | `NARROWED`. The assembly concerned cannot judge it; an outside tribunal must |
| Falsified ledgers or sensor data | a false entry | the split-sample audit of Art. 3.4.5 | `NARROWED`. Physical evidence shows the discrepancy, not the intent. The consequence that needs no finding of intent is correction and return of the excess in physical units; removal needs a pattern |
| Sabotage of code | unauthorised alteration of code or records on common infrastructure | build records (Art. 7.3.3) | `NARROWED`. Declared testing under the bounties of Art. 7.6.1 and the disclosure rule of SIM-QA-22 D-6 must be excluded |
| Theft of personal possessions | taking the object | the object | `NARROWED`. A tribunal of fifteen is too heavy; the Local Mediation Panel (Art. 4.2.1, "housing restitution") could order return |
| Stalking | following, watching or contacting again after a recorded request to stop | the request; the contacts when written; little else in a commons without cameras | `NARROWED`, weak on proof |
| Threats | a stated intent to commit physical violence against a named person (the act threatened is already inside Art. 8.4.4) | a written or recorded message | `NARROWED` when recorded; `RED_WINS` when spoken |
| Coercive control | no definition found that is conduct and not a judgment on a relationship | none of the kind Art. 8.4.3 admits | `RED_WINS` as an offence. A protective measure without a finding of fault fits better (SIM-QA-39), with the dwelling question in SIM-QA-36 |

#### **4.4 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-8, proof. `RED_WINS` under current text and under every hypothesis.** Section 4.1: physical evidence only, and no recording of persons. The canon chose both on purpose. Admitting testimony would open the offences above to proof and to false accusation at once. For the maintainer (D-33.2).
* **RED-9, the accusation as a weapon. `NARROWED`.** A complaint is used against a rival practitioner (SIM-QA-32), a rival association ("instructing a member"), or a partner in a separation to win the dwelling. *Guards:* the outside panel; a minimum of evidence before anything is ordered; interim measures short and neutral (the person asking for protection is offered another dwelling at once; no one is moved out before a finding).
* **RED-10, no accuser, no investigator. `RED_WINS` under H2 as worded;** revised in section 4.2.
* **RED-11, attempt and preparation. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Nothing reaches a poisoning prepared and not carried out, or a pathogen under construction. Joined with the high end of H3.
* **RED-12, collective authors. `NARROWED`.** When a node falsifies its accounts or an association rewards a member, who answers? An association cannot be dissolved (Art. 1.4.1). *Guards:* nullity and return of the excess by the collective; removal from function of the persons who signed; never a ban on associating. For guilds, with SIM-QA-32 RED-14.
* **RED-13, drawn members answering for their votes. `NARROWED`.** If a council that orders surveillance exposes its members to a tribunal, people refuse to serve (SIM-QA-18). *Guard:* an act of a body is voided under Art. 4.6 and no member answers for a vote; a member answers only for conduct outside the vote (taking a reward, falsifying a record).
* **RED-14, relocation against Art. 10.4.3. Open.** Moving a persistent author to another node is "involuntary deportation" on a strict reading. Art. 8.3.2 already orders it for physical violence, so the tension exists in the canon today.
* **RED-15, the transition. Cross-docket.** Until a phase gate, state law covers every act on this list. Under the direction of SIM-QA-40 a node's forum would be voluntary mediation only. This docket describes the rule after that.

#### **4.5 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** for acts that leave a record, with the six points below; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H4, on H2 as worded, on proof (RED-8), on coercive control as an offence, and at both ends of the scale of consequences.
1. A closed list of acts added to Art. 8.4.4, each defined by conduct that leaves a record; the content of an opinion is never an element.
2. Any person may file; a panel of three drawn from outside checks form and a minimum of evidence and gathers records from bodies and guilds, never from a person's own devices.
3. Acts against the commons go to a tribunal drawn from another watershed. Acts between persons go through the ladder; a person who declines dialogue goes to the tribunal. The return of a possession can be ordered by the Local Mediation Panel.
4. Consequences: return or restitution, nullity, removal from the function or facility abused, a distance measure. Publication only for acts in a public function, for a fixed time. Pressure only through Tier 3 access, as in Art. 5.2.2. Never Tier 1.
5. An act of a body is voided under Art. 4.6; no member answers for a vote.
6. Where no conduct can be defined (coercive control), protection is offered without a finding of fault.

#### **4.6 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
A new jurisdiction and its consequences touch rights, so the core stays in the Constitution, in Chapter VIII. Chapter VIII holds 862 words today and 902 with SIM-QA-22 D-3; SIM-QA-37 will add a right of self-defence and a defender. A list of nine acts with definitions does not fit in 98 words. Under the placement test the definitions cannot simply move to Organic Law, since a 60% majority could then widen an offence. *Options:* **O-1,** the Constitution names the acts and the limit ("defined by conduct, never by opinion; Organic Law may narrow a definition and never widen it"), and Organic Law holds the definitions; **O-2,** an own ceiling for Chapter VIII, by separate decision.

#### **4.7 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | tribunals have jurisdiction "exclusively" over four physical matters | Art. 8.4.4 | a closed list of further acts | **weakened** as a guarantee against prosecution; the bar on ideological prosecution is kept |
| 2 | proceedings evaluate "strictly verifiable physical evidence and open logs" | Art. 8.4.3 | only if D-33.2 admits testimony | **weakened**, conditional |
| 3 | custody only for "predatory violence resisting stabilization" | Art. 8.5.2 | only if D-33.4 extends it to preparation of mass harm | **weakened**, conditional |
| 4 | no "involuntary deportation" | Art. 10.4.3 | only if D-33.3 allows relocation of a persistent author | **weakened**, conditional |

#### **4.8 Residual Weaknesses**
* Most of these acts stay hard to prove in a commons that records no one. That is the price of Arts. 3.4.4 and 8.2.2, and this docket does not remove it.
* Favours leave no trace; the rule on rewarding drawn members reaches clumsy cases only.
* Nothing is measured: the number of cases, the seats they need.
* Five guards rest on other dockets: SIM-QA-29, 36, 37, 39, 41.
* No lawyer and no one who works with victims of stalking or domestic abuse has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-33.1 to D-33.6):**
* **D-33.1:** accept the six points of section 4.5 as the direction, or change them; and which acts of the table in section 4.3 enter the list.
* **D-33.2:** proof (RED-8): (a) keep Art. 8.4.3 as it stands and accept that unrecorded acts go unproven; (b) admit testimony for the listed acts with a rule of corroboration. The red team gives no preference: (a) leaves victims without a remedy, (b) opens false accusation.
* **D-33.3:** the persistent non-violent author: (a) accept that the measure is unenforced; (b) exclusion from Tier 3, computing and transit until the breach ends (Art. 5.2.2 model); (c) relocation to another node by ruling of the tribunal, with force allowed to carry it out (changes D-37.1 point 4 and meets Art. 10.4.3). The red team supports (b) first and (c) only on repeated breach.
* **D-33.4:** preparation of mass harm (interlock bypass, weapons): keep "never custody without violence", or extend Art. 8.5.2 to these acts with all its guarantees.
* **D-33.5:** placement: O-1 or O-2.
* **D-33.6:** a full sweep of the canon for acts named and left without a forum, before drafting.

**Note 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch):** the maintainer decided that Chapter I will name a closed list of cases in which a person may be held (SIM-QA-37, item 2). Preparation of mass harm (D-33.4) was put to the maintainer with no pick from the red team and is **still open**; if it is to be a case, it must enter that list before the lock.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch 2, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md); answered item by item; directions only):** **item 3** (D-33.4): option C. No custody for the preparation of mass harm. A person may be held to stop the making or the use of a means of mass death, for the time needed to take that means away, with review within 72 hours. A case is added to the Chapter I list (SIM-QA-37, Draft C). *Residuals recorded with the decision:* a person who starts again is stopped again each time; a sentence outside Chapter I must say that a means of mass death is not a personal possession under Art. 2.2.1 (with D-37.4).

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-33.3; conflicts batch, conflict 2, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** a ladder of three steps for the persistent non-violent author, proposed after the maintainer asked why such a person should not go to a sanctuary as a violent person does when no measure succeeds. (1) Exclusion from Tier 3, computing and transit until the breach ends; never Tier 1. (2) If the acts continue, a tribunal orders relocation to another node, away from the harmed person, and the order is enforced. (3) If the person returns and continues, custody with every guarantee of Art. 8.5.3 (a jury drawn by lot orders it and reviews it periodically). *Guards given with the proposal:* only repeated acts against a specific person, proven by records, after a tribunal ruling was ignored; never an opinion, a belief or speech about public matters. The red team had recommended (b) alone; not adopted. *Consequences, to carry at drafting:* **a case is added to the closed list of Draft C** (Art. 1.2.5, Chapter I, permanent; the draft and its count must be revised); SIM-QA-37 point 4 changes; Art. 10.4.3 (no "involuntary deportation") is **weakened** (disclosure line 4 of section 4.7, now unconditional: a decision of its own); Art. 8.5.2 ("predatory violence") is widened. Proof remains the weak point (RED-8, D-33.2). Direction only; no wording.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 7, item 7c):** a sexual act of an adult with a child is violence under Art. 8.4.4; it is recorded in SIM-QA-25 as the rule and is within the tribunals' jurisdiction as violence, not as an act added to the list of point 1. This does not decide D-33.1.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-33.2; conflicts batch, conflict 10, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** option (b), with an addition by the maintainer. Testimony is admitted for the listed non-physical acts, never alone: it needs corroboration by a record or a second independent source. **A person may give the jury their own messages, recordings and videos** (the maintainer's point: the canon bars public sensors and bodies from recording persons, Art. 8.2.2; it does not bar a person from keeping what they received or recording what is done to them); nobody is made to; such a recording counts as corroboration. *Guards given with the proposal:* a recording is admitted only after the independent audit of Art. 8.4.5 (fabricated material); recording what is done to oneself is allowed, following a person to record them over time is the profiling of Art. 3.4.4 (SIM-QA-41 point 1). The records of what bodies and guilds do are kept and public; no body records private life. The red team's pick on testimony, adopted. *Consequences:* Art. 8.4.3 ("strictly verifiable physical evidence and open logs") is **weakened**: a compression disclosure line, a decision of its own at drafting; false accusation is opened, with corroboration as the only guard; detecting fabricated recordings grows harder with time; a guild member's work log is both a body's record and a person's (not resolved). Direction only; no wording.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 12):** point 5 of section 4.5 is confirmed (an act of a body is voided under Art. 4.6; no member answers for a vote); a person answers only for their own act on the closed list. Recorded in full in SIM-QA-31. This does not decide the other points of D-33.1.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-33.1 to D-33.6; then article text and Organic Law definitions as hypothesis, each definition red-teamed again against RED-6; case-load estimate before any number; legal review.
