## **SIM-QA-34: Allocation Above the Survival Floor**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 4 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`** with four points; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on immobility (the canon's own choice), on "no per-person record" taken as absolute, and under current text on the scope of Tier 1 care, the transit sanction and the tool-library deposit. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20. Citation check: Art. 3.3.3 does not govern machine time alone; it distributes surplus "through quadratic preference signaling and participatory Fab-Lab time rosters". Only machine time has rules; the preference signal is undefined. Art. 3.4.4 bans "individual domestic profiling" (the wording used below), within sensor networks.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 3.3.3 (Tier 3; Anti-Brokerage for machine slots), Art. 3.6.2 (60% for Tier 3 allocations), Art. 10.4.2 (Green Status grants "immediate residential usufruct"; Amber sets priorities), Art. 2.1.3 (abandoned dwellings return to the local commons), Art. 2.3.4 (relocation only to an equivalent dwelling), Art. 5.2.2 (reassignment of floor area as a reciprocity measure only), Art. 1.3.2 ("Comprehensive primary, preventative, and emergency healthcare"), Art. 2.2.1 (personal possessions under "absolute, permanent sovereignty"), Arts. 3.1.2, 5.1.2, 9.3.2 (no prices, no traded labour, no currency inside).  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.3.2, 2.1.3, 2.2.1, 2.2.2, 2.3.4, 3.1.2, 3.3.1, 3.3.3, 3.4.4, 3.6.2, 5.1.2, 5.2.2, 9.3.2, 10.4.2.  
* **Kept apart from:** SIM-QA-30 (admission to a seed node), SIM-QA-21 (OL-10 point 2(e) and N-3: which body allocates free dwellings in a small node, and a deadline; not the rule of allocation), SIM-QA-04 (fab queues, merged), SIM-QA-22 D-5 (care in Art. 1.3.2).

### **1. The Friction**
* A person is promised a dwelling; no rule says which one, or how to change it when a household grows or shrinks.
* Healthcare is promised as "comprehensive"; some treatments are scarce and no rule says who comes first. Art. 7.1.2 bars leaving it to an algorithm.
* Personal possessions are the person's own; nothing says whether they may be given or swapped, or what keeps a commonly accepted good from working as money.
* Planning must learn what people want; individual profiling is a constitutional crime.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, dwellings by acquaintance:** free dwellings go to friends of whoever keeps the list (SIM-QA-21 N-3).
* **RED-2, immobility:** no path to change dwelling; large dwellings stay with small households, since Art. 5.2.2 reassigns area only as a measure against refusal of shifts.
* **RED-3, key money:** dwellings are swapped with side payments in goods or favours.
* **RED-4, the scarce treatment:** one organ, a few dialysis places; allocation by clinician discretion or by connections.
* **RED-5, a barter medium:** a durable good, a booking or a ledger of hours circulates as currency; Art. 3.3.3 makes only machine slots non-transferable.
* **RED-6, the ban that overreaches:** forbidding gifts and swaps breaches Art. 2.2.1.
* **RED-7, blind planning:** without data on wants the plan misses them and informal markets appear; or planners collect per-person data against Art. 3.4.4.
* **RED-8, the undefined signal:** "quadratic preference signaling" needs an identity and a budget per person (SIM-QA-29) and invites collusion.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, dwellings:** a public list of free dwellings; criteria of need fixed in advance (household size, accessibility), then lot; exchange by mutual consent through the list, with nothing given in return; a voluntary request to move.
* **H2, scarce treatment:** criteria of clinical need and expected benefit fixed in advance by a drawn body on rival evidence (Art. 4.4), applied by clinicians, ties by lot, never status or contribution (Art. 1.3.3), open to review.
* **H3, gifts and swaps:** free for personal possessions; the bar falls only on transferable standard claims on the commons (slots, rations, hours) and on any enforceable debt (Art. 3.2).
* **H4, the demand signal:** aggregate, voluntary and anonymous: stock drawdown per node, open requests, and the preference signal of Art. 3.3.3 defined in Organic Law; no per-person record.

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team H1 to H4 against RED-1 to RED-8~~ (done, section 4); align H1 with the SIM-QA-21 OL-10 draft and H4 with SIM-QA-29 at drafting.

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 2.2.2 makes dwelling usufruct "perpetual" with "hereditary succession of occupancy among active co-inhabitants".** Free dwellings are therefore only the abandoned (Art. 2.1.3) and the newly built. The canon chose security of tenure; efficient use of space is not a goal it states.
* **Art. 2.5.3 already allocates a positional good by lot** (retreats in sanctuaries, "transparent civic sortition"). Dwellings differ in position and quality in the same way.
* **Art. 1.3.2 promises "Comprehensive primary, preventative, and emergency healthcare".** Specialised care (a transplant, dialysis, cancer treatment) is none of the three. On the words, the scarce treatments of this docket are outside the Tier 1 baseline, and the protection of Art. 1.3.3 (survival never used as leverage) does not clearly cover them.
* **Art. 5.2.2 excludes a person who refuses shifts from "inter-basin transit commons".** Nothing exempts travel to a hospital in another basin (RED-11).
* **Art. 2.2.1 has tokens and a paid priority inside the canon.** Tool-library checkout "operates under time-bounded mesh tokens; voluntary deposits are maintained at community expense, granting priority booking". Whether a token can pass to another person is not said, and a deposit buys a better place in the queue (RED-12).
* **Art. 5.1.2: "human effort can neither be bought, rented, traded".** Read strictly, a haircut given for a jar of honey is traded effort. Read with the rest of the sentence ("nor subordinated through wage contracts or debt extortion"), the bar is on subordination. The text does not choose (RED-14).
* **Art. 3.1.2 strips prices of "legal standing as claims on the biophysical commons".** It does not forbid two persons from swapping their own things, or from counting in some good while they do. The canon denies money a claim on the commons; it does not ban it between persons.
* **Arts. 3.3.3 and 3.6.2 both allocate Tier 3:** the first "through quadratic preference signaling" and "without central bureaucracy", the second by a 60% vote of the assembly. Which decides what is not stated.

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Hypotheses**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1, RED-3, RED-4 and RED-5 succeed as written.
* **H1, dwellings: `NARROWED` on RED-1 and RED-3; `RED_WINS` on RED-2.**
  * *RED-1, acquaintance.* A public list, criteria of need fixed in advance and a public lot remove the keeper's choice. What remains: a dwelling kept off the list ("under repair"), and a household declared larger than it is. *Guards:* the state of every dwelling is public (occupied, free, under repair) as counts and addresses without names; the keeper applies the rule and never chooses (SIM-QA-32 point 6).
  * *RED-3, key money.* "Nothing given in return" cannot be observed. A swap also steers a dwelling past the lot: a household about to leave swaps its good dwelling with a friend's poor one and then departs. *Guard:* a dwelling vacated within a set time after a swap goes back with the dwelling it was swapped for, and both go to the lot. *Residual:* favours given on the side.
  * *RED-2, immobility.* A voluntary request to move frees nothing. A large dwelling stays with one person and passes to whoever lives there at the end, so the best dwellings stay in families over generations. Any duty to move breaches Arts. 2.2.2 and 2.3. This is the canon's own choice (section 4.1), and no hypothesis changes it. *Two guards that need no compulsion:* the foundational energy allotment of Art. 1.3.2 counted per person and not per square metre, so that a large dwelling costs its occupant comfort (Thermodynamic architect: it also stops heating empty rooms from the Tier 1 budget); a minimum time of shared residence before a co-inhabitant succeeds (RED-9).
* **H2, scarce treatment: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` under current text on the scope of Tier 1 (section 4.1).** Attacks on H2: (a) "expected benefit" set by a lay body can rank the old and the disabled last; the criteria must pass the justification test of SIM-QA-23 (Art. 1.8). (b) Clinicians who "apply" the criteria still score the patient, and connections work there; two clinicians score independently, one from outside the node, on a file without a name. (c) An organ is offered for hours; review can only come after, on the pattern of decisions. (d) H2 does not say at which level the pool sits; a node with the machines will serve its own first. The pool belongs at the level where the resource is shared, and residents and arrivals count alike, which invites travel toward basins with treatment (SIM-QA-38). (e) A published scoring rule adopted by a drawn body is not delegation to an algorithm, on the line drawn in SIM-QA-31 point 2.
* **H3, gifts and swaps: `NARROWED`.** With Tier 1 free and no body enforcing a debt, a medium of exchange cannot buy survival, land or a claim on the commons. It will still appear for Tier 3 goods, and where it meets things the commons allocates it does damage: a dwelling swap, a place in a queue, and above all a shift. A person who gives goods to another to do their rotational shifts recreates paid labour and the underclass of Art. 5.1.1 (RED-13). *Revised H3:* things a person owns pass freely; every claim on the commons (a slot, a shift, a ration, a dwelling, a queue place, a signal credit) is personal, expires and never transfers; shifts are exchanged one for one through the roster only; no body enforces an agreement between persons beyond the return of a thing taken (SIM-QA-33). *Residual:* substitution off the record, which meets the lent credentials of SIM-QA-29 RED-5.
* **H4, the demand signal: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` on "no per-person record" taken as absolute.** (a) Drawdown shows what was taken, not what was missing; stockouts and unfilled requests must be counted. (b) Open anonymous requests can be stuffed; a budget per person is needed, hence a proof of personhood per period (Art. 7.4.4 allows it without identity). (c) Counts per node identify people in a small node (SIM-QA-29 measured 69% re-identification at fifty adults from two attributes, synthetic); a minimum cell applies. (d) Two things cannot work with no record at all: rationing in a certified shortage (SIM-QA-30 D-30.7, "in proportion to need") and the dampening of repeated wins (OL-2). Both need to know that this person already received. A pseudonym per use (SIM-QA-41) is the least that works.

#### **4.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-9, succession arranged at the end. `NARROWED`.** A relative is entered as co-inhabitant shortly before a death to keep the dwelling. *Guard:* a minimum period of shared residence, in Organic Law. "Co-inhabitant" itself is undefined (SIM-QA-36).
* **RED-10, an anonymous claim is a transferable claim (Cyberneticist). `NARROWED`, technical residual.** A ration or signal credit that cannot be linked to its holder can be handed to someone else. For food this equals giving the food and does no harm. For signal credits it lets one person voice more than a share at a flat cost, which defeats the quadratic rule. *Guards:* short expiry; no carry-over. The trade-off between anonymity and non-transferability needs a specialist in mechanism design; no parameter is proposed.
* **RED-11, the sanction that blocks care. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Exclusion from inter-basin transit (Art. 5.2.2) stops a person travelling to treatment. *Guard:* travel for care is never inside a reciprocity measure.
* **RED-12, tokens and deposits in Art. 2.2.1. `RED_WINS` under current text.** A deposit that grants priority booking is a purchased place in a queue, against the Anti-Brokerage rule of Art. 3.3.3. For the maintainer (D-34.5).
* **RED-13, paid shifts.** Under H3, above.
* **RED-14, the reading of Art. 5.1.2. Open.** The strict reading forbids ordinary favours and breaches Art. 2.2.1 in practice (RED-6); the loose reading allows a person to work for another's goods every day. The line that survives is the one in revised H3: effort is freely given; a civic obligation is never done by another for a return.
* **RED-15, outside money held by persons. Cross-docket.** During the transition a resident can hold legacy cash or receive goods from relatives outside and use them inside; a person can also sell outside what was made with commons materials, past the External Trade Interface (Art. 9.3.3). Goes to SIM-QA-40 and SIM-QA-42 (civic compact).
* **RED-16, what the signal decides. `NARROWED`.** *Proposed reading of Arts. 3.3.3 and 3.6.2:* the assembly sets the shares of Tier 3 at 60%; inside a share, the signal orders what is produced; a scarce item goes by lot among those who asked, with dampening. The signal then says what to make, never who receives.

#### **4.4 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the four points below; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on immobility (RED-2, the canon's own choice), on "no per-person record" taken as absolute, and under current text on the scope of Tier 1 care, the transit sanction and the tool-library deposit.
1. **Dwellings.** The state of every dwelling is public. A free dwelling goes by criteria of need fixed in advance, then by public lot; no body votes on persons. Swaps pass through the list, with the guard against steering. No one is made to move. The energy allotment is per person. Succession requires a minimum period of shared residence.
2. **Scarce treatment.** Criteria adopted in advance by a drawn body on rival evidence and tested for equal protection; two independent scores on a file without a name; ties by lot; never status, contribution or origin; the pool at the level where the resource is shared; review afterwards; travel for care never inside a sanction.
3. **Between persons.** What a person owns passes freely. Claims on the commons are personal, expire and never transfer. Shifts are exchanged one for one through the roster. No body enforces a private agreement.
4. **Demand.** Stockouts and unfilled requests published as counts above a minimum cell; an equal signal budget per person and per period, with no carry-over; the assembly sets Tier 3 shares and the signal orders production inside them; scarce items by lot with dampening, under a pseudonym per use.

#### **4.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
Most of this is procedure and belongs in Organic Law. What the placement test keeps in the Constitution: allocation of dwellings by need and lot with no vote on persons (Chapter II); "never status, contribution or origin" for scarce care and whether specialised care is in the baseline (Chapter I); claims on the commons never transfer, and no enforcement of private agreements (Chapter III). No text is drafted.

| I | 808 | 1,181 of 1,200 after SIM-QA-22 D-4, D-5 and SIM-QA-23 | 19, **within fifty**; SIM-QA-24, 25, 27, 28 and 41 also need it |
| II | 869 | SIM-QA-21 wording not recounted; SIM-QA-32 O-1 asks for about one hundred words | about 130 before SIM-QA-32 |
| III | 886 | not recounted | about 114 |

Chapter I cannot take the care sentence without a ceiling decision or a move (instruction 10a point 5): the conflict goes to the maintainer. Chapter II cannot take both this docket and SIM-QA-32 O-1 in full.

#### **4.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "voluntary deposits … granting priority booking" | Art. 2.2.1 | the priority is removed (only if D-34.5 so decides) | **dropped**, conditional |
| 2 | "hereditary succession of occupancy among active co-inhabitants" | Art. 2.2.2 | a minimum period of shared residence | **qualified** |
| 3 | Tier 3 distributed "through quadratic preference signaling", "without central bureaucracy" | Art. 3.3.3 | the signal orders production inside shares set by the assembly | **qualified** |
| 4 | exclusion from "inter-basin transit commons" | Art. 5.2.2 | travel for care excepted | **weakened** as a sanction; strengthens Art. 1.3 |
| 5 | "Comprehensive primary, preventative, and emergency healthcare" | Art. 1.3.2 | D-34.3 either extends it to specialised care (an expansion, Art. 4.5.1) or states that it does not reach it | **expanded or clarified** |

#### **4.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* Favours on the side and substitution off the record cannot be seen.
* Under-used dwellings stay under-used; the answer left is to build, inside the carrying capacity.
* The signal is not designed. Budget, expiry and the lot with dampening are named, not sized, and collusion is not analysed.
* Nothing is measured: waiting times for a dwelling, the stock a node must build, the effect of a per-person energy allotment.
* No housing practitioner, clinician, medical ethicist or mechanism designer has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-34.1 to D-34.7):**
* **D-34.1:** accept the four points of section 4.4 as the direction, or change them.
* **D-34.2:** immobility: confirm that no one is made to move, with the two guards (energy per person; minimum shared residence), or ask for something stronger.
* **D-34.3:** specialised care: inside the Tier 1 baseline (an expansion of Art. 1.3.2, with the Chapter I ceiling conflict), or outside it with the protections of point 2 written elsewhere.
* **D-34.4:** confirm the line between persons: swaps and favours free and unenforced; a civic obligation never done by another for a return (reading of Art. 5.1.2).
* **D-34.5:** the tool-library deposit that grants priority (Art. 2.2.1): remove the priority, or keep it.
* **D-34.6:** accept a pseudonym per use for rationing in a shortage and for dampening, with SIM-QA-41.
* **D-34.7:** placement, and the order of claims on Chapter II between this docket and SIM-QA-32.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 7** (D-34.3): B, a third course beside the two listed in D-34.3: specialised care is not added to the baseline, and Chapter I says that access to any treatment the commons provides is never conditioned on status, contribution or origin. To be drafted with Art. 1.8 (SIM-QA-23) in view, since "origin" is already a ground there and "contribution" is not. **Still open:** D-34.1, D-34.2, D-34.4 to D-34.7.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 5, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** the energy allotment is **per person**, and it is enforced by the meter in the dwelling, which reports nothing but the total of its branch (SIM-QA-41 point 2, design proposal A). The red team's pick, adopted. This settles the first guard of D-34.2 (the allotment per person); the second guard (minimum shared residence before succession) and "or something stronger" stay open. *Residuals:* a device that enforces can be tampered with unseen; the number of persons in a dwelling must reach the device (the sealed household record of SIM-QA-36). A specialist reads the design before any wording. Direction only.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-34.1 to D-34.7; then article text and Organic Law draft as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass; the signal to a specialist before any parameter; a SIMONE or script estimate of dwelling turnover before any number.

## **SIM-QA-35: Correcting an Error, and Leaving**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 5 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4): **`NARROWED`**, on condition that three points are done before the Genesis Lock; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H2, on exit with territory, on both failure criteria as worded, and on three limits no text removes. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no legal review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20. Citation check: Art. 1.7.2 bars suspending, diminishing or alienating Chapter I, and Art. 4.5.1 allows its expansion at 75%; the open point is whether changing a technical element counts as a diminution.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.7 (primacy; contrary acts void), Art. 4.5.1 (75% for amendments and for "expansions (never diminutions, Article 1.7) of Invariant Rights"), Art. 1.4.3 (the 7% reserve; interlocks on synthesis hardware, registry "as specified in the Organic Law Annex"), Art. 10.6.2 (75% to "admit new federated bioregions"), Art. 8.6.3 (a person may "depart the bioregion unhindered with personal possessions"), Art. 10.3.3 (Systemic Decoupling suspends links with a basin that hoards or cuts off water), Art. 10.1.4 (higher tiers act only on exported pollution, shared aquifers, invariant rights), Art. 3.1.6 (annual Autarkic Closure audit).  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.4.3, 1.7, 3.1.6, 4.5.1, 8.6.3, 10.1.4, 10.3.3, 10.6.2, 10.6.3.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-17 (section 1: detail in Chapter I is frozen; the hazard registry moved to Organic Law, the 7% and the interlock stayed), SIM-QA-22 D-1 (anti-haste amendment rule), SIM-QA-23 (residual: a ground added in error cannot be removed), SIM-QA-30 F-6 (a node that fails physically).

### **1. The Friction**
* Chapter I is closed against every majority, and it contains two technical choices: a percentage and a mechanism. If either proves wrong, no path corrects it.
* A bioregion enters by a 75% vote. Nothing says whether it may leave. Only the individual has an exit.
* Nothing says when a node has failed, who finds it, or what follows for its people and its assets.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, the frozen parameter:** evidence shows the 7% is wrong, or the interlock method unsafe or obsolete; any change is arguably a diminution and void (SIM-QA-17 RED-5).
* **RED-2, the repair door as back door:** a correction path is used to weaken a right under the name of a technical fix.
* **RED-3, the mistaken expansion:** a right added at 75% proves harmful and cannot be narrowed (SIM-QA-23 residual).
* **RED-4, the captive bioregion:** a large majority of a bioregion wants out; with no path it leaves in fact, taking assets, and the federation has no lawful answer.
* **RED-5, exit to enclose:** a departing bioregion takes a watershed and Tier 2-A facilities out of the commons (Arts. 1.1.2, 2.1); the minority inside loses Chapter I.
* **RED-6, the failed node:** a node cannot cover Tier 1 or seats no body; nothing names the condition; abandoned assets "return" under Art. 2.1.3 to a local commons that no longer functions.
* **RED-7, failure as takeover:** neighbours declare a node failed in order to absorb it, against Art. 10.1.4.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, placement:** apply the test of instruction 10 to Chapter I once: the right and its bound stay entrenched; the technical means moves to Organic Law under the ratchet rule. Needs a path to make that one move.
* **H2, correction procedure:** harder than an amendment: a closed list of technical elements; 75% at the global tier; a review jury (Art. 4.6) certifying that no right is diminished; a delay on the model of SIM-QA-22 D-1.
* **H3, exit of a node or bioregion:** by 75% of its residents after a delay; biomes and commons assets stay in trust and cannot be enclosed; any person who wishes to remain in the commons keeps Chapter I and the right to move (Art. 10.4); exported harm stays under Art. 10.1.4.
* **H4, failure:** a criterion from physical accounts and seated bodies (a repeated failed audit under Art. 3.1.6; no body seated for a stated period), certified by a panel drawn from outside; aid first; then time-limited administration by a drawn outside panel; relocation offered, never imposed; assets stay in the commons.

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team H1 to H4 against RED-1 to RED-7~~ (done, section 4); link to the transitional amending body of the Foundation docket (SIM-QA-42, D-42.1 point 3); ~~Chapter I ceiling check~~ (section 4.6).

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no constitutional lawyer has read it; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 1.7.2 protects "the provisions of this Bill of Rights", not only the rights.** Every sentence of Chapter I is covered, the technical ones included. Art. 1.7 is itself in Chapter I, so it protects itself.
* **After the Foundation Genesis Lock no path exists, by design. Before it, none is needed.** Until the lock, the text changes by docket and maintainer decision, as SIM-QA-17 did for the hazard registry. The question of this docket is therefore mostly one of timing: what must be moved or reworded before the lock.
* **Who says what a "diminution" is.** Only a review jury of 101 (Art. 4.6.2), case by case, "resolving doubts under Chapter I in favor of the person", and with no binding precedent (SIM-QA-22 D-12). The meaning of Chapter I will move by jury, with no rule of consistency (RED-12).
* **Leaving with territory is already void under Chapter I.** Art. 1.1.2: no portion of the commons may be "subjected to sovereign territorial enclosure". Art. 10.1.1 abolishes frontiers. A bioregion that leaves and keeps its watershed encloses it. No vote at any threshold can authorise that (Art. 1.7.2). The Preamble calls the covenant "perpetual" and Art. 10.6.3 pledges "perpetual peace".
* **A bioregion is already close to autonomous.** Art. 10.1.4 gives higher tiers "zero jurisdiction" except exported pollution, shared aquifers and invariant rights. What a bioregion can leave is narrower than it looks: the compacts of Art. 10.3, the solidarity of Art. 10.4.4, service in higher assemblies, common standards.
* **Art. 10.1.4 is the hook for a failed node.** Tier 1 is an invariant right (Art. 1.3); when a node cannot supply it, a higher tier has jurisdiction "to defend invariant rights". The article gives the jurisdiction and no means.
* **Art. 2.3.3 already allows an ordered relocation** for "structural hazards" and "environmental remediation", through a Local Mediation Panel, with an equivalent dwelling first (Art. 2.3.4). H4 says relocation is "never imposed"; the canon is less absolute.
* **Art. 10.3.3 suspends "advanced medical buffers" for a whole basin.** It reaches every resident, including those who opposed the conduct, and sits close to Art. 1.3.3 (survival never used as punishment).

#### **4.2 Technical Choices Inside Chapter I** *(the sweep H1 needs; Arts. 1.1 to 1.7 read in full)*
| Element | Where | Kind | If it proves wrong |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| seven percent (7%) of fabrication, bandwidth and laboratories | Art. 1.4.3 | a number | too low or too high, it stays; in a shortage the reserve competes with Tier 2 (hospital compute) and Chapter I wins |
| "transparent rotational time sortition among applicants" | Art. 1.4.3 | a procedure | frozen |
| interlocks that "prevent the transcription of sequences listed in a public hazard registry" | Art. 1.4.3 | a mechanism | if screening by listed sequence can be evaded or synthesis no longer works by transcription, the mechanism is void of effect and cannot be replaced |
| three barred classes: "synthetic self-replicating lethal pathogens, mass neurological agents, or radiological weapons" | Art. 1.4.3 | a closed list | a new class of hazard cannot be added: widening the bar narrows the reserve, which is a provision of Chapter I (RED-8) |
| the six items of the baseline | Art. 1.3.2 | a list | an item can be added at 75% (Art. 4.5.1); none can be reworded |
| guardians "drawn by lot" | Art. 1.6.3 | a procedure | frozen |
| pending, not in the canon: the closed list of grounds of Art. 1.8 (SIM-QA-23); the wording of SIM-QA-22 D-4 and D-5; the data principle of SIM-QA-41 | drafts | lists and wording | each freezes on entry |

Art. 1.4.3 holds 141 of the chapter's 808 words; about 100 of them are number, procedure and mechanism.

#### **4.3 Verdicts on the Hypotheses**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-1, RED-3, RED-6 succeed as written.
* **H1, placement. Before the lock: `BLUE_HOLDS` with one residual. After the lock: `RED_WINS`.** Before the lock the move needs no path (section 4.1): the right and its bound stay in Chapter I, the means go to Organic Law under the ratchet rule. After the lock nothing can move. *Residual, RED-1 survives for bounds:* a bound kept in Chapter I is frozen. A floor that proves too high can never come down. *Gain:* the move frees roughly 60 to 100 words in a chapter that has 19 left and five dockets waiting (section 4.6). *(Corrected 2026-11-20 after drafting: the move frees 9 words, not 60 to 100; [`chapter_i_draft_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_draft_2026-11-20.md).)*
* **H2, correction procedure: `RED_WINS`.** After H1 only rights and bounds remain in Chapter I. H2 requires a jury to certify "that no right is diminished". Lowering a bound diminishes by definition, so the certified procedure can change nothing that H1 left behind. Without the certification it is the back door of RED-2. H2 is either empty or dangerous. The choice it hides is real and is for the maintainer (D-35.2): accept that an error in a Chapter I bound is permanent, or give up the absoluteness of Art. 1.7.
* **RED-3, the mistaken expansion. `NARROWED`.** No narrowing is possible afterwards, so the guards sit at adoption. (a) A guarantee that is new and untested goes into Organic Law first, which may strengthen a right at 60% and may later come back down to the constitutional floor; only what has been tried goes into Chapter I. (b) An expansion may be adopted for a stated term; the rule that a lapse is not a diminution must be written before the lock, or a jury will void the lapse. (c) The delay of SIM-QA-22 D-1. *Second finding:* an expansion of one right can narrow another (a data right against speech, isolation against movement). Art. 4.5.1 treats "expansion" as if it had no cost (RED-9).
* **H3, exit. With territory: `RED_WINS`** (void under Art. 1.1.2, section 4.1). **As withdrawal: `NARROWED`.** The lawful form is narrower than H3: by 75% of adult residents, after a delay, a bioregion withdraws from the compacts, the solidarity duties, the higher assemblies and the common standards. Chapter I continues to bind it, the commons stay commons, and Art. 10.1.4 still reaches pollution, aquifers and rights. *Attacks:* (a) hold-up: a basin that is the only maker of some output (Art. 10.3.2, "specialized metabolic organs"; a Tier 2-A fab) threatens to withdraw to win terms (RED-10). The canon's own defence is Art. 3.1.6: Tier 1 comes from local resources everywhere, so a withdrawal reaches Tier 2 and Tier 3 only. A notice period long enough to rebuild elsewhere is the second guard; its length is `NOT_RUN`. (b) The quarter that voted against loses solidarity; they keep Chapter I and the right to move (Art. 10.4). (c) *Not removed:* a bioregion that leaves in fact and encloses. The federation renounced force (Art. 8.1). What remains is decoupling (Art. 10.3.3) and sanctuary for those who leave. The minority inside loses Chapter I in fact.
* **H4, failure. Both criteria as worded: `RED_WINS`. Revised: `NARROWED`.** (a) "A repeated failed audit under Art. 3.1.6" marks nearly every transition node as failed, since the audit demands "external inputs at zero" and seed nodes import (SIM-QA-31 RED-9). (b) "No body seated for a stated period" lets neighbours take over a community that feeds itself and declines to seat a council; that is RED-7. *Revised:* failure means Tier 1 is in fact not delivered. It is the end state of the ladder already drafted in SIM-QA-30 (shortage certified by an outside panel, shared, reviewed every thirty days) when no recovery follows. The certifying panel is drawn from watersheds that do not border the node, so those who would receive the assets do not decide. Aid comes first (Art. 10.4.4). Administration from outside covers the Tier 1 functions only, for a term, under Art. 10.1.4, and ends when a local body sits. Assets pass to the bioregional commons, since the "local commons" of Art. 2.1.3 no longer exists. The absence of a seated body, alone, triggers nothing. *Residual:* the last few residents of an emptying node keep their dwellings (Art. 2.3); supplying them is costly and the canon accepts the cost, except for the ordered relocation of Art. 2.3.3.

#### **4.4 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-8, the hazard that is not on the list. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Section 4.2. Today the bar can be neither widened nor narrowed. *Guard, before the lock:* Chapter I states the bar by function (research able to cause mass death or irreversible harm) and Organic Law lists the classes.
* **RED-9, expansion as diminution. Open.** Under RED-3 above. Needs a rule on which right gives way, or at least the statement that an expansion may not narrow an existing Chapter I right.
* **RED-10, withdrawal as hold-up. `NARROWED`.** Under H3 above.
* **RED-11, the captured node. `RED_WINS`, residual.** A node feeds its people and violates Chapter I (a sect, a strongman). Art. 10.1.4 gives jurisdiction; a review jury can void its acts (Art. 4.6); nothing follows. Decoupling under Art. 10.3.3 covers hoarding, cartels and water only, and would hit the victims. Force is limited to violence in progress (SIM-QA-37). What the canon offers the people inside is the exit (Art. 8.6.3) and sanctuary elsewhere (Art. 10.4.3). Recorded as a limit of a federation without coercion.
* **RED-12, amendment by jury. Open.** Section 4.1. With no path of correction, interpretation becomes the path, and it has no memory. Touches the D-12 decision (no binding precedent).
* **RED-13, the next generations. Recorded, no verdict.** Chapter I binds people who never agreed to it and cannot change it. The canon chose this over the risk of a majority removing a right. No text reconciles the two.
* **RED-14, a failed tier.** A bioregional assembly of 101 that cannot be filled is not covered by H4, which speaks of nodes. Not treated.

#### **4.5 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`**, on condition that points 1 to 3 are done before the Genesis Lock; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on H2, on exit with territory, on both failure criteria as worded, and on three limits no text removes (a wrong bound in Chapter I; a bioregion that leaves in fact; the captured node).
1. Before the lock, Chapter I goes through the placement test once: rights and bounds stay; numbers beyond the bound, procedures, mechanisms and lists move to Organic Law under the ratchet rule (section 4.2 is the list to decide).
2. Before the lock, the biosecurity bar is stated by function, with the classes in Organic Law.
3. Before the lock, Art. 4.5.1 says that an expansion may be adopted for a term, that its lapse is not a diminution, and that an expansion may not narrow another Chapter I right. New guarantees are tried in Organic Law first.
4. No procedure lowers a Chapter I guarantee after the lock (H2 withdrawn), unless D-35.2 decides otherwise.
5. A bioregion may withdraw from compacts, solidarity, higher assemblies and standards by 75% of its adult residents after notice. It cannot leave Chapter I, enclose the commons, or escape Art. 10.1.4.
6. A node has failed when Tier 1 is in fact not delivered and the shortage ladder brings no recovery, as certified by a panel from non-bordering watersheds. Then: aid, administration of Tier 1 functions for a term, relocation offered, assets to the bioregional commons.

#### **4.6 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
This docket mostly removes words from Chapter I. Art. 1.4.3 holds 141 words; a version keeping the reserve, its bound, the lot and the bar by function would be roughly 40 to 80 (not drafted, so not counted). Chapter I stands at 1,181 of 1,200 with the accepted dockets; the move would leave room for part of what SIM-QA-24, 25, 27, 28, 34 and 41 ask. Point 3 adds a sentence to Art. 4.5.1, in Chapter IV (2,136 of 2,150, 14 left: **conflict**, to the maintainer). Points 5 and 6 belong in Chapter X (870 today, 877 with SIM-QA-30, 935 with SIM-QA-22 D-2: 65 left), with procedure in Organic Law.

#### **4.7 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8; every line needs a decision, since each touches Chapter I)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "seven percent (7%)" as an exact figure | Art. 1.4.3 | kept as a floor ("no less than"); Organic Law may raise it | **kept**, reworded as a bound |
| 2 | "transparent rotational time sortition among applicants without ideological screening" | Art. 1.4.3 | "by lot, without ideological screening" stays; the rest to Organic Law | **moved** in part |
| 3 | the interlock mechanism (tamper-evident, sequence registry) | Art. 1.4.3 | the duty to enforce the bar by technical means without surveillance of persons stays; the mechanism to Organic Law (OL-1 exists) | **moved** |
| 4 | three named classes of barred research | Art. 1.4.3 | a bar by function; classes in Organic Law, which may add | **widened as a prohibition, narrowed as a liberty of the reserve** |
| 5 | Chapter I closed against "any … qualified supermajority" | Art. 1.7.2 | unchanged unless D-35.2 opens a correction path | **weakened**, conditional |
| 6 | "perpetual covenant" | Preamble, Art. 10.6.3 | a withdrawal from compacts and assemblies is allowed | **qualified** |

#### **4.8 Residual Weaknesses**
* The whole answer depends on work done before the lock. Anything missed in the sweep of section 4.2 is permanent afterwards.
* A wrong bound stays wrong.
* The federation has no answer to a bioregion that encloses, or to a node that violates rights while feeding its people, beyond decoupling and sanctuary.
* The notice period for withdrawal and the term of outside administration are not sized.
* The claim that listed-sequence screening can be evaded is from general knowledge and unverified.
* No constitutional lawyer has read this, and Swiss foundation law (the lock itself, SIM-QA-42) is not checked here.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-35.1 to D-35.6):**
* **D-35.1:** accept the six points of section 4.5 as the direction, or change them.
* **D-35.2:** a wrong bound in Chapter I: (a) accept it as permanent and keep Art. 1.7 absolute (H2 withdrawn); (b) open a correction path and accept the back door. The red team supports (a), with the placement move of point 1 done carefully.
* **D-35.3:** the lines of section 4.7, each; in particular line 4 (the bar stated by function).
* **D-35.4:** withdrawal of a bioregion as in point 5, or no exit at all beyond the individual's.
* **D-35.5:** the captured node (RED-11): accept as a limit, or ask for a docket on what the federation may do short of force.
* **D-35.6:** amendment by jury (RED-12): keep D-12 (no precedent), or reopen it for Chapter I only.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 1** (D-35.3, lines 1 to 4): the 7% is kept as a floor, "no less than" (1a B); the reserve is taken from capacity beyond what Tier 1 needs (1b yes); "by lot, without ideological screening" stays and the rest of the allocation procedure goes to Organic Law (1c B); the biosecurity bar is stated by function, with the classes in Organic Law, which may add one only on certification by rival panels (1d B); the duty to enforce the bar by technical means on the hardware, never by surveillance of persons, stays and the mechanism goes to Organic Law (1e B); the last sentence of Art. 1.4.3 is kept (1f). **Item 12:** the texts already accepted for Chapter I are read once more against point 1 before merge (done the same day; result in the batch file). **Still open:** D-35.1 as a whole, D-35.2 (the batch assumed option a; not confirmed), lines 5 and 6 of section 4.7, D-35.4 to D-35.6.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (scope, D-35.2):** option (a). Chapter I stays absolutely closed after the lock; Art. 1.7 is not qualified; **H2 (a correction procedure) is withdrawn**; a wrong bound in Chapter I is accepted as permanent, and the care goes into what enters Chapter I before the lock. Line 5 of section 4.7 (Art. 1.7.2 unchanged) is decided with it. **Still open:** D-35.1 as a whole, line 6 of section 4.7, D-35.4 to D-35.6.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-35.4; conflicts batch, conflict 1, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** option A, **no withdrawal of a bioregion.** In the maintainer's words: the people living in a bioregion may leave, but the nodes (land, houses and the rest) belong to the federation. **Point 5 of section 4.5 is withdrawn**; H3 is refused in both forms (with territory and as withdrawal). Only the individual's exit remains (Art. 10.4). The red team had recommended B (withdrawal without shedding Tier 1 solidarity); not adopted. *Consequences:* the hold-up attack (RED-10) falls with the option; a bioregion bound by a federation rule it finds wrong has no way out but to have the rule changed; a bioregion that leaves in fact and encloses is still the limit recorded in section 4.8. Direction only; no wording.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 11; SIM-QA-38 D-38.6):** a failed audit under Art. 3.1.6 is not failure of a node; failure is Tier 1 in fact not delivered, as in point 6 of section 4.5. This confirms that reading of point 6 only; D-35.1 is not decided by it.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-35.1 to D-35.6; then a redraft of Art. 1.4.3 and the sentence for Art. 4.5.1 as hypothesis, shown beside the current text, with a second red-team pass; align with SIM-QA-42 on who may amend before the first assemblies exist.

## **SIM-QA-36: Personal Status and Family**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction, priority 6 of six. Red team 2026-11-20 (section 4), run on the provisional boundary with SIM-QA-25, which is not confirmed: **`NARROWED`** with six points; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on the forum of H3 as worded, on H1 where persons are apart or cannot declare, and toward the outside of the commons. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20.  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 2.2.2 ("hereditary succession of occupancy among active co-inhabitants", not defined), Art. 2.3.3 ("irreconcilable interpersonal domestic crises" go to a Local Mediation Panel; no rule on who keeps the dwelling), Art. 8.3.2 (after physical violence the harmed person keeps the dwelling), Art. 2.2.1 (personal possessions; nothing on death), Art. 4.2.1 ("no first-degree kin or domestic partners may serve concurrently"), Art. 10.4.2 ("family reunification"), Art. 5.4 (parenting, alloparenting). No article on unions, separation, parentage or adoption.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 2.2.1, 2.2.2, 2.3.3, 4.2.1, 5.4, 8.3.2, 10.4.2.  
* **Boundary with SIM-QA-25 (provisional, for the maintainer):** SIM-QA-25 keeps the child's own rights: not property, a voice that grows with age, corporal punishment, choice of teacher. This docket takes status between adults, parentage and adoption as status, the dwelling on separation or death, and personal possessions at death. Cross-references: SIM-QA-24 RED-1 (a fellowship's forum ruling on marriage, custody or inheritance), SIM-QA-29 (what the civic register may hold).

### **1. The Friction**
* The canon uses kin, partners, family and co-inhabitants in four articles and defines none.
* The dwelling passes to "active co-inhabitants"; who they are decides who stays in a home after a death.
* When a household separates without violence, nothing says who keeps the dwelling.
* Personal possessions are inviolable in life; nothing says where they go at death.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text)*
* **RED-1, the succession claim:** after a death, a distant relative, a friend who lived there and a person who arrived the week before all claim the dwelling.
* **RED-2, separation:** both partners claim the home; the panel of Art. 2.3.3 has no criterion.
* **RED-3, status by fellowship:** with no civil rule, religious or clan forums define union, parentage and inheritance (SIM-QA-24 RED-1).
* **RED-4, disputed parentage:** two adults claim a child, or an adoption is contested; no rule and no forum; papers of the existing state may say otherwise during the transition.
* **RED-5, possessions at death:** tools and works are taken by whoever is nearest; or an estate of tools accumulates across generations against the limit on redundant units in Art. 2.2.1.
* **RED-6, over-regulation:** defining unions gives assemblies a hold on private intimacy (Art. 1.2.3); a register of unions is a sensitive register (SIM-QA-23, Art. 7.4.4).
* **RED-7, a rule nobody can apply:** the bar on kin and partners serving together (Art. 4.2.1) needs data that no register holds.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(proposed mitigations, untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, no civil status:** the Constitution recognises no status of union; every consequence attaches to facts the persons themselves declare (living together for a stated time, caring for a child).
* **H2, the declared household:** persons may declare a household; "active co-inhabitant" is defined by length of residence; on separation, criteria fixed in advance (where the children live, need, length of residence) applied by the Local Mediation Panel, and the person who leaves receives an equivalent dwelling (Art. 2.3.4).
* **H3, parentage and adoption:** recorded as a relation of care in the sealed records of SIM-QA-29; disputes go to a drawn tribunal that hears the child (SIM-QA-25).
* **H4, possessions at death:** the person's written wish holds for personal possessions; failing that, co-inhabitants, then the commons; no wish disposes of a dwelling or any usufruct.

**Next action (Stage 1):** maintainer to confirm the boundary with SIM-QA-25 (**still open**; the red team below ran on the provisional boundary by maintainer instruction of 2026-11-20, see D-36.2); ~~red team H1 to H4 against RED-1 to RED-7~~ (done, section 4); ~~Chapter II word count~~ (section 4.5).

### **4. Stage 2: Red Team** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; run on the provisional boundary with SIM-QA-25, which is not confirmed; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no family lawyer or child-protection practitioner has read it; no article text drafted)*

#### **4.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 2.2.2, "hereditary succession of occupancy among active co-inhabitants", has two readings.** "Hereditary" points to blood; "active co-inhabitants" points to residence. Kin who live elsewhere inherit under the first and not under the second.
* **Succession of occupancy is not a contest.** It is occupancy, not ownership: everyone who lives there stays. RED-1 only arises between people who do not want to live together, and that is a separation.
* **Art. 2.3.4 gives whoever leaves "an equivalent or superior dwelling" first.** The stakes of a separation are therefore lower than under property law. The same rule means that with no free dwelling in the node, nobody can leave a home in crisis.
* **Three articles attach an advantage to a family tie:** priority in residence at Amber Status for "family reunification" (Art. 10.4.2), release from duties for "new parents" (Art. 5.4.3), exemption for "family … caregiving" (Art. 5.6.1). An advantage tied to a status invites a false claim to it.
* **Art. 8.4.2 ends a restorative circle in "binding covenants".** A ruling of a fellowship's forum can enter the civil order by that door.
* **Art. 8.4.4 gives tribunals no jurisdiction over parentage or the care of a child** (SIM-QA-33). The forum H3 relies on does not exist under the current text.
* **Arts. 8.6.3 and 10.4.1 let any person leave and travel without checkpoints.** Nothing says whether a parent may take a child along against the other parent's will.
* **Compelled genetic testing is barred** by Arts. 1.2.2 and 7.4.4 (no biometric data). A parentage dispute cannot be settled by an ordered test.
* **No one is named to speak for an adult who cannot speak** (unconscious, demented). "Next of kin" does not exist in the canon.

#### **4.2 Verdicts on the Hypotheses**
* **H0, silence: `RED_WINS`.** RED-2, RED-3 and RED-4 succeed as written.
* **H1, no civil status: `BLUE_HOLDS` against RED-6; `NARROWED` against RED-3; `RED_WINS` on one point as worded.** No status of union means no register of unions and no assembly ruling on intimacy. Against RED-3: with a dwelling, Tier 1 and an exit for every adult, a fellowship's ruling on marriage has little to hold a person with; the guard still needed is that no forum except a drawn body decides a dwelling, the care of a child or possessions, and that no covenant under Art. 8.4.2 can carry such a ruling or waive a Chapter I right. *Residual:* social pressure (SIM-QA-24). *Where H1 fails as worded:* "facts the persons themselves declare" works for people who live together and fails for the two cases where they do not: reunification (the persons are apart) and speaking for someone who cannot declare anything. See RED-9 and RED-11.
* **H2, the declared household: `NARROWED`.** *RED-1:* settled by a minimum time of residence (same guard as SIM-QA-34 RED-9) and by the reading in section 4.1; everyone who qualifies stays. *RED-2, separation:* criteria fixed in advance hold, with four attacks. (a) Children become the way to win the home; the guard is that the stakes stay low: the equivalent dwelling for whoever leaves is in the same node where capacity allows, so a child keeps both. (b) A panel of three from a small node knows both parties; either may ask for a panel drawn from outside (the device accepted in SIM-QA-19). (c) No free dwelling: Art. 2.3.4 then keeps two people in a home in crisis. An interim room at once (the hospitality space of Art. 10.4.3) and priority on the list of SIM-QA-34. (d) A sham separation to obtain a second dwelling (RED-8). *RED-6 and RED-7:* a declared household is a record of who lives where, which the dwelling list needs anyway; it must record residence and never the nature of a relationship, in the sealed records of SIM-QA-29 under the purpose rule of SIM-QA-41. The bar of Art. 4.2.1 can then be applied to persons of one household, plus first-degree kin who declare themselves when drawn, open to challenge. *Residual:* partners who do not live together are not caught.
* **H3, parentage and adoption: `RED_WINS` on the forum as worded; revised: `NARROWED`.** A tribunal of fifteen from adjacent districts, limited to "strictly verifiable physical evidence" (Art. 8.4.3) and without jurisdiction (section 4.1), cannot decide the care of a child. *Revised:* a relation of care is recorded from birth (the birth mother, and whoever is declared with her); a dispute, an adoption or a change goes to a panel drawn from outside the node that hears the child, and its decisions can be reviewed as the child grows, since nothing about a child is final. No test is compelled (section 4.1), so care that was given decides, not descent. More than two carers can be recorded; when they disagree the panel decides. *Residuals:* the weight of a genetic parent who never cared, and the child's knowledge of their origin, go to SIM-QA-25; adoption during the transition follows state law (SIM-QA-40).
* **H4, possessions at death: `NARROWED`.** A written wish holds for personal possessions and never for a dwelling or any usufruct. *Attacks:* pressure on a dying person; a wish used to reward a drawn member (SIM-QA-33); tools piling up across generations, answered by the redundancy clause of Art. 2.2.1 (redundant units go to the tool library); heirs outside the commons during the transition, where state inheritance law, including shares reserved to kin, applies whatever the wish says (SIM-QA-40, SIM-QA-42). Who certifies a death and keeps the wish is not stated; the sealed register of SIM-QA-29 is the candidate.

#### **4.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-8, the sham separation. `NARROWED`.** A household "separates" to hold two dwellings. *Guard:* when the two households join again within a set time, one dwelling returns to the list.
* **RED-9, the false family tie. `NARROWED`.** Priority for "family reunification" (Art. 10.4.2) goes to whoever claims a tie. With no status, anyone can be named; with a status, a register of kinship is needed. *Guard:* each resident may name a small, fixed number of persons to join them; a past shared household or a recorded relation of care counts without limit. The cap is for Organic Law and is not sized.
* **RED-10, leaving with the child. `NARROWED` inside the commons; `RED_WINS` toward the outside.** A parent takes the child to another basin, or out of the commons. *Guard:* a child's residence moves between nodes only with the consent of every recorded carer or by decision of the panel; nodes share the record of care (the replicated register of SIM-QA-29), so the carer left behind can act where the child now is. There are no checkpoints (Art. 10.4.1) and none are proposed. Outside the commons only state law reaches (SIM-QA-40).
* **RED-11, who speaks for a person who cannot. `RED_WINS` under current text.** *Guard to test:* every adult may name, and change at any time, the person who speaks for them in care when they cannot; the same naming serves for possessions at death and for RED-9. Choice replaces status. Without a naming: the co-inhabitants, then a person drawn by lot (compare the Advocate of SIM-QA-29).
* **RED-12, a child in danger tonight. On the boundary, not treated.** Nobody is named to take a child out of a dangerous home at once, and no guarantee exists of the kind Art. 8.5.3 gives an adult in custody (validation within 72 hours, review). It sits between SIM-QA-25 (the child's rights), SIM-QA-39 (protection without an offence) and SIM-QA-37 (who acts). For the maintainer to assign (D-36.2).
* **RED-13, no age of consent. On the boundary, not treated.** With no status of union there is no "marriage" of a minor, and the canon has no age below which an adult's relation with a young person is barred. Belongs to SIM-QA-25 and, as an act, to SIM-QA-33.
* **RED-14, state status during the transition. Cross-docket.** Residents will be married, divorced or registered as parents under state law. The commons neither recognises nor forbids it; where state law gives a spouse or a parent a claim (to a home, to an estate, over a child), it prevails until a phase gate (SIM-QA-40).

#### **4.4 What Survives** *(the rule as a set of points; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the six points below, on the provisional boundary; **`RED_WINS`** on silence, on the forum of H3 as worded, on H1 where persons are apart or cannot declare, and toward the outside of the commons (a child taken out; heirs under state law).
1. **No civil status of union.** The commons records residence and care, never the nature of a relationship. A union under a fellowship or a state has no effect in the commons. Only drawn bodies decide a dwelling, the care of a child or possessions, and no covenant waives a Chapter I right.
2. **Household.** The persons recorded as living in a dwelling, in sealed records used for stated purposes only. A co-inhabitant is a person who has lived there for a minimum time. At a death all co-inhabitants stay.
3. **Separation.** Whoever leaves receives an equivalent dwelling, in the same node where capacity allows, with an interim room at once. Who stays is decided by a panel of three, from outside on request, on criteria fixed in advance, the children's residence first.
4. **Named persons.** Every adult may name, and change at any time, who speaks for them when they cannot, who receives their possessions, and a capped number of persons they ask to join them.
5. **Care of a child.** A recorded relation of care, never an ordered test. Disputes, adoption and changes go to a panel drawn from outside that hears the child and can be asked again. A child's residence moves between nodes only with the carers' consent or the panel's decision.
6. **Possessions at death.** The written wish; then the named person or the co-inhabitants; then the commons. Never a dwelling or a usufruct.

#### **4.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
Under the placement test most of this is procedure for Organic Law. What stays in the Constitution: no forum but drawn bodies and no waiver (point 1), the definition of co-inhabitant as a bound (point 2), the right to name a person (point 4), and the panel that hears the child (point 5). No text is drafted.

| V | 855 | 14 to 44, **within fifty** | SIM-QA-22, SIM-QA-31 |
| I | 808 | 19 of 1,200 with accepted dockets | SIM-QA-24, 25, 27, 28, 34, 41; SIM-QA-35 would free words |

Three dockets now ask for the remaining room in Chapter II. The conflict goes to the maintainer (D-36.6).

| 1 | "hereditary succession of occupancy among active co-inhabitants" | Art. 2.2.2 | read as succession by residence; kin living elsewhere do not succeed; a minimum time applies | **clarified**, and **qualified** by the minimum time |

#### **4.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* The boundary with SIM-QA-25 is not confirmed, and two matters found on it are not treated (RED-12, RED-13).
* Everything that reaches outside the commons is governed by state law during the transition.
* A record of who lives where and who cares for whom is sensitive even when sealed; this docket adds to what SIM-QA-29 and SIM-QA-41 must protect.
* The minimum time of residence, the cap on named persons and the time limits are not sized.
* Social pressure inside a fellowship is untouched.
* No family lawyer, mediator or child-protection practitioner has read this.

Next of kin by rank is not used. The other two uses of naming (possessions at death; persons asked to join) stay open with D-36.4. **Item 1e:** the rest of the subject goes to a new docket, SIM-QA-43.
