## **SIM-QA-37: Physical Force Inside the Commons**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED`** with seven revised points; **`RED_WINS`** on point 1 as worded for violence in progress, on point 4 as worded for what it leaves out, on firearms, on weapons made at home and on an organised armed group. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. Reasoning on text; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass, no specialist review. No article text is drafted.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 8.5.3 (emergency detention validated by fifteen citizens within 72 hours; the Invariants Guardian, an "independent sortition guardian", protects the detained person), Art. 8.6.2 (60% to issue or renew custody; 50%+1 for the 72-hour detention "following active violence in flagrante delicto"), Art. 8.5.2 (custody for "predatory violence resisting stabilization"), Art. 8.1.2 (Civic Peace Guilds: "Unarmed practitioners trained in interpersonal mediation, de-escalation, and restorative trauma triage"), Art. 8.1.1 ("Stockpiling firearms, munitions, explosives, or neuro-irritants by civic personnel is unconstitutional"), Art. 8.1.3 (no weaponry or robotics "intended to kill or injure persons"), Art. 7.1.2 (no "coercive force against persons" delegated to autonomous systems), Art. 8.3.2 (the transgressor is relocated), Art. 8.4.3 (tribunal of fifteen; "Adversarial courtroom trials are abolished"). No article names who carries out a detention or a relocation. No right of self-defence is written.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 2.2.1, 3.3.3, 4.2.1, 5.2, 7.1.2, 8.1.1, 8.1.2, 8.1.3, 8.3.2, 8.4.3, 8.5, 8.6.2.  
* **Source notes (citation check):** (1) Art. 8.1.1 bars *stockpiling* by civic personnel; it does not say in terms that they may hold nothing. (2) "SIM-QA-22 item 3" is decision D-3, drafted in SIM-QA-22 section 8.3: "Guilds may separate a person from immediate danger but hold no power to detain." Its red team already recorded the gap as residual C-2 ("if guilds cannot detain, no one can"). (3) The public log without names and the review of each restraint by drawn citizens are already drafted in D-3; they are not repeated here. (4) Art. 8.5.3 already gives a detained person a guardian drawn by lot; the drawn defender of the direction has to be placed beside it.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-22 D-3 (section 8.3, residuals C-1 to C-3), SIM-QA-32 (what a guild is), SIM-QA-33 (distance measures without violence), SIM-QA-18 (refusal of a summons), SIM-QA-41 (anonymous makers of published files), SIM-QA-40 (during the transition, force stays with the state).

### **1. The Friction**
* The canon provides for detention and for the relocation of a violent person, and names nobody to do either.
* The only public-safety body that deals with persons is unarmed and trained to mediate. The sentence marked for restoration (D-3) removes detention power from guilds and gives it to no one.
* A person attacked may defend themselves in fact; the Constitution does not say so.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text; the last six are the residuals the direction leaves to test)*
* **RED-1, nobody acts:** violence in progress; the mediators hold no power to restrain → bystanders improvise force with no rule, or the detention of Art. 8.5.3 never happens.
* **RED-2, the ruling nobody carries out:** a tribunal relocates a transgressor under Art. 8.3.2; the person refuses.
* **RED-3, the core becomes a police:** a permanent trained core is a police force under another name (Art. 8.1.1).
* **RED-4, creep:** force reaches work shifts, dwelling area (Art. 5.2) or any ruling at all.
* **RED-5, firearms:** an armed person; means that immobilise without injuring do not suffice.
* **RED-6, weapons made at home:** personal craft tools (Art. 2.2.1) are outside the publication rule of Art. 3.3.3, which covers communal machinery only.
* **RED-7, the machine decides:** a remotely operated machine drifts into acting alone (Art. 7.1.2), or injures (Art. 8.1.3).
* **RED-8, defence as licence:** a written right of defence is used to justify retaliation or to arm.
* **RED-9, drawn helpers:** citizens drawn at each intervention are untrained, are hurt, refuse (SIM-QA-18), or cannot arrive within minutes.
* **RED-10, the drawn defender:** a lay defender is weaker than a practised accuser; or the same willing few become professionals in fact; overlap with the guardian of Art. 8.5.3.
* **RED-11, the sentence to restore:** the D-3 draft ("hold no power to detain") contradicts the direction and needs rewording.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on canon text)*
* **D-37.1, direction:**
  1. Who acts: a small trained core of the Peace Guild, joined at each intervention by citizens drawn by lot.
  2. Means: only means that immobilise without injuring (Art. 8.1.3).
  3. Machines: remotely operated machines are allowed; a human always decides (Art. 7.1.2).
  4. Cases: physical force in two cases only: stopping violence in progress, and carrying out a ruling of the tribunal of fifteen that concerns violence (for example Art. 8.3.2). Never for work shifts or dwelling area (Art. 5.2).
  5. Record and review: every use logged publicly without names and reviewed by a drawn jury (as drafted in SIM-QA-22 D-3).
  6. Self-defence: a right of immediate, proportionate defence of oneself or another, written in the Constitution.
  7. Defender: in proceedings, a right to a defender drawn by lot, never a professional.
* **Residuals named by the maintainer, to test:** firearms (RED-5); personal craft tools escaping publication (RED-6); the rewording of the sentence to restore (RED-11).

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team D-37.1 points 1 to 7 against RED-1 to RED-11~~ (done, section 5); reword the D-3 sentence in SIM-QA-22 once D-37.2 and D-37.3 are decided (what it must say: section 5.2, RED-11); ~~Chapter VIII word count~~ (section 5.5).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass; no one trained in restraint, policing or criminal defence has read it; no article text drafted)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 1.2.2 prohibits "physical subjugation" permanently, and Art. 1.7.2 voids anything contrary to Chapter I.** Restraining a person is physical subjugation. On the words, the custody of Art. 8.5 is already in conflict with Chapter I, and so is every point of this direction. SIM-QA-39 recorded the same for isolation. This has to be settled in Chapter I before the Genesis Lock (SIM-QA-35 point 1); afterwards it cannot be.
* **Art. 8.1.1 bars "stockpiling" by "civic personnel", and Art. 8.1.3 bars designing, manufacturing or compiling weapons.** Nothing bars a private person from possessing or carrying a weapon that already exists (RED-18).
* **Art. 8.4.4 gives tribunals four matters: assault, somatic violation, enclosure, ecological poisoning.** The direction allows force only for rulings "that concern violence". Rulings on enclosure and on poisoning would then be carried out by no one.
* **Art. 8.4.3 abolishes adversarial trials, and the canon has no accuser** (SIM-QA-33). A "defender" has no opponent. The nearest figure is the guardian of Art. 8.5.3, who "protects somatic well-being, expressive silence, and exculpatory telemetry" of a detained person.
* **Art. 8.4.1 puts an "unarmed mediator" at the first stage of every dispute.** Under the direction, the mediators and the people who restrain belong to the same guild.
* **Arts. 1.2.2 and 8.5.2 bar "pharmacological manipulation" and "chemical blunting".** A person in an acute crisis cannot be sedated without consent; physical restraint is then the only means and lasts longer (RED-16).
* **Art. 8.2.1 stops every machine "upon human approach".** A machine that restrains must approach and touch (SIM-QA-31 point 4, which also requires consent: restraint has none).

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **Point 1, who acts: `RED_WINS` as worded for violence in progress; revised: `NARROWED`.** Violence in progress needs an answer in minutes; a draw "at each intervention" cannot deliver people in that time (RED-9). Those it delivers are untrained, and restraint by untrained hands is where people are hurt, on both sides (general knowledge of restraint injuries; `PROVISIONAL — UNVERIFIED`). *Revised:* in the first minutes whoever is present acts under the defence of point 6, and trained members on call follow. Citizens are drawn for a short on-call term, with training first and a hiatus after (Art. 4.3.3), and attend as witnesses and as help. Any drawn citizen may decline to lay hands on a person (Art. 1.4.1, conscience). For a ruling carried out at a planned time, drawn citizens are always present. *RED-3, a police by another name:* a standing group with the only training in force is a police in function. What differs is what it may not do: no weapons, no investigation, no holding on suspicion, force only in the listed cases, every use logged and reviewed. The rules of SIM-QA-32 apply (a mandate with a term, coordinators who rotate, audit by outside peers), and a member who restrains never mediates the same case (RED-14). `NARROWED`; the function exists and the text should not pretend otherwise. *Residuals:* a node of sixty has one or two trained members and at night has neighbours; those who do not decline are a self-selected few.
* **Point 2, means: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` on firearms and on weapons made at home.** No hold can promise not to injure. The rule that can be kept is about design and intent, in the words of Art. 8.1.3: means designed and used to immobilise, never to injure, the least that works, with a list in Organic Law (the model is OL-7) and a review of every injury. No drugs and no irritants (section 5.1). *RED-5, firearms:* against a person who shoots, shields, barriers, evacuation and a remote machine are what the direction allows. People can die before the person is held. During the transition this is the state's matter (SIM-QA-40); after it, the canon accepts the risk by Art. 8.1.3, and no hypothesis here removes it. *RED-6, weapons made at home:* making one is barred (Art. 8.1.3) and has no forum (SIM-QA-33); personal tools are outside the publication rule and homes are not inspected (Art. 1.2.3). It cannot be detected before use. Both are limits of a commons without weapons and without surveillance.
* **Point 3, machines: `NARROWED`, with the largest residual of the docket.** *RED-7:* the human decides to engage, and the grip is then a control loop; it needs the force-limiting supervisor of SIM-QA-31 point 1, an exception to Art. 8.2.1 and an exception to the consent rule of SIM-QA-31 point 4. *RED-15 (new):* a body that needs many cooperating people to coerce can be refused by them. Machines of restraint remove that brake: a few operators can hold many. Operating at a distance also lowers the operator's own restraint. *Guards to test:* one operator for one machine; short range, no network control (a hijacked machine, SIM-QA-09); activation only with keys held by drawn citizens, on the model of the dual key of Art. 8.1.4; a capped number per node; every activation in the log. Whether these are enough is for the maintainer (D-37.5).
* **Point 4, cases: `NARROWED` against creep; `RED_WINS` as worded on what the two cases leave out.** *Creep (RED-4):* a closed list in the Constitution, with the rule that force outside it is assault and the person who used it answers like anyone else, with no immunity. *Left out:* (a) holding the person after the violence stops, until the validation of Art. 8.5.3; (b) violence about to begin (a person advancing with a weapon); "in progress" waits for the first blow; (c) an act in progress that endangers life without violence against a person: poisoning a water source, or sitting in the control room of a life-support plant and refusing to move (RED-12); (d) rulings on enclosure and poisoning (section 5.1); (e) cases other dockets may need: the persistent author and the preparation of mass harm (SIM-QA-33, D-33.3 and D-33.4), isolation and the protective hold (SIM-QA-39). Each addition is the creep the list exists to prevent, so each is a separate decision (D-37.3). *RED-2:* a relocated person who returns is moved again, each time by force; the only end the canon has is custody, limited to "predatory violence resisting stabilization" (Art. 8.5.2). `NARROWED`.
* **Point 5, record and review: `NARROWED`,** as in SIM-QA-22 section 8.3 (C-1 to C-3). One addition: with no recording of persons (Art. 8.2.2), the reviewing panel has only accounts. The drawn witnesses of point 1 are then the independent account, which is the strongest reason to keep them.
* **Point 6, self-defence: `NARROWED`.** *RED-8:* "immediate" excludes retaliation. "Defence of another" can be claimed by a group that patrols. *Guards:* the right is a justification for an act, weighed afterwards by the tribunal; it gives no right to arm in advance, to patrol, to pursue or to organise. It covers persons, not possessions. *Open:* proportionate defence against a lethal attack may injure or kill; the direction does not say so, and a reader will ask. *RED-17 (new):* every restrained person will claim defence against the intervention itself; a rule is needed on force used against an intervention inside the listed cases. *Placement:* written in Chapter I it is frozen for good (SIM-QA-35); as a rule on how an act is judged it fits Chapter VIII.
* **Point 7, the defender: `NARROWED`.** *RED-10:* with no accuser (section 5.1) the defender's task is the guardian's task: to see that the person's side and the evidence in their favour are before the tribunal. One drawn figure can do both; the guardian of Art. 8.5.3 is extended from detained persons to every person before a tribunal. The Peace Guild appears in every case and learns; a drawn defender appears once. *Guards:* drawn from outside the node; the person may refuse one draw; the person may also bring someone they trust, unpaid; a limit on how often the same person serves, so that no profession forms. *Residual:* the difference in experience remains.
* **RED-1, nobody acts: `NARROWED`** by points 1 and 6 together. **RED-11, the sentence to restore:** the D-3 sentence ("Guilds may separate a person from immediate danger but hold no power to detain") must be replaced by one that says who may restrain, in which cases, and that a hold beyond the first hours falls under Art. 8.5.3. It is not redrafted here because its content depends on D-37.2 and D-37.3.

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-12, passive obstruction of life support.** Under point 4 above. Protest is protected (Art. 1.4.1); a water plant is Tier 1.
* **RED-14, mediator and restrainer in one guild.** Under point 1.
* **RED-15, machines remove the need for cooperating people.** Under point 3.
* **RED-16, no sedation. Open, with SIM-QA-39.** Physical restraint of a person in an acute crisis is the riskiest kind and the canon leaves no other means.
* **RED-17, force against the intervention.** Under point 6.
* **RED-18, possession and carrying of weapons. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Section 5.1. Whether a private person may keep or carry a weapon is not stated anywhere.
* **RED-19, the transition. Cross-docket.** Until a phase gate, force belongs to the state (SIM-QA-40, D-40.1). A node that restrains commits an offence under state law outside what that law allows any citizen. This docket describes the rule after the gate.
* **RED-20, an organised armed group. Not treated.** Art. 8.1.4 answers uncrewed machines only. A few trained members and drawn citizens do not answer armed people acting together. Recorded as outside this docket.

#### **5.4 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the seven points below; **`RED_WINS`** on point 1 as worded for violence in progress, on point 4 as worded for what it leaves out, on firearms, on weapons made at home, and on an organised armed group.
1. **Who.** Trained members of the Peace Guild on call, under the guild rules of SIM-QA-32. Citizens drawn for a short on-call term, trained first, present as witnesses and as help, free to decline to lay hands. Whoever restrains does not mediate the same case. In the first minutes, anyone present under point 6.
2. **Means.** Designed and used to immobilise, never to injure; the least that works; a list in Organic Law; no drugs, no irritants; every injury reviewed.
3. **Machines.** Remotely operated, one operator each, short range, behind a force limit, activated with keys held by drawn citizens, few in number. Never acting alone.
4. **Cases.** A closed list in the Constitution. Force outside it is assault, with no immunity.
5. **Record.** The log and review of SIM-QA-22 D-3; the drawn witnesses report to the reviewing panel.
6. **Defence.** A justification for immediate, proportionate defence of a person, weighed afterwards. No right to arm, patrol, pursue or organise.
7. **Guardian and defender.** One figure drawn from outside the node for every person before a tribunal; the person may refuse one draw and may bring someone they trust.

#### **5.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
Every point concerns a power over persons or a right, so the core stays in the Constitution, in Chapter VIII. Chapter VIII holds 862 words, 902 with SIM-QA-22 D-3, leaving 98. SIM-QA-33 asks for the same room. Seven points and a closed list of cases will not fit in 98 words together with SIM-QA-33; nothing is drafted, so no count exists. The conflict goes to the maintainer: an own ceiling for Chapter VIII by separate decision (instruction 10a point 4), which SIM-QA-33 O-2 also raised.

#### **5.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "physical subjugation" is "permanently prohibited" | Art. 1.2.2 | restraint in listed cases must be stated as compatible, before the lock | **weakened**, or clarified if read as already allowing Art. 8.5 |
| 2 | Peace Guilds are "Unarmed practitioners trained in interpersonal mediation, de-escalation, and restorative trauma triage" | Art. 8.1.2 | some members are also trained to restrain | **weakened** as a guarantee that no body of force exists |
| 3 | machinery stops "upon human approach" | Art. 8.2.1 | machines of restraint excepted | **weakened** (with SIM-QA-31) |
| 4 | "Guilds … hold no power to detain" | SIM-QA-22 D-3 draft, not canon | reversed for the Peace Guild | draft changed |
| 5 | weapons and robotics "intended to kill or injure" are barred "to humans and machines alike" | Art. 8.1.3 | unchanged; point 6 must say whether a defender who injures is covered | **to clarify** |

#### **5.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* A person with a firearm, a weapon made at home, and an armed group are not answered.
* A standing trained group exists; the checks on it are rules, and rules erode.
* Machines of restraint are the means by which a small group could hold a large one.
* Restraint without sedation carries its own risk of harm.
* Nothing is measured: response times, the number of trained members a node needs, the load on drawn citizens.
* No one trained in restraint, policing, emergency psychiatry or criminal defence has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-37.2 to D-37.9):**
* **D-37.2:** point 1 as revised (drawn citizens on a short on-call term, trained, present as witnesses and help), or as given.
* **D-37.3:** the closed list of cases. To the two given, add or refuse each of: (a) holding until the validation of Art. 8.5.3; (b) violence about to begin; (c) an act in progress that endangers life (poisoning; obstruction of life support); (d) rulings on enclosure and poisoning; (e) the cases of SIM-QA-33 and SIM-QA-39, when those are decided. The red team supports (a) and (b), and (c) limited to danger to life.
* **D-37.4:** firearms and weapons made at home: accept as residuals; and decide whether the Constitution bars a private person from keeping or carrying a weapon (RED-18).
* **D-37.5:** machines of restraint: allow with the guards of point 3, or allow none beyond shields and barriers.
* **D-37.6:** self-defence in Chapter VIII as a rule of judgment, for persons only; and say whether it covers a defender who injures.
* **D-37.7:** one drawn figure, guardian and defender, with a person of trust allowed.
* **D-37.8:** "physical subjugation" in Art. 1.2.2: reword before the lock, with SIM-QA-35 and SIM-QA-39.
* **D-37.9:** an own ceiling for Chapter VIII, with SIM-QA-33.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 2** (D-37.8): option B, Chapter I itself names a closed list of cases in which a person may be held, and prohibits holding a person outside them; the guarantees stay in Chapter VIII. **Cases accepted for that list** (D-37.3 in part): violence against a person in progress; violence about to begin; holding until the validation of Art. 8.5.3; custody under Art. 8.5; carrying out a ruling of a tribunal that concerns violence; an act in progress that endangers the life of others, limited to danger to life; the protective hold of SIM-QA-39 (item 4). **Not accepted:** enforced isolation (item 3, O-A). **Open:** preparation of mass harm (D-33.4; no pick was given); rulings on enclosure and poisoning (D-37.3 d). **Item 11** (D-37.6 in part): self-defence is a rule of judgment in Chapter VIII, not a right in Chapter I; whether it covers a defender who injures stays open. **Still open:** D-37.2, D-37.4, D-37.5, D-37.7, D-37.9.

**Note 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch 2):** the Chapter I list of cases gains two: stopping the making or use of a means of mass death, for the time needed to take it away (item 3, D-33.4); and keeping from immediate harm a person who cannot judge the danger, with a jury finding before any continuing restriction (item 1d). Enforced isolation stays refused; the protective hold stays open until its reading. D-37.3 (d), rulings on enclosure and poisoning, is still open.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-37.5; conflicts batch, conflict 3, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** **no machine of restraint.** Only passive means (shields, barriers, doors); people restrain people. **Point 3 of section 5.4 is withdrawn.** The red team's pick, adopted. Art. 8.2.1 and SIM-QA-31 point 4 stand without exception. *Cost accepted:* against a person with a firearm, people stand behind shields and others may be hurt before the person is held (RED-5, already a limit under Art. 8.1.3). Direction only; no wording.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-37.2 to D-37.9; then the D-3 sentence and the article text as hypothesis, shown beside the current text, with a second red-team pass; reading by people with practice in restraint and in defence.

## **SIM-QA-38: Rich and Poor Basins**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED` on text, with feasibility unknown** until SIMONE has run RED-3 and RED-4; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on five points. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. No article text is drafted. No number is given: every quantity is for SIMONE (instruction 7), still `NOT_RUN`.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 3.1.6 (Tier 1 demand met "from local resources and installed capacity alone, with external inputs at zero"), Art. 3.6.2 (75% "to alter, diminish, or recalculate per capita Tier 1 survival allocations"), Art. 4.5.1 (75% for "modifications to the biological survival baseline"), Art. 10.3 (bilateral physical compacts; decoupling "until reciprocity is restored"), Art. 10.4.4 (mutual aid binding on neighbours "When a watershed incurs emergency Red Status"), Art. 3.1.4 ("society's non-negotiable ceiling"), Art. 3.3.1 ("The absolute, non-negotiable priority"), Art. 10.4.3 (no involuntary deportation), Art. 1.6.2 (exhaustion of non-renewable aquifers and irreversible destruction of habitats are crimes), Art. 4.2.4 (Continental Chambers coordinate "macro-balances"), Art. 4.2.5 (the Global Commons Assembly sets "planetary thermodynamic boundaries").  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.3, 1.6.2, 3.1.4, 3.1.6, 3.3.1, 3.5, 3.6.2, 4.2.4, 4.2.5, 4.5.1, 10.3, 10.4.3, 10.4.4, 10.6.2.  
* **Source note (citation check):** Art. 3.6.2 fixes the threshold for changing Tier 1 allocations; Art. 3.6.1 names only neighbourhood and bioregional bodies. That each bioregion sets its own level is the reading that follows; no article says it in terms, and none sets a common floor.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-30 D-30.7 and D-30.8 (in a transition node a certified shortage is shared and the Tier 1 floor is qualified: there the floor gives; here the direction lets the ceiling give for a limited time: the two must be reconciled), SIM-QA-10 (autarkic closure), SIM-QA-16 (inter-basin compact), SIM-QA-22 D-2 (inter-basin forum; compensation in physical flows, OL-14), SIM-QA-23 (equal protection across regions), SIM-QA-42 (the Foundation's inter-node equalisation in fiat during the transition, Statutes Art. 2.2).

### **1. The Friction**
* Tier 1 is covered locally and measured locally. A poor basin and a rich one can hold different floors, and place of birth then decides the level of survival.
* Exchange is reciprocal: a basin with nothing to offer receives nothing. Aid is owed only at Red Status.
* The ecological ceiling and the Tier 1 floor are both called non-negotiable. Where a basin cannot meet the floor inside its ceiling, the canon gives no order between them.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text or under the direction)*
* **RED-1, unequal floors:** a basin recalculates its Tier 1 downward at 75% under Art. 3.6.2.
* **RED-2, nothing to trade:** Art. 10.3 gives a basin without surplus no claim; Art. 10.3.3 can cut it off.
* **RED-3, the share donors can carry** *(residual for SIMONE)*: standing contributions erode the donors' own Tier 2; donors refuse.
* **RED-4, transport** *(residual for SIMONE)*: moving water, food or energy costs more exergy than it delivers beyond some distance.
* **RED-5, standing dependence:** a basin above its carrying capacity stays there because transfers arrive.
* **RED-6, relocation by pressure:** relocation is "offered" and is the only real option.
* **RED-7, the excess that stays:** a time-limited excess is renewed again and again; a regenerating resource is pushed past recovery and the damage becomes irreversible (Art. 1.6.2); the certifier is captured.
* **RED-8, one floor for every climate:** a worldwide floor in physical measures fits the tropics and the Arctic differently.
* **RED-9, apportionment by the recipients:** a Continental Chamber with a majority from receiving basins votes itself the transfers.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on canon text)*
* **D-38.1, direction:**
  1. One worldwide Tier 1 floor in physical measures, set by the Global Commons Assembly; bioregions may go above it, never below.
  2. Standing solidarity with nothing in return: basins with surplus beyond their own Tier 1 and Tier 2 contribute a share in kind, apportioned by the Continental Chamber, separate from Art. 10.3.
  3. Voluntary relocation is offered, never forced (Art. 10.4.3).
  4. When ceiling and floor collide: transfers first; then a time-limited excess with a recovery plan certified from outside the basin, on regenerating resources only; never irreversible damage (Art. 1.6.2).
* **Residuals named by the maintainer, to test in SIMONE:** the maximum share donors can carry (RED-3); transport cost limits (RED-4).

**Next action (Stage 1):** SIMONE runs for RED-3 and RED-4 before any figure is written (**still `NOT_RUN`**); ~~red team D-38.1 against RED-1 to RED-9~~ (done on text, section 5); ~~reconcile point 4 with SIM-QA-30 D-30.7 and D-30.8~~ (proposed order in section 5.2, point 4, for the maintainer).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; **no quantity is given and none is implied: RED-3 and RED-4 remain `NOT_RUN`**, so whether the direction is physically feasible is not known; first pass, no specialist review; no article text drafted)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist, Thermodynamic architect)*
* **The collision of point 4 is inside Chapter I.** Art. 1.3 guarantees the baseline to "every inhabitant". Art. 1.6.1 says "Human metabolisms must operate within the … carrying capacity of local watersheds". Both are provisions of Chapter I and Art. 1.7.2 forbids diminishing either. A rule placed in Chapter III or X that says which gives way would be void. The order has to be written in Chapter I, before the Genesis Lock (SIM-QA-35 point 1).
* **Art. 3.1.6 requires Tier 1 from local resources "with external inputs at zero".** A basin that lives on standing transfers fails this audit every year, by design of the direction.
* **Art. 10.3.2 already has basins that cannot close Tier 1 locally:** "Dense urban agglomerations operate as specialized metabolic organs … trading advanced industrial outputs and scientific research for food and energy surpluses." The canon holds both the autarky rule and its exception without relating them.
* **Art. 10.3.3 suspends "inter-basin grid interties, transit corridors, and advanced medical buffers".** The same interties and corridors would carry solidarity. A decoupled basin loses its Tier 1 transfers, and its residents pay for a decision of its bodies (against Art. 1.3.3).
* **Art. 10.1.4 gives higher tiers jurisdiction "to defend invariant rights".** Tier 1 is one. A worldwide floor and an apportionment have a footing there.
* **Art. 4.4.4 names the precautionary principle "In dubio pro natura et vita".** Point 4 is the case where nature and life pull in opposite directions; the principle gives no answer.
* **The direction names the Continental Chamber only.** A continent with no basin in surplus has no donor.

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **Point 1, one worldwide floor: `NARROWED`.** *RED-8, climates:* a floor in inputs (energy for heating) fits no two climates. Stated as results it holds: water fit to drink, food sufficient for the person's age and activity, a dwelling kept within a range of temperature, care reached within a time. *RED-10 (new), the floor used against the ceiling:* a Global Commons Assembly drawn from a population that is mostly poor sets a floor the planet cannot supply inside its boundaries; point 4 is then triggered everywhere and the ceiling falls by vote. *Guard (conservation):* the floor is adopted only on a certified planetary balance showing that it can be met for everyone inside the ceilings (the check of Art. 3.1.5 at global scale); a floor that fails the balance has no effect. *Lowering:* never by vote; only through a certified shared shortage (SIM-QA-30 D-30.8). *RED-11 (new), gamed accounts:* a basin understates what it has to receive, or a donor hides surplus; need and surplus are certified by panels from outside, with the split-sample audit of Art. 3.4.5. *RED-1* is answered: a basin can no longer vote its own Tier 1 below the floor (Art. 3.6.2 is limited to that extent). Unequal levels above the floor remain, by design.
* **Point 2, standing solidarity: `NARROWED` on the rule; `RED_WINS` on four points as worded.**
  * *RED-9, recipients vote themselves the transfers.* A Chamber drawn in proportion to population can have a majority from receiving basins. *Guard:* the Chamber adopts a published formula at 60% (Art. 10.6.2) and never votes on a single transfer; the inputs are certified accounts; a contribution never cuts into the donor's own Tier 1, Tier 2 or reserves (Art. 3.5); the share has a cap, which is the SIMONE residual RED-3.
  * *RED-12 (new), hidden surplus.* "Surplus beyond their own Tier 1 and Tier 2" disappears if a donor enlarges its Tier 2. The accounts must be per flow (energy, food, water, equipment), since a basin can be a donor in one flow and a recipient in another, and Tier 2 must be measured against a common definition.
  * *RED-5, standing dependence, and RED-4, transport (qualitative only).* Flows differ in how far they can be carried. Energy and food travel; water in the amounts farming needs mostly does not, except downhill; a dwelling does not travel at all. A basin short of water is better helped with food, which carries the water used to grow it, and with equipment. Installed capacity (generators, treatment units, tools, knowledge) is given once and raises what the basin makes for itself, which is what Art. 3.1.6 asks; a flow must be sent every year. *Proposed rule:* solidarity is delivered first as capacity, and as flows only to bridge. Art. 10.4.4 already lists capacity (desalination units, microgrids, mobile medical hubs). The distances and the costs are RED-4, `NOT_RUN`.
  * *`RED_WINS` as worded:* (a) the Continental Chamber alone leaves a continent without surplus with no donor; a global tier is needed. (b) Against Art. 3.1.6 as written (section 5.1): the audit must count certified solidarity as such, or every recipient is a failed node (SIM-QA-35 H4). (c) Against Art. 10.3.3: solidarity for Tier 1 must never be suspended by decoupling. (d) Against SIM-QA-35 point 5 (RED-13, new): that point lets a bioregion withdraw from "solidarity duties". The rich would leave. If solidarity for Tier 1 rests on Chapter I, withdrawal cannot shed it, and SIM-QA-35 point 5 must say so.
  * *"Nothing in return."* A plan for the recipient to close its own Tier 1 is a condition, and conditions give donors a hold. *Guard:* what covers Tier 1 is never conditioned (Art. 1.3.3); the plan concerns the capacity delivered and is certified by a panel from outside, never by the donors. *RED-3:* a donor that refuses meets only the forum of SIM-QA-22 D-2 and then decoupling. The federation has nothing else (SIM-QA-35 RED-11).
* **Point 3, relocation offered: `NARROWED`.** *RED-6:* where no transfer can reach (water), moving is the only real choice. No assembly forces it; the place does. *Guards:* transfers are never reduced to make people leave; whoever moves receives an equivalent dwelling (Art. 2.3.4); a group may move together; a right to return. *Residual:* receiving basins at Amber or Red Status (Art. 10.4.2) create no new tenure, so an offer needs a basin with room. A basin that empties falls under the failed-node rule of SIM-QA-35.
* **Point 4, when ceiling and floor collide: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` as worded on its place in the text.** *RED-7:* the line between drawing down a resource that recovers and breaking it is uncertain and often seen only after it is crossed (soils, fisheries, aquifers that compact). "Never irreversible damage" can be aimed at, not guaranteed. *Guards:* the excess is limited to a part of a margin certified as recoverable by a panel from a non-bordering watershed on rival evidence (SIM-QA-20); indicators are measured under Art. 3.4.5 and the excess stops by itself when one is crossed; no second excess before full recovery, so periods cannot be chained; a total limit in time; basins that share the resource are heard (Art. 10.1.4). *Order proposed, joining this point to SIM-QA-30:* (1) transfers and the emergency ladder; (2) a bounded excess on recovering resources, where a certified margin exists; (3) where none exists or it is used up, the shortage is certified and shared by all present in proportion to need (D-30.7, D-30.8). The ceiling gives first, inside a recoverable margin only; then the floor. A seed node usually has no margin and no neighbour, which is why SIM-QA-30 went straight to step 3. *Place in the text:* section 5.1; the order must be stated in Chapter I.

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-10, the floor against the ceiling.** Under point 1.
* **RED-11, gamed accounts.** Under point 1; the act itself is in SIM-QA-33.
* **RED-12, hidden surplus.** Under point 2.
* **RED-13, the rich withdraw.** Under point 2; cross-docket conflict with SIM-QA-35 point 5.
* **RED-14, the floor for whom. Cross-docket, not treated.** Art. 1.3 speaks of "every inhabitant" and Art. 10.4.3 receives every traveller. During the transition most people below any floor live outside the commons. A worldwide floor inside a commons of a few nodes meets the arrivals of SIM-QA-30.
* **RED-15, the transition. Cross-docket.** Among seed nodes there is no Continental Chamber and no Global Assembly. The seed-node compact of SIM-QA-30 (D-30.6) and the Foundation's equalisation (SIM-QA-42) stand in for them; the direction applies when the assemblies exist.

#### **5.4 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED` on text, with feasibility unknown** until SIMONE has run RED-3 and RED-4; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on the Continental Chamber alone, on the conflicts with Arts. 3.1.6 and 10.3.3 and with SIM-QA-35 point 5, on the place of point 4 in the text, and on "never irreversible", which no rule can guarantee.
1. **Floor.** Worldwide, stated as results, adopted by the Global Commons Assembly at 75% only on a certified planetary balance. Bioregions go above, never below. Lowered only by a certified shared shortage.
2. **Solidarity.** Standing, unconditional for Tier 1, per flow, by a published formula on accounts certified from outside. Capacity first, flows to bridge. Never from the donor's Tier 1, Tier 2 or reserves; capped. A global tier beside the continental one. Not shed by withdrawal, not suspended by decoupling, and counted as such in the audit of Art. 3.1.6.
3. **Relocation.** Offered with an equivalent dwelling, for groups as well, with a right to return. Transfers are never reduced to push.
4. **Order in a collision.** Transfers; then a bounded excess on recovering resources inside a certified margin, with an automatic stop and full recovery before any other; then a shared shortage. Never a non-renewable resource. Stated in Chapter I.

#### **5.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
The floor, the duty of solidarity and the order of point 4 are rights and duties and stay in the Constitution; the formula, the measures, the caps and the procedures go to Organic Law. The order of point 4 belongs in Chapter I (19 words left; SIM-QA-35 would free some). The rest belongs in Chapter X (870 today, 935 with accepted dockets, 65 left, also claimed by SIM-QA-35 points 5 and 6) or Chapter III (886, about 114 left, also claimed by SIM-QA-34). Nothing is drafted. The conflicts go to the maintainer.

#### **5.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | metabolisms "must operate within the … carrying capacity of local watersheds" | Art. 1.6.1 (Chapter I) | a bounded, temporary excess to keep the Tier 1 floor | **weakened** |
| 2 | "society's non-negotiable ceiling" | Art. 3.1.4 | same | **weakened** |
| 3 | Tier 1 "from local resources and installed capacity alone, with external inputs at zero" | Art. 3.1.6 | certified solidarity counted | **weakened** as a rule of closure |
| 4 | "automatic Systemic Decoupling" of interties and corridors | Art. 10.3.3 | Tier 1 solidarity excepted | **weakened** as a sanction; strengthens Art. 1.3 |
| 5 | a basin recalculates its own Tier 1 at 75% | Art. 3.6.2 | never below the worldwide floor | **qualified** |
| 6 | "zero jurisdiction to override local autonomy" | Art. 10.1.4 | a worldwide floor and an apportionment, under the invariant-rights exception | **qualified** |

#### **5.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* **No quantity is known.** If donors can carry only a small share, or the flows needed cannot be carried, points 1 and 2 promise what cannot be delivered. This is the first thing to establish.
* A donor that refuses cannot be made to give.
* The recoverable margin of a resource is an estimate, and the excess is taken on that estimate.
* The floor applies to members; the people below it are mostly outside.
* Four guards rest on other dockets: SIM-QA-20, 30, 33, 35.
* No hydrologist, agronomist, energy engineer or development practitioner has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-38.2 to D-38.7):**
* **D-38.2:** accept the four points of section 5.4 as the revised direction, or change them.
* **D-38.3:** the order when ceiling and floor collide, joined to SIM-QA-30 (transfers; bounded excess inside a certified margin; shared shortage), and its statement in Chapter I before the lock.
* **D-38.4:** capacity first, flows to bridge.
* **D-38.5:** solidarity for Tier 1 cannot be shed by withdrawal (with D-35.4) and is never suspended by decoupling (Art. 10.3.3).
* **D-38.6:** how Art. 3.1.6 reads beside standing solidarity and beside the cities of Art. 10.3.2: closure as a goal with certified exceptions, or closure as a condition.
* **D-38.7:** placement (Chapters I, III, X), with SIM-QA-34 and SIM-QA-35.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 6** (D-38.3): B, Chapter I states the order when the floor (Art. 1.3) and the ceiling (Art. 1.6.1) collide: transfers; then a bounded, temporary excess on recovering resources inside a margin certified from outside; then a shortage certified and shared (SIM-QA-30 D-30.7, D-30.8); never a non-renewable resource, never irreversible harm. **Still open:** D-38.2, D-38.4 to D-38.7, and the go for the two SIMONE tasks.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-38.6; conflicts batch, conflict 11, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** closure under Art. 3.1.6 is **the goal the audit measures, not a condition.** The audit publishes the gap. Three kinds of input are counted as what they are and are not a failure: certified solidarity for Tier 1; the compacts of Art. 10.3.2 at the level where they close (the bioregion, not the city); imports during the building of a node, with a plan to end them. Each exception is certified from outside and has a term. A machine counts only as far as it is locally maintainable (SIM-QA-31 point 6). Failure is Tier 1 in fact not delivered (SIM-QA-35 point 6). The red team's pick, adopted. *Consequences:* Art. 3.1.6 is **weakened** from a condition to a measure with exceptions: a compression disclosure line, a decision of its own at drafting; an exception renewed each year can become permanent dependence; the level of closure and the donor share are `NOT_RUN` (RED-3, RED-4), and no figure is written before those SIMONE runs. Direction only; no wording.

**Next action:** the two SIMONE tasks (maximum share donors can carry; how far each flow can be carried), entered in `simone/TODO.md` on maintainer go; maintainer decisions D-38.2 to D-38.7; then article text and Organic Law draft as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass.

## **SIM-QA-39: Protection Where There Is No Offence**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED`** with six revised points; **`RED_WINS`** under the current text on any measure carried out by force (Chapter I), and as worded on validation by fifteen per case, on permanent incapacity and on the chronic carrier. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. No article text is drafted. First pass; no review by a public-health or mental-health specialist.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.2.2 ("physical subjugation" among the acts "permanently prohibited"), Art. 1.2.3 ("No civic mandate, collective emergency, or administrative decree may violate the physical or psychological substrate of any individual"; "compassionate, uncoerced biological exit"), Art. 10.4.1 and 10.4.3 (free movement; no pushback), Art. 8.5.2 (custody for predatory violence only), Art. 3.5.1 (epidemics named among the disruptions reserves must cover), Art. 4.4 (rival expert panels), Art. 4.5.3 (emergency decrees at simple majority, thirty-day sunset), Art. 8.1.2 (evacuation orders beyond 24 hours need re-authorisation by a drawn panel: the one existing limit on movement without an offence), Art. 5.5 (mental health), Art. 3.4.4 (sensor networks "strictly confined to abiotic and macro-ecological variables").  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.2.2, 1.2.3, 1.7, 3.4.4, 3.5.1, 4.4, 4.5.3, 5.5, 8.1.2, 8.5.2, 8.5.3, 10.4.  
* **Source notes (citation check):** (1) Art. 1.2.3 also guarantees an uncoerced biological exit; a protective hold of a person who endangers only themselves has to be read against it and against the safeguards of SIM-QA-22 D-9 (OL-17). (2) Wastewater analysis for pathogens measures a biotic variable; whether Art. 3.4.4 allows it is open. (3) SIM-QA-22 D-4 (red team E-1) states that "Quarantine and distance measures remain available" in an epidemic; no article provides them. This docket is where that basis would be made or found missing.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-35 (the error-correction docket: if isolation is read as "physical subjugation", Art. 1.7 voids it and only a path inside Chapter I can change that), SIM-QA-22 D-4 and D-9, SIM-QA-37 (who physically carries out an isolation), SIM-QA-41 (epidemic data), SIM-QA-29 (RED-10, identification in small nodes), SIM-QA-18 Y-12 (repeated decrees).

### **1. The Friction**
* The canon protects body, mind and movement without exception, and reserves custody for violence. A contagious person who refuses to keep apart, and a person in acute crisis who endangers only themselves, fall outside every provision.
* Epidemics appear once, as something reserves must cover.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text or under the direction)*
* **RED-1, no power at all:** a person with a serious transmissible disease moves freely; nothing lawful limits contact.
* **RED-2, isolation as subjugation** *(residual named by the maintainer)*: isolation is challenged under Arts. 1.2.2 and 1.2.3 and voided under Art. 1.7.
* **RED-3, quarantine as border:** an area quarantine is used to keep arrivals out, against Art. 10.4.
* **RED-4, slow or captured certification:** rival panels take weeks while an outbreak takes days; or a deadlock withholds certification.
* **RED-5, the emergency renewed:** thirty-day decrees follow one another.
* **RED-6, pressure in place of mandate:** nobody is forced to be vaccinated or treated, and those who decline are isolated longer.
* **RED-7, data that identifies:** counts per node identify persons in small nodes; wastewater sampled per building is domestic profiling.
* **RED-8, abuse of protective holds** *(residual named by the maintainer)*: the hold is used on the inconvenient, the old or the dissenting; "lucid decision in doubt" stretches; conflict with the uncoerced exit of Art. 1.2.3.
* **RED-9, the hold without end:** no imposed medication and no stated limit leave a person held with nothing changing.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on canon text)*
* **D-39.1, direction:**
  1. Isolation of a contagious person as a limit on contact, only for a disease certified serious and transmissible by rival expert panels (Art. 4.4), with the guarantees of custody: validation by fifteen citizens within 72 hours, limited duration, renewal.
  2. Area quarantine by emergency decree with the thirty-day sunset (Art. 4.5.3); arrivals are received in isolation space, never turned back.
  3. Treatment and vaccination are never compulsory.
  4. Epidemic data: anonymous counts per node and wastewater analysis; contact tracing only by choice.
  5. A short protective hold for a person endangering only themselves when lucid decision is in doubt, assessed by a panel from another node, with no imposed medication.
* **Residuals named by the maintainer, to test:** compatibility with "physical subjugation" in Chapter I, linked to SIM-QA-35 (RED-2); abuse of protective holds (RED-8).

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team D-39.1 against RED-1 to RED-9~~ (done, section 5); settle RED-2 with SIM-QA-35 before any text (**open**: D-39.2, D-39.3); specialist review (**not done**).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass; no public-health, mental-health or geriatric specialist has read it; no article text drafted)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Art. 1.2.3 names the case and answers it:** "No civic mandate, collective emergency, or administrative decree may violate the physical or psychological substrate of any individual." An epidemic is a collective emergency. The authors foresaw it and refused the exception.
* **Three different things are in the direction, and Chapter I treats them differently.** (i) An order to keep apart that nobody enforces limits movement and gatherings; movement is in Art. 10.4, outside Chapter I, and gatherings are in Art. 1.4.1 ("unconditional freedom of … peaceful assembly"), inside it. (ii) Isolation carried out by force is "physical subjugation" (Art. 1.2.2). (iii) A protective hold is physical subjugation and also touches "mental sanctuary" and the "uncoerced biological exit" (Art. 1.2.3). Under the current text (ii) and (iii) are void (Art. 1.7.2), and (i) is arguable.
* **A test is an act on the body.** A swab or a blood sample cannot be compelled (Art. 1.2). With contact tracing "only by choice" (point 4) and no recording of persons (Arts. 3.4.4, 8.2.2), a contagious person who declines the test cannot be shown to be contagious (RED-10).
* **Art. 10.4.3: "Under no circumstances shall any human being face armed pushback, physical interdiction, or involuntary deportation", and travellers keep "safe transit".** A cordon around an area cannot be held by force. What a decree can do is close common facilities and stop scheduled transit.
* **The guarantees of custody are built for one person at a time** (Art. 8.5.3: fifteen citizens within 72 hours; review every 180 days). An outbreak has hundreds of cases over days.
* **Art. 4.4.4 answers a deadlock of experts by "withholding approval".** For a new structure that is caution. For an outbreak, withholding the measure is itself the risk; the rule points the wrong way or no way.
* **Art. 3.4.4 confines sensors to "abiotic and macro-ecological variables".** A pathogen in sewage is biotic; whether a whole population's sewage is "macro-ecological" is a reading, not a text.
* **Art. 10.1.4** lets higher tiers override a node only for pollution, shared aquifers and invariant rights. A node that takes no measure in an outbreak is not clearly covered.

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **RED-2 first (the residual named by the maintainer): `RED_WINS` under the current text.** Section 5.1: enforced isolation and the protective hold are void under Chapter I, and no text outside Chapter I can change that. The fix must be made in Art. 1.2 before the Genesis Lock (SIM-QA-35 point 1, SIM-QA-37 D-37.8), and it reopens the choice the authors made in Art. 1.2.3. *The red team's finding on whether to make it:* for isolation, force adds little. Diagnosis cannot be compelled, tracing is voluntary, nobody is recorded; the measure reaches the visibly ill and the willing whether or not force stands behind it. What makes people keep apart is that it costs them nothing: full Tier 1 and care brought to the door, and shifts counted as done. Two options for the maintainer (D-39.2): **O-A,** isolation by order and support, never by force, with Art. 1.2 untouched and the refuser accepted as a residual; **O-B,** isolation enforceable with guarantees, which needs the Chapter I change and a new case in SIM-QA-37. The red team supports O-A. The protective hold is different: there the physical act is the whole measure, and it cannot exist without the Chapter I change.
* **Point 1, isolation: `NARROWED` (under O-A or O-B).** *RED-4, slow certification:* two stages. Diseases are certified in advance, in calm times, by rival panels, as a list in Organic Law. For a new pathogen a small drawn panel of practitioners certifies provisionally within days; the certification lapses unless the full rival review confirms it. On deadlock the measure lapses: liberty is the default, and the provisional period is the room for caution. *RED-12 (new), scale:* validation by fifteen for every case cannot be done in an outbreak. A person who agrees needs no validation; a person who contests gets a panel of three within 72 hours (the per-dispute panels of SIM-QA-19). *Duration:* while infectious, as certified, reviewed at short intervals; 180 days (Art. 8.5.3) is the wrong scale. *Least restriction:* at home first. *RED-11 (new), the chronic carrier:* a person infectious for years who declines treatment (point 3) is isolated for years without an offence. No rule can keep all three of: no compulsory treatment, no indefinite isolation, protection of others. The direction keeps the first; the second gives way. Conditions must then be those of Art. 8.5.2 at least (daylight, garden, care), with contact kept by every safe means. For the maintainer (D-39.4).
* **Point 2, area quarantine: `NARROWED`.** *What it can contain:* closing common facilities, stopping scheduled transit, receiving arrivals in isolation space. Not a cordon (section 5.1). Private gatherings are not reached (Art. 1.4.1), and closing the commons' own rooms is the limit of what a decree does to assembly (RED-16, new). *RED-5:* thirty days at simple majority, then ratification at 60% (Art. 4.5.3); for decrees that follow one another, the repeated-decree rule drafted in SIM-QA-18 (Y-12). *RED-3, quarantine as border:* the same rule for a resident coming home and for a newcomer (origin is a protected ground in SIM-QA-23); isolation space is finite, and beyond it the arrivals question is SIM-QA-30. *RED-13 (new), the node that takes no measure:* neighbours cannot close their limits; they can receive its travellers in isolation space. Whether a higher tier may order a measure inside that node is not answered by Art. 10.1.4.
* **Point 3, no compulsion: `BLUE_HOLDS`, with one rule to add.** *RED-6:* pressure enters when declining has a consequence. *Rule:* no consequence follows from a person's status (vaccinated or not, treated or not); consequences follow only from being infectious in fact. With the medical record under the person's own key (SIM-QA-41), status cannot be checked anyway. *Open:* who decides for a child (SIM-QA-25, SIM-QA-36).
* **Point 4, data: `NARROWED`.** *RED-7:* counts are published above a minimum cell and small nodes are counted together (SIM-QA-29 measured 69% re-identification at fifty adults, synthetic). Wastewater is sampled only where the pipe serves more people than the minimum cell, for named pathogens only, and samples are destroyed; sampling a building is domestic profiling. Human genetic material is in sewage too, which is why the target list matters. The reading of Art. 3.4.4 that allows any of this is for the maintainer (D-39.6).
* **Point 5, the protective hold: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` as worded on two points.**
  * *RED-8, abuse.* "Lucid decision in doubt" is a judgment anyone can claim to have. *Guards:* the trigger is an act in progress that endangers the person's life, never words, an opinion, a refusal of treatment, a way of living, a risk freely taken or a protest (a hunger strike is speech, Art. 1.4.1). The guardian of Art. 8.5.3 attends at once. The panel is from another node and may sit remotely; clinicians advise and do not decide (Art. 4.4.1). Every hold is logged and reviewed as a restraint (SIM-QA-37 point 5). The person keeps their dwelling, so nobody gains a home by having someone held (SIM-QA-34).
  * *RED-9, the hold without end.* With no imposed medication a long hold changes nothing. A first period counted in hours, validation within a time shorter than 72 hours, a maximum counted in days, and no new hold without a new act. After the maximum the person is released even if the risk remains. Some will die. The direction accepts this by excluding imposed treatment, and the text should say so.
  * *The exit of Art. 1.2.3.* The right to an uncoerced exit is exercised through the procedure of SIM-QA-22 D-9 (reflection, absence of pressure). An act outside that procedure, while lucidity is in doubt, may be interrupted for a short time. That sentence has to be in Chapter I.
  * *`RED_WINS` as worded:* (a) **permanent incapacity (RED-14, new).** A person with advanced dementia who walks out at night endangers only themselves and will not regain lucidity. A "short" hold does not fit, and the canon has no rule on who decides for such a person. The named person of SIM-QA-36 point 4 and a statement made in advance are the instruments consistent with Art. 1.2; the long-term question is not answered here. (b) **Restraint without sedation (SIM-QA-37 RED-16).** Hours of physical restraint of a person in acute crisis carry their own danger.
  * *RED-15 (new), the earlier self and the present self.* A person may state in advance what they want done in a crisis, including medication. When the crisis comes they refuse. "No imposed medication" does not say which will counts. For the maintainer (D-39.7).

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-10, refusal of the test.** Section 5.1. The measure reaches the willing and the visibly ill.
* **RED-11, the chronic carrier.** Under point 1.
* **RED-12, scale.** Under point 1.
* **RED-13, the node that takes no measure.** Under point 2.
* **RED-14, permanent incapacity.** Under point 5.
* **RED-15, advance statement against present refusal.** Under point 5.
* **RED-16, gatherings.** Under point 2. Drawn assemblies that must meet in person (SIM-QA-18) need the remote exception already drafted for emergencies (X-5).
* **RED-17, the transition. Cross-docket.** State public-health and mental-health law applies and may compel what point 3 excludes. A node cannot shield a member from it (SIM-QA-40).

#### **5.4 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the six points below; **`RED_WINS`** under the current text on any measure carried out by force (Chapter I), and as worded on validation by fifteen per case, on permanent incapacity and on the chronic carrier.
1. **Isolation.** By order, for a certified disease, at home first, with full Tier 1 and care delivered and shifts counted. No validation when the person agrees; a panel of three within 72 hours when the person contests. It lasts while the person is infectious, with frequent review. Force only if D-39.2 chooses O-B.
2. **Certification.** A list certified in advance by rival panels; for a new pathogen, a provisional certification by a drawn panel of practitioners, which lapses unless confirmed.
3. **Area measures.** A thirty-day decree, ratified at 60%, under the repeated-decree rule. Content: common facilities, scheduled transit, isolation space for arrivals. No cordon. One rule for residents and newcomers.
4. **No compulsion.** No test, treatment or vaccine is imposed. No consequence follows from status, only from being infectious in fact.
5. **Data.** Counts above a minimum cell; wastewater only at points that serve more people than that, for named pathogens; tracing by choice.
6. **Protective hold.** Trigger: an act in progress that endangers the person's own life, with lucidity in doubt. Never for words, refusal, protest or way of life. Hours first, a remote panel from another node with the guardian present, a maximum in days, no medication imposed, the person's advance statement and named person followed, the dwelling kept, every hold logged and reviewed.

#### **5.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
The hold, and isolation by force if chosen, need words in Art. 1.2 (Chapter I: 19 left; SIM-QA-35 would free some). The rest concerns rights and powers and belongs with health in Chapter V (within fifty words of its ceiling) or with public safety in Chapter VIII (98 left, claimed by SIM-QA-33 and SIM-QA-37); lists, intervals and procedures go to Organic Law. Nothing is drafted. The conflicts go to the maintainer.

#### **5.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "No civic mandate, collective emergency, or administrative decree may violate the physical or psychological substrate of any individual" | Art. 1.2.3 | the hold; isolation by force only under O-B | **weakened** (Chapter I) |
| 2 | "physical subjugation" permanently prohibited | Art. 1.2.2 | same | **weakened** (Chapter I; with SIM-QA-37) |
| 3 | "compassionate, uncoerced biological exit" | Art. 1.2.3 | an act outside the D-9 procedure may be interrupted briefly | **qualified** (Chapter I) |
| 4 | "unconditional freedom of … peaceful assembly" | Art. 1.4.1 | common facilities may be closed by decree; private gatherings untouched | **qualified** (Chapter I) |
| 5 | right to travel; "safe transit" | Arts. 10.4.1, 10.4.3 | scheduled transit stopped; arrivals received in isolation space | **qualified** |
| 6 | sensors "strictly confined to abiotic and macro-ecological variables" | Art. 3.4.4 | pathogens in a population's wastewater | **qualified**, or clarified if "macro-ecological" is read to cover it |

Under O-A, lines 1 and 2 are touched by the protective hold only.

#### **5.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* A contagious person who declines the test, or the order under O-A, is not reached.
* A chronic carrier who declines treatment is kept apart without limit.
* A person released at the end of a hold may die.
* Permanent incapacity has no rule.
* The measures depend on trust and on support delivered to the door; none of that is sized.
* Four lines of the disclosure touch Chapter I, which is closed after the lock.
* No public-health, mental-health or geriatric specialist, and no one with experience of being held, has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-39.2 to D-39.9):**
* **D-39.2:** isolation: O-A (order and support, never force; Art. 1.2 untouched) or O-B (enforceable with guarantees; Chapter I change; a case added to SIM-QA-37). The red team supports O-A.
* **D-39.3:** the protective hold with the guards of point 6, and the words it needs in Art. 1.2 before the lock (with D-37.8 and SIM-QA-35); or no hold.
* **D-39.4:** the chronic carrier: isolation while infectious, treatment never imposed, conditions at least those of Art. 8.5.2.
* **D-39.5:** certification in two stages, lapsing on deadlock.
* **D-39.6:** the reading of Art. 3.4.4 that allows wastewater analysis at population points.
* **D-39.7:** an advance statement against a present refusal (RED-15): which counts.
* **D-39.8:** permanent incapacity (RED-14): a docket of its own, or with the named person of SIM-QA-36.
* **D-39.9:** placement (Chapters I, V, VIII).

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 3** (D-39.2): O-A, isolation by order and support, never by force; Art. 1.2.3 is not touched for epidemics; the person who refuses is accepted as a residual. **Item 4** (D-39.3): B, narrowly: a protective hold is written into Chapter I as a closed case, with its trigger and limits in the text (an act in progress that endangers the person's own life, lucidity in doubt; hours first, a maximum in days; no medication imposed; never for words, a refusal of treatment, a protest or a way of living), and Chapter I says that the exit of Art. 1.2.3 is exercised through the Organic Law procedure; **a specialist reading comes before any wording**. **Item 8:** Art. 1.4.1 is not changed; recorded reading: closing the commons' own rooms for a limited time is the management of a facility and no decree may forbid people to gather elsewhere. **Still open:** D-39.4 to D-39.9.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch 2, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2_2026-11-20.md); answered item by item; directions only):** **item 1b:** where nobody can be asked in time, a clinician may give what is needed to save life or prevent grave harm, unless the person's refusal is known. **Item 1c** (D-39.7): the present refusal prevails for anything done to the body, so no medication is ever forced; the advance statement governs everything else. **Item 1d** (D-39.8 in part): anyone may keep a person who cannot judge the danger from immediate harm; a continuing restriction needs a jury drawn from outside to find the incapacity, with periodic review, the least restriction that works, and an Advocate. **Item 1e** (D-39.8): a new docket, SIM-QA-43, for the detail. **Item 2** (D-39.3): three readers for the protective hold (a clinician of acute mental-health emergencies; a person who has been held under such a measure; a lawyer of mental-health detention), the eight questions of the batch file, read together with item 1d; the place in Draft C stays open and nothing about the hold is worded until the reading. **The maintainer finds the readers.**

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-39.2 to D-39.9; then article text and Organic Law draft as hypothesis, shown beside the current text, with a second red-team pass; specialist review before any merge.
