## **SIM-QA-40: Collision with State Law During the Transition**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED`** with seven revised points and a first-pass table; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on point 4 (a vote as the switch) and on three statuses, and under current text on the legal organs and on leaving with nothing. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. No article text is drafted. First pass; no legal review; nothing here states the law of any country.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch).  
* **Closest current canon:** Chapter IX gives no rule for a conflict between the Constitution and the law of the state a node stands in. Art. 9.1.2 rejects "violent macro-rupture". Art. 9.2 (reclaiming land "via adverse possession"; patents and trade secrets "declared public usufruct") and Art. 9.5 (expropriation of defaulted assets; abolition of debts) describe acts that state law does not allow today. Custody under Art. 8.5 by a node would be detention without legal basis. Art. 9.6.2 requires 75% "to ratify formal phase-gate advancements" and lists neither the gates nor what each one activates. Art. 9.6.1 names bodies of 3, 15 and 101.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.7, 8.4, 8.5, 9.1 to 9.6.  
* **What `roadmap/roadmap.md` already covers (checked):** the two legal vehicles and their compliance by legal tradition (section 3.1); taxes paid and pre-funded (section 3.3, Phase 0); zoning and building rules (section 3.1, point 3); Phases 0 to 3 by degree of physical autonomy (section 4); disciplinary panels drawn from sister nodes (section 5). **Not covered:** which rule prevails in a conflict; which articles apply in which phase; violence, police and custody; school, conscription and professional licences; who declares a phase. Two points to reconcile with the direction: the heading "Code-Enforcement Stealth" (section 3.1, point 3) reads against "no member is asked to break the law"; the failure table cites an "Art. 9.7" that the canon does not have.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-30 (referral to the services of the existing state as an open, time-limited derogation; phase gate ending the transition), SIM-QA-42 (Statutes Art. 13.1 calls the Constitution the exclusive and sovereign rule of internal life, which the direction qualifies; the annex question), SIM-QA-37 (force), SIM-QA-33 (forums), SIM-QA-22 D-8 (no accession phase exists in the roadmap), SIM-QA-25 (children on site).

### **1. The Friction**
* A node founded today stands inside a state. The Constitution speaks as if it were alone.
* Members who apply Arts. 9.2, 9.5 or 8.5 as written expose themselves and the Foundation to prosecution.
* The phase gates that would switch such articles on are named and never listed.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text or under the direction)*
* **RED-1, members prosecuted:** a node applies Art. 9.2 or 9.5; persons are charged and the legal vehicles lose their standing.
* **RED-2, unlawful detention:** a node holds a person under Art. 8.5.
* **RED-3, parallel justice:** serious violence is handled inside under Art. 8.4; the harmed person loses the protection of the state.
* **RED-4, "where the law leaves room":** a node finds room where there is none; or the table shows that almost nothing of Chapters II, III and VIII is in force, and the Constitution is a programme, not a rule.
* **RED-5, obedience turned against Chapter I:** a state requires what Chapter I forbids (surveillance, eviction); the sentence on obedience meets Art. 1.7.
* **RED-6, the gate nobody can open** *(open point named by the maintainer)*: a phase gate needs 75% of a body that does not exist yet.
* **RED-7, the early gate:** a node declares a phase reached in order to switch on Arts. 9.2 and 9.5.
* **RED-8, persons who never agreed:** children, guests and neighbours are on site; "voluntary members only" does not reach them.
* **RED-9, obligations that contradict the canon:** conscription against Art. 9.4.1; taxes that require fiat income (Art. 9.3); licences against the no-gatekeeping rule of SIM-QA-20.
* **RED-10, the articles not named:** Art. 9.4 (asylum for deserters, cancellation of debts, the key sever) carries the same exposure and is not on the suspended list.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on canon text)*
* **D-40.1, direction:**
  1. A general sentence in Chapter IX: while the state functions, a node obeys mandatory state law and applies O.N.E. among its members where the law leaves room; no member is asked to break the law.
  2. A table (roadmap or Organic Law) marking each article as in force now, in force among voluntary members only, or suspended until a phase.
  3. Serious violence goes to state police and courts; Art. 8.4 runs only as voluntary mediation; no custody by a node.
  4. Arts. 9.2 and 9.5 are suspended until a 75% phase gate.
  5. School, conscription, taxes and professional licences are respected, with lawful alternatives.
* **Open, named by the maintainer:** who declares a phase gate before bioregional assemblies exist (RED-6).

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team D-40.1 against RED-1 to RED-10~~ (done, section 5); ~~draft the table of point 2 as a hypothesis~~ (first pass in section 5.4, by groups of articles); legal review per country of the first node (**not done**).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; first pass; **no lawyer has read it and nothing here states the law of any country**; examples of state rules are illustrations of a kind of rule, each to be checked per country; no article text drafted)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist)*
* **Chapter I binds the commons' own bodies; it cannot bind a state.** When a state evicts, registers or conscripts, that is not an act of an assembly, and Art. 1.7.2 does not reach it. What Art. 1.7.2 does reach is the node acting, on its own initiative, against Chapter I. The direction's sentence ("a node obeys mandatory state law") must not be read as licence for that.
* **Art. 9.4 defends against "external armed aggression, corporate syndicates, or warlord predation"** by "civil resistance", "logistical denial" and, in Art. 9.4.5, a key sever against "armed physical occupation". Nothing says that a lawful act of the state (a search, a seizure, an inspection) is not aggression. A node that read Art. 9.4 against the police would bring disaster on itself (RED-13).
* **A vote cannot make an act lawful.** Art. 9.6.2 lets 75% "enact universal Debt Jubilees" and "authorize involuntary industrial asset conversions". While a state enforces its law, such a vote is a collective decision to break it, whatever the threshold.
* **Arts. 9.2 and 9.5 are not unlawful as a whole.** Asset-locked trusts (Art. 9.2.1) and the re-engineering of plants a node holds (Art. 9.5.3) are lawful now. The exposure is in Arts. 9.2.2, 9.2.3, 9.5.1 and 9.5.2.
* **Art. 9.6.1 names bodies of 3, 15 and 101 only.** A phase gate at 75% needs one of them; the first that could carry such a decision for more than one node is the Bioregional Assembly.
* **The legal vehicles have their own organs.** Under state law a foundation, an association or a cooperative decides through a board or a members' meeting, and those persons answer for the decision. A drawn panel has no standing there (RED-11).
* **Art. 5.3.2 places adolescents from thirteen in "active research, agricultural, and fabrication collectives".** States limit the work of minors.
* **The roadmap cites an "Art. 9.7" for a "Transitional Resettlement Escrow"** for members who leave. The canon has no such article and no rule on what a member leaves with beyond "personal possessions" (Art. 8.6.3).
* **Roadmap wording.** Section headings such as "Code-Enforcement Stealth" and the claim that a labour structure works by "defeating" wage claims read against "no member is asked to break the law", even where the content under them describes compliance.

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **Point 1, the general sentence: `NARROWED`.** *RED-5, obedience against Chapter I:* the sentence needs a second half. The node obeys mandatory law; its own bodies stay bound by Chapter I at all times; it never does of its own motion what Chapter I forbids; where the law compels such an act it complies, records the conflict in public as a derogation (the model of SIM-QA-30), uses every lawful remedy, and supports the person affected. *RED-4, "where the law leaves room":* the honest result of the table (section 5.4) is that during the transition the Constitution works as the internal rules of lawful associations among people who chose them, plus a programme. That is what the direction intends, and the text should say it plainly instead of speaking "as if it were alone". *"While the state functions":* who finds that it no longer does is the same question as the phase gate (point 4); a node's own finding would be read by the state as revolt (RED-17).
* **Point 2, the table: `NARROWED`; three statuses are not enough.** Five are needed: **N,** binds the node's own bodies now; **M,** applies among members by agreement, where the law allows; **L,** may be limited or barred by state law even among members who agree (schooling, medicine, assisted dying, the work of minors, tenancy, sanctuary for persons without status); **S,** suspended until a gate; **E,** describes the end state and has no present effect. *RED-8, persons who never agreed:* children are under state law and their carers (SIM-QA-25, 36); a guest accepts written house rules on arrival (SIM-QA-30); neighbours are bound by nothing. *Where it lives:* the law differs by country, so the table is a legal annex per node, built from one common template in the roadmap and reviewed by counsel. *One rule belongs in the Constitution:* an article can be placed under S by the ordinary 60% vote, and leaves S only through the gate. Otherwise Organic Law could switch Art. 9.5 on at 60%.
* **Point 3, violence: `NARROWED`.** *RED-3, parallel justice:* closed communities fail most gravely by handling abuse inside. *Guards:* no body of the node may discourage, delay or set conditions on anyone's recourse to the state's police and courts; mediation is never a substitute for it, never a condition before it, and no agreement reached in mediation waives it; an adult who was harmed decides for themselves (Art. 8.3.1); harm to a child is always reported by the node. The node does not investigate, since that damages evidence. No custody; also no protective hold and no enforced isolation (SIM-QA-39): those belong to the state. Members use force only as the law allows any person (SIM-QA-37 RED-19).
* **Point 4, suspension until a gate: `RED_WINS` as worded; revised: `NARROWED`.** *As worded:* "suspended until a 75% phase gate" makes the vote the switch, and a vote cannot make these acts lawful (section 5.1). *RED-7, the early gate, and RED-6, the gate nobody can open:* a gate is the recognition of a fact, not a permission. The fact is that the law of the place has changed: the state has agreed, or has ceased to exist there. *Revised:* a gate needs (a) that fact, certified by a panel drawn from outside the nodes concerned, and (b) 75% of a Bioregional Assembly. No gate opens before such an Assembly exists. This answers the open point of the maintainer: before bioregional assemblies exist, nobody declares a gate, and the suspended articles stay suspended. The Foundation's Board cannot be the body: a legal entity cannot resolve to break the law, and its supervisory authority would act. *RED-10, the articles not named:* the list is longer than Arts. 9.2 and 9.5 (section 5.4): Arts. 8.1.4, 8.5, the binding stage of Art. 8.4, relocation by force under Art. 8.3.2, and Arts. 9.4.1, 9.4.3 and 9.4.5. *Mismatch:* the roadmap's Phases 0 to 3 measure physical autonomy; reaching Phase 3 changes nothing in law, and its phrase "autonomous sovereign bioregional commons" should not be read as a gate.
* **Point 5, obligations: `NARROWED`.** *RED-9:* (a) Taxes, compulsory insurance and contributions need money that a member without income does not have. During the transition the node must pay them for its members out of what the External Trade Interface earns; that is part of Tier 1 in fact, and it is a cost no model here has counted. (b) Conscription: the node neither obstructs nor compels; the member uses the lawful alternative where one exists. (c) Licences: acts that the law reserves to a licensed person are done by licensed persons; the open entry of SIM-QA-32 applies to everything else. (d) School: the education of Art. 5.3 runs where the law allows a private or home form, and otherwise beside the state school.

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-11, the legal organs and the drawn bodies. `RED_WINS` under current text.** The board members answer in law for what the node does; the drawn bodies decide under the Constitution. When a panel decides something the board finds unlawful, or the board simply decides otherwise, nothing settles it. *Guard to test:* the legal organs carry out the decisions of the drawn bodies except where a decision is unlawful; a refusal is published with its reason; a dispute goes to a panel drawn from outside, with counsel heard. Goes with SIM-QA-42 (the Board).
* **RED-12, leaving with nothing. `RED_WINS` under current text.** A member who leaves after many years has no savings, possibly no pension rights, and "personal possessions". Dependence of that kind keeps people in, against the spirit of Art. 8.6.3. The roadmap answers with an escrow under an article the canon does not have (section 5.1). *Guard to test:* a rule in Chapter IX that a member who leaves during the transition receives means to resettle, and that contributions required by the state for old age and health are paid for every member throughout.
* **RED-13, Art. 9.4 read against the state.** Section 5.1. *Guard:* the general sentence states that a lawful act of the state is never aggression under Arts. 8.1.4 or 9.4; no resistance, no concealment, no key sever.
* **RED-14, sanctuary against immigration law. Open.** The hospitality of SIM-QA-30 is for every arrival. Sheltering a person without legal status is an offence in some states. Status L in the table; for counsel.
* **RED-15, work treated as employment. Open.** If an authority treats members' work as employment, wages, contributions and insurance are owed for the past. The roadmap asserts that its structure prevents this; that assertion is unverified. The apprenticeship from thirteen (Art. 5.3.2) meets the rules on the work of minors.
* **RED-16, data the state requires. `NARROWED`.** Registration of residents, tax records and the registers of the legal vehicles oblige the node to hold identity data that Art. 7.4.4 keeps out of civic procedures. The data-protection law of the place also applies to the registers of SIM-QA-29 and SIM-QA-41. *Guard:* what the state requires is kept by the legal vehicle, apart from the civic register, and used for nothing else.
* **RED-17, a state that functions in part.** Services fail while the law stays in force. A node that steps into policing because the police no longer come is in the position of RED-3 and of SIM-QA-37 at once. No criterion is proposed; the gate of point 4 is the only switch.
* **RED-18, words as evidence. Housekeeping.** Headings and phrases in public documents (section 5.1; also Statutes Art. 13.1, "exclusive and sovereign", SIM-QA-42) would be quoted in any proceeding against a node. For the maintainer (D-40.9).

#### **5.5 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the seven points below; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on point 4 (a vote as the switch) and on three statuses, and under current text on the legal organs (RED-11) and on leaving with nothing (RED-12).
1. **The sentence.** A node obeys mandatory state law. Its own bodies stay bound by Chapter I and never do of their own motion what it forbids. A conflict imposed by law is recorded in public as a derogation. Nobody is asked to break the law. A lawful act of the state is never aggression.
2. **The table.** Five statuses; one template in the roadmap; a legal annex per node, reviewed by counsel. An article leaves S only through a gate.
3. **Offences.** To the state's police and courts. Nobody is discouraged from going to them. Mediation never replaces, precedes as a condition, or waives. Harm to a child is always reported. No custody, hold or enforced isolation by a node.
4. **The gate.** A change in the law of the place, certified from outside, and 75% of a Bioregional Assembly. None before such an Assembly exists.
5. **Obligations.** Respected. The node pays what the state requires for each member. Reserved acts by licensed persons. Lawful alternatives for school and service.
6. **Legal organs.** They carry out the decisions of drawn bodies unless unlawful, and publish any refusal (with SIM-QA-42).
7. **Leaving.** Means to resettle, and contributions for old age and health paid throughout.

#### **5.6 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
The sentence, the gate rule, the rule that an article leaves S only through a gate, and the rule on leaving belong in Chapter IX. Chapter IX holds 817 words and 969 with SIM-QA-30 (D-30.5, D-30.10): 31 left, **within fifty**. They do not fit. The table goes to the roadmap and to each node's annex. The conflict goes to the maintainer.

#### **5.8 Residual Weaknesses**
* No lawyer has read any of this. The table is a sorting by a reader of the Constitution, not a legal opinion.
* The cost of paying every member's taxes, insurance and contributions is not counted anywhere.
* The gate depends on a change no node controls. Most of Chapters II, III, VIII and IX may stay programme for a long time, and the text will say so.
* A state that functions in part has no rule.
* Whether members' work is employment is an open risk.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-40.2 to D-40.10):**
* **D-40.2:** accept the seven points of section 5.5 as the revised direction, or change them.
* **D-40.3:** five statuses; the table of section 5.4 as the template to send to counsel; template in the roadmap, annex per node; an article leaves S only through a gate.
* **D-40.4:** the gate: a certified change in the law of the place and 75% of a Bioregional Assembly; none before one exists.
* **D-40.5:** Arts. 8.1.4, 9.4.1, 9.4.3 and 9.4.5 join the suspended list; a lawful act of the state is never aggression.
* **D-40.6:** harm to a child is always reported by the node; an adult decides for themselves.
* **D-40.7:** the node pays each member's mandatory taxes, insurance and contributions; a rule on leaving with means, in place of the roadmap's "Art. 9.7".
* **D-40.8:** legal organs and drawn bodies (with SIM-QA-42).
* **D-40.9:** roadmap wording (the "Stealth" heading, "Art. 9.7", the claim on wage law, "sovereign" in Phase 3): correct now, or record under housekeeping.
* **D-40.10:** the ceiling of Chapter IX (31 words left).

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (D-40.8; conflicts batch, conflict 9, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** the legal organs carry out the decisions of the drawn bodies; they may refuse only a decision they find unlawful, and publish the refusal with its reason; a dispute goes to a panel drawn from outside, which hears counsel and publishes its finding; the finding may ground removal under SIM-QA-42 point 2; in law the Board keeps the last word on what it signs. The red team's pick, adopted. *Cost accepted:* a Board can call anything unlawful; only publication and removal answer that. **Subject to Swiss counsel** (D-42.2). Direction only; no wording.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-40.2 to D-40.10; then the Chapter IX text as hypothesis with a second red-team pass; the template to counsel in the country of the first node; a cost estimate of mandatory payments per member before any figure.

## **SIM-QA-41: A General Rule on Personal Data**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED`** with seven revised points; **`RED_WINS`** in small nodes against neighbours, on "no body may combine" as the main protection, on point 4 as worded, on a detailed rule in Chapter I, and under current text on the reading of Art. 1.2.2. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. No article text is drafted. First pass; not a specialist's privacy review.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 3.4.4 (sensor networks only: no "individual domestic profiling"), Art. 7.4.4 (identity only: personhood proven "without harvesting biometric traits, facial imagery, or personal identity data"), Art. 8.2.2 (public sensors never record speech or compile biometric registries), Art. 1.2.2 (bars "involuntary biometric tracking, neurological monitoring, pharmacological manipulation"), Art. 1.2.3 ("private intimacy, mental sanctuary"), Art. 2.2.2 (domestic privacy). Against these: Art. 3.6.1 ("Physical ledgers and thermodynamic balance sheets are public"), Art. 3.3.3 (everything executed on communal machinery is "automatically published"), Art. 5.5.3 ("high-resolution dynamic neuro-telemetry" in clinical care), Art. 4.3.5 (petitions and endorsements on an open register). No article states a general rule for data about a person.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.2.2, 1.2.3, 2.2.2, 3.3.3, 3.4.4, 3.6.1, 4.3.5, 4.5.1, 5.5.3, 7.4.4, 8.2.2.  
* **Source notes (citation check):** (1) In Art. 1.2.2 it is not certain whether "involuntary" governs "neurological monitoring" as well as "biometric tracking": on one reading Art. 5.5.3 is lawful with consent, on the other it is barred outright. Two readings; recorded, not resolved. (2) Chapter I stands at 1,181 of 1,200 words after SIM-QA-22 D-4 and D-5, with 19 left for SIM-QA-24, 25, 27 and 28. A new principle in Chapter I meets the ceiling of instruction 10a; the conflict goes to the maintainer.  
* **Already drafted elsewhere, not repeated here:** SIM-QA-29 (Art. 7.4.4 amended, sealed records of Art. 7.4.5, OL-22: minimum cell, who opens a record); SIM-QA-20 section 11, decisions A to J (pseudonym by default, withdrawal, sealed evidence, Register data for draws and challenges only); SIM-QA-23 (no sensitive registers). This docket takes the general principle those rules lack; each keeps its own text. Other cross-references: SIM-QA-33 (the non-physical harms docket: fraud on ledgers and shifts), SIM-QA-34 H4 (demand signal without per-person records), SIM-QA-39 (epidemic data), SIM-QA-37 RED-6, SIM-QA-35 (ratchet on Chapter I).

### **1. The Friction**
* Two articles protect the person against sensors and against identity harvesting. Nothing protects the person against records made in ordinary life: shifts, bookings, endorsements, travel, care.
* Public ledgers and published machine files are a design choice of the canon. Combined, they describe individuals.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under current text or under the direction)*
* **RED-1, the profile by joining:** public ledgers, bookings, published files and rosters are combined into a profile of one person, with no sensor involved.
* **RED-2, small nodes** *(residual named by the maintainer)*: a ledger per node of fifty adults is close to a ledger per household (SIM-QA-29 RED-10).
* **RED-3, pseudonyms linked:** different pseudonyms per use are tied together by time and pattern.
* **RED-4, fraud that hides** *(residual named by the maintainer)*: without per-person data, ledger and shift fraud are harder to see (SIM-QA-33; SIM-QA-29 RED-11).
* **RED-5, the jury key:** the closed list of cases grows and opening the link becomes routine.
* **RED-6, the anonymous maker:** a dangerous object is made on communal machinery; the file is public, the maker unknown (SIM-QA-37 RED-6).
* **RED-7, consent under dependence:** a person in distress consents to neuro-telemetry; revocation exists on paper.
* **RED-8, erasure against the public record:** the right to erase meets append-only ledgers, tribunal records and intervention logs.
* **RED-9, the key that is lost:** a medical record under the person's key is unreachable when the key is lost or the person unconscious.
* **RED-10, the ratchet:** a principle placed in Chapter I can never be narrowed if it proves too wide (SIM-QA-35 RED-3).

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, silence.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on canon text)*
* **D-41.1, direction:**
  1. One principle in Chapter I, as an expansion of rights (Art. 4.5.1): personal data is collected only for a stated purpose, in the minimum amount, used only for that purpose, and erased when the purpose ends; no body may combine data from different uses to profile a person. Mechanics in Organic Law.
  2. Ledgers per node or facility, never per person.
  3. Shifts, bookings, endorsements and travel under a different pseudonym per use; the link is opened only by a drawn jury, for a closed list of cases.
  4. The fab-lab file is always published; the maker's name only by choice.
  5. The medical record is under the person's key and shared by consent; neuro-telemetry only with explicit, revocable consent.
  6. A right to see, correct and erase, except for a closed list.
* **Residuals named by the maintainer, to test:** small nodes (RED-2); harder detection of ledger and shift fraud, linked to SIM-QA-33 (RED-4).

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team D-41.1 against RED-1 to RED-10~~ (done, section 5); Chapter I ceiling conflict to the maintainer (**open**, D-41.3 and section 5.5); align with SIM-QA-29 and SIM-QA-20 section 11 at drafting; specialist privacy review (**not done**).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on text only; measurement `NOT_RUN`; first pass; **not a specialist's privacy review, and no cryptographer has read the two design proposals of section 5.2**; no article text drafted)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Text First** *(Jurist, Cyberneticist)*
* **Art. 1.2.2 as written:** "Coercive genetic alteration, involuntary biometric tracking, neurological monitoring, pharmacological manipulation, and physical subjugation are permanently prohibited". "Coercive" and "involuntary" each stand before one item. Read strictly, neurological monitoring and pharmacological manipulation are barred outright, with or without consent, and Art. 5.5.3 (neuro-telemetry in care) is void under Art. 1.7.2. The same reading would bar a person from agreeing to their own medication. The sentence needs one reading, fixed before the Genesis Lock (SIM-QA-35).
* **The direction binds "bodies".** The ledgers are public (Art. 3.6.1) and executed files are published (Art. 3.3.3). Any person, any association and anyone outside the commons can join public records into a profile. A rule on what bodies may combine does not reach them.
* **Art. 4.6.1 lets any citizen ask for review of an "algorithm".** A body's data practice can be brought before a review jury today; what is missing is the rule it would be measured against.
* **Art. 5.2.2 sanctions a person who "systematically refuses" shifts,** and Art. 4.3.3 imposes a hiatus after each mandate. Both need to know something about one person over time.
* **Art. 3.3.3 publishes "all toolpaths, G-code, and CAM schematics executed on communal machinery".** A file for a prosthesis, a fitted garment or an engraved gift carries a person's body or name inside it.
* **The canon does not say whether the members of a drawn body are named, or whether each member's vote is public** (Arts. 4.3, 4.5). Art. 4.1.3 fears pressure on drawn members; pressure needs to know who they are.
* **Art. 1.5.2 and Art. 1.3.2 make knowledge an open commons.** What has been published into it cannot be recalled.

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **Point 1, the principle: `NARROWED`; placement in Chapter I `RED_WINS` for anything beyond a short sentence.** *What holds:* purpose stated, minimum amount, no other use. One addition: data taken for one purpose is never used for another by a later vote; only the person's consent opens a new use. Aggregates above a minimum cell remain free for statistics and for the project's own evidence rule (instruction 7). *What is weak:* "no body may combine" protects little, since anyone can combine what is public (section 5.1). The protection has to be made where data is created, by points 2 to 5. *RED-10, the ratchet:* a detailed rule in Chapter I ("erased when the purpose ends") is frozen for good, and it collides at once with records that must last (the civic register, service records for the hiatus, care relations, findings). SIM-QA-35 advises that a new guarantee is tried outside Chapter I first. Options in D-41.3. *RED-14 (new), persons as profilers:* systematic compiling of a profile of a person, by anyone, is the act Art. 3.4.4 already calls a crime; it belongs on the list of SIM-QA-33, with writing and speech about the public acts of bodies left outside the definition (Art. 1.4).
* **Point 2, ledgers never per person: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` in small nodes (RED-2, the residual named by the maintainer).** A ledger of a facility run by three people is a ledger of those three. In a node of fifty, neighbours know who was where; no technique hides that. What can be prevented is the record that travels and accumulates: counts below a minimum cell are published only at the next level up, with coarse times and with delay. *Design proposal A, limits kept in the device:* where a limit applies to a dwelling or a person (the energy allotment per person proposed in SIM-QA-34), the meter enforces it on the spot and reports nothing but the total of its branch. Metering per dwelling that reports is the "domestic profiling" of Art. 3.4.4.
* **RED-4, fraud that hides (the second residual named by the maintainer): `NARROWED`.** Fraud on a node's accounts is found by the physical audit of Art. 3.4.5, which needs no personal data. Shifts are the hard case, since Art. 5.2.2 needs to know that a person refused. *Design proposal B, proof held by the person:* the roster gives each person a signed attestation of each shift; the person shows, without revealing the shifts, that the obligation of the period is met, in the way Art. 7.4.4 already proves personhood. Access to Tier 3 asks for that proof; no body keeps a record per person, and the sanction of Art. 5.2.2 works by the absence of proof. The same serves the hiatus of Art. 4.3.3 and the endorsement allowance of Art. 4.3.5. *Residual:* a roster keeper who signs for a friend (SIM-QA-29 RED-11); attestations countersigned by fellow workers reduce it. Whether proposal B is practical for every case is a question for a cryptographer.
* **Point 3, a pseudonym per use and a jury key: `NARROWED`.** *RED-3, linking:* pseudonyms resist analysis from afar and at scale; they do not resist a neighbour (point 2). *Where the link lives:* a link kept anywhere can be taken: by its keepers together (SIM-QA-29 RED-9), by intrusion, or by an order of a state during the transition (RED-11, new). *Revised:* by default no link exists outside the person's own key. A link is kept in escrow only for a closed list of uses in which other people can be harmed (a hazardous class of machine, for example). *RED-5, the list that grows:* a case is added at 75% and removed at 60%; every opening is logged, the person is told afterwards, and the count of openings is published.
* **Point 4, the published file: `RED_WINS` as worded ("always published"); revised: `NARROWED`.** *RED-12 (new):* the file can itself be personal data (section 5.1). A class of files that carry a person's body or name is published without those parts, or exempted; the open question already recorded in OL-2 point 3 (personal artworks) is the same question. *RED-6, the anonymous maker:* publication after execution shows the design to everyone and comes after the object exists. The maker's link is one of the escrowed uses of point 3, opened for the making of a weapon (Art. 8.1.3; the act is in SIM-QA-33). Checking published files for barred designs is reading public documents and touches no person.
* **Point 5, the medical record and neuro-telemetry: `NARROWED`.** *RED-9, the lost key:* access in an emergency by the clinician present, logged and notified to the person; recovery through the named person of SIM-QA-36 point 4 or the custodians of SIM-QA-29 (OL-22). *RED-7, consent under dependence:* care is never made to depend on agreeing to be monitored, and an alternative is offered; consent is given per session; the data stays on the clinic's device, is erased when the purpose ends, and has no second use. *RED-17 (new):* no personal data enters the training of a model, since a trained model cannot forget (SIM-QA-31 RED-11). *RED-15 (new), accountability:* the audit of a practitioner by outside peers (SIM-QA-32) and an inquiry into a dangerous practitioner need records that the patients control. With consent or without names they remain possible; a pattern of harm across patients who do not consent stays hidden. Recorded as a cost of the rule.
* **Point 6, see, correct, erase: `NARROWED`.** *RED-8:* correction is made by a note added, since ledgers are append-only. The closed list of what is not erased is long and each entry needs a time: the row in the civic register (for life), the record that a person served (for the hiatus), relations of care (SIM-QA-36), findings of a tribunal (SIM-QA-33), the escrowed links, what was published under a person's own name (the knowledge stays, Art. 1.5.2; the name can be withdrawn). *RED-16 (new), data about several persons:* a household, a relation of care, a complaint. One person's erasure or access touches another; a person accused must see the complaint, and a complainant may need protection (SIM-QA-33 RED-9). A rule for shared data is needed and is not proposed here. *Who answers:* the holder; a refusal goes to the Local Mediation Panel.

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-11, the state's access.** Under point 3. During the transition the data-protection and procedural law of the place applies to every record a node holds (SIM-QA-40 RED-16). What is not held cannot be handed over.
* **RED-12, the file as personal data.** Under point 4.
* **RED-13, who is named in a public function. Open.** Section 5.1. Whether drawn members are named during their mandate and whether each member's vote is public is not decided anywhere. Named members with public votes can be pressed or rewarded (Art. 4.1.3); unnamed members with secret votes answer to nobody. For the maintainer (D-41.8).
* **RED-14, persons and associations as profilers.** Under point 1.
* **RED-15, accountability.** Under point 5.
* **RED-16, shared data.** Under point 6.
* **RED-17, models.** Under point 5.

#### **5.4 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not article text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the seven points below; **`RED_WINS`** in small nodes against neighbours (physical), on "no body may combine" as the main protection, on point 4 as worded, on a detailed rule in Chapter I, and under current text on the reading of Art. 1.2.2.
1. **Principle.** Bodies and guilds take personal data only for a stated purpose, in the minimum amount, with no other use even by vote. Systematic profiling of a person by anyone is the act of Art. 3.4.4. Speech about the public acts of bodies is untouched.
2. **Ledgers.** Per node or facility, above a minimum cell, coarse and delayed below it. A limit on a dwelling or person is kept in the device.
3. **Proof held by the person** for shifts, hiatus and allowances; no body keeps the record.
4. **Pseudonyms.** One per use, linked only by the person's own key. An escrowed link for a closed list of uses that can harm others; opened by a drawn jury, logged, notified, counted; a case added only at 75%.
5. **Fab file.** Published, without the personal data it may carry. The maker's name by choice; the link under point 4.
6. **Care.** The record under the person's key; emergency access logged and notified; recovery through a named person. Monitoring never a condition of care, by session, on the device, with no second use. No personal data in the training of a model.
7. **See, note, erase.** With a closed list of what stays and for how long, and a rule for data shared by several persons.

#### **5.5 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
The principle is a right and stays in the Constitution; everything else is Organic Law. Chapter I has 19 words left with the accepted dockets, and six dockets ask for them (SIM-QA-24, 25, 27, 28, 34, 41); SIM-QA-35 would free some. Chapter VII, the other place, stands at 975 of 1,000 with SIM-QA-22 D-6 and D-7 and SIM-QA-29, 25 left, **within fifty**, and SIM-QA-31 also asks for it. Neither chapter can take the principle as the direction words it (about fifty words). The conflict goes to the maintainer.

#### **5.6 Compression Disclosure** *(instruction 8)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | "neurological monitoring, pharmacological manipulation" among the acts "permanently prohibited" | Art. 1.2.2 (Chapter I) | read as barred when involuntary; allowed with consent | **weakened or clarified**, depending on the reading chosen |
| 2 | "Physical ledgers and thermodynamic balance sheets are public" | Art. 3.6.1 | public per node or facility above a minimum cell; never per person | **qualified** |
| 3 | "all toolpaths, G-code, and CAM schematics executed on communal machinery are automatically published" | Art. 3.3.3 | personal data in a file is left out, or the file exempted | **weakened** |
| 4 | "the open civic register" | Art. 4.3.5 | endorsements under a pseudonym | **qualified** |
| 5 | "high-resolution dynamic neuro-telemetry" in clinical care | Art. 5.5.3 | only by session consent, on the device, never a condition of care | **qualified** |

#### **5.7 Residual Weaknesses**
* In a small node the neighbours know. No rule on records changes that.
* Public data can be joined by anyone, inside or outside.
* The two design proposals (limits kept in the device; proof held by the person) are named, not designed. If they do not work at the scale of a node, points 2 and 3 fall back to records kept by bodies.
* Less data means less accountability for practitioners and less to investigate with.
* Shared data and the naming of people in public functions have no rule.
* Not a specialist's privacy review; no cryptographer and no data-protection lawyer has read this.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-41.2 to D-41.9):**
* **D-41.2:** accept the seven points of section 5.4 as the revised direction, or change them.
* **D-41.3:** where the principle lives: (a) a short sentence in Chapter I (frozen after the lock; needs words freed by SIM-QA-35); (b) Chapter VII, amendable at 75% (needs a ceiling decision); (c) Organic Law first, as SIM-QA-35 advises for a new guarantee. The red team supports (b), resting on the "private intimacy" of Art. 1.2.3 which is already in Chapter I.
* **D-41.4:** the reading of Art. 1.2.2: "involuntary" governs neurological monitoring and pharmacological manipulation; fixed before the lock.
* **D-41.5:** no link outside the person's key by default; an escrowed link for a closed list; a case added only at 75%.
* **D-41.6:** the two design proposals as the direction, to be put to a specialist.
* **D-41.7:** personal data inside a fab-lab file left out or exempted (with OL-2 point 3).
* **D-41.8:** whether drawn members are named during a mandate, and whether each member's vote is public (RED-13).
* **D-41.9:** systematic profiling by persons and associations as an act under SIM-QA-33.

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 5** (D-41.4): B, every item of the list in Art. 1.2.2 depends on the absence of the person's consent; to be worded before the lock. **Item 10** (D-41.3): B, the data principle lives in Chapter VII, amendable at 75%, resting on Art. 1.2.3; the ceiling of Chapter VII (25 words left) still needs its own decision. **Still open:** D-41.2, D-41.5 to D-41.9.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 5):** a limit on a dwelling or a person is kept in the device, which reports only the total of its branch; no metering per dwelling that reports; Art. 3.4.4 stands without exception. This settles the first design proposal of D-41.6 as the direction, to a specialist; the second (proof held by the person) stays open. It does not decide D-41.2.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 6, [`conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](conflicts_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md)):** **the last sentence of point 6 of section 5.4 ("No personal data in the training of a model") is changed.** Personal data may train a model used in common, on these conditions, reached with the maintainer in three steps (consent; anonymisation; rare illnesses must not be left out): (1) the person's consent, and the data anonymised first; (2) a record is used once nobody can be singled out (the minimum cell); (3) rare cases are pooled across the whole federation, and where a person could still be recognised, the data is used only with a second, separate consent that states that risk; (4) consent is never a condition of care or of anything else; (5) the person is told that consent cannot be withdrawn once a model is trained; (6) the anonymised training data is open to checking and the original records stay with the person. The red team had recommended "never"; not adopted. *Residuals:* anonymisation can fail against a determined attacker, most in small groups (compare SIM-QA-29 RED-10, measured on synthetic data); a leak from a trained model cannot be repaired; pressure to consent in a small node. A specialist reads the anonymising method before any wording. This does not decide D-41.2 as a whole. Direction only.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 10):** the line between records is confirmed: acts of bodies and guilds are recorded and public; records about a person are held by that person, who may give them as evidence (messages, recordings and videos of their own included) and is never made to. Recording what is done to oneself is not the profiling of point 1; following a person to record them over time is. This does not decide D-41.2.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-41.2 to D-41.9; then the principle and the Organic Law draft as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass and one statement of each rule shared with SIM-QA-29 and SIM-QA-20; specialist privacy review and a cryptographer's reading before any merge.

## **SIM-QA-42: The Foundation**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: OASIS_STRESS_TESTED]` (Stage 2: RED_STRESS_TEST), opened 2026-11-20 on maintainer instruction with a working direction (section 4). Red team of the direction 2026-11-20 (section 5): **`NARROWED`** with seven revised points, every legal point subject to counsel; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on four items and under current text on two. Sections 1 to 4 are as recorded at opening. No statute text is drafted; `foundation/` is unchanged. First pass; no review by Swiss counsel; the legal points are questions, not answers.  
* **Originating Context:** maintainer gap review of 2026-11-20 (second batch), reading [`foundation/statutes_foundation_one.md`](../foundation/statutes_foundation_one.md) against the canon and the Class-0 invariants.  
* **Findings in the Statutes (Part A, checked):** the Board "reconstitutes itself by co-optation" (Art. 5.1); members serve "a renewable term" of three years, with no limit (Art. 5.2); election, renewal and removal of members are the Board's own resolutions at 75% (Art. 5.4); the drawn Advisory Assembly is "consultative" and gives "non-binding recommendations" (Arts. 4, 8); assets grow by donations, bequests, subsidies, royalties and "commercialization proceeds" (Art. 3.2); after the deposit, amendments to the Constitution require ratification by "the seated sortition assemblies pursuant to Chapter IV" (Art. 13.4), which will not yet exist. The civic compact (Part C, section 7) bars any direct employment outside, takes an irrevocable cession of commercial rights over everything a citizen makes, and states the commons rights as given "In reciprocal consideration"; Part C, section 8 ends: more than sixty cumulative days in the legacy world without a mandate "forfeits their housing usufruct lock".  
* **Canon and invariants they meet:** Art. 1.3.1 (life support "fully decoupled from wage labor, market exchange, debt bondage, or ideological compliance"), Art. 1.3.3, Art. 2.3.1 (no displacement into homelessness); Class-0 invariant 1 as worded in the project rules (no donations).  
* **Source notes (citation check):** (1) The ban on outside employment and the cession of rights are also in the Statutes themselves, Art. 2.2 ("Universal Civic IP Assignment", "in reciprocal exchange for"; "De-Commodification of Citizen Labor"), and in Part B, Art. 2.2 (g) and (h). Lightening the compact touches the purpose clause, not Part C alone. (2) Art. 2.4 reads: "zero speculative commercial business, issues zero private equity or speculative tokens, and charges zero pay-to-win or monetization gates on its canonical knowledge". It does not itself mention gifts, grants or licence fees; those are in Arts. 3.2 and 2.2. Art. 2.1 says "strictly non-commercial" beside the licensing monopoly of Art. 2.2. (3) The deposit of the Constitution as Annex I is in Art. 13.4; Art. 13.1 is the demarcation clause. The annex question concerns both. (4) Part B (the German deed) is numbered differently and differs in substance in places: co-optation and re-election are in Art. 5.2, the 75% list in Art. 5.6, the curator clause in Art. 5.8, and under Art. 8 the Board "kann" (may) set up the advisory body where Part A says it "establishes" one. (5) Art. 5.6 already lets the Verein's General Assembly "(or the sortition assembly of O.N.E.)" appoint when the Board falls below three. (6) The "drawn panel" of the direction is read as the Sortition Panel of Part C, section 3 (three to five, drawn from Verein contributors). (7) Invariant 1 is worded in private project rules and, publicly, in `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("accept fiat donations" listed among the things O.N.E. will never do) and `COMPARATIVE_ANALYSIS.md`; the canon has no such sentence.  
* **Articles:** Statutes Arts. 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 3.2, 4, 5.1, 5.2, 5.4, 5.6, 8, 10, 12, 13.1, 13.4; Part B Arts. 2, 5, 8, 12, 13; Part C sections 3, 7, 8; canon Arts. 1.3, 2.3, 8.5, 9.2, 9.5.  
* **Cross-references:** SIM-QA-35 (the error-correction docket: a transitional amending body before the deposit), SIM-QA-40 (Annex I contains articles that state law does not allow today; Art. 13.1 against the direction of D-40.1), SIM-QA-38 (inter-node equalisation), SIM-QA-23 (equality clause and foundation law).

### **1. The Friction**
* The body that will hold title to every node's land renews itself, removes its own members and is advised, not bound, by the drawn bodies. The Constitution it anchors allows none of that for a public body.
* The Statutes allow income that invariant 1, as worded, forbids.
* After the deposit, nobody can amend the Constitution until assemblies exist that the Constitution itself has yet to produce.
* The civic compact conditions what Chapter I gives without condition.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure under the Statutes as they stand or under the direction)*
* **RED-1, the self-renewing board:** co-optation, unlimited terms and self-removal keep one group in control of all title.
* **RED-2, the anchor nobody can move:** an error found after the deposit cannot be corrected; or it is corrected in fact by the Board and the supervisory authority, outside the rule (SIM-QA-35).
* **RED-3, income against the invariant:** donations and licensing proceeds contradict invariant 1 as worded; critics and the tax authority read the two texts together.
* **RED-4, the compact as servitude:** the employment ban, the cession of all rights, the sixty-day forfeiture and Tier 1 framed as consideration contradict Arts. 1.3 and 2.3 and may not bind a person at all.
* **RED-5, the annex refused:** the supervisory authority declines a deed whose annex contains Arts. 8.5, 9.2 and 9.5.
* **RED-6, exclusions that do not hold:** the clauses excluding a court-appointed curator and ordinary courts are void, and the fallback fails when needed.
* **RED-7, drawn seats captured:** the lot is taken among a small or packed association membership.
* **RED-8, the handover:** somebody must certify that "the first Bioregional Assembly of 101" exists; a premature or staged assembly appoints the Board.
* **RED-9, gifts with conditions:** once gifts and grants are allowed, donors shape the work.
* **RED-10, lot against duty:** a drawn member is unwilling or unable to carry a trustee's legal duties and liability.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H0, the Statutes as they stand.**
* **H1, the working direction of section 4,** each point a hypothesis until red-teamed.

### **4. Maintainer Working Direction (2026-11-20)** *(recorded as given; not red-teamed; not a decision on statute or canon text)*
* **D-42.1, direction:**
  1. Reword invariant 1 in the sense of Statutes Art. 2.4: nothing sold to persons, no tokens, no paid access; gifts, grants and outside licence fees allowed.
  2. Board: at most two terms; at least one seat in three drawn by lot among association members; removal also on proposal of the drawn panel.
  3. A transitional amending body, written before the deposit (linked to SIM-QA-35).
  4. Civic compact lightened: outside employment allowed; the sixty-day forfeiture deleted; cession of rights limited to works made with the node's common means; Tier 1 unconditional.
  5. Once the first Bioregional Assembly of 101 exists, it appoints the Board.
* **D-42.2, list for legal review, as questions** *(none answered here)*:
  1. Can statutes exclude a court-appointed curator (Art. 5.6)?
  2. Can statutes waive the ordinary courts (Art. 12)?
  3. Would Art. 13.1, with an annex (Art. 13.4) containing Arts. 8.5, 9.2 and 9.5 of the Constitution, pass the supervisory authority?
  4. Do a ban on outside employment and an irrevocable cession of all rights hold as personal commitments?

**Next action (Stage 1):** ~~red team D-42.1 against RED-1 to RED-10~~ (done, section 5); Swiss counsel on D-42.2 (**not done**; seven questions added in section 5.4); decide where invariant 1 is worded for the public (**open**, D-42.4); no change to `foundation/` before the maintainer's decision (**none made**).

### **5. Stage 2: Red Team of the Working Direction** *(2026-11-20, on maintainer instruction; reasoning on the text of the Statutes and the canon only; first pass; **no Swiss counsel has read it; every statement about what foundation, tax, labour or copyright law allows is a question, not an answer**; no statute or canon text drafted; `foundation/` unchanged)*

#### **5.1 Reading the Current Texts First** *(Jurist)*
* **Everything in this docket has to be settled before the notarial deed.** After it, the purpose clause changes only through the supervisory authority (Statutes Art. 10), and the Constitution is locked (Art. 13.4). It is the same timing as SIM-QA-35.
* **"Removal also on proposal of the drawn panel" proposes to the body it would remove.** Under Art. 5.4 the removal of a Board member is the Board's own resolution at 75%.
* **Invariant 1 as worded forbids what the canon provides.** The project rules list "consultancies"; canon Art. 9.3.3 names "specialized engineering consultations" as an outward activity of the External Trade Interface. The Statutes add tuition, elder care and workshop fees charged to outside persons by local entities (Part C, section 4), and the roadmap sells pre-paid vouchers to persons. "Nothing sold to persons" (direction, point 1) is not true of these texts either.
* **The licences and the monopoly contradict each other.** Art. 2.2 keeps works "free … for humanity's non-commercial use" and gives the Foundation "an exclusive legal monopoly" on commercial licensing. Part C, section 7 names the licences: AGPL-3.0, CERN-OHL, CC BY-SA. Those licences allow commercial use by anyone who keeps their terms. A work cannot be under them and reserved for non-commercial use. Canon Art. 1.5.2 also makes discoveries and designs "permanently exempt from monopolistic patents, intellectual property claims, and proprietary restrictions" (Chapter I).
* **The canon does not name the body that amends it.** Art. 4.5.1 requires 75% "for structural amendments to this Constitution" and says of whom nowhere. Statutes Art. 13.4 refers to "the seated sortition assemblies pursuant to Chapter IV". Even after assemblies exist, which one is competent is open.
* **The lock is as strong as Art. 10.** The Board, at 75% and with the supervisory authority, can request amendments of the Statutes; the Constitution is an annex of the deed. Whether an amendment of the annex is an amendment of the deed, needing the authority each time, is a question for counsel. If it is, the state approves every future amendment of the Constitution (RED-12).
* **The Founder keeps a right to request a change of purpose for twenty years** (Art. 10.2), beside the waiver of Art. 13.4.
* **Part B is the deed.** Where the German text differs from Part A (source note 4: "kann" against "establishes"; different numbering), the German text is what would be signed.
* **Art. 2.2 lets the Foundation hold "100% of the governance rights" in land vehicles in every country.** All title, worldwide, then depends on one board, one authority and one jurisdiction. Canon Art. 6.1.3 calls a single-point dependency in utilities unconstitutional, and Art. 7.4 disperses keys for the same reason.
* **Board members of a foundation are entered in a public register under their names** (to confirm with counsel). The project's authorship is pseudonymous by rule.

#### **5.2 Verdicts, Point by Point**
* **Point 1, invariant 1 reworded: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` on "nothing sold to persons".** *RED-3:* the rewording removes a contradiction that critics and a tax office would find. It needs two halves to be true (section 5.1). Inside: the project and the commons never sell, never gate access to knowledge, never track, never issue a tradable token. Toward the outside: nodes and the Foundation trade through the External Trade Interface (canon Art. 9.3) and may receive gifts, grants and licence fees. *RED-9, gifts with conditions:* every gift and grant above a threshold is published with its source; none carries a condition on governance or content; a cap on the share from one source; a conditional gift is refused. *Open:* whether income from licensing to large commercial buyers, if it becomes the main income, is compatible with tax exemption and with "strictly non-commercial" (Art. 2.1): for counsel. It also pulls the work toward what sells.
* **Point 2, the Board: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` on two items as worded.** *RED-1:* two terms and a drawn third reduce entrenchment; two thirds are still co-opted and the drawn members are always a minority. *Removal:* as worded it is empty (section 5.1). The decision must lie with a body outside the Board, on the drawn panel's proposal; whether statutes may give that power to such a body is for counsel. *RED-7, the roll:* a lot among "association members" is a lot among people the association's committee admitted. *Revised:* the roll is every adult resident of an affiliated node and every contributor of some standing; the draw is public and verifiable (canon Art. 4.3.1); below a minimum number on the roll the seat is drawn from sister nodes. *RED-10, lot against duty:* a Board member carries personal duties and liability. A drawn person may decline, the draw continues until one accepts, and the Foundation insures and instructs them. Those who accept are then partly self-chosen. Residence or signature requirements for members drawn abroad: for counsel. *Added:* the Advisory Assembly stays "consultative" (Art. 8) under the direction. The drawn panel's assent before the gravest acts (ending a node's usufruct, alienating land, amending the Statutes) would give it weight; whether that can bind a board is for counsel, and a delay with referral to the authority is the fallback. *The Founder's right* (Art. 10.2) should be waived or tied to the drawn panel's assent, or the lock has a private key.
* **Point 3, a transitional amending body: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` under current text on what comes after it.** *What it can touch:* never Chapter I (SIM-QA-35); elsewhere at 75%. *Capture:* an early community of a dozen people draws its amending body from the founders' friends. A minimum roll is needed, and below it nothing is amended, which is safer than a body of five. Urgent corrections required by mandatory law are the one case to provide for (SIM-QA-40). *After it:* the body it hands over to is not named in the canon (section 5.1); that must be written in Chapter IV before the lock. *RED-2 and RED-12:* if the full text is annexed, counsel must say whether the authority approves each amendment. An annex that identifies the Constitution by reference and by its own amending rule, instead of fixing its words, would avoid that and would also lower the risk of RED-5 (an annex containing Arts. 8.5, 9.2 and 9.5); whether the authority accepts a reference is the question.
* **Point 4, the compact lightened: `NARROWED`.** It removes the conflict with Arts. 1.3 and 2.3 (RED-4). *What changes with it:* members with outside wages hold money that others do not. Inside the node money buys nothing (SIM-QA-34 point 3), so the difference shows in personal possessions brought in from outside. The alternative, handing the wage over, is the condition the direction removes. *"Works made with the node's common means":* a person fed and housed by the commons makes everything with common means. A narrow test is needed: work done in assigned civic time or on shared equipment. *The cession itself* meets the contradiction of section 5.1: under the named licences anyone may already use the work commercially, so what is ceded has to be defined (the right to grant a different licence), limited in time or to the work, and reconciled with Art. 1.5.2. *"Tier 1 unconditional"* requires rewriting the purpose clause (Art. 2.2, "in reciprocal exchange for") and Part C, section 7 ("In reciprocal consideration"), before the deed. *Deleting the sixty-day forfeiture* leaves absence to canon Art. 2.1.3, which gives no duration for "abandoned" (SIM-QA-34, SIM-QA-36). *Not removed:* work done for outside clients through the Foundation may be treated as employment (SIM-QA-40 RED-15).
* **Point 5, the handover: `NARROWED`; `RED_WINS` as worded on one assembly.** *RED-8:* a foundation that holds land in several bioregions would have its Board appointed by the first one to seat an assembly. *Revised:* the Statutes define the appointing body now, by criteria anyone can check: all seated Bioregional Assemblies together, or a draw across them, with the existence of each certified from outside the nodes concerned. Whether a body without legal personality can be the appointing body is for counsel. *A point of canon:* an assembly that votes people into office holds an election, which Art. 4.1.2 abolishes for public bodies. A draw among candidates who meet published criteria is closer to the canon.
* **RED-5 and RED-6** are the questions of D-42.2 and are not answered here.

#### **5.3 New Vectors Found in This Pass**
* **RED-11, names in a public register. Open, personal to the maintainer.** Section 5.1. The first Board is appointed by the Founder; both are named in public records if counsel confirms the rule. This meets the zero-leak rule of the project.
* **RED-12, the state as gatekeeper of amendments.** Under point 3.
* **RED-13, licences against the monopoly, and against Art. 1.5.2. `RED_WINS` under current text.** Section 5.1. Either the works are under the named open licences and the "non-commercial" wording and the "exclusive monopoly" go, or they are not free in the sense the project states publicly. For the maintainer (D-42.8).
* **RED-14, the amending body unnamed.** Under point 3.
* **RED-15, Part A and Part B.** Section 5.1. Every decision here has to be written into the German deed; Part A is a description.
* **RED-16, the Founder's reserved right.** Under point 2.
* **RED-17, all title in one place. Open.** Section 5.1. A sanction, a court order or a captured Board in one country reaches every node. The alternative is a land trust per country, each with its own asset lock and each bound to the others by contract, with the Foundation holding knowledge and the common pool. For the maintainer (D-42.10).

#### **5.4 Questions Added for Counsel** *(continuing D-42.2; none answered)*
5. May the Statutes give a body outside the Board (a drawn panel, an association's assembly, a drawn assembly without legal personality) the power to appoint and to remove Board members, and to withhold assent from listed acts?
6. If the Constitution is annexed in full, does each amendment of it need the supervisory authority? Would an annex by reference to the Constitution and its amending rule be accepted?
7. Are Board members and the Founder named in public records, and what residence or signature requirements apply to members drawn abroad?
8. Can the Foundation hold exclusive commercial rights in works published under AGPL-3.0, CERN-OHL and CC BY-SA; and is tax exemption compatible with licensing income as a main source?
9. Is work done for outside clients through the Foundation or a local entity employment, with the contributions and insurance that follow?
10. Can the Founder waive the right of Art. 10.2 in the deed, or tie it to another body's assent?
11. Which text governs where Part A and Part B differ, and should Part A exist as a statute text at all?

#### **5.5 What Survives** *(the direction as revised; not statute text)*
Verdict for the docket: **`NARROWED`** with the seven points below, every legal point subject to counsel; **`RED_WINS`** as worded on "nothing sold to persons", on removal "on proposal", on a lot among association members and on one assembly appointing the Board; under current text on the licences (RED-13) and on the unnamed amending body (RED-14).
1. **Invariant 1** in two halves: inside, nothing sold, gated, tracked or tokenised; toward the outside, trade through the External Trade Interface and gifts, grants and licence fees that are published, unconditional and capped per source.
2. **Board.** Two terms at most. At least a third drawn from a roll of all adult residents of affiliated nodes and standing contributors, by public draw, with a minimum roll. Removal decided outside the Board on the drawn panel's proposal. The panel's assent for the gravest acts. The Founder's reserved right waived or tied to that assent.
3. **Amending.** An annex by reference if the authority accepts it. A transitional drawn body with a minimum roll, at 75%, never touching Chapter I, ending when the competent assembly exists. The canon names that assembly, before the lock.
4. **Compact.** Outside employment allowed. No forfeiture. Cession limited to works made in civic time or on shared equipment, defined so that it agrees with the open licences. Tier 1 never "in exchange"; the purpose clause rewritten before the deed.
5. **Handover.** The appointing body defined now: all seated Bioregional Assemblies together, by draw among candidates who meet published criteria, each assembly's existence certified from outside.
6. **Title.** One foundation for everything, or a land trust per country bound by contract (D-42.10).
7. **One text.** Part B carries every decision; Part A follows it.

#### **5.6 Placement and Word Count** *(instructions 10 and 10a)*
The Statutes are outside the Constitution's ceilings. Two sentences would enter the canon: the body that amends the Constitution (Chapter IV: 2,136 of 2,150, 14 left, **conflict**) and, if kept, a transitional rule (Chapter IX: 31 left, also claimed by SIM-QA-40). Invariant 1 is worded in the project rules and in two public files; no canon article states it.

#### **5.7 Disclosure of Points Changed** *(instruction 8 applied to the Statutes and the invariant; none is a canon point)*
| # | Point | Source | Change | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | no donations, no consultancies | Class-0 invariant 1 (project rules; `CONTRIBUTING.md`) | gifts, grants, licence fees and outward trade allowed under rules | **weakened** as an invariant; brought into line with canon Art. 9.3 and the Statutes |
| 2 | citizens "strictly prohibited" from outside employment | Statutes Art. 2.2; Part C, section 7 | removed | **dropped** |
| 3 | forfeiture of the dwelling after sixty days outside | Part C, section 8 | removed | **dropped** |
| 4 | cession of "everything" a citizen makes | Statutes Art. 2.2; Part C, section 7 | limited to civic time and shared equipment | **narrowed** |
| 5 | commons rights "in reciprocal exchange" | Statutes Art. 2.2; Part C, section 7 | unconditional | **removed as a condition** |
| 6 | Board "reconstitutes itself by co-optation"; renewable terms; self-removal | Statutes Arts. 5.1, 5.2, 5.4 | drawn seats, two terms, removal outside the Board | **limited** |
| 7 | "exclusive legal monopoly" on commercial licensing; "non-commercial use" | Statutes Art. 2.2 | to reconcile with the named licences and canon Art. 1.5.2 (D-42.8) | **open** |
| 8 | the Founder's right to request a change of purpose | Statutes Art. 10.2 | waived or tied to assent | **limited** |

#### **5.8 Residual Weaknesses**
* Nothing here is legal advice, and several points may not be possible under foundation law at all.
* A small early community cannot supply a meaningful lot; until it grows, the Board governs in fact.
* The drawn third is a minority, and those who accept a seat with personal liability are partly self-chosen.
* Licensing income from large buyers shapes what gets made.
* One foundation holding all title is one point of failure.
* The Founder and the first Board act before any of these checks exists.

**Maintainer decisions needed (D-42.3 to D-42.11):**
* **D-42.3:** accept the seven points of section 5.5 as the revised direction, or change them.
* **D-42.4:** invariant 1 in two halves, and where it is reworded (project rules, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `COMPARATIVE_ANALYSIS.md`).
* **D-42.5:** removal decided outside the Board; the drawn panel's assent for the gravest acts.
* **D-42.6:** the roll for the lot: all adult residents and standing contributors, with a minimum number.
* **D-42.7:** annex by reference or in full; the transitional amending body; the amending body named in Chapter IV before the lock (with SIM-QA-35).
* **D-42.8:** the exclusive commercial licence against the open licences and canon Art. 1.5.2: drop the exclusivity and the "non-commercial" wording, or keep them as a stated transitional derogation.
* **D-42.9:** the appointing body: all seated Bioregional Assemblies together, by draw among candidates who meet criteria.
* **D-42.10:** one foundation for all title, or a land trust per country bound by contract.
* **D-42.11:** the Founder's reserved right (Art. 10.2), and the public register against pseudonymous authorship (RED-11).

**Maintainer decisions 2026-11-20 (Chapter I batch, [`chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_decision_batch_2026-11-20.md); the maintainer adopted the red team's pick on every item; directions only, no wording decided):** **item 9** (D-42.8): A, Chapter I is not changed; the Statutes drop the exclusivity and the "non-commercial" wording; the Foundation may offer other licence terms for works whose authors assigned that right. `foundation/` is not edited yet: the German deed is redrafted after the other decisions and counsel. **Still open:** D-42.3 to D-42.7 (D-42.7 was assumed by the batch, not confirmed), D-42.9 to D-42.11.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (scope, D-42.7 in part):** a transitional amending body is written before the lock: drawn by lot, deciding at 75%, never touching Chapter I, with a minimum roll below which nothing is amended except a correction that mandatory law requires; it ends when the competent assembly exists. **Still open under D-42.7:** which assembly is the permanent amending body (the red team's lean, the highest tier seated at the time, at 75%, with the delay of SIM-QA-22 D-1, is **not red-teamed**); whether the Constitution is annexed in full or by reference (waits for counsel, question 6); the size of the minimum roll. Consequence recorded in [`pre_lock_consolidation_2026-11-20.md`](pre_lock_consolidation_2026-11-20.md): with a transitional body, the canon text of section 3.2 there need not be merged before the lock.

**Maintainer decision 2026-11-20 (conflicts batch, conflict 9; with SIM-QA-40 D-40.8):** the rule on legal organs and drawn bodies recorded in SIM-QA-40 is adopted as the direction; the finding of the outside panel may ground removal under point 2 of section 5.5. D-42.5 itself (removal decided outside the Board; the drawn panel's assent for the gravest acts) is **not decided by this** and stays subject to counsel: whether a removal power outside the Board is valid is among the eleven questions.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions D-42.3 to D-42.11; the eleven questions of D-42.2 and section 5.4 to Swiss counsel; then the German deed redrafted first and Part A after it, as hypothesis, with a second red-team pass; nothing in `foundation/` changes before that.

## **SIM-QA-43: Adults Who Cannot Decide**

* **Date Recorded:** 2026-11-20  
* **Epistemic Status:** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` (Stage 1: HYPOTHESIS), opened 2026-11-20 by maintainer decision (Chapter I batch 2, item 1e). Nothing below is red-teamed beyond what SIM-QA-36, 39 and 41 already recorded. No article text is drafted here; the Chapter I clauses are in [`chapter_i_draft_2026-11-20.md`](chapter_i_draft_2026-11-20.md).  
* **Originating Context:** found three times in the red team of 2026-11-20: nobody speaks for an adult who cannot (SIM-QA-36 RED-11); a short protective hold does not fit a person who will not regain lucidity (SIM-QA-39 RED-14); the consent sentence and the list of cases in which a person may be held leave such a person out (Chapter I drafts B and C).  
* **Closest current canon:** Art. 1.2.1 ("Every human being possesses absolute, unconditional sovereignty over their biological, physical, and cognitive substrate"), Art. 1.2.2, Art. 1.2.3; Art. 5.5 (mental health); Art. 5.6.1 (elder accompaniment as care); Art. 2.3 (no displacement). No article on incapacity.  
* **Constitutional Articles:** Arts. 1.2, 1.3.2, 2.3, 5.5, 5.6, 8.5.3; SIM-QA-22 D-5 (geriatric care); SIM-QA-29 (Advocates; exemption from the draw "on request of the person, or of their legal representative with a medical opinion"); SIM-QA-36 (named persons); SIM-QA-39 (protective hold); SIM-QA-41 (medical record under the person's key).
* **Already decided (Chapter I batch 2, 2026-11-20), not reopened here:** who decides (the named person, then those who live with the person, then a person drawn by lot); emergency treatment when nobody can be asked, unless a refusal is known; a present refusal prevails for anything done to the body; keeping a person from immediate harm by anyone, and a continuing restriction only after a jury drawn from outside finds the incapacity, with review, least restriction and an Advocate.

### **1. The Friction**
* The decisions above fix the principle in Chapter I. Everything that makes it work is missing: who assesses capacity, by what test, how often it is reviewed, what a continuing restriction may consist of, and what a place of care may and may not do.
* A finding of incapacity removes a person's own voice from decisions about them. It is the measure most open to abuse against the old, the odd and the inconvenient.

### **2. Preliminary Red Vectors** *(attack → expected failure; untested)*
* **RED-1, incapacity declared for convenience:** a family or a node has a person found incapable to settle a dispute, free a dwelling or end a nuisance.
* **RED-2, all or nothing:** a person able to decide some things and not others loses every decision.
* **RED-3, the finding that never ends:** review exists on paper; nobody asks for it.
* **RED-4, the locked ward:** "the least restriction that works" becomes a closed building.
* **RED-5, the named person who gains:** the person named stands to receive the dwelling or the possessions (SIM-QA-34, SIM-QA-36).
* **RED-6, refusal that kills:** under the decided rule a person with dementia who refuses an antibiotic is not treated.
* **RED-7, the jury that cannot judge:** lay jurors asked to find incapacity depend on one clinician's opinion.
* **RED-8, the draw:** the SIM-QA-29 draft speaks of a "legal representative"; the decided order (named person, co-inhabitants, a drawn person) must replace it.

### **3. Preliminary Hypotheses** *(untested)*
* **H1, capacity by decision:** capacity is found for a kind of decision, never for the whole person.
* **H2, review without a request:** every finding lapses after a period unless renewed by a new jury.
* **H3, restriction listed:** Organic Law lists what a continuing restriction may be; anything else is holding outside the Chapter I cases.
* **H4, no gain:** a named person who would gain from a decision does not take it.
* **H5, two opinions:** the jury hears two clinicians who have not conferred, and the person.

**Next action:** held at Stage 1 until the readers of the protective hold (SIM-QA-39, batch 2 item 2) have read it together with this subject; then red team.
