## **Cross-Docket Check 2026-11-13: SIM-QA-18, 19, 20, 21, 22** *(after the maintainer decisions of 2026-11-13; inconsistencies listed, none resolved here)*

**Resolved today and verified across dockets:** X-1 (panels of three per dispute: SIM-QA-19 Art. 4.2 and H6 clause, SIM-QA-21 Art. 4.2.1 and OL-10 point 8, OL-11 point 6); X-2 (notice, SIM-QA-19); X-3 (one outside-panel rule: Art. 4.2 closing paragraph, Art. 4.3.6, OL-10 point 8, OL-11 point 6); X-4 (OL-9.3, SIM-QA-18); X-5 and X-10 (Art. 4.3.2 and OL-9.11, SIM-QA-18; SIM-QA-20 section 9); X-7, X-8, X-9 (SIM-QA-21); X-11 (Art. 4.4.2, SIM-QA-20); I-3 (OL-11 point 5a, referenced from SIM-QA-20). Hiatus counted on length of service is the same rule for panels of three (SIM-QA-19) and Evidence Panels (SIM-QA-20).

**Still inconsistent or open:**
* **Y-1, the 15-seat fill figures disagree between dockets.** SIM-QA-19 section 8 (smallest fully seated node with H6: 60 adults at r = 0.6, 80 at r = 0.8) lets a refuser be summoned again the next month. SIM-QA-21 section 9.2 with the six-month deferral of SIM-QA-18 H2 gives 95 and 165; its one-month case reproduces 60 and 80. Both are synthetic; they differ only by how long a refusal lasts. The SIM-QA-19 row is not corrected here.
* **Y-2, X-6 (unchanged by decision).** SIM-QA-18 adds a shorter hiatus under 150 adults to Art. 4.3.3; SIM-QA-19 H6 keeps Art. 4.3.3 unchanged. Open until the SIM-QA-21 merge decision.
* **Y-3, "session in person" undefined at 1,001 seats** (SIM-QA-18 D-1). Every decision of substance at the global tier depends on it.
* **Y-4, OL-9.10 point 2.** Changed to allow a remote vote on raised hours, by consequence of X-10; the decision named complex certification, not raises. Pending confirmation.
* **Y-5, reach of OL-11 point 5a.** The maintenance certifier is the panel that serves councils of 15; proposed Art. 4.4.5 (SIM-QA-20) uses "certified routine maintenance" at every tier, and point 5a as drafted follows it there. Pending confirmation.
* **Y-6, the Triage Panel.** A panel of three with a standing monthly term (Art. 4.3.5, OL-9.5), outside X-1 and outside the Set B shares. Its service share and release are not stated anywhere.
* **Y-7, service rules for per-dispute panels and interpreters.** Panels of three and drawn interpreters serve by lot but have no tier share under Set B; Art. 4.3.2 as revised gives release in proportion to hours served, which applies by its terms, but no docket says how their hours are counted. X-11 covers Evidence Panel members only.
* **Y-8, the 24-hour evacuation check of Art. 8.1.2** is given to the Local Mediation Panel, now drawn per dispute from outside (X-1, X-3). Seating within 24 hours is not shown possible in sparse regions (SIM-QA-19 section 10.2).
* **Y-9, canon articles that still assign local matters to councils.** Art. 2.6.1 (neighborhood land-use adjustments) and Art. 3.6.1 (neighborhood utility monitoring) give these to Neighborhood Sortition Councils. Under SIM-QA-21 a node below 150 has no council; water and the local grid are internal matters of its assembly (OL-10 point 2), while land use goes to the centre council. No docket drafts the consequential wording of Arts. 2.6.1 and 3.6.1. Art. 2.6.2 (unanimous certification of usufruct revocation by the Local Mediation Panel) now means a per-dispute panel; whether it must be drawn from outside is not said.
* **Y-10, "plebiscites" in Art. 4.1.1.** SIM-QA-19 and SIM-QA-21 both add "node assemblies and plebiscites this Chapter provides". The only plebiscite in Chapter IV is the 4.2.1 escalation of 3-person panels, which SIM-QA-21 replaces, and the SIM-QA-19 plebiscite safeguards are withdrawn. "Plebiscites" may have no object after merge.
* **Y-11, OL-9.3 against the SIM-QA-11 calibration.** The restated budget (1.5 h a week at 15 seats) cuts council agenda capacity about tenfold against the model that set OL-9; not re-run.
* **Y-12, emergency chaining** (SIM-QA-21 C-6). Undrafted; X-5 makes ratification easier, which makes a chain of decrees cheaper.
* **Y-13, Chapter IV length.** Chapter IV is 948 words in the canon today, within the ceiling of 1,000 words set on 2026-11-13. The accepted-in-principle patches of SIM-QA-18 to 21 (Art. 4.2 closing paragraphs, Arts. 4.3.2, 4.3.6, 4.4.2–4.4.6, the new 4.2.1 sub-paragraph) add well over a thousand words; no docket has counted them against the chapter ceiling. Under the compression disclosure rule this is a conflict for the maintainer, not a reason to cut.
* **Y-14, SIM-QA-22 and SIM-QA-23 (reading to check, not a contradiction).** SIM-QA-22 lets "local communities choose the languages of their schools" and gives every child the right to schooling in the family's language; SIM-QA-23 Art. 1.8.4 reviews neutral rules with markedly unequal effect, language being a ground. A node that schools only in its majority language could be challenged under both. The two texts point the same way; the order of application is not stated.
* **Y-15, evidence for SIM-QA-18 section 4.** The refusal model there still uses a fixed three-month mediation panel drawn from the node; under X-1 and X-3 that panel takes no one from the node. The council results stand; the panel rows are evidence on the current text only.

**Next action:** maintainer decisions on Y-4 and Y-5; a word count of Chapter IV with every accepted patch (Y-13); consequential wording for Arts. 2.6.1, 3.6.1 and 4.1.1 (Y-9, Y-10) in SIM-QA-21; service rules for per-dispute panels, interpreters and the Triage Panel (Y-6, Y-7) in SIM-QA-18; SIM-QA-11 re-run (Y-11).

**Status after the second batch of maintainer decisions (2026-11-13):** Y-1 corrected (SIM-QA-19 section 11); Y-2 open until the SIM-QA-21 merge decision; Y-3 drafted as a hypothesis, `NARROWED` (SIM-QA-18 11.6); Y-4 confirmed (SIM-QA-18 11.1); Y-5 confirmed (SIM-QA-19 11.1); Y-6 and Y-7 drafted as OL-9.12 (SIM-QA-18 11.4); Y-8 drafted as a hypothesis, `NARROWED` (SIM-QA-19 11.2); Y-9 and Y-10 drafted (SIM-QA-21 10.3); Y-11 re-run, **council petition capacity not workable at the modelled rate** (SIM-QA-18 11.5); Y-12 drafted as a hypothesis, `NARROWED` (SIM-QA-18 11.7); Y-13 counted and split proposed, not applied (next section); Y-14 order of application stated (SIM-QA-23 8.4); Y-15 marked (SIM-QA-18 section 4). The deferral window is now three months in every docket that uses it (SIM-QA-18 H2 and Section 6, SIM-QA-19 section 8, SIM-QA-21 section 10).

**Addendum 2026-11-16 (after SIM-QA-20 Section 11 and SIM-QA-22 section 8; listed, none resolved):**
* **Z-1, release ledger against case pseudonyms.** SIM-QA-18 OL-9.12 point 5 counts hours served on a panel called by another node or bioregion in the release ledger of the calling body. SIM-QA-20 Decisions F and G record release without a stated reason and never record place. A ledger entry naming the calling bioregion tells the home node that a resident served an outside panel, and when. Either the ledger transfer is made in aggregate per period without naming persons, or F and G are read as overriding it. **Decided 2026-11-16 (SIM-QA-20 11.8 K-8):** the transfer is made in aggregate per period between bioregions, without naming persons or cases; OL-9.12 point 5 (SIM-QA-18) to read so when redrafted.
* **Z-2, Art. 7.5.3 and sealed model outputs.** SIM-QA-20 Section 6.2 met Art. 7.5.3 because anyone could read the passage a pre-check cited. Under Decision I only juries can. Chapter VII auditors (Arts. 7.5.3, 7.6.1) can test the model on synthetic filings but not on live ones. The reading of Art. 7.5.3 for sealed systems is not stated anywhere (SIM-QA-20 11.3 I-12). **Decided 2026-11-16 (K-8):** for a sealed Register, Art. 7.5.3 is met by open code and model, comprehension by the drawn jury, and public tests on synthetic filings (OL-9.7 extended point 8).
* **Z-3, Chapter IV ceiling, three claimants.** SIM-QA-20 revision 4 (2,113), SIM-QA-22 D-1 (+47) and SIM-QA-18 Y-12 with the Y-3 bound (+48) cannot all fit under 2,150 (2,208 together). Part D would save 18. For the maintainer. **2026-11-16 (K-6, K-7):** with the two bounds kept, 2,231 together; decision postponed until Y-12 and Y-3 are decided.
* **Z-4, "public record" in Art. 4.4.4.** "Written questions during deliberation are answered on the public record" stays public under Decision I. An answer that quotes sealed Register evidence would unseal it. A rule that answers cite the Register only as "criteria met" is needed. **Decided and drafted 2026-11-16 (K-8):** OL-9.7 extended point 16.
* **Z-5, SIM-QA-22 D-7 (OL-16 point 3) and SIM-QA-20 Decision D.** Both let a jury see an identity in closed session; consistent. Recorded so the two closed-session rules are drafted together.

Do not cut any point. Record a placement test next to the compression disclosure rule (board instruction 10). Red-team the test. | 20 | 4.4.2: continuity through earlier briefs (confirm or revise, and why) | OL-9.7 | — (procedure; no right or power) |
| 21 | 4.4.2: release and care replacement for panel members | covered by Art. 4.3.2, words only | civic service under Art. 4.3.2 |
| 22 | 4.4.2: relaxation order (place, then source diversity, then hiatus), each step logged; minimum pool | OL-9.7 | conflict-of-interest filter never relaxed |
| 23 | 4.4.6: evidence as a signed, tamper-evident public record (Arts. 7.3, 7.4) | OL-9.7 (technology) | — (Chapter VII applies by its own terms) |
| 24 | 4.4.6: examples of each kind of evidence | OL-9.7, words only | three kinds named |
| 25 | 4.4.6: reciprocal attestations in one category do not count | OL-9.7 | — (anti-gaming detail; see red team P-8) |
| 26 | 4.4.6: Zero-Knowledge Proof-of-Personhood named | OL-9.7 (technology) | one entry per person, proven without revealing identity |
| 27 | 4.4.6: open-model pre-check ("meets" / "to be verified", citation of passage, code for signatures, dates and counts, all filings to the jury where no model qualifies) | OL-9.7 | no model rejects an entry or decides a challenge; doubtful filings go to juries from outside the field |
| 28 | 4.4.6: waiting period; one challenge per ground | OL-9.7 | never for a proposal published before filing; anyone may challenge; eligible while pending |
| 29 | 4.4.6: replication across watersheds, key dispersion, offline draws | OL-9.7 (technology) | **New bound:** replicated so that no single site or key controls it |
| 30 | 4.5.4: worked examples (9 and 12 of 15; 61 and 76 of 101) | Organic Law, words only | the arithmetic rule |
| 31 | 4.5.4: "with the quorum fixed by Organic Law" for node assemblies | stated once, in 4.2.1 | quorum bound of row 1 |

*Points the test keeps in the Constitution that the default categories would have moved* (the test overrides the categories): the closed list of internal matters of 4.2.1 (shrinking it moves power to the centre); secret ballot on dwellings, newcomers and disputes; a chair drawn by lot for each session; the one-month and three-month notice floors of drawn mandates; one deferral without reason; orientation at a first mandate; written questions answered on the public record (an unrecorded answer is a staff framing channel, Art. 4.3.4); thresholds of the three kinds of evidence enacted together (otherwise one route, usually the diploma, can be favoured); "no plebiscite" in 4.3.6.

### **4. Red Team Against the Placement Test**
* **P-1, the council threshold** (row 4). A 60% majority raises it to 1,000 and hands most nodes' general matters to centre councils, or lowers it to 50 and brings back the fill failure. *Bound:* 100 to 200 adults, twelve months sustained. Residual: inside the band, the choice still moves power between nodes and centres; the X-8 evidence is synthetic.
* **P-2, the node quorum** (row 1). A 60% majority lowers it to a few percent so that an always-attending bloc decides (SIM-QA-21 RED-4). *Bound:* one third.
* **P-3, the service share** (row 12). A 60% majority makes the 1,001 tier full-time again, restoring the self-selection H7 was adopted to reduce (RED-2). *Bound:* never full-time. Residual: 95% of a week is not full-time; a numeric ceiling (for example one half, the Set B maximum) would be tighter. *Open.*
* **P-4, mandate ranges** (row 5). A 60% majority sets ten-year mandates at the global tier, a semi-permanent class (Art. 4.1.2). *No bound in the accepted texts or in this proposal; open:* a constitutional maximum (the canon's longest mandate today is two years).
* **P-5, the renewal count K** (row 15). A 60% majority sets K = 1, so a second reading is held by the same people. *Bound:* a majority.
* **P-6, filter definitions** (row 17). A 60% majority defines "conflict of interest" to exclude critics (anyone who published on the matter). *Bound:* countable facts, never opinion. Residual: "signed a contribution" can be stretched to "signed a petition"; review under Art. 4.6.
* **P-7, admission criteria** (already in OL-9.7). Thresholds that only one guild meets. The twelve-month delay and "never removes an admitted entry" sit only in OL-9.7, so a 60% majority can repeal them. *Open:* both are candidates for constitutional bounds under the test; not added here because neither was in an accepted constitutional text.
* **P-8, reciprocal attestations** (row 25). A 60% majority repeals the rule and rings of two count again. Under the test this is a borderline case: it is a detail, but repealing it lets a group gain weight in the Register. Moved, with the residual stated.
* **P-9, the closed list of remote votes** (row 11). A 60% majority lists a decision of substance as "procedural" to move it online. *Bound:* substance always in person; the form check of OL-9.11. Residual: the line between procedure and substance is drawn by the same 60% majority (SIM-QA-18 10.3, X-10).
* **P-10, petition parameters** (Art. 4.3.5, already "fixed by Organic Law" in the merged canon). A 60% majority raises the co-endorsement share to a level no petition reaches, so the agenda closes. *No bound in canon today; open:* a constitutional ceiling on the share. Found by the test, not created by the move.
* **P-11, the test itself is over-inclusive.** "Could someone lose a right or a group gain power?" can be answered yes for almost any rule. Applied strictly, as here, the test keeps most of the accepted text in the Constitution, which is why Chapter IV ends at 2,035 words. *Guard:* the default categories decide when the answer is only speculative; every "stays" against the categories is listed above for the maintainer.
* **P-12, the ratchet has no direction for some moves.** "Organic Law may strengthen a guarantee, never weaken it" cannot rank a change that strengthens one guarantee and weakens another (adding a filter protects against capture and narrows the pool; SIM-QA-19 I-5). *Guard:* the bound in the Constitution decides, not the ratchet. Residual: a review jury still decides contested cases.
* **P-13, a number that is its own bound** (the thirty-day sunset of 4.5.3, the hiatus of twice the mandate, 75% and 60%). Moving it saves no words, because the bound repeats it. These stay.

**Verdict on the test:** **`BLUE_HOLDS`** as a protection rule, with three open bounds (P-3 numeric ceiling, P-4 mandate maximum, P-10 co-endorsement ceiling) and one open candidate pair (P-7). It does not deliver the word ceiling.

### **5. Why Chapter IV Still Exceeds the Ceiling** *(+1,035 words; nothing cut)*
* The accepted patches add guarantees, not procedure. Of the 1,916 words they add, the test moves 807 and leaves the rest because each sentence carries a right, a prohibition or a power: the node-assembly rules of 4.2.1 (224 words after the test), the service and in-person rules of 4.3.2 (218), the second reading of 4.3.6 (146), and the Evidence Panel and Register guarantees of 4.4.2 and 4.4.6 (362 together).
* The bounds the test requires add back about 65 words (rows 1, 4, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17, 29).
* Under the compression disclosure rule, closing the gap is a maintainer decision. Options, listed only: waive the ceiling for Chapter IV; divide Chapter IV into two chapters (for example, the sortition architecture, and drawn expert evidence with the Practitioner Register); move Art. 4.4.6 to the chapter on digital infrastructure, next to Arts. 7.2 to 7.4 that it already cites; or drop named points, each recorded with date and reason. Every option except the last keeps every point. None is applied, and no other chapter is touched.

### **6. Open Decisions for the Maintainer**
1. ~~The split itself (rows 1–31), point by point.~~ **Accepted in principle 2026-11-13, as a block** (section 7.8).
2. ~~The seven new bounds (rows 1, 4, 12, 15, 16, 17, 29).~~ **Accepted in principle 2026-11-13** (section 7.8).
3. The open bounds P-3, P-4, P-10 and the candidates P-7.
4. ~~How to meet the ceiling (section 5).~~ Decided 2026-11-13: own ceiling for Chapter IV (Y-16, section 7.8).

### **7. Y-16: One Chapter IV Under Its Own Ceiling, with a Total Ceiling** `[STATUS: SIMULATION_HYPOTHESIS]` *(maintainer hypothesis 2026-11-13; **accepted 2026-11-13, section 7.8**; canon untouched; the IV-A / IV-B trial is dropped by maintainer instruction and not recorded)*

#### **7.1 The Hypothesis**
Chapter IV stays one chapter at about 2,035 words (Part B). The single ceiling becomes a rule: a general ceiling of 1,000 words per chapter; own ceilings for Chapter I and Chapter IV, the two chapters that hold the guarantees the placement test keeps in the Constitution (starting point 1,200 and 2,100); a ceiling on the total words of the Constitution (starting point 11,000).

#### **7.2 Counts** *(tokens containing a letter or a digit, headings included)*
| Text | Today (canon) | With every accepted patch | Under the 1,000 ceiling? |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Title block | 55 | 55 | — |
| Chapter I | 808 | 1,134 (Art. 1.8 with 1.8.6: +316; Art. 1.4.1 phrase: +10) | no |
| Chapter II | 869 | 903 (Y-9: Arts. 2.6.1 +21, 2.6.2 +13) | yes |
| Chapter III | 886 | 922 (Y-9: Art. 3.6.1 +36) | yes |
| Chapter IV | 926 | 2,035 (Part B; 2,842 before the placement test) | no |
| Chapter V | 855 | 986 (SIM-QA-22 Art. 5.3.3: +131) | yes, 14 left |
| Chapters VI–XI | 765 / 712 / 862 / 817 / 870 / 839 | unchanged | yes |
| **Total** | **9,264** | **10,900** (11,707 with the consolidated Chapter IV) | — |

*Consequences of the counts.* The Chapter V waiver of SIM-QA-22 is no longer needed under the 1,000 ceiling (986). The Chapter I waiver of SIM-QA-23 would be replaced by the own ceiling. With patches, the nine general chapters hold 7,676 words.

#### **7.3 Draft Rule: Addition to Board Instruction 10** *(proposed; not in force; the board carries it as 10a, marked as a proposal)*
"10a. **Word ceilings.** (1) Each chapter holds at most 1,000 words, counted as tokens containing a letter or a digit, headings included. (2) Chapter I holds at most [1,200] and Chapter IV at most [2,150] words, because they carry the rights and the powers of bodies that the placement test keeps in the Constitution. No other chapter has an own ceiling; one is added only by a recorded maintainer decision, after the whole chapter has been through the placement test and still exceeds 1,000 words with no point that can be moved. (3) The Constitution as a whole holds at most [11,500] words. (4) No docket raises the ceiling it needs; a ceiling changes only by a separate maintainer decision, with a recount of every chapter. (5) A chapter within fifty (50) words of its ceiling receives new detail in Organic Law by default; text that the placement test keeps in the Constitution is never moved to meet a ceiling, and the conflict goes to the maintainer under instruction 8. (6) A ceiling is never met by dropping a point."

*Changes to the starting numbers, and why.*
* **Chapter I, 1,200: kept.** With patches it holds 1,134, leaving 66.
* **Chapter IV, 2,100 → [2,150].** 2,100 holds for Part B with Y-3 and Y-12 (2,083) but not with the readability layout of section 7.6 as well (2,112); 2,150 holds every case reported here with about 40 words left (7.5). If the readability layout is not adopted, 2,100 holds.
* **Total, 11,000 → [11,500].** With patches the Constitution holds 10,900; with the readability layout, Y-3 and Y-12, about 10,977, which leaves 23 words under 11,000. At 11,000, with Chapters I and IV at their own ceilings, the nine general chapters could hold only 7,595 to 7,645 words together, less than the 7,676 they hold with patches today: the total would force cuts elsewhere, the compression that produced the losses of SIM-QA-22 section 7. At 11,500 the nine chapters can hold up to 8,095 (average 899), about 420 above today, and no third own ceiling of any size fits without cuts elsewhere (7.4, T-1).

#### **7.4 Red Team**
* **T-1, another chapter claims the same exemption. `NARROWED`.** Chapter X (node and bioregional rights, envelopes), Chapter III (the Tier 1 floor and thermodynamic thresholds) and Chapter V (care and languages, 986 words) all hold guarantees the placement test keeps. "Holds guarantees" alone fits almost every chapter. *Guard:* point (2): an own ceiling only after a full placement test of that chapter still leaves it above 1,000 with nothing movable, by recorded decision; the list is closed. *Does the total ceiling stop it?* At 11,500: only in part. With Chapters I and IV full, the general chapters share about 420 words of growth, so a third own ceiling of, say, 1,300 fits only if the others stay still; a large exemption (Chapter X at 2,000) does not fit without cuts elsewhere. At 11,000: yes, completely, but it also stops ordinary growth and pushes toward cuts (7.3). Residual: the total stops large claims, not small ones; small claims are stopped by point (2) only.
* **T-2, a race for the remaining headroom. `NARROWED`.** Under a total ceiling, the first docket to use the last 400 words wins, and later dockets with stronger claims find no room. *Guard:* point (4) separates the need from the decision; the maintainer allocates headroom when deciding, not the drafter. Residual: order of arrival still matters in practice.
* **T-3, ceiling creep. `NARROWED`.** 2,150 becomes 2,300 docket after docket, as the 900 ceiling became 1,000 and the Chapter V waiver came first. *Guard:* point (4). Residual: the maintainer can always raise a ceiling; the rule makes each raise visible, it cannot forbid it.
* **T-4, chapter shopping. `BLUE_HOLDS` with guard.** Text is drafted into Chapter IV because it has room (a right placed in Art. 4.3 instead of Chapter I). *Guard:* placement by subject matter under instruction 10; the own ceiling covers Chapter IV's own subject only. Residual: borderline subjects (equal protection in drawing the lot) can sit in either.
* **T-5, the Organic Law default as a leak. `NARROWED`.** Point (5) sends new detail to Organic Law near a ceiling; a drafter could call a guarantee "detail" to fit. *Guard:* the placement test prevails; a guarantee the test keeps is never moved to meet a ceiling. Residual: classifying a clause as detail is itself the judgement the test makes.
* **T-6, the total ceiling forces compression of other chapters. `NARROWED`.** If the total is reached, the cheapest words to remove are in other chapters' older text, where nobody is watching. *Guard:* point (6) and instruction 8: no point is dropped to meet a ceiling; a breach goes to the maintainer. Residual: words can be shortened without dropping a point, and "point" is judged by the drafter first.
* **T-7, counting games. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** Numerals in parentheses ("fifteen (15)") count twice; labels and list markers with letters count; markers without letters do not. *Guard:* point (1) fixes the method. Residual: the method rewards dropping numerals in parentheses, which the canon's style requires; not a defect of the rule.
* **T-8, readability and translation. `OPEN`, recorded.** A 2,150-word chapter, translated into thirteen languages, is long to read and costly to keep in parity. The sub-item layout of 7.6 helps reading, not length.
* **T-9, waivers become ceilings. `BLUE_HOLDS`.** The ad hoc waivers of SIM-QA-22 and SIM-QA-23 are replaced by named, counted ceilings; a waiver by paragraph is no longer needed.

**Verdict:** **`NARROWED`**. The rule holds against large exemptions through the total ceiling and against small ones only through the closed list of point (2). The total of 11,000 stops everything, including the growth the accepted patches already need; 11,500 stops large claims and leaves room for ordinary growth.

#### **7.5 Headroom: Y-3 and Y-12 in Chapter IV**
* **Y-12** adds one sentence to Art. 4.5.3: **28 words**.
* **Y-3** is drafted as Organic Law (OL-9.13). Under the placement test, the meaning of "in person" is a guarantee: a 60% majority that defined it as a video call would empty the in-person rule of Art. 4.3.2. Chapter IV therefore needs a bound, for example: "A session in person gathers members physically, in one hall or in regional halls linked together as Organic Law provides." **20 words.**

#### **7.6 Readability: the Four Longest Paragraphs as Sub-Items** *(proposal; text in [`chapter_iv_placement_2026-11-13.md`](chapter_iv_placement_2026-11-13.md), Part C)*
Each paragraph keeps its lead sentence and splits into labelled sub-items. No point is added or dropped. A word-by-word comparison of each paragraph against Part B finds every word of Part B present, except two prepositions lost to rewording ("on the subject matters the jurors name" → "the panel covers the subject matters the jurors name"; "with one set of admission criteria" → "one set of admission criteria applies") and "any one of them" → "any one resident", which keeps the sub-item clear on its own. The added words are the labels, the restated subjects, and "raised" in "with release raised in proportion" (Art. 4.3.2), the wording of the accepted SIM-QA-18 text, which Part B had shortened to "with release in proportion".

#### **7.7 Decisions for the Maintainer**
1. Adopt Y-16 or not.
2. The numbers: Chapter I [1,200]; Chapter IV [2,100] or [2,150]; total [11,000] or [11,500].
3. Points (2) to (6) of the draft rule 10a, each one.
4. The readability layout of 7.6, and with it "with release raised in proportion".
5. Whether Y-3, if accepted, carries the 20-word bound in Chapter IV.
6. Retiring the Chapter V and Chapter I waivers if the rule is adopted.
7. The open decisions of section 6 of this proposal (the split, the new bounds, P-3, P-4, P-7, P-10) stand unchanged.

#### **7.8 Maintainer Decisions (2026-11-13)**
* **Y-16 adopted.** Chapter IV stays one chapter under its own ceiling.
* **Numbers:** Chapter I **1,200**; Chapter IV **2,150**; total **11,500**. The general ceiling stays 1,000.
* **Rule 10a, points (1) to (6), adopted as drafted.** Board instruction 10a is in force from this date; instruction 9 remains the general ceiling within it.
* **Readability layout (7.6) adopted** as the working text of the four paragraphs, with "with release raised in proportion" in Art. 4.3.2. Working Chapter IV: Part B with Part C, **2,064 words**, 86 under 2,150.
* **Y-3 bound:** if Y-3 is accepted, Chapter IV carries the 20-word bound on the meaning of "in person". Y-3 itself remains a hypothesis.
* **Waivers retired:** the Chapter V waiver (SIM-QA-22) and the Chapter I waiver (SIM-QA-23), replaced by rule 10a. Chapter V has 14 words of headroom; any further addition there needs its own decision.
* **Y-13 split (rows 1–31) accepted in principle as a block**, with the clauses listed in section 3 going to the Organic Law destinations named there.
* **The seven new bounds accepted in principle:** quorum of node assemblies never below one third of adult residents; council threshold between 100 and 200 adults, sustained twelve months both ways; service never full-time; second reading only after a majority of the council is renewed; irreversibility certification within thirty days; Evidence Panel filters on countable facts, never on opinion; the Register replicated so that no single site or key controls it.
* **Still open:** the missing bounds P-3 (numeric ceiling on the service share), P-4 (maximum length of a drawn mandate), P-10 (ceiling on the co-endorsement share for petitions) and the P-7 pair (twelve-month delay for criteria changes; no removal of admitted entries) are not decided. Y-3 and Y-12 remain hypotheses.
* Accepted in principle is not merged. The canon and the merged Annex are unchanged; the Organic Law destinations of the split are drafts.

*Headroom after the decisions:* Chapter IV 2,064 of 2,150 (86 words); with Y-12 and the Y-3 bound, 2,112 (38 left). Chapter I 1,134 of 1,200 (66). Total with accepted patches and the layout 10,929 of 11,500 (571).
